### Question 1
**Subject:** Indian Polity & Governance | **Topic:** Making of the Constitution

Match List-I (Committees of Constituent Assembly) with List-II (Chairmen) and select the correct answer using the codes given below:

**List-I (Committee)**
A. Steering Committee  
B. Provincial Constitution Committee  
C. Union Powers Committee  
D. Fundamental Rights Sub-Committee  

**List-II (Chairman)**
1. Jawaharlal Nehru  
2. Sardar Vallabhbhai Patel  
3. Dr. Rajendra Prasad  
4. J.B. Kripalani  

**Options:**
A. A-3, B-2, C-1, D-4  
B. A-2, B-3, C-1, D-4  
C. A-3, B-1, C-2, D-4  
D. A-4, B-2, C-1, D-3  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:** 
  - Steering Committee was chaired by Dr. Rajendra Prasad (also Rules of Procedure Committee).
  - Provincial Constitution Committee was chaired by Sardar Vallabhbhai Patel.
  - Union Powers Committee and Union Constitution Committee were chaired by Pt. Jawaharlal Nehru.
  - Fundamental Rights Sub-Committee was chaired by J.B. Kripalani (while Minorities Sub-Committee was chaired by H.C. Mukherjee, and the main Advisory Committee was under Sardar Patel).
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** UPPSC repeatedly traps candidates between the main Advisory Committee on Fundamental Rights & Minorities (headed by Sardar Patel) and its Sub-Committees (FR Sub-committee under J.B. Kripalani, Minorities Sub-committee under H.C. Mukherjee).

### Question 2
**Subject:** Geography | **Topic:** Mountain Passes of India

Match List-I (Mountain Pass) with List-II (State / UT) and select the correct answer:

**List-I (Pass)**
A. Shipki La  
B. Lipulekh  
C. Nathu La  
D. Zoji La  

**List-II (State / UT)**
1. Sikkim  
2. Himachal Pradesh  
3. Ladakh  
4. Uttarakhand  

**Options:**
A. A-2, B-4, C-1, D-3  
B. A-2, B-1, C-4, D-3  
C. A-4, B-2, C-1, D-3  
D. A-3, B-4, C-1, D-2  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - **Shipki La:** Located in Himachal Pradesh (Kinnaur district); the Sutlej river enters India through this pass from Tibet.
  - **Lipulekh:** Located at the tri-junction of Uttarakhand (Pithoragarh), India, Nepal, and China; vital for the Kailash Mansarovar pilgrimage route.
  - **Nathu La:** Located in Sikkim (East Sikkim); historic Silk Route branch connecting India with Tibet, reopened in 2006.
  - **Zoji La:** Located in the UT of Ladakh (connecting Srinagar with Kargil and Leh on Great Himalayas).
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** Note that Zoji La is in Ladakh (post-2019 reorganization), not J&K UT! Banihal pass is in J&K (Pir Panjal range), whereas Zoji La connects Kashmir valley with Ladakh.

### Question 3
**Subject:** Modern Indian History | **Topic:** Gandhian Movements Chronology

Arrange the following events of Mahatma Gandhi's early political career in chronological order:
1. Ahmedabad Mill Strike
2. Champaran Satyagraha
3. Kheda Satyagraha
4. Rowlatt Satyagraha

Select the correct answer using the codes given below:

**Options:**
A. 2 — 1 — 3 — 4  
B. 2 — 3 — 1 — 4  
C. 1 — 2 — 3 — 4  
D. 2 — 1 — 4 — 3  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  1. **Champaran Satyagraha (April 1917):** First Civil Disobedience in India against Tinkathia system in Bihar (invited by Raj Kumar Shukla).
  2. **Ahmedabad Mill Strike (February–March 1918):** First Hunger Strike by Gandhi over plague bonus dispute.
  3. **Kheda Satyagraha (March 1918):** First Non-Cooperation movement by Gandhi due to crop failure and revenue remission demand in Gujarat.
  4. **Rowlatt Satyagraha (April 1919):** First all-India mass protest against the Anarchical and Revolutionary Crimes Act (Rowlatt Act).
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** Mnemonic: **CAK** (Champaran -> Ahmedabad -> Kheda). Ahmedabad Mill Strike preceded Kheda Satyagraha by a few weeks in early 1918!

### Question 4
**Subject:** General Science (Biology) | **Topic:** Vitamins & Deficiency Diseases

Match List-I (Vitamin) with List-II (Deficiency Disease / Feature) and select the correct answer:

**List-I (Vitamin)**
A. Vitamin B1 (Thiamine)  
B. Vitamin B3 (Niacin)  
C. Vitamin D (Calciferol)  
D. Vitamin B12 (Cyanocobalamin)  

**List-II (Disease / Feature)**
1. Pellagra (4D syndrome)  
2. Beri-Beri  
3. Pernicious Anemia & contains Cobalt  
4. Rickets in children & Osteomalacia in adults  

**Options:**
A. A-2, B-1, C-4, D-3  
B. A-1, B-2, C-4, D-3  
C. A-2, B-4, C-1, D-3  
D. A-3, B-1, C-4, D-2  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - **Vitamin B1 (Thiamine):** Deficiency causes Beri-Beri (affects cardiovascular and nervous systems; common in populations consuming polished rice).
  - **Vitamin B3 (Niacin):** Deficiency causes Pellagra, characterized by dermatitis, diarrhea, dementia, and death (4Ds).
  - **Vitamin D (Calciferol):** Synthesized in skin via sunlight; deficiency causes Rickets (bow legs) in children and Osteomalacia in adults.
  - **Vitamin B12 (Cyanocobalamin):** Contains the metallic element Cobalt; essential for RBC maturation; deficiency causes Pernicious Anemia.
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** UPPSC frequently asks which vitamin contains Cobalt (Cyanocobalamin / B12). Remember also: Vitamin C is Ascorbic acid (heat-sensitive, lost on cooking), and Vitamin K (Phylloquinone) is needed for prothrombin synthesis in blood clotting.

### Question 5
**Subject:** Uttar Pradesh Special | **Topic:** One District One Product (ODOP)

Match List-I (District of UP) with List-II (ODOP Product) and select the correct answer:

**List-I (District)**
A. Bhadohi  
B. Kannauj  
C. Firozabad  
D. Siddharthnagar  

**List-II (ODOP Product)**
1. Glassware & Bangles  
2. Handmade Carpets (Dhurries)  
3. Kalanamak Rice ("Buddha's Rice")  
4. Attar (Perfume) & Essential Oils  

**Options:**
A. A-2, B-4, C-1, D-3  
B. A-4, B-2, C-1, D-3  
C. A-2, B-1, C-4, D-3  
D. A-3, B-4, C-1, D-2  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - **Bhadohi (Sant Ravidas Nagar):** Known globally for hand-knotted Carpets (GI tagged).
  - **Kannauj:** Known as "Perfume City of India" (Itra/Attar industry from rose, jasmine, and mitti attar).
  - **Firozabad:** Known as "Suhag Nagari" for Glass bangles and glassware.
  - **Siddharthnagar:** Famous for aroma-rich Kalanamak Rice (often termed Buddha ka Prasad).

### Question 6
**Subject:** Environment & Ecology | **Topic:** Aquatic Ecosystem & Eutrophication

Given below are two statements, one is labelled as Assertion (A) and the other as Reason (R):

**Assertion (A):** In a water body suffering from eutrophication, the Biochemical Oxygen Demand (BOD) increases sharply.  
**Reason (R):** Excessive proliferation of algae and phytoplankton increases the rate of decomposition, which rapidly consumes dissolved oxygen.

Select the correct answer from the codes given below:

**Options:**
A. Both (A) and (R) are true and (R) is the correct explanation of (A)  
B. Both (A) and (R) are true, but (R) is not the correct explanation of (A)  
C. (A) is true, but (R) is false  
D. (A) is false, but (R) is true  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - Eutrophication occurs due to nutrient enrichment (especially nitrates and phosphates from agricultural runoff and domestic sewage).
  - This leads to algal blooms. When algae die, decomposers (aerobic bacteria) break down the organic matter and consume high quantities of Dissolved Oxygen (DO).
  - Therefore, Biochemical Oxygen Demand (BOD)—which measures the amount of oxygen required by aerobic microorganisms to decompose organic matter—surges dramatically, leading to hypoxia and death of aquatic fauna.
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** Inverse relationship trap: High BOD means Low Dissolved Oxygen (DO) and HIGH organic pollution. UPPSC often flips this by asking: "Does high BOD mean clean water?" No, high BOD indicates heavily polluted water!

### Question 7
**Subject:** Indian Economy | **Topic:** Poverty Estimation Committees

Arrange the following committees associated with Poverty Estimation in India in chronological order:
1. Suresh Tendulkar Committee
2. Y.K. Alagh Committee
3. D.T. Lakdawala Committee
4. C. Rangarajan Committee

Select the correct answer from the codes given below:

**Options:**
A. 2 — 3 — 1 — 4  
B. 3 — 2 — 1 — 4  
C. 2 — 1 — 3 — 4  
D. 1 — 2 — 3 — 4  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  1. **Y.K. Alagh Committee (1979):** Defined official poverty line based on calorie intake (2400 kcal/day in rural areas and 2100 kcal/day in urban areas).
  2. **D.T. Lakdawala Committee (Task Force 1989, submitted 1993):** Recommended state-specific poverty lines using CPI-AL (rural) and CPI-IW (urban).
  3. **Suresh Tendulkar Committee (constituted 2005, submitted 2009):** Shifted away from calorie anchor to a uniform consumption basket including expenditure on health and education (Mixed Reference Period - MRP). Set poverty line at Rs 27/day rural, Rs 33/day urban (21.9% poverty in 2011-12).
  4. **C. Rangarajan Committee (constituted 2012, submitted 2014):** Modified Mixed Reference Period (MMRP), revised poverty line to Rs 32/day rural, Rs 47/day urban (29.5% poverty in 2011-12).
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** Chronology order: **Alagh (1979) -> Lakdawala (1993) -> Tendulkar (2009) -> Rangarajan (2014)**. Tendulkar moved away from calorie-only to education & health consumption.

### Question 8
**Subject:** Current Affairs | **Topic:** Nobel Prizes 2024

Consider the following statements regarding the Nobel Prize in Physiology or Medicine 2024:
1. It was awarded jointly to Victor Ambros and Gary Ruvkun.
2. The prize was awarded for the discovery of microRNA and its role in post-transcriptional gene regulation.
3. MicroRNAs are large protein molecules that destroy foreign antigens in human blood.

Which of the statements given above is/are correct?

**Options:**
A. 1 and 2 only  
B. 2 and 3 only  
C. 1 only  
D. 1, 2 and 3  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - Statements 1 and 2 are correct: The 2024 Nobel Prize in Physiology or Medicine was awarded jointly to Victor Ambros and Gary Ruvkun for their groundbreaking discovery of microRNA and its role in post-transcriptional gene regulation.
  - Statement 3 is incorrect: MicroRNAs are not proteins; they are tiny non-coding RNA molecules (typically 20–24 nucleotides long) that regulate gene expression by binding to messenger RNA (mRNA) and preventing protein translation or triggering mRNA degradation.
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** UPPSC tests fundamental scientific nature: microRNAs are non-coding ribonucleic acid (RNA) sequences, not antibodies or proteins!

### Question 9
**Subject:** Ancient Indian History | **Topic:** Ashoka's Major Rock Edicts (MRE)

Match List-I (Ashoka's Major Rock Edict) with List-II (Core Subject) and select the correct answer:

**List-I (Edict)**
A. Major Rock Edict I  
B. Major Rock Edict II  
C. Major Rock Edict XII  
D. Major Rock Edict XIII  

**List-II (Subject)**
1. Kalinga war, victory of Dhamma, and mention of Greek rulers (Antiochus, Ptolemy, etc.)  
2. Medical treatment for humans and animals; mentions Cholas, Pandyas, Satyaputra, Keralaputra  
3. Prohibition of animal sacrifice and festive gatherings  
4. Religious tolerance and growth of the essence of all religions  

**Options:**
A. A-3, B-2, C-4, D-1  
B. A-3, B-4, C-2, D-1  
C. A-1, B-2, C-4, D-3  
D. A-2, B-3, C-1, D-4  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - **MRE I:** Prohibits animal slaughter in sacrifice and bans festive gatherings (samajas).
  - **MRE II:** Mentions medical care for humans and animals, planting of medicinal herbs, and southern contemporary kingdoms (Chola, Pandya, Satyaputra, Keralaputra) and Greek king Antiochus II.
  - **MRE XII:** Plea for mutual respect and tolerance among all religious sects (Sarva Dharma Sambhava).
  - **MRE XIII:** Describes the remorse of Ashoka after the horrific Kalinga War (8th regnal year / 261 BCE) and conquest through Dhamma (Dhamma Vijaya) over 5 Hellenistic Greek kings (Antiochus of Syria, Ptolemy of Egypt, Antigonus of Macedonia, Magas of Cyrene, Alexander of Epirus).
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** MRE XIII is the longest and most frequently tested edict. Note: Rummindei pillar inscription mentions reduction of land revenue (Bhaga reduced to 1/8th and Bali abolished) at Buddha's birthplace Lumbini!

### Question 10
**Subject:** World Geography | **Topic:** Major Straits of the World

Match List-I (Strait) with List-II (Water Bodies Connected) and select the correct answer:

**List-I (Strait)**
A. Strait of Hormuz  
B. Bab-el-Mandeb  
C. Strait of Malacca  
D. Strait of Gibraltar  

**List-II (Water Bodies Connected)**
1. Red Sea and Gulf of Aden  
2. Persian Gulf and Gulf of Oman  
3. Mediterranean Sea and Atlantic Ocean  
4. Andaman Sea and South China Sea  

**Options:**
A. A-2, B-1, C-4, D-3  
B. A-1, B-2, C-4, D-3  
C. A-2, B-4, C-1, D-3  
D. A-3, B-1, C-4, D-2  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - **Strait of Hormuz:** Chokepoint connecting Persian Gulf with Gulf of Oman (and Arabian Sea); separates Oman/UAE from Iran.
  - **Bab-el-Mandeb ("Gate of Tears"):** Connects the Red Sea with the Gulf of Aden; separates Yemen (Arabian Peninsula) from Djibouti/Eritrea (Horn of Africa).
  - **Strait of Malacca:** Connects Andaman Sea (Indian Ocean) with South China Sea (Pacific Ocean); separates Malay Peninsula from Sumatra (Indonesia).
  - **Strait of Gibraltar ("Key to the Mediterranean"):** Connects Atlantic Ocean with Mediterranean Sea; separates Spain (Europe) from Morocco (Africa).
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** Remember: Bab-el-Mandeb links Red Sea to Gulf of Aden; Suez Canal links Mediterranean Sea to Red Sea.

### Question 11
**Subject:** Indian Polity | **Topic:** Parliament & Rajya Sabha Powers

Given below are two statements, one is labelled as Assertion (A) and the other as Reason (R):

**Assertion (A):** The Rajya Sabha possesses exclusive constitutional powers not shared by the Lok Sabha to initiate legislation on certain matters.  
**Reason (R):** Under Article 249, the Rajya Sabha can authorize Parliament to make laws on a State List subject, and under Article 312, it can authorize the creation of one or more All-India Services.

Select the correct answer from the codes given below:

**Options:**
A. Both (A) and (R) are true and (R) is the correct explanation of (A)  
B. Both (A) and (R) are true, but (R) is not the correct explanation of (A)  
C. (A) is true, but (R) is false  
D. (A) is false, but (R) is true  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - Assertion is true: As the council representing states in a federal setup, Rajya Sabha has special exclusive powers.
  - Reason is true: 
    1. **Article 249:** If Rajya Sabha passes a resolution supported by not less than two-thirds of members present and voting declaring that it is necessary in the national interest, Parliament can legislate on a State List subject (valid for 1 year).
    2. **Article 312:** Parliament can create new All-India Services only if Rajya Sabha passes a resolution by 2/3rd majority of members present and voting.
    3. **Article 67(b):** A resolution for the removal of the Vice-President can only originate in the Rajya Sabha.
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** Resolutions under Article 249 and 312 require a special majority of "2/3rd of members present and voting" in the Rajya Sabha only, not of total membership!

### Question 12
**Subject:** Medieval Indian History | **Topic:** Delhi Sultanate Administration

Match List-I (Administrative Department) with List-II (Founder / Associated Sultan) and select the correct answer:

**List-I (Department)**
A. Diwan-i-Arz (Military Dept)  
B. Diwan-i-Kohi (Agriculture Dept)  
C. Diwan-i-Mustakhraj (Arrears / Revenue Dept)  
D. Diwan-i-Khairat (Charity Dept)  

**List-II (Sultan)**
1. Alauddin Khalji  
2. Ghiyasuddin Balban  
3. Firoz Shah Tughlaq  
4. Muhammad bin Tughlaq  

**Options:**
A. A-2, B-4, C-1, D-3  
B. A-4, B-2, C-1, D-3  
C. A-2, B-1, C-4, D-3  
D. A-3, B-4, C-1, D-2  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - **Diwan-i-Arz:** Military department established by Balban to counter Mongol invasions (headed by Ariz-i-Mumalik).
  - **Diwan-i-Kohi:** Ministry of agriculture founded by Muhammad bin Tughlaq to bring barren land under cultivation and distribute Sondhar/Takkavi agricultural loans.
  - **Diwan-i-Mustakhraj:** Department to inquire into and recover arrears of revenue from corrupt officials, created by Alauddin Khalji.
  - **Diwan-i-Khairat:** Department of charity to assist poor Muslims, orphans, and arrange marriages of destitute girls, established by Firoz Shah Tughlaq (who also created Diwan-i-Bandagan for slaves and Dar-ul-Shafa charity hospital).
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** Balban created Diwan-i-Arz, but Alauddin Khalji introduced Dagh (branding of horses) and Huliya/Chehra (descriptive rolls of soldiers).

### Question 13
**Subject:** Uttar Pradesh Special | **Topic:** UP Census 2011 Demographics

Consider the following statements regarding Census 2011 data for Uttar Pradesh:
1. Gautam Buddha Nagar has the highest overall literacy rate among all districts of Uttar Pradesh.
2. Shravasti district has the lowest overall literacy rate as well as the lowest female literacy rate in Uttar Pradesh.
3. Kanpur Nagar has the highest female literacy rate in the state.

Which of the statements given above are correct?

**Options:**
A. 1 and 2 only  
B. 2 and 3 only  
C. 1 and 3 only  
D. 1, 2 and 3  

**Answer:** D

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - All three statements are factually accurate:
    - **Highest Overall Literacy:** Gautam Buddha Nagar (80.12%), followed by Kanpur Nagar (79.65%) and Auraiya (78.95%).
    - **Lowest Overall Literacy:** Shravasti (46.74%), followed by Bahraich (49.36%) and Balrampur (49.51%).
    - **Highest Female Literacy:** Kanpur Nagar (75.05%), followed by Lucknow (71.54%) and Gautam Buddha Nagar (70.82%).
    - **Lowest Female Literacy:** Shravasti (34.75%), followed by Balrampur (38.43%) and Bahraich (39.18%).
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** Common trap: Gautam Buddha Nagar leads in overall literacy and male literacy, but Kanpur Nagar tops in female literacy!

### Question 14
**Subject:** General Science (Physics) | **Topic:** Optical Phenomena in Daily Life

Match List-I (Phenomenon) with List-II (Underlying Optical Principle) and select the correct answer:

**List-I (Phenomenon)**
A. Mirage in deserts & Sparkle of diamond  
B. Blue colour of clear sky  
C. Rainbow formation  
D. Twinkling of stars  

**List-II (Optical Principle)**
1. Atmospheric Refraction  
2. Dispersion, Refraction & Internal Reflection  
3. Total Internal Reflection  
4. Rayleigh Scattering of light  

**Options:**
A. A-3, B-4, C-2, D-1  
B. A-4, B-3, C-2, D-1  
C. A-3, B-1, C-2, D-4  
D. A-2, B-4, C-3, D-1  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - **Total Internal Reflection (TIR):** Occurs when light travels from denser to rarer medium at an angle greater than the critical angle. Explains mirages, brilliance of diamonds, and light propagation through optical fibres and endoscopes.
  - **Scattering of Light:** Rayleigh's scattering law states scattering intensity is inversely proportional to $\lambda^4$. Shorter wavelengths (blue/violet) scatter much more than longer ones, giving the sky its blue color.
  - **Rainbow:** Composite optical phenomenon involving dispersion, refraction, and internal reflection within water droplets.
  - **Twinkling of Stars:** Caused by atmospheric refraction through layers of air with continuously varying refractive indices.
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** In space or on the Moon (where there is no atmosphere), the sky appears black because there is no scattering of light!

### Question 16
**Subject:** Indian Economy | **Topic:** Inflation Indices (WPI vs CPI)

Consider the following statements regarding the Wholesale Price Index (WPI) and Consumer Price Index (CPI) in India:
1. WPI is released by the Office of the Economic Adviser, Ministry of Commerce and Industry, whereas CPI is released by the National Statistical Office (NSO), MoSPI.
2. WPI accounts for the price changes of both goods and services, while CPI accounts only for goods.
3. In CPI (Combined), the 'Food and Beverages' group holds the highest weightage (~45.86%).

Which of the statements given above is/are correct?

**Options:**
A. 1 and 3 only  
B. 1 and 2 only  
C. 2 and 3 only  
D. 1, 2 and 3  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - Statement 1 is correct: WPI is published monthly by the Office of Economic Adviser (DPIIT), Ministry of Commerce & Industry. CPI is published by NSO, Ministry of Statistics & Programme Implementation.
  - Statement 2 is incorrect: WPI tracks ONLY goods; services are completely excluded from WPI. CPI covers both goods and services!
  - Statement 3 is correct: In CPI (Combined base 2012), Food & Beverages has the single largest weight of 45.86%, followed by Housing (10.07%) and Fuel & Light (6.84%). In WPI, Manufactured Products has the highest weight (~64.23%).
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** Base year trap: Base year of WPI is 2011–12; Base year of CPI is 2012. RBI uses CPI-Combined (Headline) for its inflation targeting framework (4% +/- 2%).

### Question 17
**Subject:** Modern Indian History | **Topic:** Revolt of 1857 Centers & Leaders

Match List-I (Center of 1857 Revolt) with List-II (Indian Leader) and select the correct answer:

**List-I (Center)**
A. Bareilly  
B. Faizabad  
C. Allahabad (Prayagraj)  
D. Jagdishpur (Bihar)  

**List-II (Leader)**
1. Maulvi Ahmadullah Shah  
2. Khan Bahadur Khan  
3. Kunwar Singh  
4. Maulvi Liaquat Ali  

**Options:**
A. A-2, B-1, C-4, D-3  
B. A-1, B-2, C-4, D-3  
C. A-2, B-4, C-1, D-3  
D. A-3, B-1, C-4, D-2  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - **Bareilly (Rohilkhand):** Led by Khan Bahadur Khan, grandson of Hafiz Rahmat Khan.
  - **Faizabad:** Led by Maulvi Ahmadullah Shah (native of Madras/Awadh, declared Jihad against the British; British announced a ₹50,000 bounty on him).
  - **Allahabad (Prayagraj):** Led by Maulvi Liaquat Ali; British forces under Colonel Neill suppressed the rebellion here.
  - **Jagdishpur (Arrah, Bihar):** Led by the octogenarian zamindar Kunwar Singh and his brother Amar Singh; defeated William Taylor and Vincent Eyre.
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** British suppressors: Delhi = John Nicholson & Hudson; Kanpur = Colin Campbell; Lucknow = Colin Campbell; Jhansi = Hugh Rose; Allahabad = Colonel Neill.

### Question 18
**Subject:** Current Affairs | **Topic:** Bharat Ratna Awards 2024

In 2024, the Government of India conferred the Bharat Ratna upon five eminent personalities. Who among the following was NOT among the five recipients?

**Options:**
A. Karpoori Thakur  
B. Chaudhary Charan Singh  
C. M.S. Swaminathan  
D. Ratan Tata  

**Answer:** D

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - The 5 recipients of Bharat Ratna in 2024 were:
    1. **Karpoori Thakur (Posthumous):** Former Chief Minister of Bihar and champion of social justice ("Jan Nayak").
    2. **Lal Krishna Advani:** Veteran statesman and 7th Deputy Prime Minister of India.
    3. **P.V. Narasimha Rao (Posthumous):** 9th Prime Minister of India, architect of 1991 economic reforms.
    4. **Chaudhary Charan Singh (Posthumous):** 5th Prime Minister of India and champion of farmers.
    5. **Dr. M.S. Swaminathan (Posthumous):** Renowned agricultural scientist, father of India's Green Revolution.
  - Ratan Tata was not awarded the Bharat Ratna in 2024 (he was awarded Padma Vibhushan in 2008 and Assam Baibhav in 2021).
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** 2024 saw the highest number of Bharat Ratna awards conferred in a single calendar year (5 recipients), surpassing 1999 (when 4 were awarded).

### Question 19
**Subject:** World Geography | **Topic:** Ocean Currents (Atlantic & Pacific)

Which of the following ocean currents is a COLD current of the Atlantic Ocean?

**Options:**
A. Gulf Stream  
B. Kuroshio Current  
C. Benguela Current  
D. Brazil Current  

**Answer:** C

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - **Benguela Current:** Cold ocean current that flows northward along the western coast of Southern Africa (Namibia/South Africa) in the South Atlantic Ocean; responsible for the Namib desert aridity.
  - **Gulf Stream:** Warm current of the North Atlantic Ocean.
  - **Brazil Current:** Warm current of the South Atlantic Ocean.
  - **Kuroshio (Black) Current:** Warm current of the North Pacific Ocean (flowing past Japan).
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** Master classification of cold currents:
  - *Atlantic:* Canary (North), Labrador (North), Benguela (South), Falkland (South).
  - *Pacific:* California (North), Oyashio/Kamchatka (North), Humboldt/Peru (South).
  - *Indian Ocean:* West Australian Current (cold).

### Question 20
**Subject:** Indian Polity | **Topic:** Schedules of the Indian Constitution

Match List-I (Schedule) with List-II (Subject Matter) and select the correct answer:

**List-I (Schedule)**
A. Fourth Schedule  
B. Seventh Schedule  
C. Eighth Schedule  
D. Tenth Schedule  

**List-II (Subject Matter)**
1. Allocation of seats in the Council of States (Rajya Sabha)  
2. Official Languages recognized by the Constitution (22 languages)  
3. Division of legislative powers into Union, State, and Concurrent Lists  
4. Provisions as to disqualification on ground of defection (Anti-Defection Law)  

**Options:**
A. A-1, B-3, C-2, D-4  
B. A-3, B-1, C-2, D-4  
C. A-1, B-2, C-3, D-4  
D. A-4, B-3, C-2, D-1  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - **4th Schedule:** Allocation of seats in Rajya Sabha to States and Union Territories (UP has highest with 31 seats).
  - **7th Schedule:** Division of powers between Union and States (List I: Union 100 items, List II: State 61 items, List III: Concurrent 52 items).
  - **8th Schedule:** 22 official languages (originally 14; 21st CAA 1967 added Sindhi; 71st CAA 1992 added Konkani, Manipuri, Nepali; 92nd CAA 2003 added Bodo, Dogri, Maithili, Santhali).
  - **10th Schedule:** Anti-Defection Law, added by the 52nd Constitutional Amendment Act, 1985 (modified by 91st CAA 2003).
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** Memorize: 5th Schedule = Administration of Scheduled Areas in other states; 6th Schedule = Tribal areas in 4 northeastern states (**AMTM**: Assam, Meghalaya, Tripura, Mizoram; NOT Manipur!).

### Question 21
**Subject:** Ancient Indian History | **Topic:** Indus Valley Civilization Archaeological Sites

Match List-I (Harappan Site) with List-II (Unique Archaeological Finding) and select the correct answer:

**List-I (Site)**
A. Lothal  
B. Kalibangan  
C. Dholavira  
D. Banawali  

**List-II (Finding)**
1. Ploughed field surface & fire altars  
2. Giant water reservoirs & 10-character signboard inscription  
3. Tidal Dockyard built of baked bricks  
4. Terracotta replica of a plough & high-quality barley  

**Options:**
A. A-3, B-1, C-2, D-4  
B. A-1, B-3, C-2, D-4  
C. A-3, B-2, C-1, D-4  
D. A-4, B-1, C-2, D-3  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - **Lothal (Gujarat, on Bhogava river):** Rectangular tidal dockyard, rice husk, bead-making factory, Persian gulf seal, double burial.
  - **Kalibangan (Rajasthan, on Ghaggar river):** Pre-Harappan ploughed agricultural field with furrow marks, rows of fire altars, camel bones, wooden furrow.
  - **Dholavira (Gujarat, Rann of Kutch):** Unique tripartite city planning (Citadel, Middle Town, Lower Town), sophisticated storm-water harvesting and reservoirs, 10-symbol signboard inscription.
  - **Banawali (Haryana, Fatehabad district):** Terracotta model of an agricultural plough, radial street pattern, abundance of barley grains.
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** Dholavira is divided into THREE parts, unlike all other major Harappan cities which are divided into TWO parts (Citadel and Lower Town). Rakhigarhi (Haryana) is the largest IVC site in India.

### Question 22
**Subject:** General Science (Biology) | **Topic:** Endocrine System & Hormones

Match List-I (Hormone) with List-II (Endocrine Gland / Source) and select the correct answer:

**List-I (Hormone)**
A. Insulin  
B. Thyroxine  
C. Adrenaline  
D. Oxytocin  

**List-II (Source Gland)**
1. Posterior Pituitary gland  
2. Thyroid gland  
3. Beta cells of Islets of Langerhans (Pancreas)  
4. Adrenal Medulla  

**Options:**
A. A-3, B-2, C-4, D-1  
B. A-2, B-3, C-4, D-1  
C. A-3, B-4, C-2, D-1  
D. A-4, B-2, C-3, D-1  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - **Insulin:** Peptide hormone secreted by Beta ($eta$) cells of the Islets of Langerhans in the pancreas; lowers blood glucose by promoting glycogenesis.
  - **Thyroxine ($T_4$):** Secreted by the thyroid gland; regulates basal metabolic rate (BMR); iodine deficiency leads to Goitre.
  - **Adrenaline (Epinephrine):** Secreted by the adrenal medulla; known as the "Emergency / 3F Hormone" (Fight, Flight, Fright); elevates heart rate and blood pressure.
  - **Oxytocin:** Synthesized in the hypothalamus and released by the posterior pituitary gland; stimulates uterine contractions during childbirth and milk ejection from mammary glands ("Love / Bonding hormone").
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** Glucagon is secreted by Alpha ($lpha$) cells of pancreas (raises blood sugar). Oxytocin is synthesized in Hypothalamus, but STORED and RELEASED by Posterior Pituitary (Neurohypophysis).

### Question 24
**Subject:** Medieval Indian History | **Topic:** Mughal Battles Chronology

Arrange the following battles fought during the Mughal era in chronological order:
1. Battle of Khanwa
2. Battle of Chausa
3. Battle of Kannauj (Bilgram)
4. Battle of Samugarh

Select the correct answer from the codes given below:

**Options:**
A. 1 — 2 — 3 — 4  
B. 1 — 3 — 2 — 4  
C. 2 — 1 — 3 — 4  
D. 1 — 2 — 4 — 3  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  1. **Battle of Khanwa (16 March 1527):** Babur defeated Rana Sanga of Mewar (Babur assumed the title of *Ghazi* and declared *Jihad*).
  2. **Battle of Chausa (26 June 1539):** Sher Khan (Sher Shah Suri) defeated Mughal Emperor Humayun on the banks of the Ganges.
  3. **Battle of Kannauj / Bilgram (17 May 1540):** Sher Shah decisively defeated Humayun, forcing him into 15 years of exile.
  4. **Battle of Samugarh (29 May 1658):** Decisive war of succession near Agra where Aurangzeb and Murad defeated their elder brother Dara Shikoh.
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** Succession war sequence of Shah Jahan's sons: **Bahadurpur (Feb 1658) -> Dharmat (April 1658) -> Samugarh (May 1658) -> Khajwa (Jan 1659) -> Deorai (April 1659)**. Mnemonic: **B-D-S-K-D**.

### Question 26
**Subject:** Indian Economy | **Topic:** Monetary Policy Instruments

Consider the following statements regarding the quantitative monetary policy tools of the Reserve Bank of India (RBI):
1. Cash Reserve Ratio (CRR) is the share of Net Demand and Time Liabilities (NDTL) that commercial banks must maintain in liquid cash with the RBI, and the RBI pays no interest on these reserves.
2. Statutory Liquidity Ratio (SLR) can be maintained by banks in the form of cash, gold, or approved government securities.
3. Standing Deposit Facility (SDF) allows the RBI to absorb liquidity from commercial banks without providing government collateral in return.

Which of the statements given above are correct?

**Options:**
A. 1 and 2 only  
B. 2 and 3 only  
C. 1 and 3 only  
D. 1, 2 and 3  

**Answer:** D

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - All three statements are correct:
    - **CRR:** Mandated under Section 42 of RBI Act 1934; banks hold it as unencumbered cash balance with RBI; RBI does NOT pay any interest on CRR balances.
    - **SLR:** Mandated under Section 24 of Banking Regulation Act 1949; maintained with banks themselves in specified assets (cash, gold, unencumbered G-Secs/Treasury bills).
    - **SDF:** Introduced in 2022 under amended Section 17 of RBI Act 1934 based on Urjit Patel Committee recommendations; operates as an uncollateralized liquidity absorption window placed 25 bps below the Repo Rate.
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** Statutory distinction trap: CRR is under RBI Act 1934, whereas SLR is under the Banking Regulation Act 1949!

2. The theme of India's G20 Presidency was "Vasudhaiva Kutumbakam — One Earth, One Family, One Future".
3. The 19th G20 Summit in November 2024 was hosted by South Africa.

Which of the statements given above is/are correct?

A. 1 and 2 only  
B. 2 and 3 only  
C. 1 only  
D. 1, 2 and 3  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - Statements 1 and 2 are correct: During the 18th G20 Summit held at Bharat Mandapam, New Delhi (September 2023), the 55-nation African Union was formally inducted as the 21st permanent member. The theme was drawn from Maha Upanishad: "Vasudhaiva Kutumbakam".
  - Statement 3 is incorrect: The 19th G20 Summit in November 2024 was hosted by **Brazil** (in Rio de Janeiro under President Lula da Silva). South Africa hosts the 20th G20 Summit in **2025**.

### Question 28
**Subject:** Indian Polity | **Topic:** Fundamental Rights & Prerogative Writs

Match List-I (Prerogative Writ under Article 32) with List-II (Literal Meaning / Purpose) and select the correct answer:

**List-I (Writ)**
A. Habeas Corpus  
B. Mandamus  
C. Quo-Warranto  
D. Certiorari  

**List-II (Meaning / Purpose)**
1. "We Command" — issued to a public official to perform a mandatory statutory duty  
2. "To have the body of" — remedy against illegal or arbitrary detention  
3. "To be certified" — issued to quash an order of an inferior court acting without jurisdiction  
4. "By what authority" — issued to prevent unlawful usurpation of a public office  

**Options:**
A. A-2, B-1, C-4, D-3  
B. A-1, B-2, C-4, D-3  
C. A-2, B-4, C-1, D-3  
D. A-3, B-1, C-4, D-2  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - **Habeas Corpus:** "You may have the body"; can be issued against BOTH public authorities and private individuals to produce an illegally detained person.
  - **Mandamus:** "We Command"; issued against public bodies/tribunals/officials to perform a public legal duty; CANNOT be issued against the President, State Governors, or private bodies.
  - **Quo-Warranto:** "By what authority or warrant"; inquires into the legality of a claim by a person to a substantive public office.
  - **Certiorari:** "To be certified / informed"; issued by a superior court to quash an order passed by an inferior court/tribunal in excess of jurisdiction or violation of natural justice (both curative and preventive).
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** Writ jurisdiction of High Courts (Article 226) is broader than that of the Supreme Court (Article 32) because the High Court can issue writs for Fundamental Rights AND ordinary legal rights, whereas the Supreme Court can issue writs ONLY for Fundamental Rights!

### Question 30
**Subject:** Modern Indian History | **Topic:** 19th Century Socio-Religious Reform Movements

Match List-I (Organization) with List-II (Founder) and select the correct answer:

**List-I (Organization)**
A. Satyashodhak Samaj  
B. Arya Samaj  
C. Tattvabodhini Sabha  
D. Prarthana Samaj  

**List-II (Founder)**
1. Swami Dayananda Saraswati  
2. Jyotirao Phule  
3. Dr. Atmaram Pandurang  
4. Debendranath Tagore  

**Options:**
A. A-2, B-1, C-4, D-3  
B. A-1, B-2, C-4, D-3  
C. A-2, B-4, C-1, D-3  
D. A-3, B-1, C-4, D-2  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - **Satyashodhak Samaj (1873, Pune):** Founded by Jyotirao Govindrao Phule to liberate lower castes from Brahminical dominance; authored *Gulamgiri* (1873) and *Shetkaryacha Asud*.
  - **Arya Samaj (1875, Bombay):** Founded by Swami Dayananda Saraswati (Mula Shankara); slogan: "Go Back to the Vedas"; authored *Satyarth Prakash*.
  - **Tattvabodhini Sabha (1839, Calcutta):** Founded by Debendranath Tagore to promote religious inquiry and Upanishadic wisdom; published *Tattvabodhini Patrika*.

- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** Do not confuse Atmiya Sabha (founded by Raja Ram Mohan Roy in 1815) with Tattvabodhini Sabha (Debendranath Tagore in 1839). Arya Samaj was founded in Bombay in 1875, and shifted headquarters to Lahore in 1877!

### Question 31
**Subject:** General Science (Chemistry) | **Topic:** Chemical Formulas of Daily Compounds

Match List-I (Common Name) with List-II (Chemical Formula) and select the correct answer:

**List-I (Compound)**
A. Baking Soda  
B. Washing Soda  
C. Plaster of Paris  
D. Bleaching Powder  

**List-II (Chemical Formula)**
1. $Na_2CO_3 \cdot 10H_2O$  
2. $NaHCO_3$  
3. $CaOCl_2$  
4. $CaSO_4 \cdot rac{1}{2}H_2O$  

**Options:**
A. A-2, B-1, C-4, D-3  
B. A-1, B-2, C-4, D-3  
C. A-2, B-4, C-1, D-3  
D. A-3, B-1, C-4, D-2  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - **Baking Soda:** Sodium Hydrogen Carbonate ($NaHCO_3$); releases $CO_2$ upon heating, causing dough to rise.
  - **Washing Soda:** Sodium Carbonate Decahydrate ($Na_2CO_3 \cdot 10H_2O$); used for removing permanent hardness of water.
  - **Plaster of Paris:** Calcium Sulphate Hemihydrate ($CaSO_4 \cdot rac{1}{2}H_2O$); prepared by heating Gypsum ($CaSO_4 \cdot 2H_2O$) at 373 K.
  - **Bleaching Powder:** Calcium Hypochlorite / Oxychloride ($CaOCl_2$); prepared by the action of chlorine gas on dry slaked lime $Ca(OH)_2$.
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** Plaster of Paris has HALF a water molecule ($rac{1}{2}H_2O$), while Gypsum has TWO water molecules ($2H_2O$).

**Subject:** Uttar Pradesh Special | **Topic:** Geographical Limits & Bordering Entities

Consider the following statements regarding the geography and boundaries of Uttar Pradesh:
1. Uttar Pradesh shares its state boundary with eight states and one Union Territory.
2. Sonbhadra is the only district in India that shares its borders with four different states.
3. Lalitpur district of Uttar Pradesh is surrounded on three sides by the state of Rajasthan.

Which of the statements given above is/are correct?

**Options:**
A. 1 and 2 only  
B. 2 and 3 only  
C. 1 only  
D. 1, 2 and 3  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - Statement 1 is correct: UP touches 8 States (Uttarakhand, Himachal Pradesh, Haryana, Rajasthan, Madhya Pradesh, Chhattisgarh, Jharkhand, Bihar) + 1 UT (NCT of Delhi) + 1 Country (Nepal).
  - Statement 2 is correct: Sonbhadra shares borders with 4 states: Madhya Pradesh (West), Chhattisgarh (South), Jharkhand (South-East), and Bihar (East).
  - Statement 3 is incorrect: Lalitpur district is surrounded on three sides by **Madhya Pradesh**, NOT Rajasthan!
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** Extremities of UP:

  - Easternmost district = Ballia
  - Westernmost district = Shamli.

### Question 33
**Subject:** Environment & Ecology | **Topic:** Ecological Principles & Energy Transfer

Consider the following statements regarding ecological pyramids and energy flow:
1. In all ecosystems, the pyramid of energy is always upright and can never be inverted.
2. In deep aquatic ecosystems (ponds and oceans), the pyramid of biomass is typically inverted.
3. Raymond Lindeman's 10% law states that approximately 10% of the energy is transferred from one trophic level to the next higher level.

Which of the statements given above are correct?

**Options:**
A. 1 and 2 only  
B. 2 and 3 only  
C. 1 and 3 only  
D. 1, 2 and 3  

**Answer:** D

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - All three statements are correct:
    - **Pyramid of Energy:** Follows the Second Law of Thermodynamics; energy is continuously dissipated as metabolic heat at each trophic transfer, hence it is ALWAYS upright without exception.
    - **Pyramid of Biomass in Aquatic Ecosystems:** In oceans and ponds, the standing crop biomass of primary producers (phytoplankton) at any given moment is small because of their microscopic size and rapid turnover, whereas long-lived primary and secondary consumers (zooplankton, fishes) possess larger standing biomass, creating an INVERTED biomass pyramid.
    - **10% Law (1942):** Formulated by Raymond Lindeman; only about 10% of the organic energy from food is converted into flesh/tissue at the next trophic level.
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** Remember: Pyramid of energy is NEVER inverted. Pyramid of numbers can be inverted (e.g., single large oak tree supporting thousands of insects and parasites).

### Question 34
**Subject:** Medieval Indian History | **Topic:** Foreign Travelers to Medieval India

Match List-I (Foreign Traveler) with List-II (Visiting Ruler / Dynasty) and select the correct answer:

**List-I (Traveler)**
A. Nicolo de' Conti  
B. Abdur Razzaq  
C. Domingo Paes  
D. Francois Bernier  

**List-II (Ruler / Court)**
1. Deva Raya II (Vijayanagara)  
2. Krishnadeva Raya (Vijayanagara)  
3. Deva Raya I (Vijayanagara)  
4. Aurangzeb / Dara Shikoh (Mughal)  

**Options:**
A. A-3, B-1, C-2, D-4  
B. A-1, B-3, C-2, D-4  
C. A-3, B-2, C-1, D-4  
D. A-2, B-1, C-3, D-4  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - **Nicolo de' Conti (Italian, c. 1420):** Visited Vijayanagara during the reign of Deva Raya I.
  - **Abdur Razzaq (Persian ambassador from Timurid ruler Shah Rukh, c. 1443):** Visited Vijayanagara during the reign of Deva Raya II; remarked: "The city is such that eye has not seen nor ear heard of any place resembling it upon the whole earth."
  - **Domingo Paes & Duarte Barbosa (Portuguese, c. 1520):** Visited Vijayanagara during the glorious reign of Krishnadeva Raya (Tuluva dynasty).
  - **Francois Bernier (French physician):** Lived in Mughal India (1656–1668) during the war of succession and reign of Aurangzeb; wrote *Travels in the Mogul Empire*.
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** Ibn Battuta visited Delhi during Muhammad bin Tughlaq (wrote *Rihla*); William Hawkins (1608) and Sir Thomas Roe (1615) visited the court of Jahangir.

**Options:**
A. A-2, B-1, C-4, D-3  
B. A-1, B-2, C-4, D-3  
C. A-2, B-4, C-1, D-3  
D. A-3, B-1, C-4, D-2  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - **Article 39A:** Added by the 42nd Amendment Act, 1976; provides for equal justice and free legal aid to the poor (led to the Legal Services Authorities Act, 1987).
  - **Article 40:** Gandhian principle directing the State to organize Village Panchayats as units of self-government.
  - **Article 44:** Liberal-intellectual principle directing the State to secure a Uniform Civil Code (UCC) for all citizens throughout India.
  - **Article 48A:** Added by the 42nd Amendment Act, 1976; directs State to protect and improve the environment and safeguard forests and wildlife.
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** Four DPSPs added by 42nd CAA 1976: Article 39(f) (healthy development of children), Article 39A (free legal aid), Article 43A (workers' participation in management), and Article 48A (environment). Article 43B (cooperative societies) was added by 97th CAA 2011!

**Subject:** Ancient Indian History | **Topic:** Rigvedic Rivers & Modern Equivalents

Match List-I (Rigvedic Ancient Name) with List-II (Modern River Name) and select the correct answer:

A. Vitasta  
B. Asikni  
C. Parushni  
D. Shutudri  

**List-II (Modern Name)**
1. Chenab  
2. Jhelum  
3. Sutlej  
4. Ravi  

**Options:**
A. A-2, B-1, C-4, D-3  
B. A-1, B-2, C-4, D-3  
C. A-2, B-4, C-1, D-3  
D. A-3, B-1, C-4, D-2  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - **Vitasta:** Modern Jhelum river (site of the Battle of the Hydaspes between Alexander and Porus).
  - **Asikni:** Modern Chenab river (largest tributary of the Indus).
  - **Parushni:** Modern Ravi river (banks of which the famous Battle of Ten Kings / *Dasarajna* was fought).

  - Suvastu = Swat
  - Sadanira = Gandak.

### Question 37
**Subject:** Indian Economy | **Topic:** National Income & Macroeconomic Aggregates

Consider the following statements regarding National Income accounting in India:
1. Gross National Product (GNP) at market prices equals Gross Domestic Product (GDP) plus Net Factor Income from Abroad (NFIA).
2. Net National Product (NNP) at Factor Cost is officially defined as the "National Income" of an economy.
3. Real GDP measures the value of all final goods and services calculated at constant base-year prices, thereby filtering out the effects of price inflation.

Which of the statements given above are correct?

**Options:**
A. 1 and 2 only  
B. 2 and 3 only  
C. 1 and 3 only  
D. 1, 2 and 3  

**Answer:** D

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - All three statements are correct:
    - **GNP = GDP + NFIA:** Where NFIA = Factor income earned by residents abroad minus Factor income paid to non-residents within the domestic territory.
    - **National Income = NNP at Factor Cost ($NNP_{FC}$):** $NNP_{FC} = GNP - 	ext{Depreciation} - 	ext{Net Indirect Taxes}$.
    - **Real GDP vs Nominal GDP:** Nominal GDP evaluates output at current prevailing market prices; Real GDP evaluates output at constant base year prices (current base year 2011–12), isolating pure volume growth.
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** GDP Deflator formula: $	ext{GDP Deflator} = rac{	ext{Nominal GDP}}{	ext{Real GDP}} 	imes 100$. Unlike CPI or WPI which use a fixed basket, the GDP Deflator reflects the price changes of all goods and services produced domestically!

### Question 39
**Subject:** Geography of India | **Topic:** Peninsular Drainage System

Which of the following Indian rivers flows through a rift valley between the Vindhya and Satpura ranges and empties into the Arabian Sea?

**Options:**
A. Narmada  
B. Godavari  
C. Mahanadi  
D. Krishna  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - **Narmada River:** Originates from the Amarkantak plateau in Madhya Pradesh; flows westward through a tectonic rift valley bounded by the Vindhyas on the north and the Satpura range on the south.
  - Forms the picturesque Marble Rocks gorge and Dhuandhar Waterfalls near Jabalpur before draining into the Gulf of Khambhat (Arabian Sea) forming an estuary.
  - Tapi river also flows in a rift valley south of the Satpura range.
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** Most peninsular rivers flow eastward into the Bay of Bengal because the Deccan plateau tilts gently eastward. Only Narmada, Tapi, Mahi, and Sabarmati flow west due to faulting/rift valleys!

### Question 40
**Subject:** General Science (Physics) | **Topic:** Human Eye & Corrective Lenses

Match List-I (Visual Defect) with List-II (Corrective Lens / Feature) and select the correct answer:

**List-I (Defect)**
A. Myopia (Short-sightedness)  
B. Hypermetropia (Long-sightedness)  
C. Presbyopia (Old-age defect)  
D. Astigmatism  

**List-II (Correction / Cause)**
1. Convex lens (converging lens)  
2. Concave lens (diverging lens)  
3. Cylindrical lens  
4. Bifocal lens  

**Options:**
A. A-2, B-1, C-4, D-3  
B. A-1, B-2, C-4, D-3  
C. A-2, B-4, C-1, D-3  
D. A-3, B-1, C-4, D-2  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - **Myopia:** Image forms in front of the retina because the eyeball is elongated; person cannot see distant objects clearly; corrected using a Concave (diverging) lens.
  - **Hypermetropia:** Image forms behind the retina because the eyeball is too short; person cannot see nearby objects clearly; corrected using a Convex (converging) lens.
  - **Presbyopia:** Age-related stiffening of the crystalline lens and weakening of ciliary muscles; corrected using Bifocal lenses (upper portion concave for distance, lower convex for reading).
  - **Astigmatism:** Caused by irregular curvature of the cornea or lens, preventing uniform focus in all meridians; corrected using Cylindrical lenses.
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** Quick mnemonic: **My Cave** (Myopia = Concave lens). Power of a concave lens is negative (e.g. -2.5 D); power of a convex lens is positive (+1.5 D).

**Subject:** Uttar Pradesh Special | **Topic:** Minerals and Mining in UP

Match List-I (Mineral) with List-II (District in Uttar Pradesh) and select the correct answer:

A. Uranium  
B. Glass Sand (Silica Sand)  
C. Limestone  
D. Bauxite  

**List-II (District)**

2. Lalitpur  
3. Banda & Chandauli  
4. Prayagraj (Shankargarh)

B. A-4, B-2, C-1, D-3  
C. A-2, B-1, C-4, D-3  
D. A-3, B-4, C-1, D-2

### Question 42
**Subject:** Modern Indian History | **Topic:** British Land Revenue Settlements

Match List-I (Land Revenue System) with List-II (Key Architect / Area) and select the correct answer:

**List-I (System)**
A. Permanent Settlement (Zamindari)  
B. Ryotwari System  
C. Mahalwari System  

**List-II (Architect & Area)**
1. Thomas Munro and Captain Alexander Read; Madras and Bombay Presidencies  
2. Holt Mackenzie and Robert Merttins Bird; Gangetic Valley, NWFP, and Punjab  
3. Lord Cornwallis and Sir John Shore; Bengal, Bihar, and Orissa  

**Options:**
A. A-3, B-1, C-2  
B. A-1, B-3, C-2  
C. A-3, B-2, C-1  
D. A-2, B-1, C-3  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - **Permanent Settlement (1793):** Introduced by Lord Cornwallis in Bengal, Bihar, Orissa, and Varanasi (covering ~19% of British India). Zamindars were recognized as owners of land; revenue fixed perpetually; Company got 10/11th share, Zamindar retained 1/11th. Subject to the infamous "Sunset Law".
  - **Ryotwari System (1820):** Devised by Alexander Read and Thomas Munro in Madras, Bombay, parts of Assam (~51% of British India). Direct contract between Company and cultivator (Ryot); revenue assessed at 50% of produce and revised periodically.
  - **Mahalwari System (1822/1833):** Designed by Holt Mackenzie (formalized under William Bentinck by Merttins Bird) in UP, Punjab, and Central Provinces (~30% of British India). Unit of assessment was the village or estate (*Mahal*); village headman (*Lambardar*) collected revenue.
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** Robert Merttins Bird is revered as the "Father of Land Revenue Settlement in Northern India" (UP/NWFP).

### Question 43
**Subject:** Indian Polity | **Topic:** Constitutional Bodies & Corresponding Articles

Match List-I (Constitutional Body) with List-II (Article of Constitution) and select the correct answer:

**List-I (Body)**
A. Comptroller and Auditor General of India (CAG)  
B. Finance Commission  
C. Election Commission of India  
D. Attorney General for India  

**List-II (Article)**
1. Article 280  
2. Article 148  
3. Article 76  
4. Article 324  

**Options:**
A. A-2, B-1, C-4, D-3  
B. A-1, B-2, C-4, D-3  
C. A-2, B-4, C-1, D-3  
D. A-3, B-1, C-4, D-2  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - **CAG (Article 148):** Appointed by President; holds office for 6 years or up to 65 years; guardian of public purse; friend, philosopher, and guide of Public Accounts Committee (PAC).
  - **Finance Commission (Article 280):** Quasi-judicial body constituted by the President every 5th year (16th FC chaired by Dr. Arvind Panagariya).
  - **Election Commission of India (Article 324):** Superintendence, direction, and control of elections to Parliament, State Legislatures, and offices of President and Vice-President.
  - **Attorney General (Article 76):** Highest law officer; right of audience in all courts; under Article 88, has right to speak in Parliament without right to vote.
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** Advocate General of the State is under Article 165; State Finance Commission is under Article 243-I; State Election Commission is under Article 243-K.

### Question 44
**Subject:** Environment & Ecology | **Topic:** Wildlife Protection Act, 1972 & Amendments

Consider the following statements regarding the Wildlife (Protection) Amendment Act, 2022:
1. It rationalized the number of schedules from six to four.
2. Schedule I provides the highest level of protection to endangered animal species.
3. The separate schedule for 'Vermin' (formerly Schedule V) has been completely removed.

Which of the statements given above are correct?

**Options:**
A. 1 and 2 only  
B. 2 and 3 only  
C. 1 and 3 only  
D. 1, 2 and 3  

**Answer:** D

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - All three statements are correct:
    - The Wildlife (Protection) Amendment Act 2022 (effective from April 1, 2023) restructured the schedules:
      - **Schedule I:** Animal species that enjoy the highest level of absolute protection (e.g. Tiger, Snow Leopard, Great Indian Bustard).
      - **Schedule II:** Animal species with a lesser level of protection.
      - **Schedule III:** Protected plant species.
      - **Schedule IV:** Specimens of scheduled fauna and flora listed under CITES Appendices.
    - The old Schedule V (Vermin) was repealed; the Central Government now issues specific notifications for vermin declarations.
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** Under the 2022 Amendment, CITES provisions were formally incorporated into Indian law through Schedule IV and designated Management Authorities!

**Subject:** Geography of India | **Topic:** Highest Peaks of Major Ranges

Match List-I (Mountain Range) with List-II (Highest Peak) and select the correct answer:

B. Satpura Range  
C. Western Ghats (Sahyadri)  
D. Nilgiri Hills  

**List-II (Highest Peak)**
1. Dhupgarh  
2. Guru Shikhar  
3. Dodabetta  
4. Anaimudi

A. A-2, B-1, C-4, D-3  
B. A-1, B-2, C-4, D-3  
C. A-2, B-4, C-1, D-3  
D. A-3, B-1, C-4, D-2  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**

### Question 46
**Subject:** Ancient Indian History | **Topic:** Buddhist Councils Master Matrix

Match List-I (Buddhist Council) with List-II (Patron Ruler & Chairman) and select the correct answer:

**List-I (Council)**
A. First Buddhist Council (483 BCE)  
B. Second Buddhist Council (383 BCE)  
C. Third Buddhist Council (250 BCE)  
D. Fourth Buddhist Council (72 CE)  

**List-II (Patron & Chairman)**
1. Kalashoka (Shishunaga) & Sabakami; Vaishali  
2. Ajatashatru (Haryanka) & Mahakassapa; Rajgriha  
3. Kanishka (Kushan) & Vasumitra / Ashvaghosha; Kundalvana (Kashmir)  
4. Ashoka (Maurya) & Moggaliputta Tissa; Pataliputra  

**Options:**
A. A-2, B-1, C-4, D-3  
B. A-1, B-2, C-4, D-3  
C. A-2, B-4, C-1, D-3  
D. A-3, B-1, C-4, D-2  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - **1st Council (Rajgriha, Sattapanni cave):** Patron = Ajatashatru; President = Mahakassapa. Compilation of *Sutta Pitaka* (by Ananda) and *Vinaya Pitaka* (by Upali).
  - **2nd Council (Vaishali):** Patron = Kalashoka (Kakarvarna); President = Sabakami. First schism into Sthaviravadins and Mahasanghikas.
  - **3rd Council (Pataliputra):** Patron = Ashoka; President = Moggaliputta Tissa. Compilation of *Abhidhamma Pitaka* (philosophical treatise) and expulsion of heretics.
  - **4th Council (Kundalvana, Kashmir):** Patron = Kanishka; President = Vasumitra (Vice-President = Ashvaghosha). Division into Mahayana and Hinayana sects; *Mahavibhasha Shastra* composed.
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** Mnemonic for Kings: **AK-AK** (Ajatashatru -> Kalashoka -> Ashoka -> Kanishka). Mnemonic for Places: **R-V-P-K** (Rajgriha -> Vaishali -> Pataliputra -> Kashmir).

### Question 48
**Subject:** Medieval Indian History | **Topic:** Bhakti Movement & Vedantic Philosophies

Match List-I (Bhakti Acharya) with List-II (Philosophical System) and select the correct answer:

**List-I (Acharya)**
A. Shankaracharya  
B. Ramanujacharya  
C. Madhvacharya  
D. Nimbarkacharya  

**List-II (Philosophy)**
1. Vishishtadvaita (Qualified Non-Dualism)  
2. Advaita (Absolute Non-Dualism)  
3. Dvaitadvaita (Bhedabheda / Dualistic Non-Dualism)  
4. Dvaita (Pure Dualism)  

**Options:**
A. A-2, B-1, C-4, D-3  
B. A-1, B-2, C-4, D-3  
C. A-2, B-4, C-1, D-3  
D. A-3, B-1, C-4, D-2  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - **Shankaracharya (8th Century, Kaladi Kerala):** Propounded *Advaita Vedanta* (Nirguna Brahman; Maya concept; established 4 Mathas at Sringeri, Puri, Dwarka, Badrinath).
  - **Ramanujacharya (11th Century, Sriperumbudur):** Propounded *Vishishtadvaita* (Saguna Brahman; soul and God are distinct yet interconnected; Sri Sampradaya).

### Question 49
**Subject:** Indian Economy & Demographics | **Topic:** Census 2011 Master Indicators

2. Meghalaya recorded the highest decadal population growth rate among all states.
3. Nagaland was the only state that recorded a negative decadal population growth rate (-0.58%).

Which of the statements given above are correct?

**Options:**
A. 1 and 2 only  
B. 2 and 3 only  
C. 1 and 3 only  
D. 1, 2 and 3  

**Answer:** D

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - All three statements are correct:
    - **Decadal Growth Rate:** India's population grew by 17.70% (17.64% in rural areas, 31.80% in urban areas).
    - **Highest Decadal Growth:** Meghalaya recorded the highest growth of 27.95% among states (Dadra & Nagar Haveli was highest among UTs at 55.88%).
    - **Negative Growth:** Nagaland registered a negative decadal growth of -0.58% (declined from 19.9 lakh to 19.78 lakh).
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** India's overall Sex Ratio in Census 2011 is 943 (up from 933 in 2001). However, Child Sex Ratio (0–6 years) dropped alarmingly from 927 (2001) to 919 (2011)!

**Subject:** Uttar Pradesh Special | **Topic:** Agro-Climatic Zones & Bundelkhand Soils

2. In the Bundelkhand region, the coarse red and brown soils are locally known as 'Rakar' and 'Parwa'.
3. The black soils of Bundelkhand are locally termed 'Mar' and 'Kabar'.

Which of the statements given above are correct?

**Options:**
A. 1 and 2 only  
B. 2 and 3 only  
C. 1 and 3 only  
D. 1, 2 and 3  

**Answer:** D

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - All three statements are correct:
    - **9 Agro-Climatic Zones of UP:** 1. Bhabhar & Tarai Zone, 2. Western Plain, 3. Mid-Western Plain, 4. South-Western Semi-Arid Zone, 5. Central Plain, 6. Bundelkhand Zone, 7. North-Eastern Plain, 8. Eastern Plain, 9. Vindhyan Zone.
    - **Bundelkhand Soils:**
      - *Red Soil group:* **Parwa** (light sandy-loam, light brown) and **Rakar** (coarse, gravelly red soil found on hill slopes).

### Question 51
**Subject:** General Science (Biology) | **Topic:** Human Blood Groups & Circulation

Consider the following statements regarding the human ABO and Rh blood group systems:
1. Karl Landsteiner discovered the ABO blood group system in humans.
2. Individuals with blood group O negative ($O^-$) are universal donors because their red blood cells carry neither A nor B surface antigens nor the Rh antigen.
3. Blood group AB positive ($AB^+$) individuals are universal recipients because their plasma contains both anti-A and anti-B antibodies.

Which of the statements given above is/are correct?

**Options:**
A. 1 and 2 only  
B. 2 and 3 only  
C. 1 only  
D. 1, 2 and 3  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - Statements 1 and 2 are correct: Karl Landsteiner discovered the ABO system (awarded Nobel Prize in 1930). $O^-$ blood lacks A, B, and Rh antigens, hence causing no agglutination in recipients.
  - Statement 3 is incorrect: Blood group AB individuals have NEITHER anti-A NOR anti-B antibodies in their plasma (if they had antibodies, they would destroy their own red blood cells!). Because of the absence of antibodies, $AB^+$ can receive red blood cells from any blood group.
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** Antigens reside on RBC membranes; Antibodies reside in blood plasma! Erythroblastosis foetalis occurs when an $Rh^-$ mother carries an $Rh^+$ fetus for the second time.

### Question 52
**Subject:** Modern Indian History | **Topic:** Constitutional Acts of British India

Arrange the following British enactments in chronological order:
1. Pitts India Act
2. Regulating Act
3. Charter Act establishing open competition for Civil Services (Charter Act of 1853)
4. Government of India Act (Act for the Better Government of India)

Select the correct answer from the codes given below:

**Options:**
A. 2 — 1 — 3 — 4  
B. 1 — 2 — 3 — 4  
C. 2 — 3 — 1 — 4  
D. 2 — 1 — 4 — 3  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  1. **Regulating Act of 1773:** First attempt by British Parliament to regulate East India Company; Governor of Bengal made Governor-General of Bengal (Warren Hastings); Supreme Court established at Calcutta (1774, Chief Justice Sir Elijah Impey).
  2. **Pitts India Act of 1784:** Established Dual Control (Board of Control for political affairs and Court of Directors for commercial affairs).
  3. **Charter Act of 1853:** Introduced open competitive examination for Civil Services (Macaulay Committee on ICS appointed in 1854); separated executive and legislative functions of the Governor-General's Council.
  4. **Government of India Act of 1858:** Abolished Company rule; transferred governance to the British Crown; created office of Secretary of State for India and made Governor-General the Viceroy (Lord Canning).
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** The Charter Act of 1833 attempted open competition for ICS but it was negated due to opposition from the Court of Directors; open competition was finally enacted in 1853!

### Question 53
**Subject:** Indian Polity | **Topic:** Panchayati Raj Committees Chronology

Arrange the following committees related to Panchayati Raj in India in chronological sequence:
1. Ashok Mehta Committee
2. Balwant Rai Mehta Committee
3. L.M. Singhvi Committee
4. G.V.K. Rao Committee

Select the correct answer from the codes given below:

**Options:**
A. 2 — 1 — 4 — 3  
B. 1 — 2 — 4 — 3  
C. 2 — 4 — 1 — 3  
D. 2 — 1 — 3 — 4  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  1. **Balwant Rai Mehta Committee (1957):** Recommended a 3-tier Panchayati Raj system (Gram Panchayat, Panchayat Samiti, Zilla Parishad). Rajasthan was the 1st state to establish PR at Nagaur (2 Oct 1959), followed by Andhra Pradesh.
  2. **Ashok Mehta Committee (1977, Janata Govt):** Recommended a 2-tier system (Mandal Panchayat and Zilla Parishad) and compulsory taxation powers.
  3. **G.V.K. Rao Committee (1985, Planning Commission):** Emphasized making Zilla Parishad the pivotal tier ("grass without roots").
  4. **L.M. Singhvi Committee (1986):** First strongly recommended **Constitutional recognition, protection, and preservation** for Panchayati Raj bodies, leading to the 73rd Amendment Act 1992.
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** Chronology mnemonic: **B-A-G-L** (Balwant Rai 1957 -> Ashok Mehta 1977 -> GVK Rao 1985 -> LM Singhvi 1986). P.K. Thungon Committee followed in 1988!

### Question 54
**Subject:** Geography of India | **Topic:** Major Mineral Mines of India

Match List-I (Mining Center) with List-II (Principal Mineral Extracted) and select the correct answer:

**List-I (Mine)**
A. Khetri  
B. Kolar & Hutti  
C. Kudremukh  
D. Jaduguda  

**List-II (Mineral)**
1. Uranium  
2. Gold  
3. Copper  
4. Iron Ore  

**Options:**
A. A-3, B-2, C-4, D-1  
B. A-2, B-3, C-4, D-1  
C. A-3, B-4, C-2, D-1  
D. A-1, B-2, C-4, D-3  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - **Khetri (Jhunjhunu, Rajasthan):** India's premier copper mining center operated by Hindustan Copper Ltd.
  - **Kolar & Hutti (Karnataka):** Historic deep gold mines of India (Hutti is the only active gold-producing mine in India).
  - **Kudremukh (Chikkamagaluru, Karnataka):** Famous magnetite iron ore deposits (named after its horse-face shaped peak).
  - **Jaduguda (Singhbhum, Jharkhand):** India's first and primary uranium mine, operated by Uranium Corporation of India (UCIL).

### Question 55
**Subject:** Environment & Ecology | **Topic:** International Environmental Treaties

Match List-I (International Convention) with List-II (Objective / Target Substance) and select the correct answer:

**List-I (Convention)**
A. Montreal Protocol (1987)  
B. Basel Convention (1989)  
C. Stockholm Convention (2001)  
D. Minamata Convention (2013)  

**List-II (Target / Objective)**
1. Control of transboundary movements of hazardous wastes  
2. Protection of human health and environment from Mercury emissions  
3. Phasing out Ozone Depleting Substances (CFCs, Halons)  
4. Elimination or restriction of Persistent Organic Pollutants (POPs)  

**Options:**
A. A-3, B-1, C-4, D-2  
B. A-1, B-3, C-4, D-2  
C. A-3, B-4, C-1, D-2  
D. A-2, B-1, C-4, D-3  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - **Montreal Protocol (1987):** Most successful treaty; phases out ODS (CFCs, HCFCs); amended by Kigali Amendment (2016) to phase down HFCs (potent greenhouse gases).
  - **Basel Convention (1989):** Regulates transboundary movement and disposal of hazardous wastes.
  - **Stockholm Convention (2001):** Aims to eliminate the "Dirty Dozen" Persistent Organic Pollutants (e.g. DDT, Dioxins, PCBs).
  - **Minamata Convention (2013):** Global treaty to protect human health and environment from anthropogenic emissions of Mercury.
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** Rotterdam Convention (1998) is for "Prior Informed Consent (PIC) procedure for certain hazardous chemicals and pesticides in international trade".

### Question 56
**Subject:** Current Affairs | **Topic:** Global Indices & Reports 2024

Consider the following statements regarding international indices:
1. The Human Development Index (HDI) is published annually by the United Nations Development Programme (UNDP).
2. The four indicators used in HDI calculation are: Life Expectancy at birth, Expected Years of Schooling, Mean Years of Schooling, and Gross National Income (GNI) per capita (PPP).
3. The Global Hunger Index (GHI) is jointly published by Welthungerhilfe and Concern Worldwide.

Which of the statements given above are correct?

**Options:**
A. 1 and 2 only  
B. 2 and 3 only  
C. 1 and 3 only  
D. 1, 2 and 3  

**Answer:** D

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - All three statements are correct:
    - **HDI:** Formulated by Pakistani economist Mahbub ul Haq and Amartya Sen; published by UNDP since 1990.
    - **4 Indicators across 3 Dimensions:** Health (Life expectancy at birth), Education (Expected years of schooling for children and Mean years of schooling for adults aged 25+), Standard of Living (GNI per capita in PPP \$). In HDI 2023–24, India ranked 134th (score 0.644, medium human development).
    - **Global Hunger Index (GHI):** Released by Concern Worldwide (Ireland) and Welthungerhilfe (Germany); calculates hunger based on 4 indicators: Undernourishment, Child Stunting, Child Wasting, and Child Mortality.
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** Note that World Bank does NOT publish HDI. World Bank publishes the World Development Report and Logistics Performance Index. WEF publishes Global Gender Gap Report!

### Question 57
**Subject:** Uttar Pradesh Special | **Topic:** Historical Monuments and Architecture of UP

Match List-I (Monument in UP) with List-II (Builder / Ruler) and select the correct answer:

**List-I (Monument)**
A. Bara Imambara (Lucknow)  
B. Buland Darwaza (Fatehpur Sikri)  
C. Tomb of Itmad-ud-Daulah (Agra)  
D. Atala Masjid (Jaunpur)  

**List-II (Builder / Architect)**
1. Akbar  
2. Asaf-ud-Daula  
3. Ibrahim Shah Sharqi  
4. Nur Jahan  

**Options:**
A. A-2, B-1, C-4, D-3  
B. A-1, B-2, C-4, D-3  
C. A-2, B-4, C-1, D-3  
D. A-3, B-1, C-4, D-2  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - **Bara Imambara (Lucknow):** Built in 1784 by Nawab Asaf-ud-Daula as a famine relief project; famous for its unsupported central vaulted gallery and Bhool Bhulaiya (labyrinth).
  - **Buland Darwaza (Fatehpur Sikri):** Constructed by Mughal Emperor Akbar in 1601 to commemorate his victory over Gujarat.
  - **Tomb of Itmad-ud-Daulah (Agra):** Built by Empress Nur Jahan for her father Mirza Ghiyas Beg (1622–1628); first Mughal structure constructed entirely in white marble utilizing extensive *Pietra Dura* (inlaid precious stonework).
  - **Atala Masjid (Jaunpur):** Completed in 1408 by Sultan Ibrahim Shah Sharqi; masterpiece of the Sharqi style of architecture (Jaunpur was known as *Shiraz-e-Hind*).
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** Itmad-ud-Daulah's tomb is often termed the "Baby Taj" or draft of the Taj Mahal due to its pioneering Pietra Dura marble work.

### Question 58
**Subject:** Ancient Indian History | **Topic:** 16 Mahajanapadas & Capitals (6th Century BCE)

Match List-I (Mahajanapada) with List-II (Capital) and select the correct answer:

**List-I (Mahajanapada)**
A. Kosala  
B. Vatsa  
C. Anga  
D. Matsya  

**List-II (Capital)**
1. Kaushambi  
2. Shravasti  
3. Viratnagar  
4. Champa  

**Options:**
A. A-2, B-1, C-4, D-3  
B. A-1, B-2, C-4, D-3  
C. A-2, B-4, C-1, D-3  
D. A-3, B-1, C-4, D-2  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - **Kosala:** Capital was Shravasti (Northern) / Ayodhya-Kushavati (Southern); King Prasenjit was a contemporary of Buddha.
  - **Vatsa:** Capital was Kaushambi on the Yamuna river (near modern Prayagraj); King Udayana was a contemporary of Buddha.
  - **Anga:** Capital was Champa (near Bhagalpur/Munger in Bihar); later annexed to Magadha by Bimbisara.
  - **Matsya:** Capital was Viratnagar (Jaipur-Alwar-Bharatpur region of Rajasthan).
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** Out of 16 Mahajanapadas mentioned in the Buddhist text *Anguttara Nikaya* and Jain text *Bhagavati Sutra*, **8 Mahajanapadas were located in present-day Uttar Pradesh**: Kashi, Kosala, Vatsa, Malla, Chedi, Kuru, Panchala, and Surasena!

### Question 59
**Subject:** General Science (Biology) | **Topic:** Plant Hormones (Phytohormones)

Match List-I (Plant Hormone) with List-II (Primary Physiological Function) and select the correct answer:

**List-I (Hormone)**
A. Auxin  
B. Gibberellin  
C. Cytokinin  
D. Ethylene  

**List-II (Function)**
1. Cell division and delaying leaf senescence  
2. Apical dominance and phototropism  
3. Gaseous hormone responsible for fruit ripening  
4. Stem elongation and breaking seed dormancy (bolting)  

**Options:**
A. A-2, B-4, C-1, D-3  
B. A-4, B-2, C-1, D-3  
C. A-2, B-1, C-4, D-3  
D. A-3, B-4, C-1, D-2  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - **Auxin (IAA):** Promotes apical dominance, cell elongation, and phototropic bending of shoot tips.
  - **Gibberellin ($GA_3$):** Induces internodal elongation, stem growth, breaks seed dormancy, and causes "bolting" in rosette plants (like cabbage).
  - **Cytokinin:** Promotes rapid cell division (cytokinesis); overcomes apical dominance and delays senescence (Richmond-Lang effect).
  - **Ethylene ($C_2H_4$):** The ONLY natural gaseous plant hormone; accelerates fruit ripening and triggers abscission of leaves and flowers.
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** Abscisic Acid (ABA) is known as the "Stress Hormone" because it induces stomatal closure during water deficit and promotes dormancy.

### Question 60
**Subject:** Indian Economy | **Topic:** Types of Unemployment

Match List-I (Type of Unemployment) with List-II (Definition / Key Feature) and select the correct answer:

**List-I (Type)**
A. Disguised Unemployment  
B. Structural Unemployment  
C. Frictional Unemployment  
D. Cyclical Unemployment  

**List-II (Feature)**
1. Mismatch between the skills possessed by workers and the requirements of available jobs  
2. Temporary unemployment while a worker searches for or transitions between jobs  
3. Marginal productivity of the worker is zero or near zero (common in Indian agriculture)  
4. Results from economic downturns or recessions due to deficiency of aggregate demand  

**Options:**
A. A-3, B-1, C-2, D-4  
B. A-1, B-3, C-2, D-4  
C. A-3, B-2, C-1, D-4  
D. A-4, B-1, C-2, D-3  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - **Disguised Unemployment:** Situation where more people are engaged in an activity than actually required; if some workers are withdrawn, total output remains unaffected (Marginal Physical Productivity = 0). Rampant in small-holder Indian farming.
  - **Structural Unemployment:** Arises from long-term changes in economic structure, technological obsolescence, or skill mismatches.
  - **Frictional Unemployment:** Voluntary and short-term unemployment when individuals search for better employment opportunities.
  - **Cyclical Unemployment:** Caused by trade cycles and recessions (Keynesian unemployment).
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** UPPSC question trap: "In which sector is disguised unemployment most prevalent in India?" Answer: Agriculture (Primary Sector).

### Question 61
**Subject:** Indian Polity | **Topic:** Basic Structure Doctrine Landmark Cases

Arrange the following Supreme Court landmark judgments in chronological sequence:
1. Golaknath v. State of Punjab
2. Shankari Prasad v. Union of India
3. Kesavananda Bharati v. State of Kerala
4. Minerva Mills v. Union of India

Select the correct answer from the codes given below:

**Options:**
A. 2 — 1 — 3 — 4  
B. 1 — 2 — 3 — 4  
C. 2 — 3 — 1 — 4  
D. 3 — 1 — 2 — 4  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  1. **Shankari Prasad (1951):** SC ruled Parliament has absolute power to amend Fundamental Rights under Article 368; constitutional amendment acts are not "law" within the meaning of Article 13(2).
  2. **Golaknath (1967):** 11-judge bench ruled Fundamental Rights are transcendental and sacrosanct; Parliament CANNOT amend or abridge Fundamental Rights.
  3. **Kesavananda Bharati (24 April 1973):** Historic 13-judge bench (7:6 majority); overruled Golaknath; ruled Parliament can amend any part of the Constitution including Fundamental Rights, BUT cannot alter the "Basic Structure" of the Constitution.
  4. **Minerva Mills (1980):** Reaffirmed Basic Structure doctrine; struck down clauses (4) and (5) of Article 368 added by 42nd CAA; held that the balance between Fundamental Rights (Part III) and DPSPs (Part IV) is a bedrock of the Basic Structure.
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** Landmark sequence: **Shankari Prasad (1951) -> Sajjan Singh (1965) -> Golaknath (1967) -> Kesavananda (1973) -> Minerva Mills (1980) -> Waman Rao (1981)**.

### Question 62
**Subject:** Modern Indian History | **Topic:** Tribal and Peasant Uprisings Chronology

Arrange the following tribal and peasant uprisings in chronological order:
1. Santhal Rebellion
2. Indigo Revolt (Neel Bidroha)
3. Pabna Peasant Uprising
4. Moplah (Malabar) Rebellion

Select the correct answer from the codes given below:

**Options:**
A. 1 — 2 — 3 — 4  
B. 2 — 1 — 3 — 4  
C. 1 — 3 — 2 — 4  
D. 1 — 2 — 4 — 3  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  1. **Santhal Rebellion (1855–1856):** Led by brothers Sidhu and Kanhu in Daman-i-Koh (Rajmahal Hills, Bihar/Jharkhand) against zamindars, moneylenders (Mahajans), and British officials.
  2. **Indigo Revolt (1859–1860):** Started at Govindpur (Nadia district, Bengal) led by Bishnu Charan Biswas and Digambar Biswas against oppressive European planters; dramatized by Dinabandhu Mitra in *Nil Darpan*.
  3. **Pabna Peasant Revolt (1873–1876):** Agrarian league formed in Yusufshahi pargana of Pabna (Bengal) against illegal cesses and evictions by zamindars; slogan: "We want to be the ryots of Her Majesty the Queen and of her only".
  4. **Moplah Rebellion (1921):** Uprising of Muslim peasants (Moplahs) against Hindu landlords (Jenmis) and British authorities in the Malabar region of Kerala during the Non-Cooperation and Khilafat movements.
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** Indigo revolt (1859–60) occurred immediately after the 1857 Revolt. Eka Movement in Awadh occurred in 1921 (led by Madari Pasi).

### Question 63
**Subject:** World Geography | **Topic:** Major Lakes of the World

Match List-I (Lake) with List-II (Distinct Geographical Superlative) and select the correct answer:

**List-I (Lake)**
A. Lake Baikal  
B. Caspian Sea  
C. Lake Tanganyika  
D. Lake Superior  

**List-II (Superlative)**
1. World's largest inland body of water by surface area  
2. World's deepest and oldest freshwater lake  
3. World's largest freshwater lake by surface area  
4. World's longest freshwater lake  

**Options:**
A. A-2, B-1, C-4, D-3  
B. A-1, B-2, C-4, D-3  
C. A-2, B-3, C-4, D-1  
D. A-3, B-1, C-4, D-2  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - **Lake Baikal (Siberia, Russia):** Deepest lake in the world (1,642 m) and holds ~20% of the world's unfrozen surface freshwater.
  - **Caspian Sea:** Largest enclosed inland body of water on Earth (saline); bordered by 5 countries (Russia, Kazakhstan, Turkmenistan, Iran, Azerbaijan).
  - **Lake Tanganyika (East Africa):** Longest freshwater lake in the world (~673 km) and second deepest.
  - **Lake Superior (USA–Canada border):** Largest freshwater lake in the world by surface area (82,100 sq km).
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** Lake Victoria is the largest lake in Africa, and the Equator passes directly through it! Aral Sea (between Kazakhstan and Uzbekistan) has shrunk drastically due to diversion of Amu Darya and Syr Darya rivers.

### Question 65
**Subject:** Uttar Pradesh Special | **Topic:** National Parks and Tiger Reserves of UP

Consider the following statements regarding Tiger Reserves in Uttar Pradesh:
1. Dudhwa National Park is the only National Park located in Uttar Pradesh.
2. Amangarh Tiger Reserve acts as an eco-buffer zone for the Jim Corbett National Park.
3. Ranipur Tiger Reserve in Chitrakoot district was notified as the 4th Tiger Reserve of Uttar Pradesh.

Which of the statements given above are correct?

**Options:**
A. 1 and 2 only  
B. 2 and 3 only  
C. 1 and 3 only  
D. 1, 2 and 3  

**Answer:** D

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - All three statements are correct:
    - **Dudhwa National Park:** Situated in Lakhimpur Kheri district (Terai belt); established in 1977; declared a Tiger Reserve under Project Tiger in 1987. It is the ONLY National Park in UP.
    - **Amangarh Tiger Reserve (Bijnor district):** Formed out of the buffer zone of Corbett Tiger Reserve after Uttarakhand was carved out of UP in 2000.
    - **Ranipur Tiger Reserve (Chitrakoot district, Bundelkhand):** Notified in 2022 as UP's 4th Tiger Reserve (and India's 53rd Tiger Reserve).
    - **Pilibhit Tiger Reserve:** Situated in Pilibhit and Shahjahanpur districts (won the international TX2 award for doubling tiger numbers).
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** 4 Tiger Reserves in UP: **1. Dudhwa, 2. Pilibhit, 3. Amangarh, 4. Ranipur**.
