### Question 66
**Subject:** Medieval Indian History | **Topic:** Mughal Land Revenue & Administrative Terms

Match List-I (Mughal Term) with List-II (Definition / Nature) and select the correct answer:

**List-I (Term)**
A. Khalisa  
B. Jagir  
C. Madad-i-Maash (Sayurghal)  
D. Muqaddam / Patel  

**List-II (Definition)**
1. Land whose revenue was directly collected for the Imperial Royal Treasury  
2. Village headman responsible for local administration and revenue assistance  
3. Revenue assignment granted to mansabdars in lieu of cash salary  
4. Rent-free land grant given to scholars, religious saints, and charitable institutions  

**Options:**
A. A-1, B-3, C-4, D-2  
B. A-3, B-1, C-4, D-2  
C. A-1, B-4, C-3, D-2  
D. A-2, B-3, C-4, D-1  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - **Khalisa:** Crown land administered directly by the central revenue ministry, revenue from which went directly to the imperial treasury (*Bait-ul-Mal*).
  - **Jagir:** Land revenue assignment granted to a Mansabdar according to his salary rank (*Tankhwah Jagir*); Mansabdar did not own the land, only held the right to collect revenue.
  - **Madad-i-Maash (also called Suyurghal):** Tax-free land grant bestowed upon learned men, poets, religious mendicants, and destitute widows.
  - **Muqaddam / Patel:** Village headman who assisted the Patwari in collecting village revenues.
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** Todar Mal introduced the **Ain-i-Dahsala** system in 1580 under Akbar, where average produce and average prices of preceding 10 years (1570–1580) were calculated.

### Question 67
**Subject:** Environment & Ecology | **Topic:** Biosphere Reserves under UNESCO MAB Program

Which was the FIRST Biosphere Reserve established in India, and in which year was it designated?

**Options:**
A. Nilgiri Biosphere Reserve (1986)  
B. Sundarbans Biosphere Reserve (1989)  
C. Nanda Devi Biosphere Reserve (1988)  
D. Gulf of Mannar Biosphere Reserve (1989)  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - **Nilgiri Biosphere Reserve:** India's FIRST Biosphere Reserve, established in **1986**. It spans across parts of three states: Tamil Nadu, Kerala, and Karnataka.
  - Encompasses protected areas including Mudumalai, Mukurthi, Wayanad, Bandipur, Nagarhole, and Silent Valley National Parks.
  - Home to the endangered Lion-tailed Macaque and Nilgiri Tahr.
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** India has 18 designated Biosphere Reserves, out of which **12 are recognized under UNESCO's World Network of Biosphere Reserves (WNBR - Man and the Biosphere Program)**. Panna Biosphere Reserve (MP) was the 12th reserve inducted into UNESCO MAB in 2020!

### Question 69
**Subject:** Indian Polity | **Topic:** Preamble to the Indian Constitution

Consider the following statements regarding the Preamble of the Indian Constitution:
1. The Preamble is based on the 'Objective Resolution' moved by Pt. Jawaharlal Nehru on December 13, 1946.
2. The words "Socialist", "Secular", and "Integrity" were added to the Preamble by the 42nd Constitutional Amendment Act, 1976.
3. In the Kesavananda Bharati case (1973), the Supreme Court held that the Preamble is NOT a part of the Constitution.

Which of the statements given above is/are correct?

**Options:**
A. 1 and 2 only  
B. 2 and 3 only  
C. 1 only  
D. 1, 2 and 3  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - Statements 1 and 2 are correct: Pt. Nehru moved the historic Objectives Resolution on 13 Dec 1946 (unanimously adopted on 22 Jan 1947). The 42nd Amendment Act 1976 added three new terms: "Socialist", "Secular", and "and Integrity" (the text changed from "Sovereign Democratic Republic" to "Sovereign Socialist Secular Democratic Republic", and "unity of the Nation" to "unity and integrity of the Nation").
  - Statement 3 is incorrect: In the **Berubari Union case (1960)**, the SC had held that Preamble is NOT part of the Constitution. BUT in **Kesavananda Bharati (1973)**, the SC explicitly overruled Berubari and held that the **Preamble IS an integral part of the Constitution** and can be amended under Article 368 without destroying its basic structure.
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** Correct sequence of words in the Preamble: **Sovereign, Socialist, Secular, Democratic, Republic**! Date of adoption stated in Preamble is **26th November 1949** (Miti Margashirsha Shukla Saptami, Samvat 2006).

### Question 70
**Subject:** Indian Economy | **Topic:** Five-Year Plans & Growth Models

Match List-I (Five-Year Plan) with List-II (Underlying Model / Core Strategy) and select the correct answer:

**List-I (Plan)**
A. First Five-Year Plan (1951–56)  
B. Second Five-Year Plan (1956–61)  
C. Third Five-Year Plan (1961–66)  
D. Twelfth Five-Year Plan (2012–17)  

**List-II (Model / Theme)**
1. P.C. Mahalanobis Model (Rapid industrialization & heavy capital goods)  
2. Harrod-Domar Model (Agricultural development & price stability)  
3. Theme: "Faster, Sustainable, and More Inclusive Growth"  
4. John Sandy and Sukhamoy Chakravarty Model (Gadgil Yojana)  

**Options:**
A. A-2, B-1, C-4, D-3  
B. A-1, B-2, C-4, D-3  
C. A-2, B-4, C-1, D-3  
D. A-3, B-1, C-4, D-2  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - **1st Plan (1951–56):** Based on Harrod-Domar growth model; top priority given to agriculture, irrigation, and power (Bhakra Nangal, Hirakud, DVC started).
  - **2nd Plan (1956–61):** Based on Nehru-Mahalanobis 4-sector model; prioritized heavy engineering and basic industries (Rourkela, Bhilai, Durgapur steel plants).
  - **3rd Plan (1961–66):** Known as "Gadgil Yojana"; aimed at self-reliant and self-generating economy; suffered setbacks due to 1962 China War, 1965 Pak War, and severe drought (led to Plan Holiday 1966–69).
  - **12th Plan (2012–17):** Last five-year plan of India; official theme was "Faster, Sustainable and More Inclusive Growth". NITI Aayog replaced Planning Commission on 1 January 2015.
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** "Rolling Plan" was introduced by the Janata Party Government under Morarji Desai for 1978–80 based on Gunnar Myrdal's concept!

---

### Question 71
**Subject:** Ancient Indian History | **Topic:** Historical Inscriptions & Associated Kings

Match List-I (Ancient Inscription) with List-II (Associated King / Composer) and select the correct answer:

**List-I (Inscription)**
A. Aihole Inscription  
B. Prayag Prashasti (Allahabad Pillar)  
C. Junagarh Rock Inscription  
D. Mehrauli Iron Pillar Inscription  

**List-II (King / Composer)**
1. Samudragupta (composed by Harishena)  
2. Pulakeshin II (composed by Ravikirti)  
3. Chandragupta II ('Chandra')  
4. Rudradaman I (Saka ruler)  

**Options:**
A. A-2, B-1, C-4, D-3  
B. A-1, B-2, C-4, D-3  
C. A-2, B-4, C-1, D-3  
D. A-3, B-1, C-4, D-2  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - **Aihole Inscription (Karnataka):** Written in Sanskrit by court poet Ravikirti; records Chalukya king Pulakeshin II's military conquests and his decisive defeat of King Harsha on the banks of Narmada.
  - **Prayag Prashasti:** Inscribed on an Ashokan pillar in Allahabad; composed in chaste Sanskrit Champu kavya style by Harishena; catalogues the conquest routes of Gupta emperor Samudragupta (*Napoleon of India*).
  - **Junagarh Inscription (Gujarat):** First major long inscription in chaste Sanskrit (150 CE) by Saka ruler Rudradaman I; records the history and repairs of the Sudarshana Lake (originally constructed under Chandragupta Maurya and canals added under Ashoka).
  - **Mehrauli Pillar (Delhi):** Rustless iron pillar commemorating King 'Chandra', universally identified with Gupta emperor Chandragupta II Vikramaditya.
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** Sudarshana Lake involves 4 rulers: Chandragupta Maurya (built by Pushyagupta) -> Ashoka (canals by Tushaspha) -> Rudradaman I (repaired by Suvisakha) -> Skandagupta (repaired by Chakrapalita).

### Question 72
**Subject:** World Geography | **Topic:** Planetary & Local Winds

Match List-I (Local Wind) with List-II (Region & Characteristic) and select the correct answer:

**List-I (Wind)**
A. Chinook  
B. Sirocco  
C. Harmattan  
D. Mistral  

**List-II (Region & Nature)**
1. Cold and dry northerly wind blowing down the Rhone valley (France) towards Mediterranean  
2. Warm, dry wind on the eastern slopes of the Rocky Mountains ("Snow Eater")  
3. Warm, dry, dust-laden wind of West Africa ("Doctor Wind")  
4. Hot, dry southerly wind from Sahara causing "Blood Rain" in Southern Italy  

**Options:**
A. A-2, B-4, C-3, D-1  
B. A-1, B-4, C-3, D-2  
C. A-2, B-3, C-4, D-1  
D. A-4, B-2, C-3, D-1  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - **Chinook:** Warm and dry foehn-type katabatic wind on the leeward (eastern) slopes of Rocky Mountains in USA/Canada; melts snow rapidly, hence called "Snow Eater".
  - **Sirocco:** Hot, dry, dust-laden wind originating in Sahara; picks up moisture over Mediterranean and causes reddish mud rain ("Blood Rain") in Italy.
  - **Harmattan:** Dry, dusty trade wind blowing from Sahara southwestward to the Gulf of Guinea; brings welcome relief from humid coastal weather, hence called "The Doctor".
  - **Mistral:** Cold, violent northerly wind that blows down the Rhone Valley in France towards the Gulf of Lion (Mediterranean).
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** Cold winds: Mistral (France), Bora (Adriatic coast), Pampero (Argentina), Blizzard (Canada/USA), Buran (Siberia).

### Question 73
**Subject:** Modern Indian History | **Topic:** Landmark Indian National Congress (INC) Sessions

Match List-I (INC Session) with List-II (President & Significance) and select the correct answer:

**List-I (Session)**
A. 1906 Calcutta Session  
B. 1907 Surat Session  
C. 1916 Lucknow Session  
D. 1929 Lahore Session  

**List-II (President & Milestone)**
1. Rashbehari Ghosh; Formal split between Moderates and Extremists  
2. Dadabhai Naoroji; The goal of 'Swaraj' proclaimed for the first time  
3. Jawaharlal Nehru; Resolution of 'Purna Swaraj' (Complete Independence) passed  
4. Ambica Charan Mazumdar; Lucknow Pact signed between Congress and Muslim League  

**Options:**
A. A-2, B-1, C-4, D-3  
B. A-1, B-2, C-4, D-3  
C. A-2, B-4, C-1, D-3  
D. A-3, B-1, C-4, D-2  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - **1906 Calcutta:** Presided by Dadabhai Naoroji (Grand Old Man of India); passed resolutions on Swadeshi, Boycott, National Education, and Swaraj.
  - **1907 Surat:** Presided by Dr. Rashbehari Ghosh on the banks of Tapi; split between Moderates and Extremists (Tilak, Gokhale confrontation).
  - **1916 Lucknow:** Presided by Ambica Charan Mazumdar; historic reunion of Moderates and Extremists; Congress-League Pact accepted separate electorates for Muslims.
  - **1929 Lahore:** Presided by Jawaharlal Nehru on the banks of Ravi; adopted 'Purna Swaraj' resolution; tri-colour flag unfurled on midnight of 31 Dec 1929; decided to celebrate 26 January 1930 as Independence Day.
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** 1924 Belgaum session was the ONLY session presided over by Mahatma Gandhi! Sarojini Naidu was the first Indian woman president (1925 Kanpur). Annie Besant was the first woman president (1917 Calcutta).

### Question 74
**Subject:** Uttar Pradesh Special | **Topic:** Folk Dances, Music & Traditions of UP

Match List-I (Folk Dance / Music Form) with List-II (Region of Uttar Pradesh) and select the correct answer:

**List-I (Folk Form)**
A. Charkula Dance  
B. Alha Folk Song  
C. Kajari Song  
D. Birha Song  

**List-II (Region in UP)**
1. Bundelkhand  
2. Braj Region (Mathura)  
3. Purvanchal (Bhojpuri Belt)  
4. Mirzapur & Varanasi  

**Options:**
A. A-2, B-1, C-4, D-3  
B. A-1, B-2, C-4, D-3  
C. A-2, B-4, C-1, D-3  
D. A-3, B-1, C-4, D-2  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**

**Subject:** Current Affairs | **Topic:** Major National & International Literary Awards 2024

2. The 33rd Saraswati Samman was awarded to Malayalam poet Prabha Varma for his verse novel *Roudrasathwikam*.
3. The Booker Prize 2024 was awarded to British author Samantha Harvey for her novel *Orbital*.

Which of the statements given above are correct?

**Options:**
A. 1 and 2 only  
B. 2 and 3 only  
C. 1 and 3 only  
D. 1, 2 and 3

### Question 76
**Subject:** Indian Polity | **Topic:** Fundamental Rights & Judicial Review (Article 13)

Consider the following statements regarding Article 13 of the Indian Constitution:
1. Under Article 13(2), any law made by Parliament or State Legislatures that abridges or takes away Fundamental Rights is void to the extent of the contravention.
2. The 'Doctrine of Eclipse' and 'Doctrine of Severability' have evolved primarily through judicial interpretation of Article 13.
3. The Supreme Court in the Kesavananda Bharati case held that a Constitutional Amendment Act under Article 368 is considered ordinary "law" within the meaning of Article 13(2).

Which of the statements given above are correct?

**Options:**
A. 1 and 2 only  
B. 2 and 3 only  
C. 1 and 3 only  
D. 1, 2 and 3  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - Statements 1 and 2 are correct: Article 13 provides the constitutional backbone for Judicial Review. Clause (2) mandates that the State shall not make any law which takes away or abridges Part III rights. The Supreme Court formulated the *Doctrine of Severability* (valid portions survive if invalid portions can be cleanly severed) and *Doctrine of Eclipse* (pre-constitutional laws inconsistent with FRs remain dormant/shadowed, not dead).
  - Statement 3 is incorrect: The Supreme Court in *Kesavananda Bharati (1973)* upheld the validity of clause (4) of Article 13 (inserted by 24th CAA 1971), affirming that a **Constitutional Amendment Act passed under Article 368 is NOT ordinary 'law' under Article 13(2)**. However, amendments can still be struck down if they violate the "Basic Structure".
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** Remember: The term "Judicial Review" is nowhere explicitly mentioned in the text of the Constitution, but Article 13, 32, 136, 226, and 227 vest this power explicitly in the higher judiciary!

### Question 77
**Subject:** World Geography | **Topic:** World Deserts & Adjacent Cold Currents

Match List-I (Desert of the World) with List-II (Continent / Region & Influencing Cold Current) and select the correct answer:

**List-I (Desert)**
A. Atacama Desert  
B. Namib Desert  
C. Mojave Desert  
D. Great Victoria Desert  

**List-II (Region & Ocean Current)**
1. Southern Africa; Benguela Cold Current  
2. South America (Chile/Peru); Humboldt (Peru) Cold Current  
3. Australia; West Australian Cold Current  
4. North America (USA); California Cold Current  

**Options:**
A. A-2, B-1, C-4, D-3  
B. A-1, B-2, C-4, D-3  
C. A-2, B-4, C-1, D-3  
D. A-3, B-1, C-4, D-2  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - **Atacama Desert (Northern Chile):** Driest non-polar desert on Earth; coastal fog (*Camanchaca*) exists due to the offshore Humboldt (Peru) cold current.
  - **Namib Desert (Namibia):** Ancient coastal desert formed by the desiccating effects of the cold Benguela Current.
  - **Mojave Desert (Southwestern USA):** Aridity reinforced by the cold California Current and rain-shadow of Sierra Nevada.
  - **Great Victoria Desert (Western & South Australia):** Aridity caused by subtropical high pressure and the cold West Australian Current.
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** Most tropical hot deserts are located on the **western margins of continents between 15° and 30° latitudes** due to two factors: Trade winds blowing offshore and Cold ocean currents running offshore!

### Question 78
**Subject:** Modern Indian History | **Topic:** Swadeshi Movement (1905–1908)

Consider the following statements regarding the Swadeshi and Boycott Movement:
1. The formal proclamation of the Swadeshi Movement was made on August 7, 1905, at the Calcutta Town Hall.
2. The 'Carlyle Circular' was issued by the British administration to prevent school and college students from participating in nationalist agitation.
3. The National Council of Education was set up in August 1906, with Aurobindo Ghosh as its first Principal.

Which of the statements given above are correct?

**Options:**
A. 1 and 2 only  
B. 2 and 3 only  
C. 1 and 3 only  
D. 1, 2 and 3  

**Answer:** D

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - All three statements are correct:
    - On August 7, 1905, the famous Boycott Resolution was passed at Calcutta Town Hall, inaugurating the Swadeshi Movement.
    - Chief Secretary Carlyle issued the repressive 'Carlyle Circular' threatening to withdraw grants, scholarships, and university affiliations from institutions whose students joined protests. In response, nationalists established the Anti-Circular Society (led by Sachindra Prasad Bose).
    - On August 15, 1906, the National Council of Education was inaugurated, and the Bengal National College was founded with Sri Aurobindo Ghosh as its principal.
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** On 16 October 1905 (the day Partition took effect), Rabindranath Tagore suggested observing the day as **Raksha Bandhan Day** to demonstrate Hindu-Muslim brotherhood, and composed *Amar Shonar Bangla*!

### Question 79
**Subject:** General Science (Biology) | **Topic:** Human Diseases, Pathogens & Diagnostic Tests

Match List-I (Disease) with List-II (Causative Organism & Diagnostic Test) and select the correct answer:

**List-I (Disease)**
A. Tuberculosis  
B. Typhoid Fever  
C. Malaria  
D. Kala-Azar (Visceral Leishmaniasis)  

**List-II (Organism & Vector / Test)**
1. *Salmonella typhi* bacteria; Widal Test  
2. *Mycobacterium tuberculosis* bacteria; Mantoux Tuberculin Skin Test  
3. *Leishmania donovani* protozoan; transmitted by Sandfly (*Phlebotomus*)  
4. *Plasmodium* protozoan; transmitted by female *Anopheles* mosquito  

**Options:**
A. A-2, B-1, C-4, D-3  
B. A-1, B-2, C-4, D-3  
C. A-2, B-4, C-1, D-3  
D. A-3, B-1, C-4, D-2  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - **Tuberculosis (TB):** Caused by *Mycobacterium tuberculosis*; airborne transmission; diagnosed via Mantoux test and sputum microscopy; vaccine is BCG (Bacillus Calmette-Guérin).
  - **Typhoid:** Caused by *Salmonella typhi*; transmitted via contaminated food/water; diagnosed via the **Widal Test**.
  - **Malaria:** Caused by protozoan parasite *Plasmodium* (falciparum, vivax, malariae, ovale); vector is female *Anopheles* mosquito; treated with Quinine (extracted from Cinchona tree bark) and Artemisinin.
  - **Kala-Azar (Black Fever):** Caused by protozoan *Leishmania donovani*; vector is the female **Sandfly** (*Phlebotomus*); targets bone marrow, spleen, and liver.
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** Vector matching trap: Sleeping Sickness = Tsetse fly; Dengue & Chikungunya = *Aedes aegypti* mosquito; Filariasis = *Culex* mosquito; Plague = Rat flea (*Xenopsylla cheopis*).

### Question 80
**Subject:** Uttar Pradesh Special | **Topic:** River Drainage of Uttar Pradesh

Consider the following statements regarding the rivers of Uttar Pradesh:
1. Gomti river originates from Fulhar Lake (Gomat Taal) near Madho Tanda in Pilibhit district of Uttar Pradesh.
2. The Betwa river joins the Yamuna river near Hamirpur in Uttar Pradesh.
3. Ken river joins the Yamuna river at Chilla near Banda in Uttar Pradesh.

Which of the statements given above are correct?

**Options:**
A. 1 and 2 only  
B. 2 and 3 only  
C. 1 and 3 only  
D. 1, 2 and 3  

**Answer:** D

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - All three statements are correct:
    - **Gomti:** An exceptional alluvial plain river; originates not from the Himalayas, but from Gomat Taal / Fulhar lake in **Pilibhit**; flows through Lucknow, Sultanpur, and Jaunpur before meeting the Ganga at Kaithi (Ghazipur/Varanasi border).
    - **Betwa (Vetravati):** Originates in Raisen (MP); enters UP in Lalitpur and Jhansi; joins Yamuna near **Hamirpur**.
    - **Ken (Karnavati):** Originates in Kaimur hills (MP); enters Banda district of UP and merges with Yamuna at **Chilla** (Banda).
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** The Ken-Betwa River Interlinking Project transfers surplus water from Ken basin to Betwa basin to irrigate the drought-prone Bundelkhand region (submerging part of Panna Tiger Reserve).

### Question 81
**Subject:** Environment & Ecology | **Topic:** Biodiversity Hotspots in India

The concept of 'Biodiversity Hotspots' was first introduced by Norman Myers in 1988. How many biodiversity hotspots cover parts of India?

**Options:**
A. 2  
B. 3  
C. 4  
D. 5  

**Answer:** C

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - Globally, Conservation International recognizes 36 Biodiversity Hotspots.
  - **4 Hotspots cover parts of India:**
    1. **The Western Ghats:** High degree of endemism (Nilgiri Tahr, Lion-tailed Macaque, Malabar Civet).
    2. **The Himalayas:** Entire Indian Himalayan region (spanning J&K, Ladakh, Himachal, Uttarakhand, Sikkim, Arunachal Pradesh).
    3. **Indo-Burma:** Covers Northeastern India (south of the Brahmaputra river) and Andaman Islands.
    4. **Sundaland:** Covers the Nicobar group of Islands (extending to Malaysia, Indonesia, Brunei).
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** Qualifying criteria for a Hotspot: Must contain at least **1,500 species of vascular plants as endemics** (> 0.5% of world total) AND must have lost at least **70% of its original primary habitat**!

### Question 82
**Subject:** Indian Economy | **Topic:** Budget Deficits & Fiscal Indicators

Match List-I (Type of Deficit) with List-II (Formula / Definition) and select the correct answer:

**List-I (Deficit)**
A. Fiscal Deficit  
B. Revenue Deficit  
C. Primary Deficit  
D. Effective Revenue Deficit  

**List-II (Formula / Definition)**
1. Fiscal Deficit minus Interest Payments  
2. Revenue Deficit minus Grants for Creation of Capital Assets  
3. Revenue Expenditure minus Revenue Receipts  
4. Total Expenditure minus Total Receipts excluding borrowings  

**Options:**
A. A-4, B-3, C-1, D-2  
B. A-3, B-4, C-1, D-2  
C. A-4, B-1, C-3, D-2  
D. A-2, B-3, C-1, D-4  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - **Fiscal Deficit:** Total Expenditure - (Revenue Receipts + Non-debt Capital Receipts). Represents the total borrowing requirements of the government from all sources.
  - **Revenue Deficit:** Revenue Expenditure - Revenue Receipts. Indicates that the government is dissaving and using borrowings to finance routine current consumption.
  - **Primary Deficit:** Fiscal Deficit - Interest Payments. Indicates the borrowing requirements of the government excluding the interest liability on past accumulated debt.
  - **Effective Revenue Deficit:** Introduced in Union Budget 2011–12 (amended FRBM Act); excludes grants given to States/local bodies for the creation of capital assets from Revenue Deficit.
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** If Primary Deficit is zero, it means: Fiscal Deficit = Interest Payments (i.e., the entire borrowing is being consumed just to pay interest on past loans!).

**Subject:** Current Affairs | **Topic:** Cinema & Cultural Awards (Dadasaheb Phalke 2024)

Who was conferred with the prestigious Dadasaheb Phalke Award—India's highest recognition in the field of cinema—for the year 2022 at the 70th National Film Awards ceremony held in October 2024?

A. Mithun Chakraborty

  - Previous recipients: Waheeda Rehman (for 2021), Asha Parekh (for 2020), Rajinikanth (for 2019), Amitabh Bachchan (for 2018).

### Question 84
**Subject:** Ancient Indian History | **Topic:** Sangam Literature & Ancient Ports

Consider the following statements regarding the Sangam Age:
1. *Tolkappiyam*, authored by Tolkappiyar, is the oldest extant Tamil grammatical work.
2. The epic *Silappadikaram* was composed by Ilango Adigal, centering around the tragic story of Kovalan, Kannagi, and the Pandya king of Madurai.
3. Muziris was a celebrated port city of the Chola kingdom located on the Coromandel coast.

Which of the statements given above are correct?

**Options:**
A. 1 and 2 only  
B. 2 and 3 only  
C. 1 and 3 only  
D. 1, 2 and 3  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - Statements 1 and 2 are correct: *Tolkappiyam* is a masterpiece of grammar, linguistics, and poetics dating to the Second Sangam. *Silappadikaram* (The Jeweled Anklet) is an epic written by Jain prince Ilango Adigal about Kannagi's vengeance on Madurai.
  - Statement 3 is incorrect: **Muziris** (modern Kodungallur / Pattanam in Kerala) was the premier ancient port of the **Chera kingdom on the Malabar (western) coast**, famous for Roman pepper trade. The chief port of the Cholas was **Puhar (Kaveripattinam)** on the eastern Coromandel coast!
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** Sangam kingdoms and their capitals & ports:
  - **Cheras:** Capital = Vanji / Karur; Port = Muziris & Tondi; Emblem = Bow.
  - **Cholas:** Capital = Uraiyur / Puhar; Port = Kaveripattinam (Puhar); Emblem = Tiger.
  - **Pandyas:** Capital = Madurai; Port = Korkai; Emblem = Carp (Fish).

### Question 85
**Subject:** Geography of India | **Topic:** Godavari River System & Tributaries

Which of the following is a LEFT-BANK tributary of the Godavari River?

**Options:**
A. Manjira  
B. Penganga  
C. Pranhita  
D. Both B and C  

**Answer:** D

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - The Godavari (known as *Dakshin Ganga* or *Vriddha Ganga*) originates from Trimbakeshwar in the Nashik district of Maharashtra; length 1,465 km.
  - **Left-Bank Tributaries:** Dharna, Penganga, Wainganga, Wardha, **Pranhita** (formed by the confluence of Wardha and Wainganga), Pench, Kanhan, Sabari, and Indravati.
  - **Right-Bank Tributaries:** Pravara, Mula, and **Manjira** (Manjira is the only major right-bank tributary, originating in Balaghat range).
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** Manjira is a RIGHT-bank tributary; Indravati and Pranhita are LEFT-bank tributaries!

### Question 86
**Subject:** Indian Polity | **Topic:** Fundamental Duties (Article 51A)

Consider the following statements regarding the Fundamental Duties in the Indian Constitution:
1. Fundamental Duties were incorporated into Part IV-A of the Constitution upon the recommendation of the Swaran Singh Committee.
2. The 86th Constitutional Amendment Act, 2002 added the 11th Fundamental Duty regarding education of children between 6 and 14 years.
3. Fundamental Duties are directly enforceable by writs issued under Article 32.

Which of the statements given above is/are correct?

**Options:**
A. 1 and 2 only  
B. 2 and 3 only  
C. 1 only  
D. 1, 2 and 3  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - Statements 1 and 2 are correct: The Swaran Singh Committee (1976) recommended 8 duties; the 42nd CAA 1976 inserted Article 51A with 10 duties borrowed from the USSR constitution. The 86th CAA 2002 added the 11th duty [Article 51A(k)]: duty of a parent or guardian to provide educational opportunities to their child between ages 6 and 14.
  - Statement 3 is incorrect: Fundamental Duties, like DPSPs, are **non-justiciable and non-enforceable by judicial writs**. However, Parliament can enforce them by enacting specific substantive laws (e.g. Prevention of Insults to National Honour Act 1971, Wildlife Protection Act 1972).
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** Swaran Singh Committee had recommended a "Duty to Pay Taxes" and penalties for non-compliance, but the Indira Gandhi government REJECTED the duty to pay taxes and penalties!

### Question 87
**Subject:** Medieval Indian History | **Topic:** Administration of Sher Shah Suri (1540–1545)

Consider the following statements regarding Sher Shah Suri:
1. He introduced the silver coin called 'Rupiya' (178 grains) and copper coin called 'Dam' (380 grains), which formed the basis of the currency system up to the British period.
2. He revived the grand highway running from Sonargaon (Bengal) to Peshawar, known as the *Sadak-i-Azam* (later called Grand Trunk Road).
3. His monumental octagonal tomb situated in the middle of an artificial lake is located at Sasaram in Bihar.

Which of the statements given above are correct?

**Options:**
A. 1 and 2 only  
B. 2 and 3 only  
C. 1 and 3 only  
D. 1, 2 and 3  

**Answer:** D

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - All three statements are correct:
    - **Currency Reform:** Sher Shah abolished all debased mixed-metal coins and introduced standard coins: silver *Rupiya* (178 grains of pure silver) and copper *Dam* (ratio was 1 Rupiya = 64 Dams).
    - **Communications:** Reconstructed the imperial highway connecting Sonargaon (East Bengal) through Agra and Delhi to Attock/Peshawar (*Sadak-i-Azam*), constructing 1,700 *Sarais* (inns) with separate accommodation for Hindus and Muslims.
    - **Architecture:** Built the majestic red sandstone octagonal tomb at **Sasaram** (Rohtas district, Bihar) standing in an artificial lake; also built the *Purana Qila* (Qila-i-Kuhna Mosque) in Delhi.
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** Sher Shah died in May 1545 during the siege of **Kalinjar Fort** (Banda, Bundelkhand, UP) due to an accidental gunpowder explosion of a rocket named *Uqqa*!

### Question 88
**Subject:** Uttar Pradesh Special | **Topic:** Cultural Folk Music & Occasions

Match List-I (Folk Music Tradition of UP) with List-II (Core Theme / Occasion) and select the correct answer:

**List-I (Tradition)**
A. Birha  
B. Kajari  
C. Alha  
D. Rasiya  

**List-II (Theme / Occasion)**
1. Heroic tales of medieval warriors and martial chivalry  
2. Songs celebrating Lord Krishna and Radha, sung during Holi in Braj  
3. Yearning for the beloved, sung during monsoon season by women  
4. Narrative ballads depicting social events and heroism, sung in Purvanchal  

**Options:**
A. A-4, B-3, C-1, D-2  
B. A-3, B-4, C-1, D-2  
C. A-4, B-1, C-3, D-2  
D. A-2, B-3, C-1, D-4  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - **Birha:** Bhojpuri/Purvanchal narrative singing; recounts folklore, social satire, and heroism.
  - **Kajari:** Sung in Mirzapur/Varanasi during monsoon/Sawani teej; expresses separation, longing, and joy of rainy season.
  - **Alha:** Sung in Bundelkhand; tells martial ballads of Alha and Udal fighting against Prithviraj Chauhan.
  - **Rasiya:** Sung in Braj; filled with devotion, romance, and playful lyrics of Radha-Krishna during Holi.

### Question 89
**Subject:** General Science (Chemistry) | **Topic:** Polymers, Plastics & Synthetic Fibres

Match List-I (Polymer / Substance) with List-II (Monomer / Unique Application) and select the correct answer:

**List-I (Polymer)**
A. Teflon  
B. Natural Rubber  
C. Bakelite  
D. Polyvinyl Chloride (PVC)  

**List-II (Monomer / Application)**
1. Isoprene (2-methyl-1,3-butadiene)  
2. Polytetrafluoroethylene; non-stick coating for cookware  
3. Thermosetting polymer of Phenol and Formaldehyde; electrical switches  
4. Vinyl Chloride; pipes, insulation, and raincoats  

**Options:**
A. A-2, B-1, C-3, D-4  
B. A-1, B-2, C-3, D-4  
C. A-2, B-3, C-1, D-4  
D. A-4, B-1, C-3, D-2  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - **Teflon (PTFE):** Formed by polymerization of tetrafluoroethylene ($F_2C=CF_2$); high thermal stability and chemical inertness; used for non-stick pans and gasket seals.
  - **Natural Rubber:** Addition polymer of **Isoprene** (cis-1,4-polyisoprene). Vulcanization involves heating natural rubber with Sulphur to introduce cross-links and elasticity.
  - **Bakelite:** Oldest synthetic **thermosetting plastic**; condensation polymer of Phenol and Formaldehyde; infusible once molded; used for electrical switches, plugs, and cooker handles.
  - **PVC:** Polymer of vinyl chloride ($CH_2=CHCl$); widely used for water pipes and cable sheaths.
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** Distinction: Bakelite is a *thermosetting* plastic (cannot be remolded on heating); PVC and Polythene are *thermoplastics* (can be melted and reshaped repeatedly).

### Question 90
**Subject:** Environment & Ecology | **Topic:** Air Quality Index (AQI) Parameters

The National Air Quality Index (AQI) launched under the Swachh Bharat Abhiyan in India measures the concentration of how many atmospheric pollutants?

**Options:**
A. 6  
B. 8  
C. 10  
D. 12  

**Answer:** B

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - The National AQI monitors **8 criteria pollutants**:
    1. Particulate Matter 10 ($PM_{10}$)
    2. Particulate Matter 2.5 ($PM_{2.5}$)
    3. Nitrogen Dioxide ($NO_2$)
    4. Sulphur Dioxide ($SO_2$)
    5. Carbon Monoxide ($CO$)
    6. Ozone ($O_3$)
    7. Ammonia ($NH_3$)
    8. Lead ($Pb$)
  - AQI has 6 color-coded categories: Good (0–50), Satisfactory (51–100), Moderate (101–200), Poor (201–300), Very Poor (301–400), and Severe (401–500).
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** Carbon Dioxide ($CO_2$) is NOT one of the 8 pollutants measured by AQI! (UPPSC loves testing if $CO_2$ is in AQI — it is an atmospheric greenhouse gas, but not an AQI criteria pollutant).

### Question 91
**Subject:** Indian Economy | **Topic:** Priority Sector Lending (PSL) Targets

Consider the following statements regarding Priority Sector Lending (PSL) norms mandated by the Reserve Bank of India:
1. Domestic scheduled commercial banks are required to allocate 40% of their Adjusted Net Bank Credit (ANBC) to priority sectors.
2. The sub-target for Agriculture lending is 18% of ANBC.
3. The sub-target for Micro Enterprises is 7.5% of ANBC.

Which of the statements given above are correct?

**Options:**
A. 1 and 2 only  
B. 2 and 3 only  
C. 1 and 3 only  
D. 1, 2 and 3  

**Answer:** D

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - All three statements are correct:
    - Overall PSL target for domestic commercial banks and foreign banks with 20+ branches: **40% of ANBC** (or CEOBE).
    - **Agriculture Sub-target:** 18% of ANBC (within which 10% is earmarked for Small and Marginal Farmers - SMFs).
    - **Micro Enterprises Sub-target:** 7.5% of ANBC.
    - **Weaker Sections Sub-target:** 12% of ANBC.
    - Regional Rural Banks (RRBs) and Small Finance Banks (SFBs) have a higher overall target of **75% of ANBC**.
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** If banks fall short of their PSL targets, the shortfall must be deposited into the **Rural Infrastructure Development Fund (RIDF)** maintained by NABARD!

### Question 92
**Subject:** Current Affairs | **Topic:** Space & Defence (Aditya-L1 Mission)

Consider the following statements regarding ISRO's Aditya-L1 Mission:
1. It is India's first dedicated solar observatory in space.
2. It was successfully inserted into a halo orbit around the Sun-Earth Lagrange Point 1 (L1), located approximately 1.5 million kilometres from Earth.
3. The Visible Emission Line Coronagraph (VELC) is the primary payload designed to observe the solar corona.

Which of the statements given above are correct?

**Options:**
A. 1 and 2 only  
B. 2 and 3 only  
C. 1 and 3 only  
D. 1, 2 and 3  

**Answer:** D

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - All three statements are correct:
    - Aditya-L1 was launched on September 2, 2023, aboard PSLV-C57 from Satish Dhawan Space Centre, Sriharikota.
    - On January 6, 2024, it achieved final halo-orbit insertion around **Lagrange Point 1 (L1)**, ~1.5 million km from Earth (~1% of Earth-Sun distance).
    - Carries 7 scientific payloads: 4 remote sensing payloads including **VELC** (developed by Indian Institute of Astrophysics, Bengaluru) and **SUIT** (Solar Ultraviolet Imaging Telescope), plus 3 in-situ payloads (ASPEX, PAPA, and Magnetometer).
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** A satellite in halo orbit around L1 enjoys the major advantage of continuously viewing the Sun without any occultation or eclipses!

### Question 93
**Subject:** Modern Indian History | **Topic:** Quit India Movement (1942)

Consider the following statements regarding the Quit India Movement:
1. The resolution was ratified at the Gowalia Tank Maidan session of the All India Congress Committee in Bombay on August 8, 1942.
2. Under 'Operation Zero Hour', all top leaders of Congress were arrested in early morning raids on August 9, 1942.
3. During the movement, Chittu Pandey established a parallel national government at Ballia in Uttar Pradesh.

Which of the statements given above are correct?

**Options:**
A. 1 and 2 only  
B. 2 and 3 only  
C. 1 and 3 only  
D. 1, 2 and 3  

**Answer:** D

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - All three statements are correct:
    - Gandhi gave the historic call "Do or Die" (*Karo ya Maro*) at Gowalia Tank (August Kranti Maidan), Bombay.
    - British government launched **Operation Zero Hour** at dawn on August 9, arresting Mahatma Gandhi (confined at Aga Khan Palace, Pune) and CWC members (confined at Ahmednagar Fort).
    - **Parallel Governments (*Prati Sarkar*):**
      - **Ballia (UP):** Established for a week under Chittu Pandey ("Sher-e-Ballia").
      - **Tamluk (Midnapore, Bengal):** *Jatiya Sarkar* (featuring Matangini Hazra).
      - **Satara (Maharashtra):** Longest lasting *Prati Sarkar* led by Nana Patil and Y.B. Chavan.
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** Secret underground radio during Quit India Movement was operated by **Usha Mehta** and Babubhai Prasad from Bombay!

### Question 94
**Subject:** World Geography | **Topic:** Major Canals and Waterways

Match List-I (Canal) with List-II (Bodies of Water Connected) and select the correct answer:

**List-I (Canal)**
A. Suez Canal  
B. Panama Canal  
C. Kiel Canal  
D. Soo Canal  

**List-II (Bodies Connected)**
1. Atlantic Ocean and Pacific Ocean  
2. Mediterranean Sea and Red Sea  
3. Lake Superior and Lake Huron  
4. North Sea and Baltic Sea  

**Options:**
A. A-2, B-1, C-4, D-3  
B. A-1, B-2, C-4, D-3  
C. A-2, B-4, C-1, D-3  
D. A-3, B-1, C-4, D-2  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - **Suez Canal (Egypt, opened 1869):** Engineered by Ferdinand de Lesseps; connects the Mediterranean Sea (Port Said) with the Red Sea (Port Tewfik/Suez); cuts sea route between Europe and Asia by ~7,000 km.
  - **Panama Canal (opened 1914):** Cuts through Isthmus of Panama; connects the Atlantic Ocean (Caribbean Sea) with the Pacific Ocean utilizing a lock system.
  - **Kiel Canal (Germany):** Connects the North Sea with the Baltic Sea; busiest artificial waterway in the world.
  - **Soo Canal (USA/Canada):** Connects Lake Superior with Lake Huron bypass around St. Mary's Falls.
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** Suez Canal has NO locks (sea-level canal throughout), whereas Panama Canal uses an intricate 3-tiered lock system (Gatun, Pedro Miguel, Miraflores)!

### Question 95
**Subject:** Indian Polity | **Topic:** Parliamentary Financial Committees

Match List-I (Parliamentary Committee) with List-II (Composition & Salient Feature) and select the correct answer:

**List-I (Committee)**
A. Public Accounts Committee (PAC)  
B. Estimates Committee  
C. Committee on Public Undertakings  

**List-II (Composition & Feature)**
1. 30 members, ALL from Lok Sabha only; largest committee of Parliament  
2. 22 members (15 Lok Sabha + 7 Rajya Sabha); examines reports of CAG  
3. 22 members (15 Lok Sabha + 7 Rajya Sabha); examines PSUs and Krishna Menon Committee recommendations  

**Options:**
A. A-2, B-1, C-3  
B. A-1, B-2, C-3  
C. A-2, B-3, C-1  
D. A-3, B-1, C-2  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - **PAC:** Oldest financial committee (set up in 1921 under GOI Act 1919); 22 members (15 LS, 7 RS); by convention since 1967, its chairman is from the Opposition; acts as watchdog over government spending based on CAG audit reports.
  - **Estimates Committee:** Constituted in 1950 on recommendations of Finance Minister John Mathai; has **30 members, ALL drawn from Lok Sabha**; Rajya Sabha has NO representation; known as "Continuous Economy Committee".
  - **Committee on Public Undertakings:** Created in 1964 on recommendation of Krishna Menon Committee; 22 members (15 LS, 7 RS).
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** A **Minister CANNOT be elected** as a member of ANY of these three financial committees!

A. Samaharta  
B. Sannidhata  
C. Sitadhyaksha  
D. Samsthadhyaksha  

**List-II (Portfolio)**
1. Superintendent of Crown Agricultural Lands  
2. Chief Collector of Revenue and Assessor  
3. Superintendent of Markets / Trade routes  
4. Chief Custodian of the State Treasury and Storehouse

A. A-2, B-4, C-1, D-3  
B. A-4, B-2, C-1, D-3  
C. A-2, B-1, C-4, D-3  
D. A-3, B-4, C-1, D-2  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - **Samaharta:** Chief collector of revenue; responsible for assessing and collecting revenues across the entire realm.
  - **Sannidhata:** Chief treasurer and in-charge of royal storehouses (*Koshadhyaksha*).
  - **Sitadhyaksha:** Superintendent of *Sita* (crown agricultural lands cultivated by slaves, prisoners, and state tenants).

### Question 97
**Subject:** General Science (Physics) | **Topic:** Sound Waves & Frequencies

Match List-I (Wave Category) with List-II (Frequency Range / Characteristic) and select the correct answer:

A. Infrasonic Waves  
B. Audible Sound Waves  
C. Ultrasonic Waves  
D. Mach Number  

**List-II (Range / Characteristic)**
1. Frequencies above 20,000 Hz; used in SONAR and Echocardiography  
2. Frequencies below 20 Hz; produced by earthquakes, volcanoes, and whales  
3. Frequencies between 20 Hz and 20,000 Hz  
4. Ratio of the speed of a moving body to the speed of sound in the same medium  

**Options:**
A. A-2, B-3, C-1, D-4  
B. A-3, B-2, C-1, D-4  
C. A-2, B-1, C-3, D-4  
D. A-1, B-2, C-3, D-4  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - **Infrasonic (<20 Hz):** Inaudible to humans; elephants, rhinoceroses, and earthquakes generate infrasound.
  - **Audible (20 Hz – 20,000 Hz):** Normal human hearing range.
  - **Ultrasonic (>20,000 Hz):** Produced by bats and dolphins for echolocation; applied in SONAR (Sound Navigation and Ranging) to map ocean depth and ultrasonography (USG).

Which of the statements given above are correct?

A. 1 and 2 only  
B. 2 and 3 only  
C. 1 and 3 only  
D. 1, 2 and 3  

**Answer:** D

- **Core Fact & Solution:**

    - **NTCA:** Established following the 2006 amendment to the Wildlife (Protection) Act 1972 (chaired by the Union Minister of Environment, Forest and Climate Change).

### Question 100
**Subject:** Indian Economy | **Topic:** Goods and Services Tax (GST) & GST Council

Consider the following statements regarding the Goods and Services Tax (GST):
1. GST was introduced in India by the 101st Constitutional Amendment Act, 2016.
2. The GST Council is established under Article 279A of the Constitution.
3. In the GST Council, the Central Government has a weightage of one-half of the total votes cast, while all State Governments combined have one-half.

Which of the statements given above is/are correct?

**Options:**
A. 1 and 2 only  
B. 2 and 3 only  
C. 1 only  
D. 1, 2 and 3  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - Statements 1 and 2 are correct: GST came into effect on July 1, 2017 (101st CAA 2016). Article 279A empowered the President to constitute the GST Council within 60 days (chaired by the Union Finance Minister).
  - Statement 3 is incorrect: In the voting mechanism of the GST Council:
    - The vote of the **Central Government has a weightage of one-third ($rac{1}{3}$)** of total votes cast.
    - The votes of all **State Governments combined have a weightage of two-thirds ($rac{2}{3}$)** of total votes cast.
    - Every decision requires a majority of not less than **three-fourths ($rac{3}{4}$ / 75%)** of the weighted votes of members present and voting.
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** Voting weights trap: Central Govt = $rac{1}{3}$, State Govts = $rac{2}{3}$, Approval threshold = $rac{3}{4}$!

### Question 101
**Subject:** Current Affairs | **Topic:** Global Rankings & Indexes 2024

Match List-I (Global Report / Index) with List-II (Publishing Organization) and select the correct answer:

**List-I (Report / Index)**
A. Global Innovation Index (GII)  
B. World Press Freedom Index  
C. Global Gender Gap Report  
D. Logistics Performance Index (LPI)  

**List-II (Publishing Body)**
1. Reporters Without Borders (RSF)  
2. World Intellectual Property Organization (WIPO)  
3. World Bank  
4. World Economic Forum (WEF)  

**Options:**
A. A-2, B-1, C-4, D-3  
B. A-1, B-2, C-4, D-3  
C. A-2, B-4, C-1, D-3  
D. A-3, B-1, C-4, D-2  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - **Global Innovation Index:** Published annually by **WIPO**; India climbed to 39th rank in GII 2024 (Switzerland ranked 1st).
  - **World Press Freedom Index:** Published by Paris-based **Reporters Sans Frontières (RSF)**; Norway ranked 1st.
  - **Global Gender Gap Report:** Published by **World Economic Forum (WEF)** across 4 dimensions: Economic Participation, Educational Attainment, Health & Survival, Political Empowerment.
  - **Logistics Performance Index:** Published by **World Bank**; evaluates customs, infrastructure, international shipments, logistics competence, tracking & tracing, and timeliness.

### Question 102
**Subject:** Medieval Indian History | **Topic:** Religious Policy of Mughal Emperor Akbar

Arrange the following measures undertaken by Akbar in chronological order:
1. Abolition of Jizya tax
2. Establishment of the Ibadat Khana at Fatehpur Sikri
3. Promulgation of the Mahzarnama (Infallibility Decree)
4. Promulgation of the Tauhid-i-Ilahi (Din-i-Ilahi)

Select the correct answer from the codes given below:

**Options:**
A. 1 — 2 — 3 — 4  
B. 1 — 3 — 2 — 4  
C. 2 — 1 — 3 — 4  
D. 1 — 2 — 4 — 3  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  1. **Abolition of Jizya (1564):** Abolished the discriminatory tax on non-Muslim subjects (Akbar had already abolished the pilgrim tax in 1563).
  2. **Ibadat Khana (1575):** Hall of Prayer built at Fatehpur Sikri; initially open only to Sunni scholars, opened to all religions (Hindus, Jains, Parsis, Christians) in 1578.
  3. **Mahzarnama (September 1579):** Drafted by Sheikh Mubarak (father of Abul Fazl and Faizi); declared Akbar the *Imam-i-Adil* (supreme arbiter on Islamic law in case of conflicting interpretations).
  4. **Tauhid-i-Ilahi / Din-i-Ilahi (1582):** Socio-religious ethical order based on universal peace (*Sulh-i-Kul*); Birbal was the only Hindu noble who accepted it.
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** Chronology mnemonic: **Pilgrim tax (1563) -> Jizya (1564) -> Ibadat Khana (1575) -> Mahzar (1579) -> Din-i-Ilahi (1582) -> Ilahi Samvat (1584)**.

### Question 103
**Subject:** Indian Polity | **Topic:** President of India (Electoral College & Impeachment)

Consider the following statements regarding the President of India:
1. The Electoral College for electing the President (Article 54) includes the elected members of both Houses of Parliament and elected members of the Legislative Assemblies of States and the UTs of Delhi, Puducherry, and Jammu & Kashmir.
2. Nominated members of Parliament do not participate in the presidential election, but they DO participate in the impeachment of the President under Article 61.
3. Elected members of State Legislative Assemblies participate in the presidential election, but they DO NOT participate in the impeachment of the President.

Which of the statements given above are correct?

**Options:**
A. 1 and 2 only  
B. 2 and 3 only  
C. 1 and 3 only  
D. 1, 2 and 3  

**Answer:** D

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - All three statements are correct:
    - **Election Electoral College (Article 54):** Only ELECTED members of LS, RS, and State Legislative Assemblies (+ Delhi & Puducherry added by 70th CAA 1992). Nominated members and Legislative Councils (MLCs) do NOT participate.
    - **Impeachment (Article 61):** Quasi-judicial procedure in Parliament. Handled solely by Parliament: ALL members of LS and RS (both ELECTED and NOMINATED) vote!
    - Members of State Legislative Assemblies have NO role in the impeachment process.
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** Contrast with Vice-President election: Electoral college for VP consists of ALL members of Parliament (both elected and nominated), and State Assemblies have zero role!

### Question 104
**Subject:** Geography of India | **Topic:** Soils of India (ICAR Classification)

Match List-I (Soil Type) with List-II (Distinct Feature / Mineral Content) and select the correct answer:

**List-I (Soil)**
A. Alluvial Soil  
B. Black Soil (Regur)  
C. Laterite Soil  
D. Peaty / Organic Soil  

**List-II (Feature)**
1. High clay content with montmorillonite; self-ploughing capacity; ideal for cotton  
2. Rich in humus and organic matter; highly acidic; found in submerged coastal areas (Kuttanad)  
3. Formed by intense leaching in tropical regions with high rainfall; rich in iron and aluminium oxides  
4. Divided into Khadar and Bhangar; rich in potash, deficient in nitrogen and phosphorus  

**Options:**
A. A-4, B-1, C-3, D-2  
B. A-1, B-4, C-3, D-2  
C. A-4, B-3, C-1, D-2  
D. A-2, B-1, C-4, D-3  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - **Alluvial Soil:** Largest soil group (~40% of India's area); transported by rivers; Khadar is new fertile fine silt; Bhangar is older alluvium with calcareous concretions (*Kankar*).
  - **Black Soil (Regur):** Formed by weathering of Deccan basaltic trap; high water-retention capacity; swells when wet and shrinks when dry developing deep cracks ("self-ploughing").
  - **Laterite Soil:** Formed by intense tropical leaching (washing away of silica); leaves behind hydrated oxides of iron and aluminium; acidic, poor fertility, suitable for cashew, tea, coffee with fertilizers.
  - **Peaty Soil:** Heavy, black, highly acidic soils with 40–50% organic matter (found in Kottayam and Alappuzha in Kerala, Sundarbans).
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** Indian soils are almost universally deficient in **Nitrogen, Phosphorus, and Humus** (NPK ratio recommended is 4:2:1).

### Question 105
**Subject:** Modern Indian History | **Topic:** Revolutionary Societies & Conspiracy Cases

Match List-I (Revolutionary Society) with List-II (Founder / Key Leader) and select the correct answer:

**List-I (Society)**
A. Abhinav Bharat  
B. Anushilan Samiti (Calcutta)  
C. Ghadar Party  
D. Hindustan Socialist Republican Association (HSRA)  

**List-II (Founder / Leader)**
1. Pramathanath Mitra & Barindra Kumar Ghosh  
2. V.D. Savarkar (Vinayak Damodar Savarkar)  
3. Chandrashekhar Azad & Bhagat Singh  
4. Lala Har Dayal & Sohan Singh Bhakna  

**Options:**
A. A-2, B-1, C-4, D-3  
B. A-1, B-2, C-4, D-3  
C. A-2, B-4, C-1, D-3  
D. A-3, B-1, C-4, D-2  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - **Abhinav Bharat (1904, Nasik):** Secret society formed by Vinayak Damodar Savarkar and Ganesh Savarkar (evolved from *Mitra Mela* 1899).
  - **Anushilan Samiti (1902, Bengal):** Founded by Pramathanath Mitra (P. Mitra), Barindra Kumar Ghosh, and Jatindranath Banerjee.
  - **Ghadar Party (1913, San Francisco, USA):** Founded by Lala Har Dayal, Sohan Singh Bhakna (President), and Kartar Singh Sarabha; weekly newspaper *The Ghadar*.

- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** HRA was founded in Kanpur in 1924 by Ram Prasad Bismil, Sachindra Nath Sanyal, and Jogesh Chandra Chatterjee. It was transformed into HSRA in Delhi in 1928!

### Question 106
**Subject:** General Science (Biology) | **Topic:** Digestive System & Enzymes

Match List-I (Digestive Enzyme) with List-II (Secretory Site & Substrate Digested) and select the correct answer:

**List-I (Enzyme)**
A. Pepsin  
B. Ptyalin (Salivary Amylase)  
C. Trypsin  
D. Lipase  

**List-II (Site & Substrate)**
1. Pancreas; digests emulsified fats into fatty acids and glycerol  
2. Gastric glands (stomach); digests proteins into peptones in acidic medium  
3. Salivary glands (mouth); digests starch into maltose in slightly acidic/neutral medium  
4. Exocrine Pancreas; digests proteins into peptides in alkaline medium  

**Options:**
A. A-2, B-3, C-4, D-1  
B. A-3, B-2, C-4, D-1  
C. A-2, B-4, C-3, D-1  
D. A-4, B-3, C-2, D-1  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - **Pepsin:** Secreted as inactive pepsinogen by chief cells of stomach; activated by hydrochloric acid ($HCl$); optimum pH ~1.5–2.0; hydrolyzes proteins.
  - **Ptyalin (Salivary Amylase):** Secreted by salivary glands; begins carbohydrate digestion in mouth by converting starch into maltose.
  - **Trypsin:** Secreted as inactive trypsinogen by pancreas; activated by enterokinase in the small intestine; digests proteins in alkaline pH (~8.0).
  - **Lipase (Steapsin):** Secreted by pancreas and intestinal glands; breaks down fats emulsified by bile salts into fatty acids and glycerol.
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** Bile juice is secreted by the Liver and stored in the Gallbladder, but **Bile contains NO digestive enzymes**! It only emulsifies fats and neutralizes acidic chyme.

### Question 108
**Subject:** Environment & Ecology | **Topic:** Atmospheric Pollution & Acid Rain

Consider the following statements regarding Acid Rain:
1. Acid rain occurs when rainwater has a pH lower than 5.6.
2. The primary chemical precursors responsible for acid rain are Sulphur Dioxide ($SO_2$) and Oxides of Nitrogen ($NO_x$).
3. The yellowing and corrosion of the Taj Mahal's marble is known as "Stone Cancer", caused by acid precipitation reacting with calcium carbonate.

Which of the statements given above are correct?

**Options:**
A. 1 and 2 only  
B. 2 and 3 only  
C. 1 and 3 only  
D. 1, 2 and 3  

**Answer:** D

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - All three statements are correct:
    - Normal unpolluted rainwater is slightly acidic ($	ext{pH} pprox 5.6$) due to dissolved atmospheric $CO_2$ forming weak carbonic acid. Any precipitation with $	ext{pH} < 5.6$ is classified as **Acid Rain**.
    - $SO_2$ and $NO_x$ emitted from coal thermal plants, refineries (like Mathura Refinery near Agra), and automobiles react with water vapor:
      $$SO_2 + rac{1}{2}O_2 + H_2O 
ightarrow H_2SO_4 	ext{ (Sulphuric Acid)}$$
      $$2NO_2 + rac{1}{2}O_2 + H_2O 
ightarrow 2HNO_3 	ext{ (Nitric Acid)}$$
    - Reaction with marble ($CaCO_3$):
      $$CaCO_3 + H_2SO_4 
ightarrow CaSO_4 + H_2O + CO_2$$
      Resulting calcium sulphate flakes away, corroding the monuments ("Marble Cancer").
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** Acid rain produces sulphuric acid (~60–70%) and nitric acid (~30–40%). Hydrochloric acid is NOT a major component of typical acid rain.

### Question 109
**Subject:** Indian Economy | **Topic:** Banking Sector & Insolvency and Bankruptcy Code (IBC)

Consider the following statements regarding the Insolvency and Bankruptcy Code (IBC), 2016:
1. The National Company Law Tribunal (NCLT) is the adjudicating authority for insolvency resolution of corporate persons and companies.
2. The Debt Recovery Tribunal (DRT) is the adjudicating authority for insolvency resolution of individuals and partnership firms.
3. The Insolvency and Bankruptcy Board of India (IBBI) regulates insolvency professionals and information utilities.

Which of the statements given above are correct?

**Options:**
A. 1 and 2 only  
B. 2 and 3 only  
C. 1 and 3 only  
D. 1, 2 and 3  

**Answer:** D

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - All three statements are correct:
    - IBC 2016 created a unified legal framework for time-bound resolution of stressed assets:
      - **NCLT:** Adjudicating Authority for corporate debtors, LLPs, and companies.
      - **DRT:** Adjudicating Authority for individuals and partnership firms.
      - **IBBI:** Apex regulatory body set up under the Code (oversees IPAs, IPs, and Information Utilities like NeSL).
    - Corporate Insolvency Resolution Process (CIRP) must be completed within 180 days (extendable by 90 days, with an outer ceiling of 330 days).
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** Remember the jurisdictional boundary: Corporates go to NCLT; Individuals go to DRT!

### Question 111
**Subject:** Ancient Indian History | **Topic:** Gupta Coinage & Epigraphic Titles

Match List-I (Gupta Ruler) with List-II (Coin Type / Imperial Title) and select the correct answer:

**List-I (Ruler)**
A. Chandragupta I  
B. Samudragupta  
C. Chandragupta II  
D. Kumaragupta I  

**List-II (Coin / Epithet)**
1. Ashvamedha-type and Veena-playing type gold coins; *Kaviraja*  
2. King-and-Queen (Kumaradevi) type gold coins; *Maharajadhiraja*  
3. Silver coins introduced for the first time; *Vikramaditya* and *Sakari*  
4. Kartikeya / Peacock-type gold coins; *Mahendraditya*  

**Options:**
A. A-2, B-1, C-3, D-4  
B. A-1, B-2, C-3, D-4  
C. A-2, B-3, C-1, D-4  
D. A-4, B-1, C-3, D-2  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - **Chandragupta I:** First Gupta ruler to assume the title *Maharajadhiraja*; issued the famous joint Chandragupta I-Kumaradevi (Lichchhavi) matrimonial gold coins.
  - **Samudragupta:** Depicted playing the lute/veena on his coins; celebrated as *Kaviraja* (King of poets) and *Param-Bhagavata*; performed the Ashvamedha sacrifice.

- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** Gold coins in Gupta era were called **Dinars**; silver coins were called **Rupakas**!

### Question 113
**Subject:** Indian Polity | **Topic:** Supreme Court Jurisdiction & Powers

Consider the following statements regarding the Supreme Court of India:
1. Under Article 131, the Supreme Court has exclusive Original Jurisdiction in disputes between the Government of India and one or more States.
2. Under Article 143, the President can seek the advisory opinion of the Supreme Court, and the opinion delivered by the court is legally binding on the President.
3. Under Article 142, the Supreme Court may pass such decree or make such order as is necessary for doing complete justice in any cause or matter.

Which of the statements given above is/are correct?

**Options:**
A. 1 and 3 only  
B. 1 and 2 only  
C. 2 and 3 only  
D. 1, 2 and 3  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - Statement 1 is correct: Article 131 gives original and exclusive jurisdiction over federal disputes (Center vs State, Center & State vs State, State vs State). It does NOT extend to inter-state water disputes (Article 262) or ordinary commercial contracts.
  - Statement 2 is incorrect: Under Article 143 (Advisory Jurisdiction), the Supreme Court's advice is **purely advisory and NOT binding on the President**! (The President is free to accept or ignore the advice).
  - Statement 3 is correct: Article 142 empowers the apex court with plenary power to pass any order necessary for doing "complete justice" between the parties.
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** Under Article 143(1) (question of public importance), SC *may* decline to answer. But under Article 143(2) (pre-constitutional treaties), SC *must* give its opinion!

**Subject:** Medieval Indian History | **Topic:** Maratha Administration (Ashtapradhan of Shivaji)

Match List-I (Ashtapradhan Officer) with List-II (Portfolio / Responsibility) and select the correct answer:

**List-I (Officer)**
A. Peshwa (Mukhya Pradhan)  
B. Amatya (Majumdar)  
C. Sumant (Dabir)  
D. Sachiv (Surunavis)  

**List-II (Responsibility)**
1. Finance and Revenue Minister (Accountant General)  
2. Foreign Affairs Minister  
3. Prime Minister / General Administration  
4. Royal Secretariat and Official Correspondence  

**Options:**
A. A-3, B-1, C-2, D-4  
B. A-1, B-3, C-2, D-4  
C. A-3, B-2, C-1, D-4  
D. A-2, B-1, C-4, D-3  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - Shivaji was assisted by a council of eight ministers known as the **Ashtapradhan**:
    - **Peshwa:** Prime Minister looking after general administration.
    - **Amatya / Majumdar:** Finance minister in charge of public accounts.
    - **Sumant / Dabir:** Minister for Foreign Affairs.
    - **Sachiv / Shurunavis:** Handled royal correspondence and royal seals.

- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** All ministers in the Ashtapradhan EXCEPT Nyayadhish and Pandit Rao were required to lead military expeditions in times of war!

### Question 115
**Subject:** General Science (Physics) | **Topic:** Magnetic & Nuclear Constants

Match List-I (Physical Quantity) with List-II (SI / Scientific Unit) and select the correct answer:

**List-I (Quantity)**
A. Magnetic Flux  
B. Magnetic Flux Density (Magnetic Field)  
C. Nuclear Radius / Size  
D. Luminous Intensity  

**List-II (Unit)**
1. Tesla (T)  
2. Weber (Wb)  
3. Candela (cd)  
4. Fermi (Femtometre, $10^{-15} 	ext{ m}$)  

**Options:**
A. A-2, B-1, C-4, D-3  
B. A-1, B-2, C-4, D-3  
C. A-2, B-4, C-1, D-3  
D. A-3, B-1, C-4, D-2  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - **Magnetic Flux ($\Phi$):** Measured in **Weber (Wb)**. $1 	ext{ Wb} = 1 	ext{ V} \cdot 	ext{s} = 1 	ext{ T} \cdot 	ext{m}^2$.
  - **Magnetic Field ($B$):** Measured in **Tesla (T)** in SI system (in CGS, it is Gauss; $1 	ext{ Tesla} = 10^4 	ext{ Gauss}$).

### Question 117
**Subject:** Environment & Ecology | **Topic:** Types of Smog (Photochemical vs Classical)

Consider the following statements regarding atmospheric smog:
1. Photochemical smog (Los Angeles smog) is an oxidizing smog formed in warm, sunny, and dry climates by the reaction of sunlight on nitrogen oxides and hydrocarbons.
2. Ozone ($O_3$) and Peroxyacetyl Nitrate (PAN) are key secondary pollutants present in photochemical smog.
3. Classical smog (London smog) is a reducing smog that occurs in cool, humid climates and contains a high mixture of smoke, fog, and sulphur dioxide ($SO_2$).

Which of the statements given above are correct?

**Options:**
A. 1 and 2 only  
B. 2 and 3 only  
C. 1 and 3 only  
D. 1, 2 and 3  

**Answer:** D

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - All three statements are correct:
    - **Photochemical Smog (Los Angeles type):** Formed during sunny summer afternoons; auto exhaust produces $NO_2$ and unburnt hydrocarbons; UV rays photolyze $NO_2$ releasing free oxygen atoms that generate ground-level Ozone ($O_3$), PAN (Peroxyacetyl Nitrate), and formaldehyde; chemically **Oxidizing**.
    - **Classical Smog (London type):** Formed in winter mornings with temperature inversion; coal smoke + fog + $SO_2$; chemically **Reducing**.
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** Photochemical smog is OXIDIZING (due to high ozone/peroxides); Classical smog is REDUCING (due to sulphur dioxide)!

### Question 118
**Subject:** Modern Indian History | **Topic:** Reforms of Lord William Bentinck (1828–1835)

Consider the following reforms undertaken during the Governor-Generalship of Lord William Bentinck:
1. Abolition of the practice of Sati through Regulation XVII of December 1829.
2. Suppression of the menace of Thuggee organized under Captain William Sleeman.
3. Passing of the English Education Act of 1835 based on Lord Macaulay's Minute.

Which of the statements given above are correct?

**Options:**
A. 1 and 2 only  
B. 2 and 3 only  
C. 1 and 3 only  
D. 1, 2 and 3  

**Answer:** D

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - All three statements are correct:
    - **Abolition of Sati:** Passed on December 4, 1829, as Regulation XVII of the Bengal Code, declaring Sati illegal and punishable as culpable homicide (extended to Madras and Bombay Presidencies in 1830), actively supported by Raja Ram Mohan Roy.
    - **Thuggee Suppression:** Bentinck appointed Captain William Henry Sleeman in 1830; hundreds of hereditary robbers/stranglers (Thugs) were arrested and suppressed.
    - **English Education:** Act of 1835 endorsed Macaulay's famous Minute of February 1835, making English the official medium of higher instruction and government transactions.
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** Bentinck was the last Governor-General of Bengal and became the **FIRST Governor-General of India** under the Charter Act of 1833!

### Question 119
**Subject:** Indian Economy | **Topic:** Concepts of Inflation

Match List-I (Economic Concept) with List-II (Definition / Impact) and select the correct answer:

**List-I (Concept)**
A. Core Inflation  
B. Headline Inflation  
C. Stagflation  
D. Demand-Pull Inflation  

**List-II (Definition)**
1. Measures total inflation in an economy including food and fuel items  
2. Inflation that excludes volatile price components (food and energy commodities)  
3. Situation of stagnant economic growth and high unemployment accompanied by high inflation  
4. Price rise driven by aggregate demand exceeding aggregate supply in an economy  

**Options:**
A. A-2, B-1, C-3, D-4  
B. A-1, B-2, C-3, D-4  
C. A-2, B-4, C-1, D-3  
D. A-3, B-1, C-4, D-2  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - **Core Inflation:** Headline inflation minus Food and Fuel; tracks underlying non-volatile inflation trend unaffected by short-term supply shocks.
  - **Headline Inflation:** Comprehensive measure of all goods and services in the consumer basket (CPI-Combined in India).
  - **Stagflation:** Portmanteau of stagnation and inflation; high unemployment, slowing GDP growth, and rising inflation (defies the classic short-run Phillips curve).
  - **Demand-Pull Inflation:** "Too much money chasing too few goods"; occurs when aggregate demand outpaces productive capacity.
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** Cost-Push Inflation is caused by supply-side shocks, like sudden spikes in international crude oil prices or agricultural crop failures!

### Question 121
**Subject:** Geography of India | **Topic:** Peninsular Mountain Sequences

Arrange the following mountain and hill ranges of Peninsular India in sequential order from NORTH to SOUTH:
1. Satpura Range
2. Ajanta Range
3. Nilgiri Hills
4. Cardamom Hills

Select the correct answer from the codes given below:

**Options:**
A. 1 — 2 — 3 — 4  
B. 2 — 1 — 3 — 4  
C. 1 — 3 — 2 — 4  
D. 1 — 2 — 4 — 3  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - Sequential alignment from North to South:
    1. **Satpura Range (MP/Maharashtra border):** Between Narmada and Tapi rivers (~21° to 22° N).
    2. **Ajanta Range (Maharashtra):** South of Tapi river in Marathwada (~19° to 20° N).
    3. **Nilgiri Hills (TN/Kerala/Karnataka junction):** Meeting point of Western and Eastern Ghats (~11° N).
    4. **Cardamom Hills (Kerala/Tamil Nadu):** Southernmost hill range of mainland India (~9° N).
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** Anaimalai Hills lie between Nilgiri Hills (separated by Palghat gap) and Cardamom Hills. Sequence North to South: **Nilgiri -> Anaimalai -> Cardamom Hills**!

### Question 122
**Subject:** Indian Polity | **Topic:** Anti-Defection Law & 91st Amendment Act

Consider the following statements regarding the 91st Constitutional Amendment Act, 2003:
1. It limited the total number of ministers, including the Prime Minister, in the Union Council of Ministers to not exceed 15% of the total strength of the Lok Sabha.
2. It mandated that the total number of ministers, including the Chief Minister, in a State shall not exceed 15% of the total strength of the Legislative Assembly, subject to a minimum of 12 ministers.
3. It deleted the provision in the Tenth Schedule that previously protected defecting legislators if one-third of a parliamentary party split.

Which of the statements given above are correct?

**Options:**
A. 1 and 2 only  
B. 2 and 3 only  
C. 1 and 3 only  
D. 1, 2 and 3  

**Answer:** D

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - All three statements are correct:
    - Enacted in 2003 to curb jumbo cabinets and horse-trading:
      - **15% Ceiling:** Union Council of Ministers cannot exceed 15% of Lok Sabha membership (Article 75(1A)).
      - **State Ceiling:** State Council of Ministers cannot exceed 15% of Legislative Assembly strength, but cannot be less than 12 ministers (Article 164(1A)).
      - **Anti-Defection:** Removed the "split" defense (former paragraph 3 of Tenth Schedule); now only a **merger** of not less than two-thirds ($rac{2}{3}$) of the members of a legislative party protects them from disqualification.
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** In Delhi (NCT), under Article 239AA, the Council of Ministers cannot exceed **10%** of the Legislative Assembly (maximum 7 ministers including CM)!

### Question 123
**Subject:** Ancient Indian History | **Topic:** Jain Tirthankaras and Their Cognizance Symbols

Match List-I (Jain Tirthankara) with List-II (Lanchhana / Cognizance Symbol) and select the correct answer:

**List-I (Tirthankara)**
A. Rishabhanatha (Adinatha)  
B. Parshvanatha  
C. Mahavira  
D. Neminatha (Arishtanemi)  

**List-II (Symbol)**
1. Serpent hood (Snake)  
2. Bull (*Vrishabha*)  
3. Conch shell (*Shankha*)  
4. Lion (*Simha*)  

**Options:**
A. A-2, B-1, C-4, D-3  
B. A-1, B-2, C-4, D-3  
C. A-2, B-4, C-1, D-3  
D. A-3, B-1, C-4, D-2  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - **Rishabhanatha (1st Tirthankara):** Bull (*Vrishabha*); born at Ayodhya.
  - **Parshvanatha (23rd Tirthankara):** Serpent (*Sarpa*); prince of Varanasi; propounded four vows: Ahimsa, Satya, Asteya, Aparigraha.
  - **Mahavira (24th Tirthankara):** Lion (*Simha*); born at Kundagrama (Vaishali); added the fifth vow: Brahmacharya (celibacy).
  - **Neminatha (22nd Tirthankara):** Conch shell (*Shankha*); considered contemporary/cousin of Krishna.
  - **Mallinatha (19th Tirthankara):** Water pot (*Kalasha*); regarded as female by Svetambara sect.
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** Sambhavnatha (3rd Tirthankara) has the **Horse** as his symbol; Ajitanatha (2nd) has the **Elephant**!

### Question 124
**Subject:** General Science (Physics) | **Topic:** Anomalous Expansion of Water & Latent Heat

Consider the following statements regarding the thermal properties of matter:
1. Water exhibits anomalous expansion between 0°C and 4°C, reaching its maximum density at 4°C.
2. Because water expands as it cools from 4°C to 0°C, ice floats on water and aquatic life survives in frozen lakes.
3. Steam at 100°C produces more severe burns than boiling water at 100°C because steam possesses extra latent heat of vaporization (approx. 540 cal/g).

Which of the statements given above are correct?

**Options:**
A. 1 and 2 only  
B. 2 and 3 only  
C. 1 and 3 only  
D. 1, 2 and 3  

**Answer:** D

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - All three statements are correct:
    - **Anomalous Expansion:** Unlike other liquids which contract uniformly upon cooling, water contracts until 4°C, where it reaches **maximum density (1 g/cm³ or 1000 kg/m³)** and minimum volume. Below 4°C, it expands.
    - **Aquatic Survival:** In winter, surface water cools to 4°C and sinks; when surface reaches 0°C, it freezes into ice. Since ice is a poor conductor of heat and less dense than water, bottom water remains liquid at 4°C, allowing aquatic animals to survive.
    - **Latent Heat:** Steam contains latent heat of vaporization ($2.26 	imes 10^6 	ext{ J/kg}$ or $pprox 540 	ext{ cal/g}$); when condensing on skin, it releases this enormous latent heat at constant temperature before cooling as water.
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** At 4°C, water has MAXIMUM density and MINIMUM volume!

### Question 126
**Subject:** Environment & Ecology | **Topic:** Mangrove Ecosystems in India

Consider the following statements regarding mangroves in India:
1. Mangrove plants exhibit specialized respiratory roots called 'pneumatophores' that grow vertically upwards to obtain atmospheric oxygen.
2. Mangroves exhibit 'viviparous germination', where seeds germinate while still attached to the parent tree before falling into saline mud.
3. West Bengal has the largest mangrove forest cover in India, followed by Gujarat and Andaman & Nicobar Islands.

Which of the statements given above are correct?

**Options:**
A. 1 and 2 only  
B. 2 and 3 only  
C. 1 and 3 only  
D. 1, 2 and 3  

**Answer:** D

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - All three statements are correct:
    - Mangroves survive in anaerobic saline intertidal mudflats via:
      - **Pneumatophores (blind roots):** Grow negative geotropically with lenticels for gas exchange.
      - **Stilt roots:** Provide mechanical anchorage against ocean waves.
      - **Vivipary:** Seed germinates into a seedling while still attached to mother plant.
    - **State Rankings (ISFR):**
      1. **West Bengal** (~42.45% of India's total mangrove cover in Sundarbans).
      2. **Gujarat** (~23.66% in Gulf of Kutch and Khambhat).
      3. **Andaman & Nicobar Islands** (~12.39%).
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** Second largest mangrove forest in India is **Bhitarkanika** (Odisha, located in the delta of Brahmani and Baitarani rivers).

A. Annie Besant  
B. Sarojini Naidu  
C. Nellie Sengupta  

**List-II (Session & Year)**

2. 1917 Calcutta Session; First woman President of Congress  
3. 1933 Calcutta Session; Third woman President of Congress  

**Options:**
A. A-2, B-1, C-3  
B. A-1, B-2, C-3  
C. A-2, B-3, C-1  
D. A-3, B-1, C-2  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - **Annie Besant (1917 Calcutta):** First EVER woman to preside over the Indian National Congress.
  - **Sarojini Naidu (1925 Kanpur):** First **INDIAN** woman to preside over the Congress; celebrated as *Nightingale of India* (*Bharat Kokila*).
  - **Nellie Sengupta (1933 Calcutta):** English-born woman married to J.M. Sengupta; third woman to preside over Congress in pre-independence India.

### Question 128
**Subject:** Indian Polity & Governance | **Topic:** Structure and Functions of NITI Aayog

Consider the following statements regarding the NITI Aayog:
1. It was established by an executive resolution of the Union Cabinet on January 1, 2015, replacing the Planning Commission.
2. The Prime Minister of India serves as the ex-officio Chairperson of NITI Aayog.
3. The Governing Council of NITI Aayog comprises the Prime Minister, Chief Ministers of all States, and Lieutenant Governors of Union Territories.

Which of the statements given above are correct?

**Options:**
A. 1 and 2 only  
B. 2 and 3 only  
C. 1 and 3 only  
D. 1, 2 and 3  

**Answer:** D

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - All three statements are correct:
    - **Establishment:** NITI (National Institution for Transforming India) Aayog was constituted on 1 January 2015 as a non-statutory, non-constitutional think-tank emphasizing "Cooperative Federalism" and "Bottom-up approach".
    - **Leadership:** Chairperson = Prime Minister. Vice-Chairperson is appointed by the PM (enjoys rank of Cabinet Minister).
    - **Governing Council:** Chief Ministers of all States, CMs of UTs with legislatures (Delhi, Puducherry, J&K), and Lt. Governors of other UTs.
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** NITI Aayog does NOT possess the power to allocate financial funds to states; that power rests with the Ministry of Finance and the Finance Commission!

### Question 130
**Subject:** Medieval Indian History | **Topic:** Vijayanagara Empire Dynasties Chronology

Arrange the four ruling dynasties of the Vijayanagara Empire in chronological order:
1. Saluva Dynasty
2. Sangama Dynasty
3. Aravidu Dynasty
4. Tuluva Dynasty

Select the correct answer from the codes given below:

**Options:**
A. 2 — 1 — 4 — 3  
B. 1 — 2 — 4 — 3  
C. 2 — 4 — 1 — 3  
D. 2 — 1 — 3 — 4  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  1. **Sangama Dynasty (1336–1485):** Founded by brothers Harihara I and Bukka I; prominent rulers: Deva Raya I and Deva Raya II.
  2. **Saluva Dynasty (1485–1505):** Founded by Saluva Narasimha (First Usurpation).
  3. **Tuluva Dynasty (1505–1570):** Founded by Vira Narasimha (Second Usurpation); reached its zenith under **Krishnadeva Raya (1509–1529)**; suffered disaster at the Battle of Talikota (1565).
  4. **Aravidu Dynasty (1570–1646):** Founded by Tirumala Raya with capital at Penukonda and Chandragiri.
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** Mnemonic: **S-S-T-A** (Sangama -> Saluva -> Tuluva -> Aravidu). Battle of Talikota (Rakshasi-Tangadi) was fought on 23 January 1565 during the reign of Sadashiva Raya (Tuluva dynasty).

### Question 131
**Subject:** World Geography | **Topic:** Ocean Trenches & Deepest Points

Match List-I (Ocean Trench / Deep) with List-II (Ocean Location) and select the correct answer:

**List-I (Trench)**
A. Mariana Trench (Challenger Deep)  
B. Puerto Rico Trench (Milwaukee Deep)  
C. Java / Sunda Trench  
D. Molloy Deep  

**List-II (Ocean)**
1. Atlantic Ocean  
2. Pacific Ocean  
3. Arctic Ocean  
4. Indian Ocean  

**Options:**
A. A-2, B-1, C-4, D-3  
B. A-1, B-2, C-4, D-3  
C. A-2, B-4, C-1, D-3  
D. A-3, B-1, C-4, D-2  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - **Mariana Trench (Challenger Deep):** Deepest oceanic point on Earth (~10,994 m); Western Pacific Ocean near Guam.
  - **Puerto Rico Trench (Milwaukee Deep):** Deepest point in the Atlantic Ocean (~8,376 m).
  - **Java / Sunda Trench:** Deepest point in the Indian Ocean (~7,450 m).

### Question 132
**Subject:** Indian Polity | **Topic:** Emergency Provisions (Part XVIII)

Consider the following statements regarding Emergency Provisions:
1. Under Article 352, a Proclamation of National Emergency must be approved by both Houses of Parliament by a special majority within one month.
2. The 44th Constitutional Amendment Act substituted the phrase "internal disturbance" with "armed rebellion" in Article 352.
3. During a National Emergency, the right to protection in respect of conviction for offences (Article 20) and right to life and personal liberty (Article 21) cannot be suspended.

Which of the statements given above are correct?

**Options:**
A. 1 and 2 only  
B. 2 and 3 only  
C. 1 and 3 only  
D. 1, 2 and 3  

**Answer:** D

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - All three statements are correct:
    - **Approval & Majority:** Article 352 proclamation must be approved within 1 month by a special majority (majority of total membership and $\ge rac{2}{3}$ of members present and voting) in each House; valid for 6 months.
    - **44th CAA 1978 Safeguards:**
      - Replaced ambiguous "internal disturbance" with "armed rebellion".
      - Mandated written advice of the Union Cabinet (only place "Cabinet" is mentioned in Constitution: Article 352(3)).
      - **Articles 20 and 21 can NEVER be suspended** even under emergency (Article 359).
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** Article 19 is automatically suspended ONLY when National Emergency is declared on grounds of "War" or "External Aggression" (External Emergency), NOT on grounds of "Armed Rebellion" (Internal Emergency)!
