### Question 133
**Subject:** General Science (Biology) | **Topic:** Cell Organelles and Functions

Match List-I (Cell Organelle) with List-II (Popular Epithet / Biochemical Function) and select the correct answer:

**List-I (Organelle)**
A. Mitochondria  
B. Lysosome  
C. Ribosome  
D. Golgi Apparatus  

**List-II (Epithet / Function)**
1. "Suicidal Bag" of the cell; contains hydrolytic enzymes  
2. "Powerhouse of the cell"; site of cellular respiration & ATP synthesis  
3. Packaging and secretion center; formation of acrosome in sperm  
4. "Protein factory" of the cell; site of translation  

**Options:**
A. A-2, B-1, C-4, D-3  
B. A-1, B-2, C-4, D-3  
C. A-2, B-4, C-1, D-3  
D. A-3, B-1, C-4, D-2  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - **Mitochondria:** Double-membraned organelle; site of Krebs cycle and oxidative phosphorylation generating ATP; possesses its own circular DNA and 70S ribosomes (semi-autonomous).
  - **Lysosome:** Single-membraned vesicle filled with acid hydrolases (optimum pH ~5.0) that digest foreign material and worn-out cell parts ("Suicidal Bags").
  - **Ribosome:** Non-membrane bound ribonucleoprotein particle; synthesizes polypeptide chains from mRNA templates ("Protein Factories").
  - **Golgi Apparatus:** Discovered by Camillo Golgi; modifies, sorts, and packages macromolecules for secretion; forms lysosomes and the sperm acrosome.
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** Both Mitochondria and Chloroplasts are **semi-autonomous organelles** having their own DNA and 70S ribosomes (like bacteria, supporting endosymbiotic theory)!

### Question 135
**Subject:** Environment & Ecology | **Topic:** CITES Appendices Classification

Consider the following statements regarding the Convention on International Trade in Endangered Species of Wild Fauna and Flora (CITES):
1. Appendix I includes species threatened with extinction, and international commercial trade in these specimens is generally prohibited.
2. Appendix II includes species that are not necessarily threatened with extinction now, but may become so unless trade is strictly regulated.
3. CITES Secretariat is administered by the United Nations Environment Programme (UNEP) and is located in Geneva, Switzerland.

Which of the statements given above are correct?

**Options:**
A. 1 and 2 only  
B. 2 and 3 only  
C. 1 and 3 only  
D. 1, 2 and 3  

**Answer:** D

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - All three statements are correct:
    - **CITES (Washington Convention, 1973):** Legally binding on Parties; protects over 38,000 species.
    - **Appendix I:** Highest protection (Tiger, Asiatic Lion, Great Indian Bustard, Red Panda); commercial trade banned.
    - **Appendix II:** Trade permitted only with export permits certifying that trade will not be detrimental to survival in the wild.
    - **Appendix III:** Contains species protected in at least one country that has asked CITES parties for assistance.
    - **Headquarters:** Geneva, Switzerland; administered by UNEP.
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** CITES regulates ONLY international commercial trade of wild specimens; it does not replace domestic wildlife management laws!

**Subject:** Modern Indian History | **Topic:** Swaraj Party (1923)

Consider the following statements regarding the Swaraj Party:
1. It was founded in January 1923 at Allahabad by Chittaranjan Das (President) and Motilal Nehru (Secretary).
2. The core objective of the Swarajists was to enter the legislative councils and "end or mend" them from within.
3. In August 1925, Vithalbhai J. Patel was elected as the first Indian Speaker (President) of the Central Legislative Assembly.

Which of the statements given above are correct?

**Options:**
A. 1 and 2 only  
B. 2 and 3 only  
C. 1 and 3 only  
D. 1, 2 and 3  

**Answer:** D

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - All three statements are correct:
    - Formed following the Gaya session (December 1922) where C.R. Das's proposal for council entry was defeated by "No-Changers" (Rajendra Prasad, Vallabhbhai Patel, C. Rajagopalachari).
    - In January 1923, Das and Motilal Nehru announced the formation of the **Congress-Khilafat Swarajya Party** at Allahabad.
    - In the November 1923 elections, Swarajists won 42 out of 101 elected seats in the Central Legislative Assembly.
    - Major constitutional victory: On August 24, 1925, **Vithalbhai Patel** was elected President (Speaker) of the Central Legislative Assembly!

### Question 137
**Subject:** Indian Economy | **Topic:** Balance of Payments (BoP) Structure

Match List-I (BoP Component) with List-II (Sub-head / Item) and select the correct answer:

**List-I (Component)**
A. Current Account  
B. Capital Account  
C. Foreign Direct Investment (FDI)  
D. Foreign Portfolio Investment (FPI)  

**List-II (Item / Characteristic)**
1. External Commercial Borrowings (ECB), NRI deposits, and foreign loans  
2. Trade in goods (merchandise), trade in services, remittances, and gifts  
3. Passive investment in stocks/bonds (<10% equity) without managerial control  
4. Non-debt creating capital flow with lasting interest ( $\ge 10\%$ equity) and active management  

**Options:**
A. A-2, B-1, C-4, D-3  
B. A-1, B-2, C-4, D-3  
C. A-2, B-4, C-1, D-3  
D. A-3, B-1, C-4, D-2  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - **Current Account:** Encompasses Visible trade (exports/imports of goods), Invisible trade (software services, tourism, shipping), and Unilateral transfers (remittances from NRIs, gifts, grants).
  - **Capital Account:** Encompasses FDI, FPI, External Commercial Borrowings (ECB), foreign aid, NRI bank deposits, and sovereign loans (affects asset/liability position).
  - **FDI vs FPI (Mayaram Committee Definition):** Foreign investment of **10% or more** in the listed equity of an Indian company is classified as FDI (stable, long-term). Investment of **less than 10%** is classified as FPI (volatile, "hot money").
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** India usually runs a **Current Account Deficit (CAD)** due to heavy crude oil/gold imports, but compensates for it with a **Capital Account Surplus**!

### Question 139
**Subject:** Ancient Indian History | **Topic:** Vedic Literature & Upanishadic Maxims

Match List-I (Upanishad) with List-II (Famous Philosophical Maxim / Dialogue) and select the correct answer:

**List-I (Upanishad)**
A. Mundaka Upanishad  
B. Kathopanishad  
C. Chandogya Upanishad  
D. Brihadaranyaka Upanishad  

**List-II (Maxim / Dialogue)**
1. "Satyameva Jayate" (Truth alone triumphs)  
2. Dialogue between young seeker Nachiketa and Lord Yama on the nature of death and soul  
3. "Tamaso Ma Jyotirgamaya" and dialogue between sage Yajnavalkya and Maitreyi  
4. First mention of Krishna as the son of Devaki and dialogue of Uddalaka Aruni with Svetaketu  

**Options:**
A. A-1, B-2, C-4, D-3  
B. A-2, B-1, C-4, D-3  
C. A-1, B-4, C-2, D-3  
D. A-3, B-2, C-1, D-4  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - **Mundaka Upanishad:** Source of India's national motto *"Satyameva Jayate"*; also compares rituals and sacrifices to "leaky boats" (*plava hyete adridha yajnarupah*).
  - **Kathopanishad:** Famous philosophical dialogue between the young boy Nachiketa and Yama (God of Death) regarding the immortality of Atman.
  - **Chandogya Upanishad:** Mentions Krishna, son of Devaki, as a disciple of sage Ghora Angirasa; contains the famous phrase *"Tat Tvam Asi"* (That Thou Art); mentions first three ashramas.
  - **Brihadaranyaka Upanishad:** Largest Upanishad; source of *"Tamaso Ma Jyotirgamaya"*; records the celebrated debate between sage Yajnavalkya and philosopher Gargi Vachaknavi at King Janaka's court.
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** All four Ashramas (Brahmacharya, Grihastha, Vanaprastha, Sannyasa) are mentioned together for the first time in the **Jabala Upanishad**!

C. A-2, B-4, C-1, D-3  
D. A-3, B-1, C-4, D-2  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - **Nile:** Longest river in the world; flows northward through 11 African nations into the Mediterranean Sea; formed by the confluence of White Nile (originates Lake Victoria) and Blue Nile (Lake Tana, Ethiopia) at Khartoum (Sudan).
  - **Amazon:** Largest river by volume and basin area; originates in Peruvian Andes; empties into the Atlantic Ocean; carries more water than the next 7 largest rivers combined.
  - **Yangtze (Chang Jiang):** Longest river in Asia and third longest in the world; Three Gorges Dam is situated on it; empties into East China Sea near Shanghai.
  - **Mississippi-Missouri:** Longest river system in North America; famous for its distinctive **Bird's foot delta** in the Gulf of Mexico.
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** River Congo (Zaire) crosses the Equator TWICE! River Limpopo (South Africa) crosses the Tropic of Capricorn TWICE! River Mahi (India) crosses the Tropic of Cancer TWICE!

### Question 141
**Subject:** Indian Polity | **Topic:** Landmark Constitutional Amendment Acts

Match List-I (Constitutional Amendment Act) with List-II (Core Subject) and select the correct answer:

**List-I (Amendment)**
A. 42nd Amendment Act, 1976  
B. 44th Amendment Act, 1978  
C. 86th Amendment Act, 2002  
D. 103rd Amendment Act, 2019  

**List-II (Subject)**
1. Right to Education (Article 21A) inserted as a Fundamental Right  
2. "Mini-Constitution"; added Fundamental Duties, 3 words to Preamble, and 4 DPSPs  
3. 10% Reservation for Economically Weaker Sections (EWS) in education and public employment  
4. Right to Property removed from Fundamental Rights; safeguards against Emergency abuse  

**Options:**
A. A-2, B-4, C-1, D-3  
B. A-4, B-2, C-1, D-3  
C. A-2, B-1, C-4, D-3  
D. A-3, B-4, C-1, D-2  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - **42nd CAA 1976:** Known as "Mini-Constitution"; based on Swaran Singh Committee; added "Socialist, Secular, Integrity" to Preamble; Part IV-A (Fundamental Duties); Articles 39A, 43A, 48A.
  - **44th CAA 1978 (Janata Govt):** Removed Right to Property from Part III (made a legal right under Article 300A); restored Lok Sabha term to 5 years; inserted safeguards for Emergency under Article 352/359.
  - **86th CAA 2002:** Inserted Article 21A (free and compulsory education for ages 6–14); substituted Article 45 in DPSPs; added Article 51A(k) (11th fundamental duty).
  - **103rd CAA 2019:** Amended Articles 15(6) and 16(6) to grant up to 10% reservation to Economically Weaker Sections (EWS).
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** 101st CAA = GST; 102nd CAA = Constitutional status to National Commission for Backward Classes (NCBC - Article 338B); 104th CAA = Extended SC/ST seats in LS/Assemblies, ended Anglo-Indian nomination; 106th CAA = Nari Shakti Vandan Adhiniyam (33% women's reservation)!

### Question 142
**Subject:** General Science (Chemistry) | **Topic:** Alloys and Their Compositions

Match List-I (Alloy) with List-II (Constituent Metals) and select the correct answer:

**List-I (Alloy)**
A. Brass  
B. Bronze  
C. German Silver  
D. Solder (Soldering Wire)  

**List-II (Constituent Metals)**
1. Copper ($Cu$) and Tin ($Sn$)  
2. Copper ($Cu$) and Zinc ($Zn$)  
3. Lead ($Pb$) and Tin ($Sn$)  
4. Copper ($Cu$), Zinc ($Zn$), and Nickel ($Ni$)  

**Options:**
A. A-2, B-1, C-4, D-3  
B. A-1, B-2, C-4, D-3  
C. A-2, B-4, C-1, D-3  
D. A-3, B-1, C-4, D-2  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - **Brass:** Alloy of Copper (~70%) and Zinc (~30%); used in utensils, cartridges, and musical instruments.
  - **Bronze:** Alloy of Copper (~88%) and Tin (~12%); used in statues, medals, and coins.
  - **German Silver:** Alloy of Copper (~50%), Zinc (~30%), and Nickel (~20%). **It contains NO SILVER (0% Ag)!** Named solely for its silvery-white luster.
  - **Solder:** Alloy of Lead (~50%) and Tin (~50%); has a low melting point, used for joining electrical wires.
  - **Stainless Steel:** Iron ($Fe$) + Chromium ($Cr \ge 10.5\%$) + Nickel ($Ni$) + Carbon ($C$).
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** Recurring UPPSC trap: "Which of the following elements is NOT present in German Silver?" Answer: **Silver ($Ag$)**!

### Question 143
**Subject:** Uttar Pradesh Special | **Topic:** Traditional Theatrical & Puppet Arts of UP

Consider the following statements regarding folk theatre and puppetry in Uttar Pradesh:
1. 'Nautanki' is a prominent folk-theatre of Uttar Pradesh having two distinct stylistic schools: the Hathras style and the Kanpur style.
2. 'Gulabo-Sitabo' is a traditional glove-puppetry art form originating from the Awadh region (Lucknow) of Uttar Pradesh.
3. In Nautanki, the musical dialogue is performed accompanied by instruments like Nagara (kettledrum), Dholak, and Harmonium.

Which of the statements given above are correct?

**Options:**
A. 1 and 2 only  
B. 2 and 3 only  
C. 1 and 3 only  
D. 1, 2 and 3  

**Answer:** D

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - All three statements are correct:
    - **Nautanki:** Highly popular musical folk theatre form across Northern India, especially UP.
      - *Hathras Style:* Traditional, emphasizes classical singing and sophisticated metered verse (*Doha, Chaubola, Chhand*).
      - *Kanpur Style:* More dramatic, contemporary, action-oriented, with colloquial language.
    - **Gulabo-Sitabo:** Rare traditional glove puppet theatre unique to Lucknow; revolves around the comical bickering between two co-wives (Gulabo, the vibrant mistress, and Sitabo, the weary wife), reflecting Awadhi social satire.
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** String puppetry of Rajasthan is *Kathputli*; Shadow puppetry of Andhra is *Tholu Bommalata*; Glove puppetry of UP is *Gulabo-Sitabo*!

### Question 144
**Subject:** Environment & Ecology | **Topic:** Stratospheric Ozone Layer Depletion

Consider the following statements regarding the Ozone layer:
1. The protective ozone layer is concentrated primarily in the lower Stratosphere between 15 km and 30 km above the Earth's surface.
2. Ozone thickness in the atmosphere is measured in Dobson Units (DU).
3. Ground-level (tropospheric) ozone is a beneficial gas, while stratospheric ozone is a harmful air pollutant.

Which of the statements given above is/are correct?

**Options:**
A. 1 and 2 only  
B. 2 and 3 only  
C. 1 only  
D. 1, 2 and 3  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - Statements 1 and 2 are correct: Stratospheric ozone (~90% of atmospheric ozone) absorbs lethal solar ultraviolet radiation (UV-B and UV-C). Normal column thickness is ~300 Dobson Units (1 DU = 0.01 mm thickness at STP). An "ozone hole" is defined when concentration drops below 220 DU.
  - Statement 3 is incorrect: The situation is the EXACT REVERSE! **Stratospheric ozone is "Good Ozone"** (shields Earth from UV radiation). **Tropospheric (ground-level) ozone is "Bad Ozone"** (a toxic secondary pollutant and greenhouse gas causing respiratory distress and crop damage).
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** "Good Ozone" = Stratosphere; "Bad Ozone" = Troposphere! World Ozone Day is celebrated annually on **16th September** (commemorating the signing of Montreal Protocol in 1987).

### Question 145
**Subject:** Modern Indian History | **Topic:** Administration and Commissions of Lord Curzon (1899–1905)

Match List-I (Commission / Reform of Lord Curzon) with List-II (Chairman / Mandate) and select the correct answer:

**List-I (Curzon Reform)**
A. Police Commission (1902)  
B. Universities Commission (1902)  
C. Irrigation Commission (1901)  
D. Ancient Monuments Preservation Act (1904)  

**List-II (Chairman / Mandate)**
1. Sir Thomas Raleigh; resulted in Indian Universities Act of 1904  
2. Sir Andrew Fraser; recommended creation of CID (Criminal Investigation Department)  
3. Established Archaeological Survey of India (ASI) on a permanent basis  
4. Sir Colin Scott-Moncrieff; planned extensive canal systems in Punjab  

**Options:**
A. A-2, B-1, C-4, D-3  
B. A-1, B-2, C-4, D-3  
C. A-2, B-4, C-1, D-3  
D. A-3, B-1, C-4, D-2  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - **Police Commission (1902):** Headed by Sir Andrew Fraser; reported that police administration was corrupt and inefficient; created Central CID and Provincial CID.
  - **Universities Commission (1902):** Headed by Sir Thomas Raleigh (included Syed Hussain Bilgrami and Justice Gurudas Banerjee); led to the **Indian Universities Act of 1904**, bringing universities under tight governmental control.
  - **Irrigation Commission (1901):** Headed by Sir Colin Scott-Moncrieff; led to massive canal expansion in the Punjab colony.
  - **Ancient Monuments Preservation Act (1904):** Curzon allocated funds to repair historical monuments, making the Archaeological Survey of India (ASI) a permanent government department under John Marshall.
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** Curzon partitioned Bengal on **October 16, 1905**; Bengal was reunited in **1911** by Lord Hardinge II at the Delhi Durbar!

### Question 146
**Subject:** Indian Economy | **Topic:** Multidimensional Poverty Index (MPI)

Consider the following statements regarding the National Multidimensional Poverty Index (MPI) published by NITI Aayog:
1. India's National MPI uses the Alkire-Foster methodology, retaining the 3 dimensions of Global MPI: Health, Education, and Standard of Living.
2. The National MPI measures poverty across 12 indicators (adding 'Maternal Health' and 'Bank Accounts' to the 10 global indicators).
3. As per NITI Aayog's MPI reports, Uttar Pradesh registered the largest decline in the number of multidimensionally poor individuals among all states.

Which of the statements given above are correct?

**Options:**
A. 1 and 2 only  
B. 2 and 3 only  
C. 1 and 3 only  
D. 1, 2 and 3  

**Answer:** D

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - All three statements are correct:
    - **Dimensions & Weights:** 3 equally weighted dimensions (Health $rac{1}{3}$, Education $rac{1}{3}$, Standard of Living $rac{1}{3}$).
    - **12 Indicators:** Health (Nutrition, Child & Adolescent Mortality, Maternal Health); Education (Years of Schooling, School Attendance); Standard of Living (Cooking Fuel, Sanitation, Drinking Water, Electricity, Housing, Assets, Bank Accounts).
    - **State Progress:** As per NITI Aayog's discussion paper (2024), ~24.82 crore people escaped multidimensional poverty in India between 2013–14 and 2022–23. **Uttar Pradesh recorded the largest escape from poverty (approx. 5.94 crore people)**, followed by Bihar and Madhya Pradesh.
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** Highest multidimensionally poor population by percentage remains Bihar, but the **largest absolute reduction** occurred in Uttar Pradesh!

### Question 147
**Subject:** Current Affairs | **Topic:** Climate Summits (COP28 & COP29)

Consider the following statements regarding the UN Climate Change Conferences:
1. COP28 was held in Dubai, United Arab Emirates (UAE) in December 2023, where the 'Loss and Damage Fund' was formally operationalized on day one.
2. COP28 concluded with the historic "UAE Consensus", calling upon countries to transition away from fossil fuels in energy systems.
3. COP29 in November 2024 was hosted by Baku, Azerbaijan, focusing on the New Collective Quantified Goal (NCQG) on climate finance.

Which of the statements given above are correct?

**Options:**
A. 1 and 2 only  
B. 2 and 3 only  
C. 1 and 3 only  
D. 1, 2 and 3  

**Answer:** D

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - All three statements are correct:
    - **COP28 (Dubai, UAE 2023):** Presided by Sultan Al Jaber. Breakthrough operationalization of the Loss and Damage Fund (hosted initially by the World Bank). The "Global Stocktake" agreed on transitioning away from fossil fuels to achieve net zero by 2050.
    - **COP29 (Baku, Azerbaijan, Nov 2024):** Dubbed the "Finance COP"; central objective was replacing the outdated \$100 billion/year climate finance target with the **New Collective Quantified Goal (NCQG)**.
    - **COP30 (2025):** Scheduled to be held in Belém (Amazon region), Brazil.
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** COP sequence: COP27 = Sharm el-Sheikh (Egypt) -> COP28 = Dubai (UAE) -> COP29 = Baku (Azerbaijan) -> COP30 = Belém (Brazil).

### Question 148
**Subject:** Geography of India | **Topic:** National Waterways of India

Match List-I (National Waterway) with List-II (River Route / Stretch) and select the correct answer:

**List-I (Waterway)**
A. National Waterway 1 (NW-1)  
B. National Waterway 2 (NW-2)  
C. National Waterway 3 (NW-3)  
D. National Waterway 4 (NW-4)  

**List-II (Route / Stretch)**
1. Brahmaputra River; Sadiya to Dhubri (Assam)  
2. Ganga-Bhagirathi-Hooghly river system; Prayagraj (Allahabad) to Haldia  
3. Krishna and Godavari rivers with Buckingham Canal; Kakinada to Puducherry  
4. West Coast Canal; Kottapuram to Kollam (Kerala)  

**Options:**
A. A-2, B-1, C-4, D-3  
B. A-1, B-2, C-4, D-3  
C. A-2, B-4, C-1, D-3  
D. A-3, B-1, C-4, D-2  

**Answer:** A

  - **NW-1:** Longest national waterway in India (1,620 km); spans Ganga-Bhagirathi-Hooghly from **Prayagraj (UP) to Haldia (WB)**; passes through UP, Bihar, Jharkhand, and WB.
  - **NW-2:** Brahmaputra River from **Sadiya to Dhubri** in Assam (891 km).

### Question 149
**Subject:** Indian Polity | **Topic:** State Legislative Councils (Article 169)

Consider the following statements regarding State Legislative Councils (Vidhan Parishad):
1. Under Article 169, Parliament can abolish or create a Legislative Council in a State if the Legislative Assembly of that State passes a resolution by a special majority.
2. The total membership of the Legislative Council shall not exceed one-third of the total membership of the Legislative Assembly, but cannot be less than 40 members.
3. At present, six states in India possess a bicameral legislature: Uttar Pradesh, Bihar, Maharashtra, Karnataka, Andhra Pradesh, and Telangana.

Which of the statements given above are correct?

**Options:**
A. 1 and 2 only  
B. 2 and 3 only  
C. 1 and 3 only  
D. 1, 2 and 3  

**Answer:** D

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - All three statements are correct:
    - **Creation/Abolition (Article 169):** State Assembly must pass resolution by a special majority ($\ge rac{2}{3}$ present & voting and majority of total membership). Parliament then passes an ordinary law by simple majority (not deemed an amendment under Article 368).
    - **Composition (Article 171):** Maximum strength = $rac{1}{3}$ of Legislative Assembly; Minimum strength = 40. UP Legislative Council has the highest strength with **100 seats**.
    - **6 Bicameral States:** **Uttar Pradesh, Bihar, Maharashtra, Karnataka, Andhra Pradesh, and Telangana** (Jammu & Kashmir had a council until its bifurcation in 2019).
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** Mnemonic for 6 states with Legislative Councils: **KUMBAT** (Karnataka, UP, Maharashtra, Bihar, Andhra Pradesh, Telangana)!

### Question 150
**Subject:** Uttar Pradesh Special | **Topic:** Irrigation Sources & Major Canals in UP

Consider the following statements regarding irrigation in Uttar Pradesh:
1. Tubewells (Nalkoop) constitute the largest source of irrigation in Uttar Pradesh, accounting for over 70% of the total net irrigated area.
2. Canals form the second largest source of irrigation in the state, accounting for approximately 15% of irrigated area.
3. The Sharda Canal is the longest and largest canal network in Uttar Pradesh, originating from Banbasa on the Sharda river.

Which of the statements given above are correct?

**Options:**
A. 1 and 2 only  
B. 2 and 3 only  
C. 1 and 3 only  
D. 1, 2 and 3  

**Answer:** D

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - All three statements are correct:
    - **Irrigation Sources Share in UP:**
      1. **Tubewells (Nalkoop):** ~73.4% (Highest in UP; first tubewell dug in Meerut in 1930).
      2. **Canals (Nahar):** ~15.2%.
      3. **Wells (Kuan):** ~8.6%.
      4. **Tanks, Lakes & Ponds:** ~1.2%.
    - **Canal Networks:**
      - **Sharda Canal:** Largest and longest canal system in UP; built between 1920–1928; originates at Banbasa (Champawat border) on the Sharda (Kali) river.
      - **Upper Ganga Canal:** Oldest major modern canal in northern India; built between 1842–1854 by Sir Proby Cautley from Haridwar.
      - **Eastern Yamuna Canal:** Oldest canal in UP (originating 1830 from Faizabad, Saharanpur).
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** Tubewells account for nearly three-fourths of all irrigation in UP; canals account for only about one-seventh!

### Question 151
**Subject:** Modern Indian History | **Topic:** Lord Dalhousie & Doctrine of Lapse Chronology

Arrange the following Indian Princely States annexed by Lord Dalhousie under the 'Doctrine of Lapse' in chronological order:
1. Satara
2. Jhansi
3. Sambalpur
4. Nagpur

Select the correct answer from the codes given below:

**Options:**
A. 1 — 3 — 2 — 4  
B. 1 — 2 — 3 — 4  
C. 3 — 1 — 2 — 4  
D. 1 — 3 — 4 — 2  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - Lord Dalhousie (1848–1856) applied the aggressive imperialist policy of **Doctrine of Lapse**, refusing to recognize adopted heirs to sovereign princely thrones:
    1. **Satara (1848):** First state annexed under the Doctrine.
    2. **Jaitpur (Bundelkhand) & Sambalpur (Odisha):** Annexed in **1849**.
    3. **Baghat (Himachal):** Annexed in **1850** (later restored by Lord Canning).
    4. **Udaipur (Chhattisgarh):** Annexed in **1852** (later restored).
    5. **Jhansi:** Annexed in **1853** (after Gangadhar Rao died without natural heir).
    6. **Nagpur:** Annexed in **1854** (Bhonsle state; royal jewels were publicly auctioned in Calcutta).
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** Awadh was NOT annexed under the Doctrine of Lapse! Awadh was annexed in **February 1856 on charges of "misgovernance and maladministration"** based on James Outram's report!

### Question 152
**Subject:** Indian Polity | **Topic:** Panchayati Raj & 73rd Constitutional Amendment Act

Consider the following statements regarding the 73rd Constitutional Amendment Act, 1992:
1. It added Part IX to the Constitution titled "The Panchayats" and the Eleventh Schedule containing 29 functional items.
2. Under Article 243D, not less than one-third of the total number of seats and offices of chairpersons are reserved for women.
3. The State Election Commission responsible for conducting panchayat elections is established under Article 243K.

Which of the statements given above are correct?

**Options:**
A. 1 and 2 only  
B. 2 and 3 only  
C. 1 and 3 only  
D. 1, 2 and 3  

**Answer:** D

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - All three statements are correct:
    - **73rd CAA 1992 (effective 24 April 1993 - National Panchayati Raj Day):** Added Part IX (Articles 243 to 243-O) and **11th Schedule containing 29 functional items**.
    - **Article 243D(3):** Mandates not less than $\frac{1}{3}$ (33%) reservation for women among seats and chairpersons (many states like Bihar, MP, Uttarakhand have raised this to 50%).
    - **Article 243K:** State Election Commissioner appointed by Governor, removed only in like manner as a High Court judge.
    - **Article 243I:** State Finance Commission constituted every 5 years by the Governor.
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** 11th Schedule (Panchayats) has **29 items**; 12th Schedule (Municipalities) has **18 items**! Gram Sabha is defined under Article 243(b) and established under Article 243A.

### Question 153
**Subject:** Geography of India | **Topic:** Origins of the Indus River Tributaries

Match List-I (Indus Tributary) with List-II (Source of Origin) and select the correct answer:

**List-I (River)**
A. Jhelum  
B. Chenab  
C. Ravi  
D. Sutlej  

**List-II (Source / Origin)**
1. Rakshastal Lake near Mansarovar (Tibet)  
2. Verinag Spring at the foot of Pir Panjal (Kashmir)  
3. Confluence of Chandra and Bhaga streams at Tandi (Himachal Pradesh)  
4. West of Rohtang Pass in Kullu Hills (Himachal Pradesh)  

**Options:**
A. A-2, B-3, C-4, D-1  
B. A-1, B-2, C-4, D-3  
C. A-2, B-4, C-3, D-1  
D. A-3, B-2, C-4, D-1  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - **Jhelum:** Originates from deep spring at Verinag; flows through Srinagar and Wular Lake before entering Pakistan through a deep gorge at Baramulla.
  - **Chenab (Asikni):** Largest Indus tributary; formed by two headstreams, Chandra and Bhaga, joining at Tandi near Keylong in Lahaul (HP).
  - **Ravi (Parushni):** Originates west of Rohtang Pass in the Kullu hills of HP; flows between Pir Panjal and Dhauladhar ranges.
  - **Sutlej (Shutudri):** Antecedent trans-Himalayan river originating from Rakshastal Lake (Tibet); cuts through Zaskar range via Shipki La pass into India.
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** Beas (Vipas) river originates near Rohtang Pass (Beas Kund) and **meets Sutlej entirely within India at Harike Wetland (Punjab)**!

### Question 154
**Subject:** General Science (Biology) | **Topic:** Genetics & Nucleic Acids (DNA vs RNA)

Consider the following statements regarding nucleic acids:
1. James Watson and Francis Crick proposed the Double Helix model of DNA in 1953 based on X-ray diffraction data of Rosalind Franklin.
2. In DNA, the purine Adenine pairs with Thymine via two hydrogen bonds ($A=T$), while Guanine pairs with Cytosine via three hydrogen bonds ($G \equiv C$).
3. In RNA, the pyrimidine base Thymine is replaced by Uracil.

Which of the statements given above are correct?

**Options:**
A. 1 and 2 only  
B. 2 and 3 only  
C. 1 and 3 only  
D. 1, 2 and 3  

**Answer:** D

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - All three statements are correct:
    - **Double Helix Model (1953):** Two antiparallel polynucleotide chains coiled around a central axis; Watson, Crick, and Maurice Wilkins shared the Nobel Prize in 1962.
    - **Base Pairing (Chargaff's Rule):** Purines = Pyrimidines ($A+G = T+C$). Adenine pairs with Thymine ($A=T$, two H-bonds); Guanine pairs with Cytosine ($G \equiv C$, three H-bonds).
    - **DNA vs RNA:** DNA contains 2-deoxyribose sugar and bases A, T, G, C. RNA contains ribose sugar and replaces **Thymine ($T$) with Uracil ($U$)**.
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** Purines are double-ringed (**Adenine, Guanine**); Pyrimidines are single-ringed (**Cytosine, Thymine, Uracil**).

### Question 155
**Subject:** Uttar Pradesh Special | **Topic:** Major Buddhist Heritage Sites in UP

Match List-I (Buddhist Site in UP) with List-II (Historical Significance) and select the correct answer:

**List-I (Site)**
A. Sarnath (Isipatana / Rishipattana)  
B. Kushinagar (Kusinara)  
C. Shravasti (Sahet-Mahet)  
D. Sankisa (Farrukhabad)  

**List-II (Significance)**
1. Site of Buddha's *Mahaparinirvana* (death) under two Sal trees; Ramabhar Stupa  
2. First Sermon of Buddha (*Dhammacakkappavattana*); Dhamek Stupa & Ashoka Lion Capital  
3. Capital of Kosala where Buddha spent maximum rainy retreats (*Vassavasa*); Jetavana Monastery  
4. Legendary site where Buddha descended from Trayastrimsa Heaven via a golden ladder  

**Options:**
A. A-2, B-1, C-3, D-4  
B. A-1, B-2, C-3, D-4  
C. A-2, B-3, C-1, D-4  
D. A-3, B-1, C-4, D-2  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - **Sarnath (Varanasi):** Buddha preached his first sermon (*Dharmachakra Pravartana*) to 5 disciples (Kaundinya, etc.); site of Dhamek and Chaukhandi stupas.
  - **Kushinagar:** Buddha attained *Mahaparinirvana* in 483 BCE at age 80; site of colossal 6-meter reclining Buddha statue and Ramabhar Stupa (cremation ground).
  - **Shravasti:** Buddha spent 24-25 rainy seasons (*Chaturmasa*); converted ruthless bandit Angulimala; wealthy merchant Anathapindika donated the Jetavana monastery.
  - **Sankisa (Farrukhabad):** Buddhist tradition holds Buddha descended from heaven here accompanied by Indra and Brahma; site of Ashokan elephant capital pillar.
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** Buddha was born in **Lumbini** (Nepal), attained Enlightenment at **Bodh Gaya** (Bihar), gave First Sermon at **Sarnath** (UP), and died at **Kushinagar** (UP)!

### Question 156
**Subject:** Environment & Ecology | **Topic:** Ecological Concepts and Principles

Match List-I (Ecological Concept) with List-II (Definition / Key Feature) and select the correct answer:

**List-I (Concept)**
A. Ecotone  
B. Ecological Niche  
C. Edge Effect  
D. Keystone Species  

**List-II (Definition)**
1. Functional role, resource utilization, and status of an organism in its ecosystem  
2. Zone of junction or transition between two or more diverse ecosystems (e.g. mangrove, marshland)  
3. Phenomenon where species diversity or population density is greater in the ecotone than in flanking communities  
4. Species whose presence and activity exerts disproportionately large influence on the entire community structure  

**Options:**
A. A-2, B-1, C-3, D-4  
B. A-1, B-2, C-3, D-4  
C. A-2, B-3, C-1, D-4  
D. A-3, B-1, C-4, D-2  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - **Ecotone:** Transition zone between two biomes (e.g., mangrove between marine and terrestrial; grassland between forest and desert).
  - **Ecological Niche (Joseph Grinnell 1917):** "Address" is habitat, but "profession" is niche; unique functional role and trophic position occupied by a species. Two species cannot occupy the exact same niche indefinitely (Gause's Competitive Exclusion Principle).
  - **Edge Effect:** The heightened richness, density, and diversity of species in the ecotone boundary compared to adjacent communities.
  - **Keystone Species (Robert Paine 1969):** Species that plays a critical role in maintaining ecological balance (e.g., Tiger in forest, Sea Otter in kelp forest, Fig tree in tropical rainforest).
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** Difference between Habitat and Niche: Many species can share the same Habitat, but NO two species can occupy the exact same Niche in the long run!

### Question 157
**Subject:** Indian Economy | **Topic:** Reserve Bank of India (RBI) Structure & Evolution

Consider the following statements regarding the Reserve Bank of India:
1. The Reserve Bank of India was established on April 1, 1935, based on the recommendations of the Royal Commission on Indian Currency and Finance (Hilton Young Commission).
2. The RBI was initially set up as a private shareholders' bank and was nationalized on January 1, 1949.
3. Sir Osborne Smith was the first Governor of the RBI, and C.D. Deshmukh was the first Indian Governor.

Which of the statements given above are correct?

**Options:**
A. 1 and 2 only  
B. 2 and 3 only  
C. 1 and 3 only  
D. 1, 2 and 3  

**Answer:** D

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - All three statements are correct:
    - **Foundation:** Established under the Reserve Bank of India Act, 1934; commenced operations on April 1, 1935, in Calcutta (shifted permanently to Bombay in 1937).
    - **Nationalization:** Under the RBI (Transfer to Public Ownership) Act, 1948, RBI was fully nationalized with effect from **January 1, 1949**.
    - **Governors:**
      - 1st Governor = **Sir Osborne Smith** (1935–1937).
      - 2nd Governor = Sir James Braid Taylor (1937–1943).
      - 1st Indian Governor = **Sir Chintaman Dwarakanath (C.D.) Deshmukh** (1943–1949).
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** Dr. Manmohan Singh is the ONLY person in Indian history to have served as BOTH Governor of the RBI (1982–1985) and Prime Minister of India!

### Question 161
**Subject:** Indian Polity | **Topic:** Right to Freedom of Religion (Articles 25 to 28)

Match List-I (Article of the Constitution) with List-II (Guaranteed Freedom) and select the correct answer:

**List-I (Article)**
A. Article 25  
B. Article 26  
C. Article 27  
D. Article 28  

**List-II (Guaranteed Freedom)**
1. Freedom from payment of taxes for promotion of any particular religion  
2. Freedom of conscience and free profession, practice, and propagation of religion  
3. Freedom from attending religious instruction or worship in state-funded educational institutions  
4. Freedom to manage religious affairs and establish institutions for religious/charitable purposes  

**Options:**
A. A-2, B-4, C-1, D-3  
B. A-4, B-2, C-1, D-3  
C. A-2, B-1, C-4, D-3  
D. A-3, B-4, C-1, D-2  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - **Article 25:** Guarantees to ALL persons freedom of conscience and right to freely profess, practice, and propagate religion (individual right, subject to public order, morality, and health).
  - **Article 26:** Guarantees to religious denominations the collective right to manage their own religious affairs and acquire property (collective denomination right).
  - **Article 27:** State cannot compel any citizen to pay any TAX whose proceeds are specifically appropriated for promoting a particular religion (fees can be levied for administrative services, but not taxes).
  - **Article 28:** Completely bans religious instruction in educational institutions wholly maintained out of State funds.
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** Article 25 protects INDIVIDUALS; Article 26 protects RELIGIOUS DENOMINATIONS/COLLECTIVES! Under Article 25 Explanation II, the reference to Hindus includes Sikhs, Jains, and Buddhists.

### Question 162
**Subject:** Medieval Indian History | **Topic:** Delhi Sultanate Fiscal Taxes

Match List-I (Fiscal Tax in Delhi Sultanate) with List-II (Nature / Levied Upon) and select the correct answer:

**List-I (Tax)**
A. Kharaj  
B. Jizya  
C. Zakat  
D. Khams  

**List-II (Nature)**
1. Poll tax levied on non-Muslim protected subjects (*Zimmis*) in lieu of military service  
2. Land revenue tax levied on agricultural produce (usually $\frac{1}{3}\text{rd}$ to $\frac{1}{2}$)  
3. State's share ($\frac{1}{5}\text{th}$) of the spoils of war and booty  
4. Religious tax of 2.5% levied exclusively on well-off Muslims for charitable purposes  

**Options:**
A. A-2, B-1, C-4, D-3  
B. A-1, B-2, C-4, D-3  
C. A-2, B-4, C-1, D-3  
D. A-3, B-1, C-4, D-2  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - **Kharaj:** Agricultural land tax on cultivable lands (Alauddin Khalji raised Kharaj to 50% of gross produce based on measurement / *Masaht*).
  - **Jizya:** Capitation/poll tax paid by non-Muslims; Firoz Shah Tughlaq was the first Sultan to levy Jizya on Brahmins as a separate tax.
  - **Zakat:** Obligatory wealth tax (2.5%) payable only by wealthy Muslims, deposited in *Bait-ul-Mal* for poor relief.
  - **Khams:** One-fifth ($\frac{1}{5}$ or 20%) of the war spoils (*Ghanimah*) went to the State, and $\frac{4}{5}$ was distributed among soldiers. (Alauddin Khalji and Muhammad bin Tughlaq reversed this rule: the State took $\frac{4}{5}$ and left only $\frac{1}{5}$ to soldiers!).
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** Sharb was an irrigation tax levied by **Firoz Shah Tughlaq** at the rate of 10% of produce from lands irrigated by royal canals!

### Question 163
**Subject:** General Science (Biology) | **Topic:** Human Circulatory System & Heart Physiology

Consider the following statements regarding the human heart and blood vessels:
1. The Sinoatrial (SA) node located in the right atrium acts as the natural pacemaker of the heart.
2. Arteries always carry oxygenated blood away from the heart, with the sole exception of the Pulmonary Artery, which carries deoxygenated blood to the lungs.
3. In a standard Electrocardiogram (ECG), the P-wave represents the ventricular repolarization.

Which of the statements given above is/are correct?

**Options:**
A. 1 and 2 only  
B. 2 and 3 only  
C. 1 only  
D. 1, 2 and 3  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - Statements 1 and 2 are correct: The SA node generates rhythmic action potentials (~72 times/minute), initiating cardiac contraction. Arteries carry blood away from the heart at high pressure; the Pulmonary Artery is the ONLY artery carrying deoxygenated blood (from right ventricle to lungs). The Pulmonary Vein is the ONLY vein carrying oxygenated blood (from lungs to left atrium).
  - Statement 3 is incorrect: In a standard ECG:
    - **P-wave:** Represents **Atrial Depolarization** (electrical excitation leading to contraction of both atria).
    - **QRS Complex:** Represents **Ventricular Depolarization** (contraction of ventricles).
    - **T-wave:** Represents **Ventricular Repolarization** (return of ventricles from excited to resting state).
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** Tricuspid valve guards the right atrioventricular opening; Bicuspid (Mitral) valve guards the left atrioventricular opening!

2. The Tharu tribe traditionally observes Diwali as a day of sorrow (*Shok Parva*).
3. The Buksa (Bhoksa) tribe resides primarily in Bijnor district of Uttar Pradesh.

Which of the statements given above are correct?

B. 2 and 3 only  
C. 1 and 3 only  
D. 1, 2 and 3  

**Answer:** D

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**

    - **Tharu Tribe:** Largest tribal group in UP; belongs to Kirat Mongoloid stock; practice Joint Family system; follow matriarchal traits; claim origin from Rajputana desert; celebrate Diwali as an occasion to mourn deceased ancestors (*Shok Parva*). A university for Tharus was established at Lakhimpur Kheri.

### Question 165
**Subject:** Environment & Ecology | **Topic:** Ramsar Convention & Montreux Record

Consider the following statements regarding the Montreux Record:
1. The Montreux Record is a register of wetland sites on the Ramsar List where ecological character has changed, is changing, or is likely to change due to human interference.
2. Currently, two Indian wetlands are listed on the Montreux Record: Keoladeo National Park (Rajasthan) and Loktak Lake (Manipur).
3. Chilika Lake (Odisha) was placed on the Montreux Record in 1993 but was successfully removed in 2002 after ecological restoration.

Which of the statements given above are correct?

**Options:**
A. 1 and 2 only  
B. 2 and 3 only  
C. 1 and 3 only  
D. 1, 2 and 3  

**Answer:** D

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - All three statements are correct:
    - **Montreux Record:** Established under Ramsar COP in 1990; highlights wetlands requiring urgent conservation priority.
    - **Current Indian Sites (2):**
      1. **Keoladeo National Park (Rajasthan):** Listed due to water shortage and grazing imbalance.
      2. **Loktak Lake (Manipur):** Listed due to deforestation in catchment and proliferation of *Phumdis* (floating vegetation mats) impacting Keibul Lamjao National Park.
    - **Chilika Lake:** India's first Ramsar site (along with Keoladeo, 1981); placed on Montreux in 1993 due to siltation; removed in 2002 (awarded the prestigious Ramsar Wetland Conservation Award).
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** Keoladeo and Loktak REMAIN on the Montreux Record; Chilika Lake is NO LONGER on it!

### Question 166

A. United States of America (USA)  
B. China  
C. United Arab Emirates (UAE)  
D. Russia  

**Answer:** B

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - In FY 2023–24, **China** emerged as India's largest two-way merchandise trading partner with total bilateral trade valued at **\$118.4 billion** (India's exports to China: \$16.67 billion; imports from China: \$101.75 billion, resulting in a massive trade deficit of \$85.08 billion).
  - The **USA** was the second largest trading partner at **\$118.3 billion** (India enjoys a significant trade surplus of over \$35 billion with the USA).
  - **Top 5 Trading Partners (2023–24):** 1. China, 2. USA, 3. UAE, 4. Russia, 5. Saudi Arabia.
  - Russia is India's 4th largest trading partner primarily driven by discounted crude oil imports.
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** India has the largest merchandise **trade SURPLUS with the USA**, and the largest merchandise **trade DEFICIT with China**!

### Question 168
**Subject:** Modern Indian History | **Topic:** Constitutional Proposals of the 1940s Chronology

Arrange the following constitutional initiatives and missions sent by the British in chronological order:
1. August Offer
2. Cripps Mission
3. Wavell Plan (Shimla Conference)
4. Cabinet Mission

Select the correct answer from the codes given below:

**Options:**
A. 1 — 2 — 3 — 4  
B. 1 — 3 — 2 — 4  
C. 2 — 1 — 3 — 4  
D. 1 — 2 — 4 — 3  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  1. **August Offer (8 August 1940):** Announced by Viceroy Lord Linlithgow; proposed Dominion status after the war and an expanded Governor-General's Executive Council; recognized Indians' right to frame their own constitution for the first time.
  2. **Cripps Mission (March–April 1942):** Led by Sir Stafford Cripps; offered Dominion status and a Constituent Assembly *after* WWII, with option for provinces to secede; rejected by Congress (Gandhi called it a "post-dated cheque on a failing bank").
  3. **Wavell Plan & Shimla Conference (June–July 1945):** Convened by Lord Wavell; broke down over Jinnah's demand that the Muslim League alone could nominate all Muslim members to the Executive Council.
  4. **Cabinet Mission (March–May 1946):** Comprising Lord Pethick-Lawrence, Sir Stafford Cripps, and A.V. Alexander; rejected Pakistan; proposed a 3-tier federation (Groups A, B, C) and laid the framework for the Constituent Assembly.
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** Chronology sequence: **August Offer (1940) -> Cripps (1942) -> C.R. Formula (1944) -> Wavell Plan (1945) -> Cabinet Mission (1946) -> Mountbatten Plan (3 June 1947)**!

### Question 169
**Subject:** Geography of India | **Topic:** Multipurpose River Valley Projects & Dams

Match List-I (Dam / Project) with List-II (River & State) and select the correct answer:

**List-I (Dam / Project)**
A. Tehri Dam  
B. Bhakra-Nangal Project  
C. Hirakud Dam  
D. Sardar Sarovar Dam  

**List-II (River & State)**
1. Sutlej River; Himachal Pradesh & Punjab  
2. Bhagirathi River; Uttarakhand  
3. Narmada River; Gujarat  
4. Mahanadi River; Odisha  

**Options:**
A. A-2, B-1, C-4, D-3  
B. A-1, B-2, C-4, D-3  
C. A-2, B-4, C-1, D-3  
D. A-3, B-1, C-4, D-2  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - **Tehri Dam:** Highest dam in India (260.5 m); rock and earth-fill embankment dam constructed at the confluence of Bhagirathi and Bhilangna rivers in Tehri, Uttarakhand.
  - **Bhakra Dam:** One of the highest straight gravity dams in the world (226 m) on Sutlej River; reservoir is Govind Sagar.
  - **Hirakud Dam:** Longest earthen dam in the world (main dam 4.8 km, total length with dykes 25.8 km) on Mahanadi River in Sambalpur, Odisha.
  - **Sardar Sarovar Dam:** Concrete gravity dam on Narmada River at Kevadiya, Gujarat (powers Gujarat, MP, Maharashtra, Rajasthan).
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** Tehri is the HIGHEST dam in India (260.5 m); Hirakud is the LONGEST dam in India (main dyke 4.8 km); Bhakra is the highest GRAVITY dam!

### Question 170
**Subject:** Indian Polity | **Topic:** Parliamentary Devices & Motions

Match List-I (Parliamentary Motion) with List-II (Objective / Requirement) and select the correct answer:

**List-I (Motion)**
A. Adjournment Motion  
B. No-Confidence Motion  
C. Calling Attention Motion  
D. Censure Motion  

**List-II (Objective / Requirement)**
1. Requires support of 50 members; moved only in Lok Sabha against the entire Council of Ministers to test its majority  
2. Moved to draw attention of the House to an urgent matter of definite public importance; requires support of 50 members  
3. Indian parliamentary innovation (since 1954) to call minister's attention to urgent public matter and seek authoritative statement  
4. Can be moved against individual ministers or entire cabinet for specific policies; if passed, Council of Ministers need not resign  

**Options:**
A. A-2, B-1, C-3, D-4  
B. A-1, B-2, C-3, D-4  
C. A-2, B-3, C-1, D-4  
D. A-3, B-1, C-4, D-2  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - **Adjournment Motion:** Extraordinary device interrupting normal business; requires support of at least 50 members to be admitted; discussions last not less than 2.5 hours; contains an element of censure against government.
  - **No-Confidence Motion (Rule 198 of Lok Sabha):** Moved ONLY in Lok Sabha; requires support of 50 members; need not state specific reasons; if passed, the entire government must resign.
  - **Calling Attention Motion:** Unique Indian parliamentary invention (1954); like Zero Hour, not mentioned in standard Westminster rules.
  - **Censure Motion:** Must specify grounds for censure; moved against individual ministers or entire council; its passage does NOT mandate resignation of the government.
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** Both "Zero Hour" and "Calling Attention Motion" are indigenous Indian parliamentary innovations! Zero Hour started in 1962; Calling Attention in 1954.

### Question 171
**Subject:** Ancient Indian History | **Topic:** Six Schools of Classical Indian Philosophy (Shad-Darshana)

Match List-I (Philosophical School) with List-II (Founder / Chief Propounder) and select the correct answer:

**List-I (Darshana)**
A. Samkhya  
B. Nyaya  
C. Vaisheshika  
D. Purva Mimamsa  

**List-II (Founder)**
1. Sage Gautama  
2. Sage Kapila  
3. Sage Jaimini  
4. Sage Kanada (Uluka)  

**Options:**
A. A-2, B-1, C-4, D-3  
B. A-1, B-2, C-4, D-3  
C. A-2, B-4, C-1, D-3  
D. A-3, B-1, C-4, D-2  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - The 6 Orthodox (*Astika*) Schools of Vedic Philosophy:
    - **Samkhya:** Founded by **Sage Kapila** (*Samkhya Sutra*); oldest system; propounds dualism of *Purusha* (consciousness) and *Prakriti* (matter).
    - **Yoga:** Founded by **Patanjali** (*Yoga Sutra*); practical path of Ashtanga yoga.
    - **Nyaya:** Founded by **Sage Gautama** (*Nyaya Sutra*); school of logic and epistemology (4 Pramanas: Pratyaksha, Anumana, Upamana, Shabda).
    - **Vaisheshika:** Founded by **Sage Kanada** (*Vaisheshika Sutra*); atomic theory of the universe (*Paramanu*).
    - **Purva Mimamsa:** Founded by **Sage Jaimini** (*Mimamsa Sutra*); emphasizes ritualism and authority of the Vedas.
    - **Uttara Mimamsa (Vedanta):** Founded by **Badarayana** (*Brahma Sutra*).
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** Mimamsa was founded by Jaimini; Vedanta (Uttara Mimamsa) was founded by Badarayana!

### Question 172
**Subject:** General Science (Biology) | **Topic:** Mineral Deficiencies and Human Disorders

Match List-I (Mineral Nutrient) with List-II (Deficiency Manifestation) and select the correct answer:

**List-I (Mineral)**
A. Iodine  
B. Iron  
C. Fluorine (in trace deficiency vs excess)  
D. Calcium  

**List-II (Manifestation)**
1. Microcytic Hypochromic Anemia  
2. Goitre (enlargement of thyroid gland) & Cretinism in infants  
3. Rickets, Osteoporosis, and muscle tetany  
4. Tooth decay (deficiency < 1 ppm) & Dental/Skeletal Fluorosis (excess > 1.5–2 ppm)  

**Options:**
A. A-2, B-1, C-4, D-3  
B. A-1, B-2, C-4, D-3  
C. A-2, B-4, C-1, D-3  
D. A-3, B-1, C-4, D-2  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - **Iodine:** Essential component of thyroxine ($T_4$) and triiodothyronine ($T_3$); deficiency causes endemic goitre and stunted physical/mental growth in infants (Cretinism).
  - **Iron:** Central constituent of hemoglobin; deficiency impairs oxygen transport, causing Nutritional Anemia.
  - **Fluorine:** Required at ~1 ppm to strengthen tooth enamel (forming fluoroapatite); deficiency causes dental caries; excess (> 1.5–2 mg/L) causes mottled teeth (**Dental Fluorosis**) and bone deformities (**Skeletal Fluorosis / Knock-Knee syndrome**).
  - **Calcium:** Major mineral in bones and teeth; required for blood clotting, nerve conduction, and muscle contraction; deficiency causes rickets, osteopenia, and tetany.
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** Itai-Itai (ouch-ouch) disease is caused by **Cadmium ($Cd$)** poisoning; Minamata disease is caused by **Methyl Mercury ($Hg$)** poisoning!

**Subject:** Uttar Pradesh Special | **Topic:** Power Infrastructure & Plants in UP

Match List-I (Power Plant in UP) with List-II (Type / District) and select the correct answer:

**List-I (Plant)**
A. Narora Atomic Power Station (NAPS)  
B. Obra Thermal Power Plant  
C. Rihand Hydro-Electric Project (Govind Ballabh Pant Sagar)  
D. Singrauli Super Thermal Power Station  

**List-II (District & Type)**
1. Sonbhadra (Pipri); Hydro-electric  
2. Bulandshahr; Nuclear Power (PHWR)  
3. Sonbhadra (Shaktinagar); NTPC Coal Thermal  
4. Sonbhadra (Obra); UP Rajya Vidyut Utpadan Nigam Coal Thermal  

**Options:**
A. A-2, B-4, C-1, D-3  
B. A-4, B-2, C-1, D-3  
C. A-2, B-1, C-4, D-3  
D. A-3, B-4, C-1, D-2  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - **Narora Atomic Power Station (NAPS):** Located on the banks of the Ganges in **Bulandshahr** district; operates 2 Pressurized Heavy Water Reactors (PHWRs, 220 MW each). It is the ONLY nuclear power plant in Uttar Pradesh.
  - **Obra Thermal Power Station:** Located on the Renu river in **Sonbhadra** district (built with Russian collaboration).
  - **Rihand Dam Project:** Built on the Rihand river (tributary of Son) at Pipri in **Sonbhadra**; its reservoir, **Govind Ballabh Pant Sagar**, is India's largest artificial reservoir by volume.
  - **Singrauli Thermal Power Station:** India's first NTPC super thermal coal plant, located at Shaktinagar in **Sonbhadra** ("Energy Capital of India").
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** Sonbhadra district is known as the "Power Capital of India" (*Urja Anchala*) due to its immense concentration of thermal and hydro power stations!

### Question 174
**Subject:** Environment & Ecology | **Topic:** Kigali Amendment to the Montreal Protocol

Consider the following statements regarding the Kigali Amendment to the Montreal Protocol:
1. It was adopted in October 2016 in Kigali, Rwanda, at the 28th Meeting of the Parties (MOP28).
2. It aims to phase down the production and consumption of Hydrofluorocarbons (HFCs), which are potent greenhouse gases.
3. Unlike CFCs, Hydrofluorocarbons (HFCs) do not deplete the stratospheric ozone layer, but they have high Global Warming Potentials (GWPs).

Which of the statements given above are correct?

**Options:**
A. 1 and 2 only  
B. 2 and 3 only  
C. 1 and 3 only  
D. 1, 2 and 3  

**Answer:** D

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - All three statements are correct:
    - **Kigali Amendment (2016):** Legally binding amendment to Montreal Protocol; entered into force on 1 January 2019.
    - **Target Substance:** Phases down **Hydrofluorocarbons (HFCs)** by more than 80% over the next 30 years (expected to avoid up to 0.4°C of global warming by 2100).
    - **Chemical Nature:** HFCs were introduced as ozone-safe substitutes for CFCs and HCFCs because HFCs contain NO chlorine or bromine, hence having **Zero Ozone Depletion Potential (ODP)**. However, their Global Warming Potential is up to 12,000 times greater than $CO_2$.
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** India belongs to Group 2 of developing countries under Kigali, committing to start phasing down HFCs in **2028** and reduce them by 85% by **2047**!

### Question 175
**Subject:** Indian Economy | **Topic:** FRBM Act & N.K. Singh Committee Recommendations

Consider the following statements regarding the Fiscal Responsibility and Budget Management (FRBM) Review Committee (headed by N.K. Singh):
1. The committee recommended adopting 'Debt-to-GDP ratio' as the primary operational fiscal anchor instead of relying solely on fiscal deficit.
2. It recommended a targeted general government debt-to-GDP ratio of 60% by FY 2023 (40% for the Central Government and 20% for State Governments).
3. It proposed an "Escape Clause" allowing the government to deviate from fiscal targets up to 0.5% of GDP in extraordinary circumstances such as war, national calamity, or severe economic crisis.

Which of the statements given above are correct?

**Options:**
A. 1 and 2 only  
B. 2 and 3 only  
C. 1 and 3 only  
D. 1, 2 and 3  

**Answer:** D

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - All three statements are correct:
    - The N.K. Singh Committee (appointed in 2016, submitted report in 2017):
      - **Debt Anchor:** 60% general government debt-to-GDP ratio (**40% for Centre, 20% for States**).
      - **Fiscal Deficit Target:** Central fiscal deficit to be gradually brought down to 2.5% of GDP.
      - **Escape Clause:** Flexibility to exceed the target by 0.5% of GDP on grounds of national security, acts of war, agricultural collapse, or structural reforms with unanticipated fiscal implications (incorporated into amended FRBM Act 2018).
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** Debt ceiling split: **40% Centre + 20% States = 60% Total General Government Debt**!

### Question 177
**Subject:** Medieval Indian History | **Topic:** Mughal Historical Literature & Authors

Match List-I (Mughal Historical Text) with List-II (Author) and select the correct answer:

**List-I (Text)**
A. Humayun-Nama  
B. Tarikh-i-Sher Shahi  
C. Akbarnama & Ain-i-Akbari  
D. Muntakhab-ut-Tawarikh  

**List-II (Author)**
1. Abbas Khan Sarwani  
2. Gulbadan Begum (Humayun's sister)  
3. Abdul Qadir Badauni  
4. Abul Fazl  

**Options:**
A. A-2, B-1, C-4, D-3  
B. A-1, B-2, C-4, D-3  
C. A-2, B-4, C-1, D-3  
D. A-3, B-1, C-4, D-2  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - **Humayun-Nama:** Written in Persian by **Gulbadan Begum**, daughter of Babur and sister of Humayun, at the request of Akbar; provides intimate glimpses of the Mughal royal harem.
  - **Tarikh-i-Sher Shahi (Tuhfat-i-Akbar Shahi):** Composed by **Abbas Khan Sarwani** by order of Akbar; provides the most detailed contemporary account of Sher Shah Suri's administration.
  - **Akbarnama:** Written by court historian **Abul Fazl** in three volumes (Volume 3 is the celebrated *Ain-i-Akbari*, a statistical compendium of Akbar's empire).
  - **Muntakhab-ut-Tawarikh:** Written by orthodox critic **Abdul Qadir Badauni**; famous for severe criticisms of Akbar's liberal religious policies and *Din-i-Ilahi*.
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** *Tuzuk-i-Baburi* (Baburnama) was written by Babur himself in **Chagatai Turki**, later translated into Persian by Abdur Rahim Khan-i-Khanan under Akbar!

### Question 178
**Subject:** World Geography | **Topic:** Mediterranean Biome and Climate

Which of the following characteristics is/are associated with the Mediterranean Climate (Cs type in Koppen classification)?
1. Dry, warm summers and mild, rainy winters.
2. Predominance of westerly winds during the winter season bringing rainfall.
3. Sclerophyllous vegetation with thick waxy leaves and extensive deep root systems.
4. Viticulture (grape cultivation) and citrus fruit orchards form the backbone of agriculture.

Select the correct answer from the codes given below:

**Options:**
A. 1, 2 and 4 only  
B. 1, 3 and 4 only  
C. 2, 3 and 4 only  
D. 1, 2, 3 and 4  

**Answer:** D

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - All four statements are correct:
    - **Distribution:** Western margins of continents between **30° and 45° latitudes** (Mediterranean basin, Central California, Central Chile, Cape Town region in South Africa, Southern and Southwestern Australia).
    - **Precipitation:** In summer, trade winds blow offshore (completely dry). In winter, shifting pressure belts bring moist on-shore **Westerlies**, causing winter rains.
    - **Vegetation:** Sclerophyllous scrub (*Maquis* in France, *Chaparral* in California, *Matorral* in Chile); stunted trees with thick bark, waxy leathery leaves to resist summer transpiration.
    - **Economy:** "Orchard lands of the World"; world capital of citrus fruits (oranges, lemons), olives, and commercial wine-making (*Viticulture*).
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** The defining diagnostic feature of Mediterranean climate: **RAIN IN WINTER, DROUGHT IN SUMMER**!

### Question 179
**Subject:** Indian Polity | **Topic:** Constitutional vs Non-Constitutional Bodies

Which of the following bodies is a NON-CONSTITUTIONAL (Statutory or Executive) body?
1. Central Vigilance Commission (CVC)
2. National Human Rights Commission (NHRC)
3. National Commission for Scheduled Tribes (NCST)
4. Lokpal and Lokayuktas

Select the correct answer from the codes given below:

**Options:**
A. 1, 2 and 4 only  
B. 2, 3 and 4 only  
C. 1 and 3 only  
D. 1, 2, 3 and 4  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - **Constitutional Bodies:** Explicitly provided in the Constitution:
    - National Commission for Scheduled Tribes (NCST) = **Article 338A** (inserted by 89th CAA 2003).
    - NCSC (Article 338), NCBC (Article 338B), Finance Commission (Article 280), Election Commission (Article 324), CAG (Article 148), UPSC (Article 315).
  - **Statutory Bodies (Created by Acts of Parliament):**
    - **CVC:** Statutory status granted under Central Vigilance Commission Act, 2003 (originally set up 1964 via executive resolution on Santhanam Committee report).
    - **NHRC:** Created under Protection of Human Rights Act, 1993.
    - **Lokpal:** Created under Lokpal and Lokayuktas Act, 2013.
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** National Commission for Women (NCW) and National Commission for Minorities (NCM) are STATUTORY bodies, NOT Constitutional bodies!

### Question 180
**Subject:** General Science (Physics) | **Topic:** Electromagnetic Spectrum

Arrange the following electromagnetic waves in increasing order of their WAVELENGTH (from shortest to longest wavelength):
1. X-rays
2. Visible Light
3. Infrared Rays
4. Microwaves

Select the correct answer from the codes given below:

**Options:**
A. 1 — 2 — 3 — 4  
B. 2 — 1 — 3 — 4  
C. 1 — 3 — 2 — 4  
D. 4 — 3 — 2 — 1  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - In the Electromagnetic Spectrum, energy and frequency are inversely proportional to wavelength ($E = h\nu = \frac{hc}{\lambda}$):
    - **Gamma rays:** Shortest wavelength ($\lambda < 10^{-12} \text{ m}$), highest frequency/energy.
    - **X-rays:** $\lambda \approx 10^{-10} \text{ m}$ (discovered by W.C. Roentgen).
    - **Ultraviolet (UV):** $\lambda \approx 10^{-8} \text{ m}$.
    - **Visible light:** $\lambda \approx 400 \text{ nm}$ (violet) to $700 \text{ nm}$ (red).
    - **Infrared (IR):** $\lambda \approx 10^{-5} \text{ m}$ (heat radiation; used in night-vision and TV remotes).
    - **Microwaves:** $\lambda \approx 10^{-2} \text{ m}$ (used in RADAR and microwave ovens).
    - **Radio waves:** Longest wavelength ($\lambda > 1 \text{ m}$), lowest frequency/energy.
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** Increasing order of Wavelength: **Gamma < X-rays < UV < Visible < Infrared < Microwaves < Radio waves**! (Energy and frequency follow the exact opposite order).

**Subject:** Uttar Pradesh Special | **Topic:** Scheduled Castes (SC) Demographics in UP

Consider the following statements regarding the Scheduled Caste (SC) population in Uttar Pradesh as per Census 2011:

2. Sitapur district has the highest Scheduled Caste population in absolute numbers in Uttar Pradesh.
3. Kaushambi district has the highest percentage proportion of Scheduled Caste population to its total population in the state.

Which of the statements given above are correct?

**Options:**
A. 1 and 2 only  
B. 2 and 3 only  
C. 1 and 3 only  
D. 1, 2 and 3  

**Answer:** D

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - All three statements are correct:
    - **State Total:** UP's SC population is **4.13 crore** (accounting for 20.69% of UP's total population and 20.5% of India's total SC population).
    - **Highest Absolute SC Population:** **Sitapur**, followed by Prayagraj (Allahabad) and Hardoi.
    - **Lowest Absolute SC Population:** **Baghpat**, followed by Shravasti and Gautam Buddha Nagar.
    - **Highest SC Percentage:** **Kaushambi (34.72%)**, followed by Machhlishahr/Jaunpur and Sitapur.

### Question 182
**Subject:** Environment & Ecology | **Topic:** Ecological Terminology and Coining Scientists

Match List-I (Ecological Term) with List-II (Scientist Who Coined It) and select the correct answer:

**List-I (Term)**
A. Ecology (*Oekologie*)  
B. Ecosystem  
C. Biodiversity  
D. Biodiversity Hotspot  

**List-II (Scientist)**
1. A.G. Tansley (1935)  
2. Ernst Haeckel (1866)  
3. Norman Myers (1988)  
4. Walter G. Rosen (1985)  

**Options:**
A. A-2, B-1, C-4, D-3  
B. A-1, B-2, C-4, D-3  
C. A-2, B-4, C-1, D-3  
D. A-3, B-1, C-4, D-2  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - **Ecology:** Coined by German zoologist **Ernst Haeckel** in 1866 (from Greek *oikos* meaning house/dwelling, and *logos* meaning study).
  - **Ecosystem:** Coined by British botanist **Arthur G. Tansley** in 1935 as a basic structural and functional unit of nature.
  - **Biodiversity (Biological Diversity):** Coined as a portmanteau by **Walter G. Rosen** in 1985 (though Raymond F. Dasmann used 'biological diversity' in 1968). E.O. Wilson popularized it.
  - **Biodiversity Hotspots:** Coined by British ecologist **Norman Myers** in 1988 in his paper "The Environmentalist".
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** Eugene P. Odum is known as the "Father of Modern Ecology"; Ramdeo Misra is revered as the "Father of Indian Ecology"!

### Question 183
**Subject:** Modern Indian History | **Topic:** Early Indian Newspapers & Editors

Match List-I (Historical Newspaper) with List-II (Founder / Editor) and select the correct answer:

**List-I (Newspaper)**
A. Bengal Gazette (Hicky's Gazette, 1780)  
B. Mirat-ul-Akhbar (1822)  
C. Rast Goftar (1851)  
D. Som Prakash (1858)  

**List-II (Founder / Editor)**
1. Raja Ram Mohan Roy (First Persian weekly)  
2. James Augustus Hicky (First printed newspaper in India)  
3. Ishwar Chandra Vidyasagar & Dwarkanath Vidyabhusan  
4. Dadabhai Naoroji (Gujarati fortnightly)  

**Options:**
A. A-2, B-1, C-4, D-3  
B. A-1, B-2, C-4, D-3  
C. A-2, B-4, C-1, D-3  
D. A-3, B-1, C-4, D-2  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - **Hicky's Bengal Gazette (1780, Calcutta):** First printed newspaper in India; confiscated by British authorities for criticizing Warren Hastings.
  - **Mirat-ul-Akhbar (1822):** Founded by Raja Ram Mohan Roy in Persian; closed down in 1823 in protest against John Adam's Licensing Regulations.
  - **Rast Goftar ("The Truth Teller", 1851, Bombay):** Founded by Dadabhai Naoroji and Naoroji Furdunji to promote socio-religious reform among Parsis.
  - **Som Prakash (1858):** Founded by Ishwar Chandra Vidyasagar and edited by Dwarkanath Vidyabhusan; actively championed the cause of indigo ryots; Lord Lytton's Vernacular Press Act 1878 was targeted primarily against it.
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** *Amrita Bazar Patrika* (founded by Sisir Kumar Ghosh and Motilal Ghosh in 1868) morphed overnight from a Bengali-English weekly into an ENGLISH daily to escape the Vernacular Press Act 1878!

### Question 184
**Subject:** Indian Economy | **Topic:** Measures of Money Supply ($M_1, M_2, M_3, M_4$)

Match List-I (Money Aggregate) with List-II (Components Included) and select the correct answer:

**List-I (Aggregate)**
A. $M_1$ (Narrow Money)  
B. $M_2$  
C. $M_3$ (Broad Money)  
D. $M_4$  

**List-II (Components)**
1. $M_1$ + Savings deposits with Post Office savings banks  
2. Currency with public + Demand deposits with banking system + 'Other' deposits with RBI  
3. $M_3$ + Total deposits with Post Office savings organisations (excluding NSC)  
4. $M_1$ + Time deposits (fixed deposits) with the banking system  

**Options:**
A. A-2, B-1, C-4, D-3  
B. A-1, B-2, C-4, D-3  
C. A-2, B-4, C-1, D-3  
D. A-3, B-1, C-4, D-2  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - Formulated by the Second Working Group on Money Supply (1977):
    - **$M_1$ (Narrow Money):** Currency notes/coins held by public + Demand Deposits (current/savings) of commercial banks + Other deposits with RBI. Most liquid.
    - **$M_2$:** $M_1$ + Post Office savings bank deposits.
    - **$M_3$ (Broad Money):** $M_1$ + Net Time Deposits of commercial banks. Most widely used measure of money supply in India.
    - **$M_4$:** $M_3$ + Total Post Office deposits (excluding National Savings Certificates - NSC). Least liquid.
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** Decreasing order of Liquidity: **$M_1 > M_2 > M_3 > M_4$**! High Powered Money / Reserve Money is designated as **$M_0$**.

### Question 186
**Subject:** Ancient Indian History | **Topic:** Harappan Burial Practices

Match List-I (Harappan Site) with List-II (Specific Burial Feature) and select the correct answer:

**List-I (Site)**
A. Lothal  
B. Harappa  
C. Ropar (Rupnagar)  
D. Kalibangan  

**List-II (Burial Feature)**
1. Wooden coffin burials and Cemetery H & R-37  
2. Joint / Double burials (male and female skeleton interred together in single grave)  
3. Circular pit burials (pot burials) and brick-lined graves  
4. Human buried along with a domesticated dog in oval pit  

**Options:**
A. A-2, B-1, C-4, D-3  
B. A-1, B-2, C-4, D-3  
C. A-2, B-4, C-1, D-3  
D. A-3, B-1, C-4, D-2  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - **Lothal:** Three double burials were discovered where two skeletons (male and female) were interred together in brick-lined graves (some scholars speculated early sati, though unsupported).
  - **Harappa:** Evidence of **wooden coffin burials** (rosewood coffin) and two distinct cemeteries: Cemetery R-37 (mature phase) and Cemetery H (post-urban phase).
  - **Ropar (Punjab):** Unique burial of a dog buried beneath a human master, reminiscent of Neolithic Burzahom (Kashmir).
  - **Kalibangan:** Three types of disposal: extended inhumation, pot burials (fractional), and symbolic rectangular pits without skeletal remains.
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** Burzahom (Kashmir Neolithic) and Ropar (Harappan) are the two classic sites showing **dog buried with human**!

### Question 187
**Subject:** Geography of India | **Topic:** Channels and Water Passages of India

Match List-I (Channel / Water Passage) with List-II (Islands Separated) and select the correct answer:

**List-I (Passage)**
A. 8 Degree ($8^\circ \text{ N}$) Channel  
B. 9 Degree ($9^\circ \text{ N}$) Channel  
C. 10 Degree ($10^\circ \text{ N}$) Channel  
D. Duncan Passage  

**List-II (Separation)**
1. Minicoy Island and Main Lakshadweep Archipelago  
2. Minicoy Island (India) and Maldives  
3. Little Andaman and South Andaman (Rutland Island)  
4. Little Andaman and Car Nicobar  

**Options:**
A. A-2, B-1, C-4, D-3  
B. A-1, B-2, C-4, D-3  
C. A-2, B-4, C-1, D-3  
D. A-3, B-1, C-4, D-2  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - **8° N Channel:** Separates **Minicoy** (southernmost island of Lakshadweep, India) from the **Maldives**.
  - **9° N Channel:** Separates **Minicoy** from the main **Lakshadweep** group (Kalpeni, Suheli, Kavaratti).
  - **10° N Channel:** Separates the **Andaman group** (Little Andaman) from the **Nicobar group** (Car Nicobar) in the Bay of Bengal (approx. 150 km wide).
  - **Duncan Passage:** Separates **South Andaman (Rutland Island)** from **Little Andaman**.
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** Grand Channel separates **Great Nicobar** from Sumatra (Indonesia)!

### Question 188
**Subject:** Indian Polity | **Topic:** Landmark Judgments on Article 21

Match List-I (Supreme Court Landmark Case) with List-II (Right Enunciated under Article 21) and select the correct answer:

**List-I (Landmark Case)**
A. Justice K.S. Puttaswamy v. Union of India (2017)  
B. Olga Tellis v. Bombay Municipal Corporation (1985)  
C. Maneka Gandhi v. Union of India (1978)  
D. Subhash Kumar v. State of Bihar (1991)  

**List-II (Right Enunciated)**
1. Right to Livelihood is an integral part of the Right to Life  
2. Fundamental Right to Privacy declared intrinsic to life and personal liberty  
3. Right to life includes the right to the enjoyment of pollution-free water and air  
4. "Procedure established by law" must be just, fair, and reasonable (Due Process)  

**Options:**
A. A-2, B-1, C-4, D-3  
B. A-1, B-2, C-4, D-3  
C. A-2, B-4, C-1, D-3  
D. A-3, B-1, C-4, D-2  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - **K.S. Puttaswamy (2017):** Unanimous 9-judge bench ruled that the **Right to Privacy** is a fundamental right protected under Article 21 and Part III.
  - **Olga Tellis (1985 - "Pavement Dwellers Case"):** 5-judge bench led by CJI Y.V. Chandrachud ruled that the **Right to Livelihood** is an inescapable facet of the Right to Life.
  - **Maneka Gandhi (1978):** Overruled *A.K. Gopalan (1950)*; imported the American doctrine of "substantive due process" into Article 21, ruling that procedure must be "just, fair, and reasonable".
  - **Subhash Kumar (1991):** Held that Right to Life includes the **right to a pollution-free environment**.
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** Right to Education was declared a fundamental right under Article 21 in *Mohini Jain (1992)* and *Unni Krishnan (1993)*, before the 86th CAA 2002 inserted Article 21A!

### Question 189
**Subject:** General Science (Chemistry) | **Topic:** Ores and Extraction of Metals

Match List-I (Metal) with List-II (Principal Mineral Ore) and select the correct answer:

**List-I (Metal)**
A. Aluminium  
B. Lead  
C. Mercury  
D. Thorium  

**List-II (Ore)**
1. Galena ($PbS$)  
2. Bauxite ($Al_2O_3 \cdot 2H_2O$)  
3. Monazite sand  
4. Cinnabar ($HgS$)  

**Options:**
A. A-2, B-1, C-4, D-3  
B. A-1, B-2, C-4, D-3  
C. A-2, B-4, C-1, D-3  
D. A-3, B-1, C-4, D-2  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - **Aluminium:** Extracted from **Bauxite** via the Bayer process and Hall-Héroult electrolytic reduction (cryolite $Na_3AlF_6$ is added to lower the melting point).
  - **Lead:** Extracted from sulphide ore **Galena ($PbS$)**.
  - **Mercury:** Extracted from sulphide ore **Cinnabar ($HgS$)** by roasting in air.
  - **Thorium:** Radioactive actinide extracted from **Monazite beach sands** (plentiful in coastal Kerala and Odisha).
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** Pitchblende is the principal ore of **Uranium**; Haematite and Magnetite are ores of **Iron**; Chalcopyrite and Malachite are ores of **Copper**!

### Question 191
**Subject:** Environment & Ecology | **Topic:** Kunming-Montreal Global Biodiversity Framework (GBF)

1. It adopted the landmark "30x30 target", committing nations to conserve at least 30% of global land, inland waters, and coastal and marine areas by 2030.
2. It aims to reduce global food waste by half and significantly reduce overconsumption and waste generation by 2030.
3. The Global Biodiversity Framework replaces the previous Aichi Biodiversity Targets (2011–2020).

Which of the statements given above are correct?

**Options:**
A. 1 and 2 only  
B. 2 and 3 only  
C. 1 and 3 only  
D. 1, 2 and 3  

**Answer:** D

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - All three statements are correct:
    - **Adoption:** Finalized in December 2022 in Montreal, Canada, under the presidency of China.
    - **Core Targets (23 global targets for 2030):**
      - **Target 3 ("30 by 30"):** Protect at least 30% of terrestrial, inland water, and coastal/marine areas by 2030.
      - **Target 16:** Halve global food waste and substantially reduce waste generation.
      - **Target 19:** Mobilize at least \$200 billion per year from all sources for biodiversity financing.
    - Replaces the 20 Aichi Biodiversity Targets adopted in Nagoya in 2010.
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** CBD has 3 main objectives: Conservation of biodiversity, Sustainable use of components, and Fair and equitable sharing of benefits arising from genetic resources (Nagoya Protocol)!

**Subject:** Medieval Indian History | **Topic:** Delhi Sultanate Architectural Monuments

B. Alai Darwaza (Delhi)  
C. Hauz Khas & Siri Fort  
D. Tughlaqabad Fort  

**List-II (Builder)**
1. Alauddin Khalji  
2. Qutbuddin Aibak  
3. Ghiyasuddin Tughlaq  
4. Alauddin Khalji

A. A-2, B-1, C-4, D-3 (or A-2, B-4, C-1, D-3)  
B. A-1, B-2, C-4, D-3  
C. A-2, B-3, C-1, D-4  
D. A-3, B-1, C-4, D-2  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - **Adhai Din Ka Jhonpra (Ajmer):** Built by **Qutbuddin Aibak** in 1199 on the ruins of a Sanskrit college (*Saraswati Mandir*) built by Chauhan king Vigraharaja IV (Visaladeva).

### Question 194
**Subject:** Current Affairs | **Topic:** Global Hunger Index (GHI) 2024

Consider the following statements regarding the Global Hunger Index (GHI) 2024:
1. GHI is jointly published annually by Welthungerhilfe (Germany) and Concern Worldwide (Ireland).
2. The index scores hunger based on four indicators: Undernourishment, Child Stunting, Child Wasting, and Child Mortality.
3. In GHI 2024, India was placed in the "Serious" hunger category, ranking 105th out of 127 countries.

Which of the statements given above are correct?

**Options:**
A. 1 and 2 only  
B. 2 and 3 only  
C. 1 and 3 only  
D. 1, 2 and 3  

**Answer:** D

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - All three statements are correct:
    - **4 Indicators across 3 Dimensions:**
      - *Inadequate food supply:* Undernourishment (share of population with insufficient caloric intake, weight $\frac{1}{3}$).
      - *Child undernutrition:* Child Wasting (low weight-for-height under age 5, acute undernutrition, weight $\frac{1}{6}$) and Child Stunting (low height-for-age under age 5, chronic undernutrition, weight $\frac{1}{6}$).
      - *Child mortality:* Under-five mortality rate (weight $\frac{1}{3}$).
    - **India in GHI 2024:** Ranked **105th out of 127 countries** with a score of 27.3, categorizing its hunger level as "Serious". India recorded the world's highest child wasting rate (~18.7%).
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** In GHI scoring: **0 is the best score (No hunger)** and **100 is the worst score**!

### Question 195
**Subject:** Modern Indian History | **Topic:** Peasant Associations in Uttar Pradesh

Consider the following peasant organizations formed in colonial Uttar Pradesh:
1. The **UP Kisan Sabha** was set up in February 1918 by Gauri Shankar Mishra, Indra Narayan Dwivedi, and supported by Madan Mohan Malaviya.
2. The **Awadh Kisan Sabha** was founded in October 1920 at Pratapgarh, actively mobilized by Baba Ramchandra.
3. The **Eka Movement** (1921–1922) spread across Hardoi, Bahraich, and Sitapur districts, led by Madari Pasi.

Which of the statements given above are correct?

**Options:**
A. 1 and 2 only  
B. 2 and 3 only  
C. 1 and 3 only  
D. 1, 2 and 3  

**Answer:** D

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - All three statements are correct:
    - **UP Kisan Sabha (Feb 1918):** Formed in Allahabad by Gauri Shankar Mishra and Indra Narayan Dwivedi with Madan Mohan Malaviya's backing; established over 450 village branches.
    - **Awadh Kisan Sabha (Oct 1920):** Formed in Pratapgarh due to ideological divergence with Malaviya's moderate approach; led by **Baba Ramchandra** (a Maharashtrian who recited Tulsidas's *Ramcharitmanas* to mobilize peasants) and Jawaharlal Nehru.
    - **Eka Movement (Unity Movement, late 1921):** Grassroots tenant movement in northern Awadh (Hardoi, Bahraich, Sitapur); spearheaded by low-caste leader **Madari Pasi**; resisted 50% above-recorded rents and forced labor (*Begar*).
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** All India Kisan Sabha (AIKS) was founded at Lucknow in **April 1936** with **Swami Sahajanand Saraswati** as President and **N.G. Ranga** as General Secretary!
