### Question 196
**Subject:** Geography of India | **Topic:** Longitudinal (Regional) Divisions of the Himalayas

Match List-I (Regional Himalayan Division) with List-II (Demarcating Rivers / Extent) and select the correct answer:

**List-I (Division)**
A. Kashmir / Punjab Himalaya  
B. Kumaon Himalaya  
C. Nepal Himalaya  
D. Assam Himalaya  

**List-II (River Extent)**
1. Between Sutlej and Kali rivers  
2. Between Indus and Sutlej rivers  
3. Between Teesta and Dihang (Brahmaputra) rivers  
4. Between Kali and Teesta rivers  

**Options:**
A. A-2, B-1, C-4, D-3  
B. A-1, B-2, C-4, D-3  
C. A-2, B-4, C-1, D-3  
D. A-3, B-1, C-4, D-2  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - Proposed by Sir Sidney Burrard based on river valleys:
    - **Kashmir / Punjab Himalaya:** Between **Indus and Sutlej** (~560 km); contains Karakoram, Ladakh, Pir Panjal, Zoji La; Karewa formations for saffron cultivation.
    - **Kumaon Himalaya:** Between **Sutlej and Kali** (~320 km) in Uttarakhand; contains Nanda Devi, Kamet, Badrinath, Kedarnath, Trishul peaks.
    - **Nepal Himalaya:** Between **Kali and Teesta** (~800 km); longest section; contains tallest peaks: Mount Everest, Kanchenjunga, Makalu, Dhaulagiri, Annapurna.
    - **Assam Himalaya:** Between **Teesta and Dihang** (~720 km); contains Namcha Barwa, Kula Kangri.
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** Longest regional division = **Nepal Himalaya (800 km)**; Highest peak in Kumaon Himalaya = **Nanda Devi (7,817 m)**!

### Question 197
**Subject:** Indian Polity | **Topic:** Comptroller and Auditor General (CAG) Duties

Consider the following statements regarding the Comptroller and Auditor General of India (CAG):
1. Under Article 149, the CAG audits all expenditure from the Consolidated Fund of India, Consolidated Fund of each State, and Consolidated Fund of each Union Territory having a Legislative Assembly.
2. The CAG submits audit reports relating to the accounts of the Union to the President, who causes them to be laid before each House of Parliament under Article 151.
3. The CAG holds office during the pleasure of the President of India.

Which of the statements given above is/are correct?

**Options:**
A. 1 and 2 only  
B. 2 and 3 only  
C. 1 only  
D. 1, 2 and 3  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - Statements 1 and 2 are correct: The CAG's duties (under CAG DPC Act 1971) encompass auditing all receipts and expenditures of Consolidated Funds, Contingency Funds, and Public Accounts of Centre and States. Under Article 151(1), Union reports go to the President (laid before Parliament); under Article 151(2), State reports go to the Governor (laid before State Legislature).
  - Statement 3 is incorrect: The CAG does **NOT hold office during the pleasure of the President**! The CAG enjoys constitutional security of tenure (6 years or until age 65) and can be removed ONLY in like manner and on like grounds as a Supreme Court judge (impeachment by special majority of Parliament on grounds of proved misbehavior or incapacity).
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** Post-retirement restriction: The CAG is **ineligible for further office** under the Government of India or any State Government after retirement (Article 148(4))!

### Question 198
**Subject:** General Science (Biology) | **Topic:** Photosynthesis & Chloroplast Biochemistry

Consider the following statements regarding photosynthesis:
1. Chlorophyll pigment contains an atom of Magnesium ($Mg$) coordinated at the center of its porphyrin ring.
2. The Light-dependent Reaction (photochemical phase) takes place in the thylakoid membranes / grana of the chloroplast, producing ATP and $NADPH$.
3. The Dark Reaction (Calvin cycle / biosynthetic phase) occurs in the stroma of the chloroplast and does not require light directly.

Which of the statements given above are correct?

**Options:**
A. 1 and 2 only  
B. 2 and 3 only  
C. 1 and 3 only  
D. 1, 2 and 3  

**Answer:** D

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - All three statements are correct:
    - **Chlorophyll:** Green photosynthetic pigment; contains a **Magnesium ($Mg^{2+}$)** ion in a tetrapyrrole porphyrin ring (similar to Iron in hemoglobin).
    - **Light Reaction:** Photolysis of water ($2H_2O \rightarrow 4H^+ + 4e^- + O_2$) occurs on the inner side of thylakoid membrane in PS-II; oxygen gas is released; ATP and NADPH are synthesized (Assimilatory Power).
    - **Dark Reaction (Calvin Cycle):** Occurs in the **Stroma**; catalyzed by the enzyme **RuBisCO** (most abundant enzyme on Earth); fixes atmospheric $CO_2$ into glucose using ATP and NADPH.
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** The Oxygen ($O_2$) evolved during photosynthesis comes from **WATER ($H_2O$)**, NOT from Carbon Dioxide ($CO_2$)! (Proven by Cornelius van Niel and Ruben & Kamen using oxygen isotope $^{18}O$).

### Question 200
**Subject:** Environment & Ecology | **Topic:** Bioaccumulation and Biomagnification

Consider the following statements:
1. 'Bioaccumulation' refers to the gradual accumulation of a toxic chemical in the tissues of a single organism over its lifetime.
2. 'Biomagnification' refers to the progressive increase in the concentration of a persistent non-biodegradable pollutant at successive higher trophic levels in a food chain.
3. For biomagnification to occur, a pollutant must be long-lived, mobile, soluble in fats (lipophilic), and biologically active.

Which of the statements given above are correct?

**Options:**
A. 1 and 2 only  
B. 2 and 3 only  
C. 1 and 3 only  
D. 1, 2 and 3  

**Answer:** D

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - All three statements are correct:
    - **Bioaccumulation:** Takes place within a **single trophic level** (intake rate exceeds metabolic excretion/breakdown rate).
    - **Biomagnification:** Occurs **across multiple trophic levels** (concentration multiplies up the food chain).
    - Classic examples:
      - **DDT:** Concentration in water ($0.003 \text{ ppb}$) biomagnifies through zooplankton ($0.04 \text{ ppm}$), small fish ($0.5 \text{ ppm}$), fish-eating birds ($25 \text{ ppm}$) by millions of times, causing eggshell thinning and bird population collapse.
      - **Methylmercury:** Concentrates in predatory fish (tuna, shark), causing neurological damage (Minamata disease).
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** Water-soluble toxins do NOT biomagnify because they are rapidly excreted in urine. ONLY **fat-soluble (lipophilic)**, persistent pollutants biomagnify!

---

### Question 201
**Subject:** World Geography | **Topic:** Major Rivers of Europe & North America

Match List-I (River) with List-II (Distinct Feature / Outflow) and select the correct answer:

**List-I (River)**
A. Danube River  
B. Rhine River  
C. St. Lawrence River  
D. Volga River  

**List-II (Feature / Outflow)**
1. Empties into Caspian Sea; longest river in Europe  
2. Flows through or touches 10 countries and passes through 4 national capitals (Vienna, Bratislava, Budapest, Belgrade)  
3. Connects the Great Lakes with the Atlantic Ocean; busiest inland waterway in North America  
4. Flows through the Ruhr industrial valley into the North Sea at Rotterdam  

**Options:**
A. A-2, B-4, C-3, D-1  
B. A-1, B-2, C-4, D-3  
C. A-2, B-3, C-4, D-1  
D. A-3, B-4, C-1, D-2  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - **Danube:** Originates in Black Forest (Germany); flows eastward for 2,850 km into the Black Sea; passes through 4 European capitals: **Vienna (Austria), Bratislava (Slovakia), Budapest (Hungary), and Belgrade (Serbia)**.
  - **Rhine:** Originates in Swiss Alps; flows through Germany's industrial heartland (Ruhr) into the North Sea at Rotterdam (Netherlands).
  - **St. Lawrence:** Drains the entire Great Lakes basin into the Atlantic Ocean (Gulf of St. Lawrence); forms part of the international boundary between USA and Canada.
  - **Volga:** Longest river in Europe (~3,530 km); flows entirely within Russia and drains into the enclosed **Caspian Sea**.

### Question 205
**Subject:** Indian Polity | **Topic:** Constitutional Powers of the State Governor

Consider the following statements regarding the Governor of a State:
1. Under Article 153, there shall be a Governor for each State, but the 7th Constitutional Amendment Act, 1956 permitted the appointment of the same person as Governor for two or more States.
2. Under Article 213, the Governor can promulgate an ordinance when the State Legislative Assembly is not in session.
3. Under Article 200, when a Bill is passed by the State Legislature, the Governor may reserve the bill for the consideration of the President, and reservation is mandatory if the bill endangers the position of the High Court.

Which of the statements given above are correct?

**Options:**
A. 1 and 2 only  
B. 2 and 3 only  
C. 1 and 3 only  
D. 1, 2 and 3  

**Answer:** D

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - All three statements are correct:
    - **Article 153:** 7th CAA 1956 added the proviso that the same person may be appointed Governor of two or more states.
    - **Article 213 (Ordinance Power):** Governor can issue ordinances only when assembly (or either house in bicameral states) is not in session; must be approved by the legislature within **6 weeks** of its reassembly.
    - **Article 200 (Reservation for President):** Discretionary power to reserve bills; second proviso makes it **obligatory** to reserve any bill that derogates from the constitutional powers of the High Court. Under Article 201, the President may give assent, withhold assent, or direct return of the bill.
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** Unlike the Governor, the President has NO time limit to decide on a reserved state bill, and the President is NOT bound to give assent even if the State Legislature repasses the bill!

Select the correct answer from the codes given below:

A. 1 — 2 — 3 — 4  
B. 2 — 1 — 3 — 4  
C. 1 — 3 — 2 — 4  
D. 1 — 2 — 4 — 3  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  1. **Ramanujacharya (1017–1137 CE):** South Indian philosopher-saint; propounded *Vishishtadvaita* Vedanta; revived Sri Vaishnavism.
  2. **Kabir Das (c. 1398–1448 / 1518 CE):** Nirguna Bhakti poet-weaver of Varanasi; disciple of Ramananda; composed *Dohas* collected in the *Bijak* (Sakhi, Sabad, Ramaini).
  3. **Guru Nanak Dev (1469–1539 CE):** Founder of Sikhism; born at Talwandi (Nankana Sahib); established *Langar* and *Sangat*.
  4. **Chaitanya Mahaprabhu (1486–1534 CE):** Mystic saint of Bengal; popularized *Kirtan* (congregational chanting of Hare Krishna mantra); Gaudiya Vaishnavism.
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** Chronology sequence: **Shankaracharya (8th C) -> Ramanuja (11th C) -> Ramananda (14th C) -> Kabir (15th C) -> Guru Nanak (1469) -> Chaitanya (1486) -> Mirabai (1498) -> Tulsidas (1532)**!

### Question 207
**Subject:** General Science (Physics) | **Topic:** Surface Tension and Capillarity

Consider the following everyday phenomena:
1. Raindrops and small liquid droplets assume a spherical shape.
2. Oil rises up through the cotton wick of an earthen lamp.
3. Adding soap or detergent to water helps remove grease and dirt from clothes.

Which of the above phenomena are directly governed by the physical property of **Surface Tension**?

**Options:**
A. 1 and 2 only  
B. 1 and 3 only  
C. 2 and 3 only  
D. 1, 2 and 3  

**Answer:** D

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - All three phenomena are governed by surface tension:
    - **Spherical Droplets:** Liquid surfaces tend to minimize surface energy and contract to the minimum possible surface area for a given volume, which is a **Sphere**.
    - **Lamp Wick (Capillarity):** Driven by surface tension and adhesive forces between liquid molecules and narrow pores of the fabric ($h = \frac{2T \cos\theta}{r\rho g}$).
    - **Detergents & Soaps:** Surfactants significantly **lower the surface tension** of water, increasing its wetting power so it can penetrate microscopic pores in fabrics and emulsify grease.
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** As temperature increases, surface tension of liquids **DECREASES** (and becomes zero at the critical temperature)!

Match List-I (Fair / Mela in UP) with List-II (District / Location) and select the correct answer:

B. Soron Fair  
C. Govind Saheb Fair  
D. Devi Patan Fair  

**List-II (District / Location)**
1. Kasganj  
2. Barsana and Nandgaon (Mathura)  
3. Balrampur  
4. Ambedkar Nagar

B. A-1, B-2, C-4, D-3  
C. A-2, B-4, C-1, D-3  
D. A-3, B-1, C-4, D-2  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - **Lathmar Holi:** Celebrated days before Holi in **Barsana** (Radha's birthplace) and **Nandgaon** (Krishna's village) in Mathura district, where women playfully beat men with wooden sticks (*lathis*).

### Question 209
**Subject:** Environment & Ecology | **Topic:** Project Rhino and Greater One-Horned Rhinoceros

Consider the following statements regarding the Greater One-Horned Rhinoceros (*Rhinoceros unicornis*):
1. India is home to over 80% of the global population of the Greater One-Horned Rhinoceros.
2. Kaziranga National Park in Assam holds the highest single population of wild rhinos in the world.
3. The species is classified as 'Critically Endangered' on the IUCN Red List.

Which of the statements given above is/are correct?

**Options:**
A. 1 and 2 only  
B. 2 and 3 only  
C. 1 only  
D. 1, 2 and 3  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - Statements 1 and 2 are correct: Global population exceeds 4,000 rhinos, with ~3,200 residing in India (primarily Assam, West Bengal, and UP). Kaziranga National Park alone hosts over 2,600 rhinos. In UP, a successful rhino reintroduction program has been running in **Dudhwa National Park** since 1984.
  - Statement 3 is incorrect: The Greater One-Horned Rhino is classified as **'Vulnerable'** (NOT Critically Endangered) on the IUCN Red List, having been downlisted due to successful anti-poaching and conservation efforts under Indian Rhino Vision 2020 (IRV 2020).
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** Javan Rhino and Sumatran Rhino are Critically Endangered, but the Indian Greater One-Horned Rhino is **Vulnerable**! Protected under Schedule I of Wildlife Protection Act 1972.

### Question 210
**Subject:** Indian Economy | **Topic:** Regulatory Institutions of India

Match List-I (Regulatory Institution) with List-II (Statutory Act / Headquarters) and select the correct answer:

**List-I (Institution)**
A. Securities and Exchange Board of India (SEBI)  
B. Insurance Regulatory and Development Authority of India (IRDAI)  
C. National Bank for Agriculture and Rural Development (NABARD)  
D. Pension Fund Regulatory and Development Authority (PFRDA)  

**List-II (Detail / Location)**
1. Established 1982 on B. Sivaraman Committee report; apex rural development bank  
2. Statutory status granted in 1992 under SEBI Act; capital markets regulator in Mumbai  
3. Statutory body under IRDA Act 1999; headquarters located at Hyderabad  
4. Regulates National Pension System (NPS); headquartered in New Delhi  

**Options:**
A. A-2, B-3, C-1, D-4  
B. A-1, B-2, C-4, D-3  
C. A-2, B-1, C-4, D-3  
D. A-3, B-2, C-1, D-4  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - **SEBI:** Non-statutory body created in 1988; given full statutory autonomous regulatory powers via SEBI Act 1992 (headquarters in Mumbai).
  - **IRDAI:** Created on recommendations of R.N. Malhotra Committee (1994); statutory body under IRDA Act 1999; relocated its headquarters from Delhi to **Hyderabad** in 2001.
  - **NABARD:** Formed on July 12, 1982, under Act 61 of 1981 based on the B. Sivaraman Committee recommendations; dedicated to agricultural credit and rural development.

### Question 211
**Subject:** Current Affairs | **Topic:** Nobel Prize in Physics 2024

The Nobel Prize in Physics 2024 was awarded jointly to John J. Hopfield and Geoffrey E. Hinton for which breakthrough achievement?

**Options:**
A. Discoveries in topological phase transitions and topological phases of matter  
B. Foundational discoveries and inventions that enable machine learning with artificial neural networks  
C. Groundbreaking contributions to the understanding of complex physical systems and climate modeling  
D. Theoretical discoveries of topological defects in quantum materials  

**Answer:** B

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - The Royal Swedish Academy of Sciences awarded the 2024 Nobel Prize in Physics to **John J. Hopfield (Princeton University)** and **Geoffrey E. Hinton (University of Toronto - "Godfather of AI")**.
  - Recognized "for foundational discoveries and inventions that enable machine learning with artificial neural networks":
    - *Hopfield:* Invented the **Hopfield Network**, an associative memory that can store and reconstruct images and data patterns using physical principles of atomic spin systems.
    - *Hinton:* Developed the **Boltzmann Machine**, which autonomously discovers properties in data and laid the groundwork for modern Deep Learning.
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** This marked the first time a Nobel Prize in Physics was awarded for core computer science / artificial intelligence algorithms!

### Question 212
**Subject:** Modern Indian History | **Topic:** British Colonial Acts (1909, 1919, 1935 Comparison)

Match List-I (British Enactment) with List-II (Key Constitutional Innovation) and select the correct answer:

**List-I (Act)**
A. Indian Councils Act 1909 (Morley-Minto Reforms)  
B. Government of India Act 1919 (Montagu-Chelmsford Reforms)  
C. Government of India Act 1935  

**List-II (Innovation)**
1. Introduced Provincial Autonomy, abolished Diarchy in Provinces, and proposed an All-India Federation  
2. Introduced Communal Electorates (Separate Electorates for Muslims) for the first time  
3. Introduced Diarchy in the Provincial Executive (Transferred and Reserved Subjects) and Bicameralism at the Centre  

**Options:**
A. A-2, B-3, C-1  
B. A-1, B-3, C-2  
C. A-2, B-1, C-3  
D. A-3, B-2, C-1  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - **Act of 1909 (Morley-Minto):** Legalized communalism; Lord Minto was hailed as "Father of Communal Electorate"; allowed Indians into Viceroy's Executive Council (Satyendra Prasad Sinha was the first Indian member as Law Member).
  - **Act of 1919 (Mont-Ford):** Introduced **Diarchy in 8 Provinces** (Reserved subjects like Law & Order/Finance under British Governor; Transferred subjects like Education/Health under Indian Ministers); introduced direct elections and bicameralism at Centre.
  - **Act of 1935:** Abolished provincial diarchy; granted **Provincial Autonomy**; introduced Diarchy at the Centre (never implemented); created Federal Court (1937) and Reserve Bank of India.
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** Diarchy in Provinces = **1919 Act**; Diarchy at Centre & Provincial Autonomy = **1935 Act**!

### Question 214
**Subject:** Indian Polity | **Topic:** Union Public Service Commission (UPSC)

Consider the following statements regarding the Union Public Service Commission (UPSC):
1. UPSC is established as an independent constitutional body under Articles 315 to 323 in Part XIV of the Constitution.
2. The Chairman and members of UPSC hold office for a term of six years or until they attain the age of 65 years, whichever is earlier.
3. The Chairman of UPSC can be removed from office by the President on the ground of misbehavior only after an inquiry by the Supreme Court under Article 317, and the advice of the Supreme Court is binding on the President.

Which of the statements given above are correct?

**Options:**
A. 1 and 2 only  
B. 2 and 3 only  
C. 1 and 3 only  
D. 1, 2 and 3  

**Answer:** D

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - All three statements are correct:
    - **Constitutional Mandate:** Article 315 provides for a Public Service Commission for the Union and for each State.
    - **Tenure (Article 316):** 6 years or age 65 for UPSC (for State PSCs, it is 6 years or age 62).
    - **Removal (Article 317):** President refers matter of alleged misbehavior to Supreme Court. SC conducts inquiry; if SC recommends removal, **the President is constitutionally BOUND to remove the Chairman/member**.
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** Post-retirement: Chairman of UPSC is **completely ineligible for any further employment** under Central or State Government!

### Question 215
**Subject:** General Science (Biology) | **Topic:** Human Eye Anatomy & Photoreceptors

Consider the following statements regarding the human eye:
1. The Cornea is the transparent front part of the eye that lacks blood vessels and can be transplanted without immunological rejection.
2. The Retina contains two types of photoreceptor cells: Rods (responsible for twilight/scotopic vision) and Cones (responsible for daylight/photopic and color vision).
3. The light-sensitive pigment in Rod cells is Rhodopsin (visual purple), which requires Vitamin A for its synthesis.

Which of the statements given above are correct?

**Options:**
A. 1 and 2 only  
B. 2 and 3 only  
C. 1 and 3 only  
D. 1, 2 and 3  

**Answer:** D

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - All three statements are correct:
    - **Cornea:** Avascular (receives oxygen directly from atmospheric air and nutrients from aqueous humor); high success rate in corneal grafts (*Keratoplasty*).
    - **Rods vs Cones:**
      - *Rods (~120 million):* Contain **Rhodopsin**; sensitive to low light (night vision); cannot distinguish color. Vitamin A deficiency impairs rhodopsin synthesis, causing **Night Blindness (Nyctalopia)**.
      - *Cones (~6 million):* Concentrated at the *Fovea centralis* (yellow spot); contain photopsins; provide high-resolution daylight vision and perceive red, green, and blue colors.
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** At the **Blind Spot (Optic Disc)**, there are NO rods or cones, hence no image can be formed there!

### Question 217
**Subject:** Environment & Ecology | **Topic:** Ecosystem Productivity (GPP vs NPP)

Consider the following statements regarding ecological productivity:
1. Gross Primary Productivity (GPP) is the total rate of production of organic matter during photosynthesis.
2. Net Primary Productivity (NPP) is the remaining biomass available to heterotrophs after accounting for respiration losses ($R$), represented as $\text{NPP} = \text{GPP} - R$.
3. Among global ecosystems, open oceans have the highest net primary productivity per unit area, while tropical rainforests have the lowest.

Which of the statements given above is/are correct?

**Options:**
A. 1 and 2 only  
B. 2 and 3 only  
C. 1 only  
D. 1, 2 and 3  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - Statements 1 and 2 are correct: $\text{GPP}$ measures total solar energy fixed into organic chemical bonds. A significant portion of this energy is consumed by plants for their own cellular respiration ($R$). What remains as standing biomass is $\text{NPP} = \text{GPP} - R$.
  - Statement 3 is incorrect: The situation is the OPPOSITE!
    - **Highest productivity per unit area ($g/m^2/year$):** **Tropical Rainforests, Estuaries, and Coral Reefs** (over $2,000 \text{ g/m}^2/\text{yr}$).
    - **Lowest productivity per unit area:** **Extreme Deserts, Tundra, and Deep Open Oceans** (less than $125 \text{ g/m}^2/\text{yr}$).
    - (However, because Open Oceans cover ~71% of Earth's surface, their *total collective global productivity* is large).
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** Coral reefs and tropical rainforests have the HIGHEST productivity per unit area; open oceans have LOW productivity per unit area!

---

### Question 218
**Subject:** Indian Economy | **Topic:** Components of Foreign Exchange Reserves of India

India's Foreign Exchange Reserves managed by the Reserve Bank of India comprise how many distinct components?

**Options:**
A. Two  
B. Three  
C. Four  
D. Five  

**Answer:** C

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - India's forex reserves consist of **4 distinct components**:
    1. **Foreign Currency Assets (FCA):** Largest component (~85–88% of total); held in multi-currency assets (US Dollars, Euros, Pound Sterling, Japanese Yen).
    2. **Gold Reserves:** Physical gold held by RBI (stored in RBI vaults and Bank of England).
    3. **Special Drawing Rights (SDR):** International reserve asset created by the IMF in 1969.
    4. **Reserve Tranche Position (RTP):** The reserve tranche that India maintains with the International Monetary Fund (IMF).
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** Foreign Currency Assets (FCA) is the LARGEST component; Gold is the second largest!

### Question 220
**Subject:** Ancient Indian History | **Topic:** Harappan Seals and Pashupati Seal

Consider the following statements regarding the famous 'Pashupati Seal' discovered at Mohenjodaro:
1. It is a steatite seal depicting a three-faced male deity seated in a yogic posture (*Padmasana*) wearing a horned headdress.
2. Four wild animals surround the central deity: an Elephant and a Tiger on the right, and a Rhinoceros and a Buffalo on the left.
3. Two antelopes / deer are depicted beneath the stool/seat of the deity.

Which of the statements given above are correct?

**Options:**
A. 1 and 2 only  
B. 2 and 3 only  
C. 1 and 3 only  
D. 1, 2 and 3  

**Answer:** D

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - All three statements are correct:
    - Discovered in 1928–29 by Ernest Mackay at Mohenjodaro; identified by Sir John Marshall as *Proto-Shiva* (*Pashupati* - Lord of Beasts).
    - **Surrounding Animals (Total 5 species / 6 animals):**
      - Right side of deity: **Elephant** and **Tiger**.
      - Left side of deity: **Rhinoceros** and **Water Buffalo**.
      - Beneath the seat / at his feet: **Two Deer / Antelopes**.
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** The **HORSE and COW are completely absent** from the Pashupati Seal (and Harappan seals in general)!

### Question 221
**Subject:** Geography of India | **Topic:** Famous Waterfalls of Peninsular India

Match List-I (Waterfall) with List-II (River & State) and select the correct answer:

A. Jog / Gersoppa Falls  
B. Shivanasamudra Falls  
C. Dhuandhar Falls  
D. Hundru Falls  

**List-II (River & State)**
1. Kaveri River; Karnataka  
2. Sharavathi River; Karnataka  
3. Subarnarekha River; Jharkhand  
4. Narmada River; Madhya Pradesh  

**Options:**
A. A-2, B-1, C-4, D-3  
B. A-1, B-2, C-4, D-3  
C. A-2, B-4, C-1, D-3  
D. A-3, B-1, C-4, D-2  

**Answer:** A

- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - **Jog Falls (Gersoppa / Mahatma Gandhi Falls):** Plunge waterfall (253 m) on **Sharavathi River** in Shimoga, Karnataka; consists of four distinct cascades: Raja, Roarer, Rocket, and Rani.

### Question 222
**Subject:** Indian Polity | **Topic:** 74th Constitutional Amendment Act (Urban Local Bodies)

Consider the following statements regarding the 74th Constitutional Amendment Act, 1992:
1. It added Part IX-A to the Constitution titled "The Municipalities" and the Twelfth Schedule containing 18 functional items.
2. Under Article 243S, Ward Committees must be constituted in all municipalities having a population of three lakhs or more.
3. Under Article 243ZE, a Metropolitan Planning Committee must be constituted in every Metropolitan area to prepare a draft development plan.

Which of the statements given above are correct?

**Options:**
A. 1 and 2 only  
B. 2 and 3 only  
C. 1 and 3 only  
D. 1, 2 and 3  

**Answer:** D

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - All three statements are correct:
    - **74th CAA 1992 (effective 1 June 1993):** Constitutionalized Urban Local Bodies (Articles 243P to 243ZG); added **12th Schedule with 18 functional subjects** (urban planning, regulation of land use, roads, water supply, public health).
    - **Article 243S:** Mandatory constitution of **Wards Committees** in all municipalities with population $\ge 3,00,000$.
    - **Article 243ZD & 243ZE:** District Planning Committee (DPC) under 243ZD; Metropolitan Planning Committee (MPC) under 243ZE.
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** 11th Schedule (Panchayats) = **29 items**; 12th Schedule (Municipalities) = **18 items**!

### Question 223
**Subject:** General Science (Chemistry) | **Topic:** Naturally Occurring Organic Acids

Match List-I (Natural Source) with List-II (Organic Acid Present) and select the correct answer:

**List-I (Source)**
A. Ant sting & Nettle leaf sting  
B. Vinegar  
C. Tamarind & Unripe Grapes  
D. Sour Milk / Curd  

**List-II (Organic Acid)**
1. Acetic Acid ($CH_3COOH$)  
2. Formic Acid / Methanoic Acid ($HCOOH$)  
3. Lactic Acid  
4. Tartaric Acid  

**Options:**
A. A-2, B-1, C-4, D-3  
B. A-1, B-2, C-4, D-3  
C. A-2, B-4, C-1, D-3  
D. A-3, B-1, C-4, D-2  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - **Ant sting & Nettle:** Contains **Methanoic acid (Formic acid, $HCOOH$)**; causes burning pain, neutralized by applying a mild base like baking soda ($NaHCO_3$) or calamine lotion ($ZnCO_3$).
  - **Vinegar:** 5–8% aqueous solution of **Acetic acid ($CH_3COOH$)**.
  - **Tamarind & Grapes:** Rich in **Tartaric acid**.
  - **Curd / Sour Milk:** Contains **Lactic acid** produced by *Lactobacillus* fermenting lactose.
  - **Tomato & Spinach:** Contain **Oxalic acid** (excess intake can form calcium oxalate kidney stones).
  - **Citrus fruits (lemon, orange):** Contain **Citric acid**.
  - **Apple:** Contains **Malic acid**.
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** Vitamin C is **Ascorbic Acid**, found abundantly in Amla (Indian gooseberry) and guava!

Consider the following statements regarding the High Court of Judicature at Allahabad:

2. The High Court was shifted from Agra to Allahabad in 1869.
3. Sir Walter Morgan was the first Chief Justice of the High Court.

Which of the statements given above are correct?

**Options:**
A. 1 and 2 only  
B. 2 and 3 only  
C. 1 and 3 only  
D. 1, 2 and 3  

**Answer:** D

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - All three statements are correct:
    - **Foundation:** Established on June 11, 1866, at **Agra** (4th High Court in India after Calcutta, Bombay, Madras).
    - **Relocation:** Shifted to **Allahabad** in 1869; name officially changed to 'High Court of Judicature at Allahabad' in 1919.
    - **Chief Justices:** First Chief Justice was **Sir Walter Morgan** (1866–1871). First Indian Chief Justice was **Sir Shah Muhammad Sulaiman** (1932).
    - Under the UP High Court (Amalgamation) Order 1948, the Chief Court of Oudh was amalgamated into Allahabad High Court, retaining a permanent bench at **Lucknow**.
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** Allahabad High Court has the LARGEST sanctioned judicial strength among all High Courts in India (160 judges)!

### Question 225
**Subject:** Modern Indian History | **Topic:** Subhas Chandra Bose and Azad Hind Fauj (INA)

Consider the following statements regarding Subhas Chandra Bose and the Indian National Army (INA):
1. Subhas Chandra Bose formed the 'Forward Bloc' within the Congress in May 1939 after resigning from the Congress presidency.
2. The Indian National Army (INA) was originally conceptualized and founded in Malaya by Captain Mohan Singh with Japanese Major Iwaichi Fujiwara.
3. Subhas Chandra Bose proclaimed the formation of the Provisional Government of Free India (*Arzi Hukumat-e-Azad Hind*) in Singapore on October 21, 1943.

Which of the statements given above are correct?

**Options:**
A. 1 and 2 only  
B. 2 and 3 only  
C. 1 and 3 only  
D. 1, 2 and 3  

**Answer:** D

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - All three statements are correct:
    - **Forward Bloc:** Founded at Unnao/Makur (UP) in May 1939 after resigning following the Tripuri crisis with Gandhi and CWC.
    - **INA Genesis:** Conceived by **Captain Mohan Singh** in Malaya in 1941 from British-Indian prisoners of war; Rash Behari Bose organized the Indian Independence League in Tokyo and handed over supreme leadership to Subhas Bose in July 1943.
    - **Provisional Government:** Proclaimed on October 21, 1943, at Cathay Cinema in Singapore; recognized by 9 Axis powers. Japan handed over Andaman and Nicobar Islands to INA, renamed by Bose as **Shaheed Dweep** and **Swaraj Dweep**.
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** Famous slogan *"Dilli Chalo"* and *"Tum Mujhe Khoon Do, Main Tumhe Azadi Doonga"* were given in Burma/Singapore! INA Red Fort trials (1945) defended by Bhulabhai Desai, Tej Bahadur Sapru, and Jawaharlal Nehru.

### Question 226
**Subject:** Indian Polity | **Topic:** Right to Equality (Articles 14 to 18)

Match List-I (Article of the Constitution) with List-II (Specific Fundamental Right) and select the correct answer:

**List-I (Article)**
A. Article 14  
B. Article 15  
C. Article 16  
D. Article 17  

**List-II (Specific Right)**
1. Equality of opportunity in matters of public employment  
2. Equality before the law and equal protection of the laws  
3. Abolition of Untouchability and prohibition of its practice in any form  
4. Prohibition of discrimination on grounds only of religion, race, caste, sex, or place of birth  

**Options:**
A. A-2, B-4, C-1, D-3  
B. A-1, B-2, C-4, D-3  
C. A-2, B-1, C-4, D-3  
D. A-3, B-4, C-1, D-2  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - **Article 14:** Two concepts: "Equality before law" (negative concept borrowed from British common law) and "Equal protection of laws" (positive concept borrowed from the American Constitution).
  - **Article 15:** Prohibits discrimination on five grounds ONLY: religion, race, caste, sex, place of birth.
  - **Article 16:** Equality of opportunity in public employment (grounds: religion, race, caste, sex, descent, place of birth, residence).
  - **Article 17:** Absolute right; abolishes Untouchability; enforced via Protection of Civil Rights Act, 1955.
  - **Article 18:** Abolishes titles (except military and academic distinctions).
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** Articles available ONLY to Indian citizens: **Articles 15, 16, 19, 29, and 30**! All other Fundamental Rights (including Articles 14, 20, 21, 21A, 22, 25-28) apply to all persons (both citizens and foreigners).

### Question 227
**Subject:** Geography of India | **Topic:** Passes of the Western Ghats (Sahyadri)

Match List-I (Mountain Pass in Western Ghats) with List-II (Route / Cities Connected) and select the correct answer:

**List-I (Pass)**
A. Thalghat (Kasara Ghat)  
B. Bhorghat (Khandala Ghat)  
C. Palghat Gap (Palakkad)  
D. Shencottah Gap  

**List-II (Cities Connected)**
1. Connects Mumbai with Pune and further to Chennai / Bengaluru  
2. Connects Mumbai with Nashik and further to Kolkata / Northern India  
3. Connects Madurai (Tamil Nadu) with Kollam (Kerala)  
4. Connects Palakkad / Kochi (Kerala) with Coimbatore (Tamil Nadu) between Nilgiri and Anaimalai hills  

**Options:**
A. A-2, B-1, C-4, D-3  
B. A-1, B-2, C-4, D-3  
C. A-2, B-4, C-1, D-3  
D. A-3, B-1, C-4, D-2  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - **Thalghat:** Located in Maharashtra; carries the NH-3 and central railway connecting **Mumbai with Nashik** and northern/eastern India.
  - **Bhorghat:** Located in Maharashtra; carries the Mumbai-Pune Expressway connecting **Mumbai with Pune** and southern India.
  - **Palghat Gap:** Natural 30 km wide gap in Western Ghats between **Nilgiri Hills to the north and Anaimalai Hills to the south**; connects Coimbatore (TN) with Palakkad (Kerala).
  - **Shencottah Gap:** Located between Cardamom hills and Agasthyamalai hills; connects **Kollam (Kerala) with Madurai (TN)**.
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** Sequence from North to South in Western Ghats: **Thalghat -> Bhorghat -> Palghat -> Shencottah**!

### Question 228
**Subject:** Ancient Indian History | **Topic:** Later Vedic Rituals and Sacrifices

Match List-I (Vedic Sacrifice / Ritual) with List-II (Significance / Nature) and select the correct answer:

**List-I (Sacrifice)**
A. Rajasuya Yajna  
B. Ashvamedha Yajna  
C. Vajapeya Yajna  
D. Agnistoma Yajna  

**List-II (Significance)**
1. Chariot race in which the royal chariot was made to win; drink of soma; rejuvenated king's vitality  
2. Consecration ceremony to bestow supreme sovereign power upon the king; involved ratnin offerings  
3. Horse sacrifice to assert imperial territorial supremacy over neighboring realms  
4. Extensive sacrifice involving animal offering to Agni and pressing of Soma juice lasting for days  

**Options:**
A. A-2, B-3, C-1, D-4  
B. A-3, B-2, C-1, D-4  
C. A-2, B-1, C-3, D-4  
D. A-1, B-3, C-2, D-4  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - **Rajasuya:** Royal consecration ritual; king visited homes of 12 *Ratnins* (jewels/officers) to secure their loyalty; included a game of dice.
  - **Ashvamedha:** King let loose a consecrated royal stallion to wander for a year; any king who stopped it had to battle the imperial army; concluded with sacrifice of the horse.
  - **Vajapeya ("Drink of Strength"):** Chariot race staged so the king won against competitors, renewing his vigor.
  - **Agnistoma:** Dedicated to Agni and Indra; accompanied by chanting of Sama hymns and consumption of Soma.
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** The Pushyamitra Shunga performed **two Ashvamedha Yajnas** at Ayodhya, recorded in the Ayodhya inscription of Dhanadeva!

---

### Question 229
**Subject:** General Science (Biology) | **Topic:** Endocrine Disorders in Humans

Match List-I (Endocrine Disorder) with List-II (Hormonal Cause) and select the correct answer:

**List-I (Disorder)**
A. Diabetes Mellitus  
B. Diabetes Insipidus  
C. Addison's Disease  
D. Cushing's Syndrome  

**List-II (Hormonal Cause)**
1. Hyposecretion of Antidiuretic Hormone (ADH / Vasopressin) from posterior pituitary  
2. Deficiency of Insulin secretion from beta-cells of pancreas (hyperglycemia)  
3. Hypersecretion of Cortisol (glucocorticoids) from adrenal cortex  
4. Hyposecretion of Corticoid hormones (cortisol & aldosterone) from adrenal cortex  

**Options:**
A. A-2, B-1, C-4, D-3  
B. A-1, B-2, C-4, D-3  
C. A-2, B-4, C-1, D-3  
D. A-3, B-1, C-4, D-2  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - **Diabetes Mellitus:** Caused by insulin deficiency or receptor insensitivity; high blood glucose; glucose appears in urine (*Glycosuria*).
  - **Diabetes Insipidus:** Caused by ADH (Vasopressin) deficiency; distal tubules fail to reabsorb water; massive water loss in urine (*Polyuria*) without glucose!
  - **Addison's Disease:** Adrenal cortical insufficiency; bronze pigmentation of skin, low blood pressure, severe fatigue, hypoglycemia.
  - **Cushing's Syndrome:** Excess cortisol secretion; characterized by "Moon face", "Buffalo hump", obesity, and high blood pressure.
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** Diabetes *Insipidus* involves WATER balance (ADH deficiency); urine is tasteless (*insipid*) with NO sugar. Diabetes *Mellitus* involves SUGAR balance (Insulin deficiency); urine is sweet (*mellitus* = honey)!

### Question 230
**Subject:** Uttar Pradesh Special | **Topic:** Religious Fairs and Pilgrimage Sites in UP

Match List-I (Fair / Festival in UP) with List-II (District / Shrine) and select the correct answer:

**List-I (Fair)**
A. Deva Sharif Fair  
B. Kampil Fair  
C. Bateshwar Fair  
D. Magh Mela  

**List-II (District / Shrine)**
1. Agra; annual animal fair on Yamuna river  
2. Barabanki; shrine of Sufi saint Waris Ali Shah  
3. Prayagraj; annual holy bathing festival at Triveni Sangam  
4. Farrukhabad; Jain fair held at the ancient capital of South Panchala  

**Options:**
A. A-2, B-4, C-1, D-3  
B. A-4, B-2, C-1, D-3  
C. A-2, B-1, C-4, D-3  
D. A-3, B-4, C-1, D-2  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - **Deva Sharif (Barabanki):** Annual fair held at the Dargah of revered Sufi saint Haji Waris Ali Shah; symbol of Hindu-Muslim unity (held during Karwa Chauth / October–November).
  - **Kampil Fair (Farrukhabad):** Held at Kampilya (historic capital of Panchala Mahajanapada); sacred Jain pilgrimage site (birthplace of 13th Tirthankara Vimalanatha).
  - **Bateshwar Fair (Agra):** Famous Shiva temple complex with 101 temples and immense cattle/camel mela.
  - **Magh Mela (Prayagraj):** Annual festival held in the Hindu month of Magh at Triveni Sangam (Kumbh occurs every 12 years; Maha Kumbh held in Prayagraj).
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** Deva Sharif is in **Barabanki**; Syed Salar Masud Ghazi Dargah is in **Bahraich**!

### Question 231
**Subject:** Environment & Ecology | **Topic:** Famous Tiger Reserves of India

Match List-I (Tiger Reserve) with List-II (State) and select the correct answer:

**List-I (Tiger Reserve)**
A. Bandhavgarh Tiger Reserve  
B. Nagarhole (Rajiv Gandhi) Tiger Reserve  
C. Ranthambore Tiger Reserve  
D. Valmiki Tiger Reserve  

**List-II (State)**
1. Karnataka  
2. Madhya Pradesh  
3. Bihar  
4. Rajasthan  

**Options:**
A. A-2, B-1, C-4, D-3  
B. A-1, B-2, C-4, D-3  
C. A-2, B-4, C-1, D-3  
D. A-3, B-1, C-4, D-2  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - **Bandhavgarh (MP):** Highest tiger density in India; former hunting preserve of Maharaja of Rewa; white tigers originated here.
  - **Nagarhole (Karnataka):** Part of Nilgiri Biosphere Reserve; located on Kabini river basin.
  - **Ranthambore (Rajasthan):** Famous for tigers thriving around historical 10th-century Ranthambore Fort.
  - **Valmiki (West Champaran, Bihar):** The ONLY Tiger Reserve and National Park in the state of Bihar (contiguous with Nepal's Chitwan National Park).

### Question 232
**Subject:** Indian Economy | **Topic:** Money Market Instruments

Consider the following statements regarding Treasury Bills (T-Bills) in India:
1. Treasury Bills are zero-coupon debt instruments issued by the Government of India to meet short-term liquidity mismatches.
2. They are issued at a discount to face value and redeemed at par on maturity.
3. Treasury Bills are currently issued in three tenors: 91-day, 182-day, and 364-day.
4. State Governments in India also issue their own Treasury Bills to raise short-term finance.

Which of the statements given above are correct?

**Options:**
A. 1, 2 and 3 only  
B. 1, 3 and 4 only  
C. 2, 3 and 4 only  
D. 1, 2, 3 and 4  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - Statements 1, 2, and 3 are correct: T-Bills are short-term sovereign money market instruments auctioned by the RBI; issued at discount and redeemed at par (the difference is the interest yield). Active tenors are 91 days, 182 days, and 364 days.
  - Statement 4 is incorrect: **State Governments DO NOT issue Treasury Bills!** Only the Central Government issues T-Bills. State Governments issue State Development Loans (SDLs) and obtain short-term advances via Ways and Means Advances (WMA) from the RBI.
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** Massive UPPSC trap: Treasury Bills are issued EXCLUSIVELY by the Central Government, NEVER by State Governments!

---

### Question 233
**Subject:** Current Affairs | **Topic:** Cinema & 77th Cannes Film Festival 2024

Consider the following statements regarding the 77th Cannes Film Festival held in May 2024:
1. Indian filmmaker Payal Kapadia's film *All We Imagine as Light* won the prestigious Grand Prix (the festival's second-highest award).
2. It was the first Indian film in 30 years to compete in the main Competition section at Cannes.
3. Indian actress Anasuya Sengupta won the Best Actress award in the 'Un Certain Regard' category for her performance in *The Shameless*.

Which of the statements given above are correct?

**Options:**
A. 1 and 2 only  
B. 2 and 3 only  
C. 1 and 3 only  
D. 1, 2 and 3  

**Answer:** D

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - All three statements are correct:
    - **Payal Kapadia:** Her Malayalam-Hindi feature *All We Imagine as Light* won the prestigious **Grand Prix** at Cannes 2024 (the Palme d'Or was won by American director Sean Baker's *Anora*). It was the first Indian film in official competition since Shaji N. Karun's *Swaham* in 1994.
    - **Anasuya Sengupta:** Became the **first Indian artist** to win the Best Actress award at Cannes (in the 'Un Certain Regard' section).
    - Chidananda S. Naik's FTII student film *Sunflowers Were the First Ones to Know* won first prize in the La Cinef section.
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** Chetan Anand's *Neecha Nagar* remains the only Indian film ever to win the top prize (Palme d'Or / Grand Prix du Festival) at Cannes in 1946!

### Question 234
**Subject:** Medieval Indian History | **Topic:** Chroniclers and Literary Sources of the Sultanate

Match List-I (Medieval Text) with List-II (Chronicler / Author) and select the correct answer:

**List-I (Text)**
A. Tabaqat-i-Nasiri  
B. Tarikh-i-Firoz Shahi  
C. Futuh-us-Salatin  
D. Kitab-ur-Rihla  

**List-II (Author)**
1. Ziauddin Barani (and later completed by Shams-i-Siraj Afif)  
2. Minhaj-i-Siraj Juzjani  
3. Ibn Battuta  
4. Abdul Malik Isami  

**Options:**
A. A-2, B-1, C-4, D-3  
B. A-1, B-2, C-4, D-3  
C. A-2, B-4, C-1, D-3  
D. A-3, B-1, C-4, D-2  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - **Tabaqat-i-Nasiri:** Written in Persian by **Minhaj-i-Siraj**; dedicated to Sultan Nasiruddin Mahmud; primary source for the early Slave dynasty and Muhammad Ghori's campaigns.
  - **Tarikh-i-Firoz Shahi:** Written by **Ziauddin Barani**; chronicles the Sultanate from Balban's accession up to the 6th year of Firoz Shah Tughlaq's reign (Barani also wrote *Fatawa-i-Jahandari* on political theory).
  - **Futuh-us-Salatin:** Written in verse by **Isami**; dedicated to Alauddin Hasan Bahman Shah (founder of the Bahmani kingdom).
  - **Rihla:** Celebrated travelogue written in Arabic by Moroccan traveler **Ibn Battuta**, chronicling the 14 years he spent in India during Muhammad bin Tughlaq's reign.
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** *Khazain-ul-Futuh* (describing Alauddin Khalji's conquests) and *Tughlaqnama* were written by **Amir Khusrau**!

---

### Question 235
**Subject:** World Geography | **Topic:** Famous Straits of Europe & the Americas

Match List-I (Strait) with List-II (Geographical Separation / Water Bodies Connected) and select the correct answer:

**List-I (Strait)**
A. Bosporus Strait  
B. Dardanelles Strait  
C. Strait of Messina  
D. Cook Strait  

**List-II (Connected / Separated)**
1. Separates North Island and South Island of New Zealand  
2. Connects Black Sea with Sea of Marmara (separates European and Asian Istanbul)  
3. Separates the island of Sicily from mainland Italy (Calabria)  
4. Connects Sea of Marmara with Aegean Sea  

**Options:**
A. A-2, B-4, C-3, D-1  
B. A-4, B-2, C-3, D-1  
C. A-2, B-3, C-4, D-1  
D. A-1, B-4, C-3, D-2  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - **Bosporus Strait:** Narrowest strait used for international navigation; connects **Black Sea to Sea of Marmara**; splits Istanbul between Europe and Asia.
  - **Dardanelles Strait:** Connects **Sea of Marmara to Aegean Sea** (and Mediterranean); site of Gallipoli campaign in WWI.
  - **Strait of Messina:** Connects Tyrrhenian Sea and Ionian Sea; separates **Sicily from the "toe" of Italy**.
  - **Cook Strait:** Connects Tasman Sea and South Pacific Ocean; separates **North Island from South Island of New Zealand**.
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** Sequence from Black Sea to Mediterranean: **Black Sea -> Bosporus Strait -> Sea of Marmara -> Dardanelles Strait -> Aegean Sea / Mediterranean**!

### Question 236
**Subject:** Indian Polity | **Topic:** Parliamentary Sessions & Terms

Consider the following statements regarding the sessions of Parliament:
1. 'Prorogation' terminates a sitting as well as the session of the House, and is done by the President of India.
2. 'Adjournment' terminates a sitting only, and is done by the presiding officer (Speaker / Chairman) of the House.
3. Under Article 100(3), the Quorum to constitute a meeting of either House of Parliament is one-tenth (10%) of the total number of members of the House.

Which of the statements given above are correct?

**Options:**
A. 1 and 2 only  
B. 2 and 3 only  
C. 1 and 3 only  
D. 1, 2 and 3  

**Answer:** D

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - All three statements are correct:
    - **Adjournment:** Suspends work of the sitting for a specified time (hours, days); exercised by Speaker/Chairman.
    - **Adjournment Sine Die:** Terminating sitting of Parliament without naming a day for reassembly; exercised by Presiding Officer.
    - **Prorogation:** President issues notification under Article 85(2)(a) ending the entire session (usually done days after adjournment sine die).
    - **Quorum (Article 100):** Minimum presence required is **$rac{1}{10}	ext{th}$ of total membership** (55 members in Lok Sabha, 25 members in Rajya Sabha).
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** Prorogation does NOT cause pending bills to lapse! A bill lapses ONLY upon the **Dissolution** of the Lok Sabha under specific conditions governed by Article 107.

---

### Question 237
**Subject:** General Science (Physics & Environment) | **Topic:** Greenhouse Gases and Global Warming Potentials

Arrange the following greenhouse gases in descending order of their Global Warming Potential (GWP over 100-year horizon, from highest GWP to lowest):
1. Sulphur Hexafluoride ($SF_6$)
2. Nitrous Oxide ($N_2O$)
3. Methane ($CH_4$)
4. Carbon Dioxide ($CO_2$)

Select the correct answer from the codes given below:

**Options:**
A. 1 — 2 — 3 — 4  
B. 1 — 3 — 2 — 4  
C. 2 — 1 — 3 — 4  
D. 4 — 3 — 2 — 1  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - Global Warming Potential (GWP) measures how much heat a greenhouse gas traps in the atmosphere relative to $CO_2$ (which has a baseline GWP = 1):
    1. **Sulphur Hexafluoride ($SF_6$):** Highest GWP known; $	ext{GWP} pprox 23,500$ (used as an electrical insulator in circuit breakers).
    2. **Nitrous Oxide ($N_2O$):** $	ext{GWP} pprox 273$ to $298$ times higher than $CO_2$.
    3. **Methane ($CH_4$):** $	ext{GWP} pprox 28$ to $32$ times higher than $CO_2$ over 100 years.
    4. **Carbon Dioxide ($CO_2$):** Baseline reference ($	ext{GWP} = 1$).
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** Even though $SF_6$ has the HIGHEST GWP, $CO_2$ is responsible for the largest share of human-induced global warming because its emission VOLUME is millions of times greater!

### Question 238
**Subject:** Uttar Pradesh Special | **Topic:** Urbanization Demographics in UP (Census 2011)

Consider the following statements regarding urbanization in Uttar Pradesh:
1. In Census 2011, the urban population of Uttar Pradesh was 22.28%, while the rural population was 77.72%.
2. Ghaziabad is the most urbanized district of Uttar Pradesh by percentage of urban population (~67.55%).
3. Shravasti district has the lowest percentage of urban population in Uttar Pradesh (~3.46%).

Which of the statements given above are correct?

**Options:**
A. 1 and 2 only  
B. 2 and 3 only  
C. 1 and 3 only  
D. 1, 2 and 3  

**Answer:** D

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - All three statements are correct:
    - **Urban vs Rural Proportion:** Total UP population = 19.98 crore; Urban = **4.45 crore (22.28%)**; Rural = **15.53 crore (77.72%)**.
    - **Top 3 Urbanized Districts by %:**
      1. **Ghaziabad (67.55%)**
      2. Lucknow (66.21%)
      3. Kanpur Nagar (65.83%)
    - **Bottom 3 Urbanized Districts by %:**
      1. **Shravasti (3.46%)**
      2. Kushi Nagar (4.72%)
      3. Maharajganj (5.02%)
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** In terms of absolute numbers of urban population, Maharashtra ranks 1st in India, and Uttar Pradesh ranks **2nd in India**!

### Question 239
**Subject:** Environment & Ecology | **Topic:** Ramsar Wetland Superlatives of India

Match List-I (Ramsar Wetland Site) with List-II (State & Superlative) and select the correct answer:

**List-I (Site)**
A. Vembanad-Kol Wetland  
B. Renuka Lake  
C. Sundarban Wetland  
D. Keoladeo National Park  

**List-II (State & Superlative)**
1. Himachal Pradesh; Smallest Ramsar site in India (0.2 sq km)  
2. Kerala; Longest lake in India (spans over 96 km)  
3. Rajasthan; First Ramsar site of India (1981, with Chilika)  
4. West Bengal; Largest Ramsar site in India by surface area (4,230 sq km)  

**Options:**
A. A-2, B-1, C-4, D-3  
B. A-1, B-2, C-4, D-3  
C. A-2, B-4, C-1, D-3  
D. A-3, B-1, C-4, D-2  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - **Vembanad-Kol (Kerala):** Longest lake in India; Nehru Trophy Boat Race (*Vallam Kali*) held at Punnamada Lake.
  - **Renuka Lake (Sirmaur, HP):** Smallest designated wetland in India (~20 hectares / 0.2 sq km); freshwater lake with sacred natural history.
  - **Sundarban Wetland (WB):** Largest Ramsar site in India (~4,230 sq km); mangrove delta formed by Ganga-Brahmaputra-Meghna.
  - **Keoladeo Ghana (Bharatpur, Rajasthan):** Declared India's first Ramsar site along with Chilika Lake in October 1981; renowned bird paradise for wintering Siberian cranes.

### Question 240
**Subject:** Indian Economy | **Topic:** NITI Aayog SDG India Index

Consider the following statements regarding the SDG India Index (4th Edition, 2023–24) released by NITI Aayog:
1. Uttarakhand and Kerala emerged as the joint top-performing States with an overall composite score of 79.
2. Bihar recorded the lowest score among all states.
3. The index tracks progress across all 16 sustainable development goals using targeted socio-economic indicators.

Which of the statements given above are correct?

**Options:**
A. 1 and 2 only  
B. 2 and 3 only  
C. 1 and 3 only  
D. 1, 2 and 3  

**Answer:** D

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - All three statements are correct:
    - Released in July 2024 by NITI Aayog; tracks India's performance on Goals 1 to 16 (Goal 17 is qualitative and evaluated at the national level).
    - **Top States:** **Uttarakhand and Kerala** tied for the #1 rank with a score of 79, followed by Tamil Nadu (78) and Goa (77).
    - **Lowest State:** **Bihar (57)**, preceded by Jharkhand (62) and Nagaland (63).
    - **Top UT:** Chandigarh (score 77).
    - Uttar Pradesh moved into the "Front Runner" category (score 67).
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** SDG Index Performance bands: Aspirant (0–49), Performer (50–64), Front Runner (65–99), Achiever (100). No state has reached Achiever status yet!

### Question 241
**Subject:** Modern Indian History | **Topic:** Surat Split (1907) and Lucknow Reunion (1916)

Consider the following statements:
1. The 1907 Surat session of Congress was held on the banks of the Tapi river and presided over by Dr. Rashbehari Ghosh.
2. The split between Moderates and Extremists occurred primarily over the extension of the Swadeshi and Boycott movement beyond Bengal.
3. The reunion of Moderates and Extremists at the 1916 Lucknow session was facilitated largely through the efforts of Annie Besant and Bal Gangadhar Tilak.

Which of the statements given above are correct?

**Options:**
A. 1 and 2 only  
B. 2 and 3 only  
C. 1 and 3 only  
D. 1, 2 and 3  

**Answer:** D

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - All three statements are correct:
    - **1907 Surat:** Extremists wanted Tilak or Lala Lajpat Rai as president, and insisted on reaffirming the 1906 Calcutta resolutions (Swadeshi, Boycott, Swaraj, National Education). Moderates appointed Rashbehari Ghosh; meeting ended in hurling of shoes and police intervention.
    - **Ideological Rift:** Extremists insisted boycott must cover all British institutions across India; Moderates wanted boycott limited to foreign cloth and within Bengal.
    - **1916 Lucknow Reunion:** After Gokhale and Pherozeshah Mehta died in 1915, **Annie Besant and Tilak** brokered the readmission of Extremists into the Congress fold at Lucknow (presided by A.C. Mazumdar).
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** Do not confuse Dr. Rashbehari Ghosh (Moderate Congress president at Surat 1907 and Madras 1908) with Rash Behari Bose (Revolutionary who threw bomb at Lord Hardinge in 1912 and organized INA in Japan)!

### Question 242
**Subject:** Ancient Indian History | **Topic:** Ashokan Inscriptions and Pillars in Uttar Pradesh

Match List-I (Ashokan Edict / Pillar Site in UP) with List-II (District / Present Location) and select the correct answer:

**List-I (Site in UP)**
A. Ahraura Minor Rock Edict  
B. Topra Ashokan Pillar  
C. Meerut Ashokan Pillar  
D. Kaushambi Pillar (Allahabad-Kosam)  

**List-II (Location / Shifted To)**
1. Originally in Haryana/UP border; shifted to Feroz Shah Kotla, Delhi, by Firoz Shah Tughlaq  
2. Mirzapur district of Uttar Pradesh  
3. Shifted to Delhi Ridge near Bara Hindu Rao Hospital by Firoz Shah Tughlaq  
4. Shifted to Allahabad Fort by Mughal Emperor Akbar (contains Harishena's Prayag Prashasti)  

**Options:**
A. A-2, B-1, C-3, D-4  
B. A-1, B-2, C-3, D-4  
C. A-2, B-3, C-1, D-4  
D. A-3, B-1, C-4, D-2  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - **Ahraura:** Located in **Mirzapur** district (UP); Minor Rock Edict discovered in 1961 containing vital dates of Buddha's parinirvana.
  - **Topra Pillar:** Originally located in Topra Kalan (Yamunanagar, Haryana near Saharanpur border); transported on massive 42-wheel cart on Yamuna boats and installed at Feroz Shah Kotla, Delhi, by **Firoz Shah Tughlaq**. Contains all 7 Pillar Edicts.
  - **Meerut Pillar:** Transferred from **Meerut** to Delhi Ridge by Firoz Shah Tughlaq.

- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** Shams-i-Siraj Afif's *Tarikh-i-Firoz Shahi* gives a dramatic eyewitness description of how Firoz Shah Tughlaq transported the Topra and Meerut pillars to Delhi!

### Question 243
**Subject:** Geography of India | **Topic:** Major Coalfields of India

Match List-I (Coalfield) with List-II (State / River Valley Basin) and select the correct answer:

**List-I (Coalfield)**
A. Jharia  
B. Raniganj  
C. Singrauli  
D. Korba  

**List-II (Location / Basin)**
1. Damodar Valley, West Bengal; India's oldest coalfield (mining started 1774)  
2. Damodar Valley, Dhanbad (Jharkhand); premier prime coking coal repository  
3. Hasdeo River Basin, Chhattisgarh  
4. Son River Valley, Madhya Pradesh & Sonbhadra (Uttar Pradesh)  

**Options:**
A. A-2, B-1, C-4, D-3  
B. A-1, B-2, C-4, D-3  
C. A-2, B-4, C-1, D-3  
D. A-3, B-1, C-4, D-2  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - **Jharia (Jharkhand):** India's richest source of high-grade metallurgical coking coal; famous for deep subterranean coal fires.
  - **Raniganj (WB):** Oldest coalmine in India (commercial mining began in 1774 by Sumner and Heatly).
  - **Singrauli:** Spans Singrauli (MP) and Sonbhadra (UP); has the thickest coal seam in India (Jhingurdah seam ~132 m thick); feeds major thermal power plants.
  - **Korba (Chhattisgarh):** Hasdeo-Arand coal basin feeding NTPC Korba plant.

### Question 244
**Subject:** General Science (Chemistry) | **Topic:** Food Chemistry (Artificial Sweeteners & Preservatives)

Match List-I (Chemical Additive) with List-II (Description / Application) and select the correct answer:

**List-I (Additive)**
A. Aspartame  
B. Saccharin  
C. Sucralose  
D. Sodium Benzoate  

**List-II (Description / Application)**
1. Oldest non-nutritive artificial sweetener (550 times sweeter than cane sugar); excreted unchanged  
2. Unstable at cooking temperatures; limited to cold foods and soft drinks  
3. Trichloro derivative of sucrose; stable at baking/cooking temperatures  
4. Widely used chemical food preservative ($C_6H_5COONa$); prevents microbial growth in fruit juices  

**Options:**
A. A-2, B-1, C-3, D-4  
B. A-1, B-2, C-3, D-4  
C. A-2, B-3, C-1, D-4  
D. A-3, B-1, C-2, D-4  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - **Aspartame:** Methyl ester of phenylalanine/aspartic acid dipeptide; ~100 times sweeter than sucrose; decomposes at cooking temperatures, hence used strictly in cold soft drinks.
  - **Saccharin (o-sulfobenzimide):** Discovered in 1879; ~550 times sweeter than sugar; zero calories; harmlessly excreted in urine.
  - **Sucralose:** Trichlorosucrose; ~600 times sweeter than sugar; appearance and taste like sugar; high thermal stability (used in cooking).
  - **Sodium Benzoate:** Most common food preservative in tomato ketchups, squashes, and jams (metabolized into hippuric acid).
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** Alitame is an artificial sweetener that is 2,000 times sweeter than cane sugar! Aspartame cannot be consumed by patients suffering from Phenylketonuria (PKU).

2. The Asian Elephant (*Elephas maximus*) is declared the 'National Heritage Animal' of India.
3. The Terai Elephant Reserve (TER) notified in Uttar Pradesh spans over the Dudhwa Tiger Reserve and Pilibhit Tiger Reserve.

Which of the statements given above are correct?

A. 1 and 2 only  
B. 2 and 3 only  
C. 1 and 3 only

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - All three statements are correct:
    - **Project Elephant:** Launched in **February 1992** to protect elephant corridors, prevent human-elephant conflict, and ensure welfare of captive elephants.
    - **National Heritage Animal:** Asian Elephant declared National Heritage Animal of India in **2010** based on Elephant Task Force ("Gajah" report headed by Mahesh Rangarajan).
    - **Terai Elephant Reserve (TER):** Approved in October 2022 as India's 33rd Elephant Reserve (and 2nd in UP, after Shivalik Elephant Reserve). Spans ~3,049 sq km across **Dudhwa Tiger Reserve and Pilibhit Tiger Reserve** in Lakhimpur Kheri and Pilibhit districts.

### Question 247
**Subject:** Indian Economy | **Topic:** Currency Convertibility (Current vs Capital Account)

Consider the following statements regarding the convertibility of the Indian Rupee:
1. The Indian Rupee was made fully convertible on the Current Account in August 1994, accepting obligations under Article VIII of the IMF.
2. India currently maintains full (100%) Capital Account convertibility.
3. The S.S. Tarapore Committees (1997 and 2006) laid down preconditions for moving towards Full Capital Account Convertibility.

Which of the statements given above is/are correct?

**Options:**
A. 1 and 3 only  
B. 1 and 2 only  
C. 2 and 3 only  
D. 1, 2 and 3  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - Statements 1 and 3 are correct: In 1994, following the Liberalized Exchange Rate Management System (LERMS), India achieved **full Current Account Convertibility** (citizens/firms can freely convert currency for trade in goods/services and remittances). The RBI appointed the **Tarapore Committee on Capital Account Convertibility** (1997 & 2006), which recommended prerequisites: gross fiscal deficit below 3.5%, inflation 3–5%, and non-performing assets (NPAs) below 5%.
  - Statement 2 is incorrect: India has **PARTIAL (not full) Capital Account Convertibility**. While foreign investors and corporations enjoy broad freedom under automatic FDI/FPI routes, domestic residents face statutory ceilings (e.g., Liberalised Remittance Scheme - LRS cap of \$250,000 per financial year).
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** Remember: India has FULL Current Account Convertibility, but only PARTIAL Capital Account Convertibility!

B. 2 and 3 only  
C. 1 and 3 only  
D. 1, 2 and 3  

**Answer:** D

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - All three statements are correct:

### Question 249
**Subject:** Medieval Indian History | **Topic:** Mughal Provincial Administrative Hierarchy

Arrange the administrative divisions of the Mughal Empire in descending territorial hierarchy (from largest to smallest unit):
1. Sarkar (District)
2. Subah (Province)
3. Gram / Mauza (Village)
4. Pargana (Sub-district / Tehsil)

Select the correct answer from the codes given below:

**Options:**
A. 2 — 1 — 4 — 3  
B. 1 — 2 — 4 — 3  
C. 2 — 4 — 1 — 3  
D. 2 — 1 — 3 — 4  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - Akbar standardized the imperial provincial administration into a 4-tier hierarchy:
    1. **Subah (Province):** Headed by *Subedar / Sipahsalar* (executive/military) and *Diwan* (provincial finance/revenue, independent of Subedar).
    2. **Sarkar (District):** Headed by *Faujdar* (executive/law and order) and *Amalguzar* (chief revenue collector).
    3. **Pargana (Sub-district):** Headed by *Shiqdar* (executive) and *Amil* (revenue collector) and *Qanungo* (revenue records).
    4. **Gram / Mauza (Village):** Lowest unit; headed by *Muqaddam / Patel* (village headman) and *Patwari* (village accountant).
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** Akbar created **12 Subahs** in 1580; added 3 more in the Deccan (Berar, Khandesh, Ahmadnagar) totaling 15. Under Jahangir there were 17, under Shah Jahan 22, and under **Aurangzeb there were 21 Subahs** (maximum number)!

### Question 250
**Subject:** World Geography | **Topic:** Major World Plateaus & Geographic Features

Match List-I (Plateau of the World) with List-II (Country / Region & Superlative) and select the correct answer:

**List-I (Plateau)**
A. Tibetan Plateau  
B. Anatolian Plateau  
C. Colorado Plateau  
D. Kimberley Plateau  

**List-II (Location & Feature)**
1. Turkey; intermontane plateau between Pontic and Taurus mountains  
2. Northwestern Australia; rugged sandstone plateau rich in diamond reserves  
3. Highest and largest plateau on Earth ("Roof of the World", average elevation 4,500 m)  
4. Southwestern USA; dissected by Colorado river forming the Grand Canyon  

**Options:**
A. A-3, B-1, C-4, D-2  
B. A-1, B-3, C-4, D-2  
C. A-3, B-4, C-1, D-2  
D. A-2, B-1, C-4, D-3  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - **Tibetan Plateau:** Intermontane plateau bounded by the Himalayas to the south and Kunlun Mountains to the north; covers ~2.5 million sq km ("Third Pole of the Earth").
  - **Anatolian Plateau:** Heart of Asian Turkey; classic intermontane plateau between the Pontic Mountains (north) and Taurus Mountains (south).
  - **Colorado Plateau:** Famous for horizontal rock strata deeply incised by the Colorado River into the mile-deep **Grand Canyon**.
  - **Kimberley Plateau:** Located in northern Western Australia; famous for the Argyle diamond pipe.

### Question 251
**Subject:** Indian Polity | **Topic:** High-Yield Constitutional Amendments

Match List-I (Constitutional Amendment Act) with List-II (Core Reform) and select the correct answer:

A. 61st Amendment Act, 1988  
B. 69th Amendment Act, 1991  
C. 52nd Amendment Act, 1985  
D. 92nd Amendment Act, 2003  

**List-II (Core Reform)**
1. Disqualification on ground of defection; added Tenth Schedule  
2. Lowered voting age from 21 years to 18 years for Lok Sabha and Assemblies  
3. Added four languages (Bodo, Dogri, Maithili, and Santhali) to Eighth Schedule  
4. Accorded special status to Union Territory of Delhi as 'National Capital Territory of Delhi' (Article 239AA)  

**Options:**
A. A-2, B-4, C-1, D-3  
B. A-4, B-2, C-1, D-3  
C. A-2, B-1, C-4, D-3  
D. A-3, B-4, C-1, D-2  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - **61st CAA 1988 (effective March 1989):** Amended Article 326 to reduce the minimum voting age from **21 to 18 years** under Rajiv Gandhi.
  - **69th CAA 1991:** Created NCT of Delhi; established a 70-member Legislative Assembly and 7-member Council of Ministers (Article 239AA).
  - **52nd CAA 1985:** Anti-Defection Law; inserted Tenth Schedule.
  - **92nd CAA 2003:** Added **Bodo, Dogri, Maithili, Santhali** (BDMS) to the 8th Schedule, expanding the tally of constitutional languages to 22.

### Question 252
**Subject:** General Science (Biology) | **Topic:** Classification of Infectious Pathogens

Match List-I (Pathogenic Agent) with List-II (Structural Feature / Example Disease) and select the correct answer:

**List-I (Agent)**
A. Prions  
B. Viroids  
C. Retroviruses  
D. Bacteriophages  

**List-II (Feature / Disease)**
1. Infectious agent consisting ONLY of short, naked single-stranded RNA without protein coat; causes Potato Spindle Tuber disease  
2. Abnormally folded infectious proteins lacking any nucleic acid; causes Mad Cow Disease (BSE) and Creutzfeldt-Jakob Disease  
3. Viruses that possess RNA genome and use Reverse Transcriptase to form DNA (e.g. HIV)  
4. Specialized viruses that specifically infect and destroy bacteria  

**Options:**
A. A-2, B-1, C-3, D-4  
B. A-1, B-2, C-3, D-4  
C. A-2, B-3, C-1, D-4  
D. A-4, B-1, C-3, D-2  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - **Prions (Stanley Prusiner, Nobel Prize 1997):** Infectious protein particles with NO genetic material (no DNA/RNA); induce normal proteins to misfold into beta-pleated sheets; cause Bovine Spongiform Encephalopathy (Mad Cow) and Kuru.
  - **Viroids (T.O. Diener, 1971):** Smaller than viruses; consist solely of low molecular weight, free circular RNA with NO protein capsid.
  - **Retroviruses:** Contain single-stranded RNA; possess the enzyme *Reverse Transcriptase* that synthesizes DNA from RNA template (e.g., Human Immunodeficiency Virus - HIV).
  - **Bacteriophages:** Double-stranded DNA viruses with icosahedral head and tail that infect bacteria; abundant in holy Ganges water (responsible for its self-cleansing antibacterial properties).
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** Prions have PROTEIN only (NO nucleic acid); Viroids have RNA only (NO protein)!

---

### Question 253
**Subject:** Uttar Pradesh Special | **Topic:** Rainfall & Climatic Extremes in UP

Consider the following statements regarding the climate and rainfall of Uttar Pradesh:
1. Uttar Pradesh experiences a predominantly Subtropical Monsoon climate.
2. Gorakhpur district receives the highest average annual rainfall in the state (approx. 184 cm).
3. Mathura district receives the lowest average annual rainfall in the state (approx. 54 cm).

Which of the statements given above are correct?

**Options:**
A. 1 and 2 only  
B. 2 and 3 only  
C. 1 and 3 only  
D. 1, 2 and 3  

**Answer:** D

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - All three statements are correct:
    - **Climate:** Humid subtropical with dry winters (*Cwg* in Koppen classification); three seasons: Summer (March–June), Rainy (June–September), Winter (October–February).
    - **Rainfall Distribution:** Monsoon winds (Bay of Bengal branch / *Purvaiya*) enter through the east; rainfall decreases steadily from East to West and from North (Terai) to South (Bundelkhand).
    - **Highest Rainfall:** **Gorakhpur (~184.7 cm)** in eastern Terai.
    - **Lowest Rainfall:** **Mathura (~54.4 cm)** in western semi-arid border with Rajasthan.
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** Highest rainfall = **Gorakhpur**; Lowest rainfall = **Mathura**! Rainfall decreases from East to West and from North to South in UP.

### Question 254
**Subject:** Environment & Ecology | **Topic:** Ecological Succession

Consider the following statements regarding ecological succession:
1. Primary succession occurs on newly exposed surfaces where no soil or organic matter previously existed, such as cooled lava flows or bare rocks.
2. Secondary succession begins in areas where a natural community has been destroyed or disrupted, but the soil and organic substratum remain intact.
3. Lichens typically act as the 'pioneer species' in xerarch primary succession on bare rock.

Which of the statements given above are correct?

**Options:**
A. 1 and 2 only  
B. 2 and 3 only  
C. 1 and 3 only  
D. 1, 2 and 3  

**Answer:** D

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - All three statements are correct:
    - **Primary Succession:** Very slow process taking thousands of years; occurs on barren rock, sand dunes, newly submerged lands.
    - **Secondary Succession:** Much faster because soil, seeds, and rootstocks already exist (e.g., abandoned farmland, burnt forest).
    - **Pioneer Species in Xerarch Succession:** Crustose and foliose **Lichens** secrete organic acids to corrode rock minerals, creating a thin layer of soil that allows mosses, herbs, shrubs, and finally trees (*Climax Forest*) to establish.
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** The entire sequence of communities that successively change in a given area is called a **Sere**; individual transitional communities are called **Seral Stages**!

---

### Question 255
**Subject:** Indian Economy | **Topic:** Deficit Indicators and Zero Primary Deficit

Given below are two statements, one is labelled as Assertion (A) and the other as Reason (R):

**Assertion (A):** When the Primary Deficit of the Government of India is zero, the Fiscal Deficit becomes exactly equal to the interest payments on past debt.  
**Reason (R):** Primary Deficit is defined as Fiscal Deficit minus interest payments made on accumulated borrowings.

Select the correct answer from the codes given below:

**Options:**
A. Both (A) and (R) are true and (R) is the correct explanation of (A)  
B. Both (A) and (R) are true, but (R) is not the correct explanation of (A)  
C. (A) is true, but (R) is false  
D. (A) is false, but (R) is true  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - By definition:
    $$	ext{Primary Deficit} = 	ext{Fiscal Deficit} - 	ext{Interest Payments}$$
  - If $	ext{Primary Deficit} = 0$:
    $$0 = 	ext{Fiscal Deficit} - 	ext{Interest Payments} \implies 	ext{Fiscal Deficit} = 	ext{Interest Payments}$$
  - This indicates that the current year's fiscal operations are in balance, and the entire government borrowing is merely servicing the interest liability of historical loans!
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** Zero Primary Deficit does NOT mean zero fiscal deficit! It simply means new borrowings are equal to interest payments.

C. 1 and 3 only  
D. 1, 2 and 3

- **Core Fact & Solution:**

### Question 257
**Subject:** Current Affairs | **Topic:** Nobel Memorial Prize in Economic Sciences 2024

The Sveriges Riksbank Prize in Economic Sciences in Memory of Alfred Nobel 2024 was awarded to Daron Acemoglu, Simon Johnson, and James A. Robinson for what primary contribution?

**Options:**
A. For studies of how institutions are formed and affect prosperity  
B. For experimental approaches to alleviating global poverty  
C. For improvements to auction theory and inventions of new auction formats  
D. For empirical research on bank runs and financial crises  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - The 2024 Economics Nobel was awarded to **Daron Acemoglu (MIT), Simon Johnson (MIT), and James A. Robinson (University of Chicago)** (co-authors of the landmark book *Why Nations Fail*).
  - Awarded "for studies of how institutions are formed and affect prosperity":
    - Demonstrated that societies with poor rule of law and **"extractive institutions"** exploit populations and fail to generate sustained growth.
    - Societies that developed **"inclusive institutions"** guaranteeing property rights, democracy, and equality of opportunity achieved lasting prosperity.
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** Claudia Goldin won the Economics Nobel in 2023 for her work on women's labor market outcomes!

**Subject:** Ancient Indian History | **Topic:** Sangam Eco-Zones (Thinai System)

**List-II (Landscape & Activity)**
1. Pastoral / Grassland; cattle rearing and dairy farming

**Options:**
A. A-2, B-1, C-4, D-3  
B. A-1, B-2, C-4, D-3  
C. A-2, B-4, C-1, D-3  
D. A-3, B-1, C-4, D-2  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - The Sangam *Tolkappiyam* divides Tamilakam into 5 physiographic landscapes (*Ainthinai*):
    - **Kurinji (Hilly):** Presided by god Murugan (Seyon); hunting and honey gathering.
    - **Mullai (Pastoral/Forest):** Presided by god Mayon (Vishnu); cattle rearing and shifting cultivation.
    - **Marudham (Agricultural):** Presided by god Vendan (Indra); settled rice farming.
    - **Neydal (Coastal):** Presided by god Varunan; fishing and salt manufacturing.
    - **Palai (Arid/Desert):** Presided by goddess Korravai; highway robbery and plunder.
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** Kurinji = Hills; Mullai = Pastoral; Marudham = Plains; Neydal = Coast; Palai = Desert.

### Question 259
**Subject:** Geography of India | **Topic:** Major Lakes of Peninsular India

Match List-I (Peninsular Lake) with List-II (State & Geographical Characteristic) and select the correct answer:

**List-I (Lake)**
A. Kolleru Lake  
B. Pulicat Lake  
C. Lonar Lake  
D. Sasthamkotta Lake  

**List-II (State & Characteristic)**
1. Andhra Pradesh & Tamil Nadu border; second largest brackish water lagoon in India; separated by Sriharikota barrier island  
2. Andhra Pradesh; freshwater lake situated between the deltas of Godavari and Krishna rivers  
3. Kerala (Kollam); largest freshwater lake in Kerala ("Queen of Lakes")  
4. Maharashtra (Buldhana); hyper-saline soda lake formed by hypervelocity meteorite impact  

**Options:**
A. A-2, B-1, C-4, D-3  
B. A-1, B-2, C-4, D-3  
C. A-2, B-4, C-1, D-3  
D. A-3, B-1, C-4, D-2  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - **Kolleru Lake (AP):** Natural freshwater lake between Godavari and Krishna deltas; Ramsar site; home to migratory birds (Pelican sanctuary).
  - **Pulicat Lake:** Second largest lagoon in India (after Chilika); separated from Bay of Bengal by the barrier island of **Sriharikota** (Satish Dhawan Space Centre).

### Question 260
**Subject:** Indian Polity | **Topic:** Election Commission of India (Article 324 & 2023 Law)

Consider the following statements regarding the Election Commission of India (ECI):
1. Under Article 324, the superintendence, direction, and control of the preparation of electoral rolls and conduct of elections to Parliament, State Legislatures, and the offices of President and Vice-President are vested in the ECI.
2. The Chief Election Commissioner and other Election Commissioners (Appointment, Conditions of Service and Term of Office) Act, 2023 provides for a 3-member Selection Committee comprising the Prime Minister, a Union Cabinet Minister, and the Leader of Opposition / largest opposition party in Lok Sabha.
3. The Election Commission conducts elections to Panchayats and Municipalities in the States.

Which of the statements given above is/are correct?

**Options:**
A. 1 and 2 only  
B. 2 and 3 only  
C. 1 and 3 only  
D. 1, 2 and 3  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - Statements 1 and 2 are correct: ECI conducts elections to Parliament, State Assemblies/Councils, President, and Vice-President. Under the 2023 enactment, the selection committee consists of: Prime Minister (Chairperson), Leader of the Opposition in Lok Sabha, and a Union Cabinet Minister nominated by the PM (replacing the Chief Justice of India who was included under the earlier SC judgment in *Anoop Baranwal*).
  - Statement 3 is incorrect: Elections to local bodies (Panchayats and Municipalities) are conducted NOT by the ECI, but by the **State Election Commission (SEC)** established under Articles 243K and 243ZA!
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** Common confusion: ECI has NO jurisdiction over Panchayat or Municipal elections! Those are conducted strictly by State Election Commissions.
