### Question 261
**Subject:** General Science (Physics) | **Topic:** Nuclear Physics & Radioactive Emissions

Match List-I (Radioactive Emission) with List-II (Physical Nature & Properties) and select the correct answer:

**List-I (Emission)**
A. Alpha ($lpha$) Particles  
B. Beta ($eta^-$) Particles  
C. Gamma ($\gamma$) Rays  
D. Half-Life ($T_{1/2}$)  

**List-II (Nature & Property)**
1. High-energy electromagnetic radiation; highest penetration power; zero charge and zero mass  
2. Doubly charged Helium nuclei ($^4_2He^{2+}$); highest ionizing power; stopped by a single sheet of paper  
3. Time required for half of the radioactive nuclei in a sample to undergo decay ($T_{1/2} = rac{0.693}{\lambda}$)  
4. High-speed electrons ($e^-$); moderate ionizing and penetration power; stopped by thin aluminium sheet  

**Options:**
A. A-2, B-4, C-1, D-3  
B. A-1, B-2, C-4, D-3  
C. A-2, B-1, C-4, D-3  
D. A-3, B-4, C-1, D-2  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - **$lpha$-particles:** Identical to Helium nuclei ($^4_2He$); charge $+2e$, mass $4 	ext{ amu}$; **Highest ionizing power**, lowest penetration.
  - **$eta$-particles:** High-velocity electrons ($_{-1}^0e$); charge $-e$, negligible mass; intermediate penetration.
  - **$\gamma$-rays:** Electromagnetic photons of extremely short wavelength; uncharged; **Highest penetration power** (requires thick lead/concrete shields).
  - **Half-Life ($T_{1/2}$):** Characteristic constant for every radioactive isotope ($T_{1/2} = rac{\ln 2}{\lambda} pprox rac{0.693}{\lambda}$); independent of temperature, pressure, or physical state.
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** Penetration power order: **$\gamma > eta > lpha$**! Ionizing power order is the exact reverse: **$lpha > eta > \gamma$**!

### Question 263
**Subject:** Environment & Ecology | **Topic:** Protected Area Categories under Wildlife Protection Act

Consider the following statements regarding Protected Areas in India:
1. In a National Park, no human activities (including livestock grazing, timber harvesting, or private land ownership) are permitted.
2. In a Wildlife Sanctuary, certain limited human activities and traditional rights (like regulated cattle grazing and collection of minor forest produce) may be permitted by the Chief Wildlife Warden.
3. A National Park can be declared by an executive notification of the State Government, but its boundaries can be altered ONLY upon the recommendation of the National Board for Wildlife (NBWL).

Which of the statements given above are correct?

**Options:**
A. 1 and 2 only  
B. 2 and 3 only  
C. 1 and 3 only  
D. 1, 2 and 3  

**Answer:** D

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - All three statements are correct:
    - **National Parks (Section 35 of WPA 1972):** Enjoy the highest degree of protection; boundaries are fixed by legislation; complete ban on grazing, cultivation, and private rights.
    - **Wildlife Sanctuaries (Section 18/26A):** Species-oriented; regulated biotic interference and grazing may be permitted if not prejudicial to wildlife.
    - **Boundary Alteration:** Under the Supreme Court directives and Section 35(5) of WPA, no alteration of the boundaries of a National Park or Sanctuary can be made without the prior recommendation of the **National Board for Wildlife (chaired by the Prime Minister)**.
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** A Sanctuary can be upgraded into a National Park, but a National Park CANNOT be downgraded into a Sanctuary!

### Question 264
**Subject:** Indian Economy | **Topic:** Classical Cannons of Taxation

Adam Smith in his monumental work *The Wealth of Nations* (1776) laid down four fundamental 'Canons of Taxation'. Which of the following is NOT one of Adam Smith's four original canons?

**Options:**
A. Canon of Equality / Ability  
B. Canon of Certainty  
C. Canon of Convenience  
D. Canon of Elasticity  

**Answer:** D

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - Adam Smith enunciated **Four Classical Canons of Taxation**:
    1. **Canon of Equality / Ability:** Tax should be levied according to the taxpayer's ability to pay (progressive taxation).
    2. **Canon of Certainty:** Time of payment, manner of payment, and exact amount to be paid must be clear and plain to the contributor.
    3. **Canon of Convenience:** Every tax ought to be levied at the time or in the manner in which it is most convenient for the contributor to pay (e.g. TDS at time of salary credit).
    4. **Canon of Economy:** The cost of collecting the tax should be as small as possible relative to the revenue yield.
  - The Canon of Elasticity and Canon of Productivity were introduced later by modern economists like C.F. Bastable!
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** The four original canons are strictly: **Equality, Certainty, Convenience, and Economy**!

### Question 266
**Subject:** Medieval Indian History | **Topic:** Vijayanagara Nayankara & Ayagar Systems

Consider the following statements regarding the administration of the Vijayanagara Empire:
1. Under the 'Nayankara System', the king granted military chiefs called *Nayakas* territorial tracts called *Amaram* in exchange for maintaining a fixed contingent of troops and paying annual tribute.
2. The 'Ayagar System' was an organization of village administration where a body of twelve functionaries (*Ayagars*) managed village affairs on a hereditary basis.
3. The Vijayanagara gold coin was known as 'Varaha' (or Pagoda).

Which of the statements given above are correct?

**Options:**
A. 1 and 2 only  
B. 2 and 3 only  
C. 1 and 3 only  
D. 1, 2 and 3  

**Answer:** D

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - All three statements are correct:
    - **Nayankara System:** Distinctive feudal-military institution; *Amaranayakas* held land grants (*Amaram*) and remitted fixed revenue to the treasury while keeping standing military contingents ready for royal call.
    - **Ayagar System:** Every village was an autonomous administrative unit served by **12 functionaries (Ayagars)**, including the Karnam (accountant), Gauda (headman), Talayari (watchman), potter, blacksmith, etc., who were granted tax-free land (*Manyam*) for their upkeep.
    - **Currency:** Gold coins were called **Varaha** (weighing ~52 grains, carrying images of Vishnu's boar incarnation, Shiva-Parvati, etc.); European travelers called them *Pagodas*.
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** Krishnadeva Raya outlined his political philosophy in his Telugu masterpiece **Amuktamalyada**!

### Question 267
**Subject:** World Geography | **Topic:** Planetary Winds of the Southern Hemisphere

The maritime westerly winds that blow with furious strength between 40° S and 60° S latitudes in the Southern Hemisphere are popularly termed:
1. "Roaring Forties" between 40° S and 50° S
2. "Furious Fifties" at 50° S
3. "Shrieking (Screaming) Sixties" at 60° S

Which of the statements given above are correct?

**Options:**
A. 1 and 2 only  
B. 2 and 3 only  
C. 1 and 3 only  
D. 1, 2 and 3  

**Answer:** D

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - All three descriptions are correct:
    - In the Southern Hemisphere, the vast uninterrupted expanse of open oceans provides almost zero frictional resistance from landmasses.
    - Consequently, the **Westerlies** blow with ferocious, persistent velocity throughout the year:
      - **Roaring Forties:** 40° to 50° South latitude.
      - **Furious Fifties:** 50° South latitude.
      - **Shrieking / Screaming Sixties:** 60° South latitude.
    - Sailors historically utilized these winds on the "Clipper route" sailing from Europe to Australia.
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** These terms apply ONLY to the **Southern Hemisphere**, where ocean dominates land!

### Question 268
**Subject:** Indian Polity | **Topic:** Inter-State Council (Article 263)

Consider the following statements regarding the Inter-State Council:
1. The Inter-State Council is established under Article 263 of the Constitution by the President of India.
2. It was first constituted in 1990 on the recommendations of the Sarkaria Commission on Centre-State Relations.
3. The Prime Minister of India serves as the Chairman of the Inter-State Council.

Which of the statements given above are correct?

**Options:**
A. 1 and 2 only  
B. 2 and 3 only  
C. 1 and 3 only  
D. 1, 2 and 3  

**Answer:** D

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - All three statements are correct:
    - **Constitutional Provision:** Article 263 empowers the President to establish an Inter-State Council if public interest would be served.
    - **Establishment:** Set up on May 28, 1990, by Presidential order following the landmark recommendations of the **Sarkaria Commission (1988)**.
    - **Composition:**
      - **Chairman:** Prime Minister of India.
      - **Members:** Chief Ministers of all States, CMs of UTs having assemblies, Administrators of UTs without assemblies, and 6 Union Cabinet Ministers nominated by the PM.
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** The Inter-State Council is a CONSTITUTIONAL body (Article 263), whereas the Zonal Councils are STATUTORY bodies established under the States Reorganisation Act, 1956!

### Question 269
**Subject:** General Science (Biology) | **Topic:** Human Excretory System & Nephron Physiology

Consider the following statements regarding the human kidney:
1. The Nephron is the structural and functional microscopic filtration unit of the kidney.
2. Glomerular Ultrafiltration occurs under high hydrostatic pressure across the podocytes of Bowman's capsule.
3. The Loop of Henle is primarily responsible for the concentration of urine via a counter-current multiplier mechanism.

Which of the statements given above are correct?

**Options:**
A. 1 and 2 only  
B. 2 and 3 only  
C. 1 and 3 only  
D. 1, 2 and 3  

**Answer:** D

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - All three statements are correct:
    - Each kidney contains ~1 million **Nephrons**.
    - **Ultrafiltration:** Occurs in the Malpighian body (Glomerulus + Bowman's Capsule); blood is filtered through 3 layers into the capsular space; GFR (Glomerular Filtration Rate) is ~125 mL/min or **180 litres per day**!
    - **Reabsorption:** Over 99% of the filtrate is reabsorbed along the proximal convoluted tubule (PCT) and Loop of Henle (only ~1.5 litres of concentrated urine is excreted daily).
    - **Loop of Henle & Vasa Recta:** Counter-current mechanism maintains a hyperosmotic medullary gradient, enabling production of concentrated urine.
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** The primary site of complete reabsorption of glucose and amino acids is the **Proximal Convoluted Tubule (PCT)**!

### Question 271
**Subject:** Environment & Ecology | **Topic:** Carbon Sequestration & Types of Carbon

Match List-I (Carbon Classification) with List-II (Ecological Reservoir / Source) and select the correct answer:

**List-I (Carbon Type)**
A. Blue Carbon  
B. Green Carbon  
C. Black Carbon  
D. Brown Carbon  

**List-II (Reservoir / Source)**
1. Carbon sequestered by terrestrial ecosystems, forests, and soils through photosynthesis  
2. Carbon stored in coastal and marine ecosystems (mangroves, tidal salt marshes, seagrasses)  
3. Light-absorbing organic carbon formed by incomplete combustion of biomass (wood smoke, smoldering fires)  
4. Soot particles produced by incomplete combustion of fossil fuels, biofuels, and diesel engines  

**Options:**
A. A-2, B-1, C-4, D-3  
B. A-1, B-2, C-4, D-3  
C. A-2, B-4, C-1, D-3  
D. A-3, B-1, C-4, D-2  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - **Blue Carbon:** Carbon captured by the world's coastal and ocean ecosystems (mangroves, marshes, seagrasses sequester carbon up to 10 times faster per hectare than terrestrial tropical forests).
  - **Green Carbon:** Carbon incorporated into plant biomass and soils in terrestrial forests and agricultural ecosystems.
  - **Black Carbon (Soot):** Pure carbonaceous aerosol produced by combustion of diesel and biomass; strong short-lived climate pollutant that deposits on snow and accelerates glacier melt.
  - **Brown Carbon:** Light-absorbing brown organic carbon aerosols from wood combustion and wildfires.
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** Blue Carbon ecosystems sequester carbon permanently in sediments for thousands of years without saturating!

### Question 272
**Subject:** Modern Indian History | **Topic:** Cabinet Mission Plan (1946)

Consider the following statements regarding the Cabinet Mission Plan (May 1946):
1. It explicitly rejected the demand for a sovereign, independent Pakistan.
2. It proposed a three-tiered administrative structure with an All-India Union controlling Foreign Affairs, Defence, and Communications.
3. It proposed grouping provincial assemblies into three sections: Section A (Hindu-majority provinces), Section B (Muslim-majority northwestern provinces), and Section C (Muslim-majority northeastern provinces).

Which of the statements given above are correct?

**Options:**
A. 1 and 2 only  
B. 2 and 3 only  
C. 1 and 3 only  
D. 1, 2 and 3  

**Answer:** D

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - All three statements are correct:
    - Sent in March 1946 (Lord Pethick-Lawrence, Stafford Cripps, A.V. Alexander).
    - **Rejection of Pakistan:** Concluded a separate sovereign Pakistan would be economically unviable and create enormous minority problems.
    - **Union Government:** Limited only to Defence, External Affairs, and Communications; residual powers rested with provinces.
    - **Grouping of Provinces:**
      - *Section A:* Madras, Bombay, UP, Bihar, Central Provinces, Orissa.
      - *Section B:* Punjab, NWFP, Sindh.
      - *Section C:* Bengal and Assam.
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** The Constituent Assembly of India was formed in November 1946 under the scheme formulated by the **Cabinet Mission Plan**!

### Question 273
**Subject:** Indian Economy | **Topic:** Industrial Policy Resolution of 1956

The Industrial Policy Resolution (IPR) of 1956, often described as the "Economic Constitution of India", classified Indian industries into how many categories?

**Options:**
A. Two  
B. Three  
C. Four  
D. Five  

**Answer:** B

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - IPR 1956 formed the basis of the Second Five-Year Plan (Mahalanobis model) and divided all industries into **Three distinct Schedules**:
    1. **Schedule A (17 industries):** Reserved exclusively for the State (atomic energy, defense equipment, heavy iron & steel, heavy plant machinery, coal, railways, aircraft).
    2. **Schedule B (12 industries):** Mixed sector where State would progressively expand, but private enterprise would supplement State efforts (fertilizers, synthetic rubber, chemical pulp, road transport).
    3. **Schedule C (Remaining industries):** Left to the private sector, but regulated through industrial licensing (*License Raj*) under the Industries (Development and Regulation) Act, 1951.
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** IPR 1956 laid down the foundation of the "Socialistic Pattern of Society" adopted at the 1955 Avadi session of Congress!

### Question 275
**Subject:** Ancient Indian History | **Topic:** Harappan Trade and Raw Material Sources

Match List-I (Raw Material Imported by Harappans) with List-II (Source Region) and select the correct answer:

**List-I (Raw Material)**
A. Copper  
B. Lapis Lazuli  
C. Tin  
D. Gold  

**List-II (Source Region)**
1. Khetri mines (Rajasthan) and Oman (Magan)  
2. Shortughai / Badakhshan (Afghanistan)  
3. Afghanistan and Iran  
4. Kolar (Karnataka) and Afghanistan  

**Options:**
A. A-1, B-2, C-3, D-4  
B. A-2, B-1, C-3, D-4  
C. A-1, B-3, C-2, D-4  
D. A-4, B-2, C-3, D-1  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - **Copper:** Sourced locally from **Khetri mines** in Rajasthan; also imported from **Oman (Magan)** in the Arabian peninsula (chemical analysis shows traces of nickel in both Harappan and Omani copper artifacts).
  - **Lapis Lazuli:** Deep-blue metamorphic gemstone obtained from the Harappan trading colony of **Shortughai** in Badakhshan, northeast Afghanistan.
  - **Tin:** Scarce in India; imported from **Afghanistan and Iran** to alloy with copper to manufacture bronze.
  - **Gold:** Sourced from **Kolar** mines in Karnataka and river sands of northern Afghanistan.
  - **Steatite:** Sourced from Rajasthan and Gujarat.
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** Mesopotamian cuneiform texts refer to the Harappan region as **Meluhha**, Oman as **Magan**, and Bahrain as **Dilmun**!

4. Garhwal Himalayas; Chamoli district, Uttarakhand (7,817 m)  

**Options:**
A. A-2, B-1, C-4, D-3  
B. A-1, B-2, C-4, D-3  
C. A-2, B-4, C-1, D-3  
D. A-3, B-1, C-4, D-2  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - **K2 (Godwin Austen):** Second highest peak on Earth (8,611 m); located in the **Karakoram range** in Gilgit-Baltistan (India claims as highest peak in Indian territory).
  - **Kanchenjunga:** Third highest peak in the world (8,586 m); **highest peak located within undisputed Indian territory** (Sikkim).
  - **Nanda Devi:** Highest peak located *entirely within* a single Indian state (7,817 m, Chamoli, Uttarakhand); ringed by a sanctuary wall.
  - **Namcha Barwa:** Eastern terminus of the Great Himalayas around which the Brahmaputra (Tsangpo) river bends sharply in a hairpin turn (*Syntaxial Bend*).
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** Kanchenjunga is in the GREAT HIMALAYAS; K2 is in the KARAKORAM RANGE (Trans-Himalayas)!

### Question 278
**Subject:** General Science (Chemistry) | **Topic:** Synthetic Polymers and Fibers

Match List-I (Synthetic Fiber / Polymer) with List-II (Chemical Nature & Distinct Use) and select the correct answer:

**List-I (Fiber / Polymer)**
A. Nylon-6,6  
B. Rayon  
C. Kevlar  
D. Dacron (Terylene)  

**List-II (Nature & Use)**
1. Regenerated semi-synthetic cellulose fiber ("Artificial Silk")  
2. Fully synthetic polyamide fiber prepared from adipic acid and hexamethylenediamine  
3. Aromatic polyamide (aramid); exceptionally high tensile strength; bulletproof jackets  
4. Polyester fiber formed from ethylene glycol and terephthalic acid; crease-resistant fabrics  

**Options:**
A. A-2, B-1, C-3, D-4  
B. A-1, B-2, C-3, D-4  
C. A-2, B-3, C-1, D-4  
D. A-4, B-1, C-3, D-2  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - **Nylon-6,6:** First fully synthetic commercial fiber; condensation polymer of hexamethylenediamine and adipic acid; high tensile strength, used in bristles, ropes, parachutes.
  - **Rayon:** Regenerated cellulose derived from wood pulp chemically treated with carbon disulfide ($CS_2$); known as **Artificial Silk**.
  - **Kevlar (poly-para-phenylene terephthalamide):** Heat-resistant aramid fiber 5 times stronger than steel on an equal weight basis; used in bulletproof vests, helmets, and composite armor.
  - **Dacron / Terylene:** Common polyester prepared by condensation of ethylene glycol and terephthalic acid.
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** Rayon is a SEMI-SYNTHETIC fiber (because its raw material is natural wood cellulose), whereas Nylon is a FULLY SYNTHETIC fiber!

B. Schedule II  
C. Schedule III  
D. Schedule IV

1. Plant species that are prohibited from being picked, uprooted, or acquired  
2. Animal species that enjoy the highest level of absolute protection (highest penalties for poaching)  
3. Animal species subjected to lesser degree of protection than Schedule I  
4. Specimens of flora and fauna listed in the Appendices of CITES

A. A-2, B-3, C-1, D-4  
B. A-3, B-2, C-1, D-4  
C. A-2, B-1, C-4, D-3  
D. A-1, B-2, C-3, D-4  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - Under the Wildlife (Protection) Amendment Act 2022 (effective from April 2023), the schedules were rationalized from 6 to **4**:

    - **Schedule II:** Animal species with lesser degree of protection.
    - **Schedule III:** Protected plant species (e.g. Beddomes' cycad, Blue Vanda, Pitcher plant).
    - **Schedule IV:** Species scheduled under CITES (Convention on International Trade in Endangered Species).

C. 1 and 3 only  
D. 1, 2 and 3  

**Answer:** D

    - **Battle of Jalalabad Hill (22 April 1930):** Revolutionaries fought heroically against British troops.
    - **Women Martyrs:**

      2. RBI Deputy Governor in charge of monetary policy
      3. One officer of the RBI nominated by the Central Board

### Question 284
**Subject:** Medieval Indian History | **Topic:** Dynasties of Delhi Sultanate Chronology

Arrange the five dynasties of the Delhi Sultanate in chronological sequence of their rule:
1. Khalji Dynasty
2. Mamluk (Slave) Dynasty
3. Lodi Dynasty
4. Sayyid Dynasty
5. Tughlaq Dynasty

Select the correct answer from the codes given below:

**Options:**
A. 2 — 1 — 5 — 4 — 3  
B. 1 — 2 — 5 — 4 — 3  
C. 2 — 5 — 1 — 4 — 3  
D. 2 — 1 — 4 — 5 — 3  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - The Delhi Sultanate lasted from 1206 to 1526 CE across 5 successive dynasties:
    1. **Mamluk / Ilbari / Slave Dynasty (1206–1290 CE):** Founded by Qutbuddin Aibak (84 years).
    2. **Khalji Dynasty (1290–1320 CE):** Founded by Jalaluddin Khalji (30 years; shortest ruling dynasty).
    3. **Tughlaq Dynasty (1320–1414 CE):** Founded by Ghiyasuddin Tughlaq (94 years; longest ruling dynasty).
    4. **Sayyid Dynasty (1414–1451 CE):** Founded by Khizr Khan (37 years).
    5. **Lodi Dynasty (1451–1526 CE):** Founded by Bahlul Lodi; first Afghan dynasty (75 years); ended with Babur defeating Ibrahim Lodi at 1st Panipat.
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** Longest ruling dynasty = **Tughlaqs (94 years)**; Shortest ruling dynasty = **Khaljis (30 years)**! Mnemonic: **S-K-T-S-L** (Slave, Khalji, Tughlaq, Sayyid, Lodi).

### Question 285
**Subject:** World Geography | **Topic:** International Date Line (IDL)

Consider the following statements regarding the International Date Line (IDL):
1. The International Date Line roughly corresponds to the 180° meridian of longitude passing through the Pacific Ocean.
2. A traveler crossing the Date Line from West to East GAINS a day (repeats the same calendar date).
3. The International Date Line is not a straight line, but zig-zags to avoid dividing land territories and island groups (such as the Aleutian Islands and Kiribati).

Which of the statements given above are correct?

**Options:**
A. 1 and 2 only  
B. 2 and 3 only  
C. 1 and 3 only  
D. 1, 2 and 3  

**Answer:** D

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - All three statements are correct:
    - **Course:** Passes through the central Pacific Ocean along 180° longitude; established at the 1884 International Meridian Conference.
    - **Zig-zag Deviations:** Deviates east at the Bering Strait to keep all of Siberia in one date; bends west around the Aleutian Islands to keep them in Alaska's date; bends east around Kiribati (Line Islands).
    - **Crossing the IDL:**
      - **West to East (Asia to America):** GAIN a day (e.g., if you cross on Monday, it becomes Sunday on the other side).
      - **East to West (America to Asia):** LOSE a day (e.g., Sunday becomes Monday).
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** Crossing West to East = GAIN a day (go back in calendar date); Crossing East to West = LOSE a day (advance by one calendar date)!

### Question 286
**Subject:** Indian Polity | **Topic:** Writ Jurisdiction Comparison (Article 32 vs Article 226)

Given below are two statements, one is labelled as Assertion (A) and the other as Reason (R):

**Assertion (A):** The writ jurisdiction of a High Court under Article 226 is wider than that of the Supreme Court under Article 32.  
**Reason (R):** While the Supreme Court can issue writs only for the enforcement of Fundamental Rights, a High Court can issue writs for the enforcement of Fundamental Rights as well as for "any other ordinary legal purpose".

Select the correct answer from the codes given below:

**Options:**
A. Both (A) and (R) are true and (R) is the correct explanation of (A)  
B. Both (A) and (R) are true, but (R) is not the correct explanation of (A)  
C. (A) is true, but (R) is false  
D. (A) is false, but (R) is true  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - Assertion is true: Article 226 provides a broader scope of writ jurisdiction than Article 32.
  - Reason is true:
    - **Supreme Court (Article 32):** Can issue writs ONLY for violations of Part III (Fundamental Rights). It CANNOT issue writs for ordinary legal rights. Moreover, Article 32 is itself a Fundamental Right!
    - **High Court (Article 226):** Can issue writs for Fundamental Rights AND for "any other purpose" (ordinary legal/statutory rights).
  - However, the Supreme Court's territorial reach covers the entire territory of India, whereas a High Court's jurisdiction is limited to its state (or where the cause of action arises).
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** The remedy under Article 32 is a GUARANTEED Fundamental Right (SC cannot refuse remedy); whereas remedy under Article 226 is discretionary!

### Question 287
**Subject:** General Science (Physics) | **Topic:** Mechanical Properties of Solids (Elasticity)

Consider the following statements regarding elasticity and Hooke's Law:
1. Hooke's Law states that within the elastic limit, stress is directly proportional to strain ($	ext{Stress} = E 	imes 	ext{Strain}$).
2. Young's Modulus of elasticity ($Y$) applies to tensile/compressive deformation of solids along length.
3. Steel is more elastic than rubber because steel requires a much greater deforming force to produce the same strain.

Which of the statements given above are correct?

**Options:**
A. 1 and 2 only  
B. 2 and 3 only  
C. 1 and 3 only  
D. 1, 2 and 3  

**Answer:** D

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - All three statements are correct:
    - **Hooke's Law:** Formulated by Robert Hooke in 1676; ratio of stress to strain is constant (Modulus of Elasticity).
    - **Young's Modulus ($Y = rac{F/A}{\Delta L/L}$):** Measures resistance of a material to longitudinal stretching.
    - **Steel vs Rubber:** In everyday language, people think rubber is more elastic because it stretches easily. But scientifically, **Elasticity is the property by virtue of which a body resists deformation and restores its original shape**. Since steel requires an enormous stress to produce even a minute strain ($Y_{	ext{steel}} \gg Y_{	ext{rubber}}$), **Steel is far more elastic than rubber**!
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** Common UPPSC science question: "Which is more elastic: Steel or Rubber?" The scientific answer is unequivocally **STEEL**!

### Question 289
**Subject:** Environment & Ecology | **Topic:** Plastic Waste Management Rules & EPR

2. The minimum permissible thickness of plastic carry bags was mandated at 120 microns with effect from December 31, 2022.
3. Extended Producer Responsibility (EPR) mandates producers, importers, and brand owners (PIBOs) to collect and recycle the plastic packaging they introduce into the market.

Which of the statements given above are correct?

**Options:**
A. 1 and 2 only  
B. 2 and 3 only  
C. 1 and 3 only  
D. 1, 2 and 3  

**Answer:** D

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - All three statements are correct:
    - **Single-Use Plastic Ban:** Effective July 1, 2022, under the Plastic Waste Management Amendment Rules, banning 19 low-utility, high-littering SUP items (plastic earbuds, balloon sticks, candy sticks, thermocol plates, cups, cutlery, wrapping films).
    - **Thickness Standards:** Raised to 75 microns in September 2021, and further to **120 microns** from December 31, 2022 (to promote reuse and facilitate mechanical recycling).
    - **EPR Framework:** Legally binding targets under 4 categories (Rigid, Flexible single-layer, Multilayered, Compostable plastic); overseen via centralized CPCB online portal.

### Question 291
**Subject:** Modern Indian History | **Topic:** Non-Cooperation Movement (1920–1922)

1. The resolution for Non-Cooperation was initially approved at the Special Calcutta Session in September 1920, and formally ratified at the Nagpur Session in December 1920.
2. At the Nagpur Session, C.R. Das moved the main resolution on Non-Cooperation.
3. Mahatma Gandhi abruptly suspended the movement on February 12, 1922, following the Chauri Chaura incident in Gorakhpur district.

Which of the statements given above are correct?

**Options:**
A. 1 and 2 only  
B. 2 and 3 only  
C. 1 and 3 only  
D. 1, 2 and 3  

**Answer:** D

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - All three statements are correct:
    - **Calcutta Special Session (Sept 1920):** Presided by Lala Lajpat Rai; Gandhi's non-cooperation program passed despite initial hesitation of C.R. Das and B.C. Pal.
    - **Nagpur Session (Dec 1920):** Presided by C. Vijayaraghavachariar; historic reconciliation where **C.R. Das himself moved the main non-cooperation resolution**; Congress goal changed to attainment of *Swaraj* through peaceful and legitimate means.
    - **Chauri Chaura Incident (4 February 1922):** An angry peasant mob surrounded and set ablaze the police station in Chauri Chaura (Gorakhpur, UP), burning 22 policemen alive. Gandhi withdrew the movement at the Congress Working Committee meeting at Bardoli on **February 12, 1922** (*Bardoli Resolution*).
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** UP government officially corrected the historical date of the Chauri Chaura incident to **February 4, 1922** (earlier written as Feb 5)!

### Question 292
**Subject:** Ancient Indian History | **Topic:** Sequence of Foreign Invasions in Ancient India

3. Kushans
4. Sakas (Scythians)

Select the correct answer from the codes given below:

A. 2 — 1 — 4 — 3  
B. 1 — 2 — 4 — 3  
C. 2 — 4 — 1 — 3  
D. 2 — 1 — 3 — 4  

**Answer:** A

- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - Chronological ladder of ancient foreign incursions:
    1. **Persian Invasions (c. 518 BCE):** Achaemenid Emperor Darius I conquered Punjab/Sindh.
    2. **Alexander the Great (326 BCE):** Crossed the Indus; fought Porus at the Battle of the Hydaspes (Jhelum).
    3. **Indo-Greeks (c. 180 BCE):** First to issue gold coins bearing portrait heads and bilingual legends; King Menander (Milinda).
    4. **Sakas / Scythians (c. 1st Century BCE):** Replaced Greeks; established five branches (Taxila, Mathura, Ujjain/Western Kshatrapas); King Rudradaman I.
    5. **Parthians / Pahlavas (c. 1st Century CE):** King Gondophares (visited by St. Thomas).
    6. **Kushans (c. 1st Century CE):** Yuezhi nomads; Kujula Kadphises -> Vima Kadphises -> Kanishka I (78 CE Saka Era).
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** Sequence mnemonic: **G-S-P-K** (Greeks -> Sakas -> Parthians -> Kushans)!

### Question 294
**Subject:** Indian Polity | **Topic:** Office and Powers of the Lok Sabha Speaker

2. The Speaker does not vote in the first instance, but exercises a casting vote in the case of an equality of votes under Article 100(1).
3. Under Article 110(3), the decision of the Speaker as to whether a Bill is a Money Bill or not is final.
4. When the Lok Sabha is dissolved, the Speaker immediately vacates his office.

Which of the statements given above are correct?

**Options:**
A. 1, 2 and 3 only  
B. 1, 3 and 4 only  
C. 2, 3 and 4 only  
D. 1, 2, 3 and 4  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - Statements 1, 2, and 3 are correct: Speaker conducts business; votes ONLY in case of a tie (*Casting Vote* under Article 100); certification of Money Bills is final and non-challengeable in ordinary course; presides over Joint Sittings (Article 108).
  - Statement 4 is incorrect: Under the second proviso to **Article 94**, whenever the Lok Sabha is dissolved, **the Speaker DOES NOT vacate his office immediately**! The Speaker remains in office until immediately before the first meeting of the newly elected Lok Sabha.
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** The Speaker submits his resignation letter to the **Deputy Speaker** of the Lok Sabha (NOT to the President)!

### Question 295
**Subject:** General Science (Biology) | **Topic:** Blood Coagulation Mechanism

Arrange the sequential biochemical events involved in blood clotting (hemostasis) in chronological order:
1. Conversion of soluble Fibrinogen into insoluble Fibrin mesh by Thrombin
2. Release of Thromboplastin from injured tissues and activated platelets
3. Conversion of inactive Prothrombin into active Thrombin in the presence of Calcium ions ($Ca^{2+}$) and Thrombokinase
4. Entanglement of blood cells in the fibrin network forming a stable clot

Select the correct answer from the codes given below:

**Options:**
A. 2 — 3 — 1 — 4  
B. 3 — 2 — 1 — 4  
C. 2 — 1 — 3 — 4  
D. 1 — 2 — 3 — 4  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - Enzyme cascade mechanism of blood clotting:
    1. Injured tissues and lysed platelets release **Thromboplastin / Thrombokinase**.
    2. Thrombokinase, in the presence of **Calcium ($Ca^{2+}$)** ions, catalyzes the conversion of inactive plasma protein **Prothrombin into active Thrombin**.
    3. Thrombin then enzymatically converts soluble **Fibrinogen into insoluble thread-like Fibrin** monomers.
    4. Fibrin threads polymerize into a dense network trapping red blood cells and platelets, forming a **coagulum / clot**.
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** **Calcium ions ($Ca^{2+}$)** are essential for blood clotting (Factor IV). Heparin is the natural anticoagulant produced by liver/basophils that prevents clotting inside intact blood vessels!

**Subject:** Uttar Pradesh Special | **Topic:** Premier Universities of Uttar Pradesh

Match List-I (Historic University in UP) with List-II (Year of Establishment & Founder) and select the correct answer:

**List-I (University)**
A. Banaras Hindu University (BHU)  
B. Aligarh Muslim University (AMU)  
C. University of Allahabad  
D. Babasaheb Bhimrao Ambedkar University (BBAU)  

**List-II (Year & Key Association)**

2. Established 1916 by Pandit Madan Mohan Malaviya; Central University  
3. Established 1996 in Lucknow; Central University dedicated to Babasaheb Ambedkar  
4. Established 1920 (evolved from MAO College founded 1875 by Sir Syed Ahmad Khan)  

**Options:**
A. A-2, B-4, C-1, D-3  
B. A-1, B-2, C-4, D-3  
C. A-2, B-1, C-4, D-3  
D. A-3, B-4, C-1, D-2  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - **BHU (Varanasi, 1916):** Founded by Pandit Madan Mohan Malaviya and Dr. Annie Besant (evolved from Central Hindu College 1898); established under the BHU Act, 1915.
  - **AMU (Aligarh, 1920):** Founded as Muhammadan Anglo-Oriental (MAO) College in 1875 by Sir Syed Ahmad Khan; elevated to university status in 1920.

### Question 297
**Subject:** Environment & Ecology | **Topic:** Population Ecology & Carrying Capacity

Consider the following statements regarding population growth models:
1. In the exponential growth model (J-shaped curve), population grows unchecked when resources (food and space) are unlimited ($dN/dt = rN$).
2. In the logistic growth model (Verhulst-Pearl Logistic curve), population growth levels off as it approaches the Carrying Capacity ($K$), forming an S-shaped (sigmoid) curve.
3. The logistic growth rate is represented by the differential equation: $rac{dN}{dt} = rN \left(rac{K - N}{K}
ight)$.

Which of the statements given above are correct?

**Options:**
A. 1 and 2 only  
B. 2 and 3 only  
C. 1 and 3 only  
D. 1, 2 and 3  

**Answer:** D

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - All three statements are correct:
    - **Exponential Growth (J-curve):** Occurs in unrealistic conditions of infinite resources; growth accelerates indefinitely until crash.
    - **Verhulst-Pearl Logistic Growth (Sigmoid / S-curve):** Realistic model representing natural ecosystems where resources are finite; exhibits:
      - *Lag phase* (initial slow adaptation).
      - *Log / Exponential phase* (rapid acceleration).
      - *Deceleration phase* (environmental resistance increases).
      - *Asymptote* (population reaches stationary equilibrium at **Carrying Capacity $K$**).
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** When $N = K$ (population size equals carrying capacity), $rac{K - N}{K} = 0$, meaning population growth rate $rac{dN}{dt}$ drops to **ZERO**!

### Question 298
**Subject:** Indian Economy | **Topic:** Revised MSME Classification Criteria (2020)

Match List-I (MSME Enterprise Category) with List-II (Composite Criteria: Investment & Annual Turnover) and select the correct answer:

**List-I (Enterprise)**
A. Micro Enterprise  
B. Small Enterprise  
C. Medium Enterprise  

**List-II (Investment in Plant & Machinery AND Turnover Limits)**
1. Investment $\le 	ext{₹10 Crore}$ AND Annual Turnover $\le 	ext{₹50 Crore}$  
2. Investment $\le 	ext{₹1 Crore}$ AND Annual Turnover $\le 	ext{₹5 Crore}$  
3. Investment $\le 	ext{₹50 Crore}$ AND Annual Turnover $\le 	ext{₹250 Crore}$  

**Options:**
A. A-2, B-1, C-3  
B. A-1, B-2, C-3  
C. A-2, B-3, C-1  
D. A-3, B-1, C-2  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - Effective July 1, 2020 (under Aatmanirbhar Bharat Abhiyan), the distinction between manufacturing and services was abolished, adopting a **composite criterion of Investment and Turnover**:
    - **Micro:** Investment $\le ₹1 	ext{ Crore}$ AND Turnover $\le ₹5 	ext{ Crore}$ (1:5 ratio).
    - **Small:** Investment $\le ₹10 	ext{ Crore}$ AND Turnover $\le ₹50 	ext{ Crore}$.
    - **Medium:** Investment $\le ₹50 	ext{ Crore}$ AND Turnover $\le ₹250 	ext{ Crore}$.
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** Export turnover is COMPLETELY EXCLUDED while calculating the annual turnover threshold for all MSMEs!

### Question 300
**Subject:** Modern Indian History | **Topic:** Cripps Mission (1942) Provisions

Consider the following proposals made by the Cripps Mission in March 1942:
1. Setting up of an Indian Union with Dominion status after the cessation of World War II.
2. Creation of a Constituent Assembly consisting of members partly elected by provincial assemblies through proportional representation and partly nominated by princely states.
3. Any province not willing to accept the new constitution was given the right to frame a separate constitution and form a separate union.

Which of the proposals given above were part of the Cripps Mission offer?

**Options:**
A. 1 and 2 only  
B. 2 and 3 only  
C. 1 and 3 only  
D. 1, 2 and 3  

**Answer:** D

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - All three proposals were part of the Cripps Offer (March 1942):
    - **Dominion Status:** Offered after the war with freedom to withdraw from the Commonwealth.
    - **Constituent Assembly:** Solely Indian body to draft constitution after the war.
    - **Right to Secede:** The notorious "local option" allowing any province or princely state to opt out and sign a separate treaty with the British Crown (which Congress saw as a blueprint for the balkanization of India).
    - Mahatma Gandhi famously rejected it as a *"post-dated cheque on a crashing bank"*.
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** Jawaharlal Nehru and Maulana Abul Kalam Azad were the official negotiators appointed by the Congress Working Committee to talk with Sir Stafford Cripps!

### Question 301
**Subject:** Modern Indian History | **Topic:** Dandi March & Civil Disobedience Movement (1930)

Consider the following statements regarding the historic Dandi March:
1. Mahatma Gandhi began the march from Sabarmati Ashram on March 12, 1930, accompanied by 78 chosen ashram followers.
2. The march covered a distance of approximately 241 miles (385 km) over 24 days, reaching Dandi on April 5, 1930.
3. On the morning of April 6, 1930, Gandhi broke the Salt Law by picking up a lump of natural salt, formally inaugurating the Civil Disobedience Movement.

Which of the statements given above are correct?

**Options:**
A. 1 and 2 only  
B. 2 and 3 only  
C. 1 and 3 only  
D. 1, 2 and 3  

**Answer:** D

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - All three statements are correct:
    - **March Launch:** Started March 12, 1930, from Sabarmati Ashram with 78 volunteers; arrived at coastal Dandi (Navsari district, Gujarat) on April 5; broke the salt monopoly on **April 6, 1930**.
    - **American Journalist:** Webb Miller of United Press famously documented the heroic non-violent protest at Dharasana Salt Works (where Sarojini Naidu, Imam Saheb, and Manilal Gandhi led satyagrahis against brutal police lathi charges).
    - **Regional Parallels:**
      - Tamil Nadu: C. Rajagopalachari marched from Trichinopoly to Vedaranyam (*Vedaranyam Salt March*).
      - Malabar (Kerala): K. Kelappan ("Kerala Gandhi") marched from Calicut to Payyanur.
      - Assam: Satyagrahis marched from Sylhet to Noakhali.
      - NWFP: Khan Abdul Ghaffar Khan ("Frontier Gandhi") led the *Khudai Khidmatgars* (Red Shirts).
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** Sarojini Naidu accompanied Gandhi at Dandi and famously hailed him: *"Hail, Deliverer!"*

### Question 302
**Subject:** Indian Polity | **Topic:** Pardoning Powers: President (Article 72) vs Governor (Article 161)

Consider the following statements comparing the pardoning powers of the President and the Governor:
1. The President can grant pardon, reprieves, respites, or remissions of punishment in all cases where the sentence is by a Court Martial (Military Court), whereas the Governor has no such power.
2. The President is the exclusive constitutional authority empowered to grant pardon in cases of death sentence; the Governor CANNOT pardon a death sentence.
3. The Governor can suspend, remit, or commute a sentence of death under Article 161.

Which of the statements given above are correct?

**Options:**
A. 1 and 2 only  
B. 2 and 3 only  
C. 1 and 3 only  
D. 1, 2 and 3  

**Answer:** D

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - All three statements are correct:
    - **Court Martial:** President alone possesses the power to pardon court-martial sentences (Article 72(1)(a)). Governor has zero power regarding military courts.
    - **Death Sentence:** Under Article 72, the **President alone can PARDON a death sentence** (completely wiping out guilt and sentence).
    - **Governor's Power regarding Death Sentence:** Under Article 161, the Governor CANNOT pardon a death sentence, BUT the Governor **CAN suspend, remit, or commute** a death sentence under state law!
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** Landmark ruling *Maru Ram (1980)* and *Kehar Singh (1989)*: The pardoning power under Articles 72 and 161 is exercised by the President/Governor on the **binding advice of the Council of Ministers**, not at personal discretion!

### Question 303
**Subject:** Geography of India | **Topic:** Brahmaputra River System & Tributaries

Match List-I (Tributary of Brahmaputra) with List-II (Bank Orientation & Origin / Feature) and select the correct answer:

**List-I (Tributary)**
A. Subansiri  
B. Manas  
C. Lohit  
D. Teesta  

**List-II (Orientation & Feature)**
1. Left-bank tributary; originates in eastern Tibet; passes through Mishmi Hills; known as "River of Blood"  
2. Right-bank tributary; originates in Kangchenjunga range/Pahunri glacier (Sikkim); joins Brahmaputra in Bangladesh  
3. Right-bank tributary; antecedent river originating in Tibet; largest tributary of Brahmaputra ("Gold River")  
4. Right-bank transboundary river originating in Bhutan; flows through Manas National Park  

**Options:**
A. A-3, B-4, C-1, D-2  
B. A-1, B-4, C-3, D-2  
C. A-3, B-2, C-1, D-4  
D. A-4, B-3, C-1, D-2  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - **Subansiri ("Gold River"):** Largest tributary of Brahmaputra; antecedent river from Tibet; joins from the **Right bank**.
  - **Manas:** Originates in Bhutan; joins Brahmaputra from the **Right bank** at Jogighopa.
  - **Lohit:** Originates in Tibet (Zayal Chu); flows through Arunachal; joins Dibang and Dihang at Sadiya from the **Left bank**; carries red laterite silt ("River of Blood"); Dhola-Sadiya Bridge (Bhupen Hazarika Setu, 9.15 km) is built on it.
  - **Teesta:** Originates from Tso Lhamo lake / Pahunri glacier in Sikkim; historically flowed into Ganga, but switched eastward into Brahmaputra (Jamuna) after the 1787 mega-floods.
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** Left-bank tributaries: **Dibang, Lohit, Dhansiri, Burhi Dihing, Kalang**. Right-bank tributaries: **Subansiri, Kameng (Jia Bhoreli), Manas, Sankosh, Teesta**!

### Question 304
**Subject:** General Science (Biology & Physics) | **Topic:** Pathologies of the Human Eye

Match List-I (Eye Disorder) with List-II (Pathological Cause / Nature) and select the correct answer:

**List-I (Disorder)**
A. Presbyopia  
B. Astigmatism  
C. Cataract  
D. Glaucoma  

**List-II (Pathology)**
1. Progressive clouding/opacity of the crystalline eye lens, blocking light from reaching the retina  
2. Loss of elasticity of the eye lens and gradual weakening of ciliary muscles with aging  
3. Increased intraocular fluid pressure (aqueous humor) causing irreversible damage to the optic nerve  
4. Aspherical / uneven curvature of the cornea or lens, causing light to focus at multiple points  

**Options:**
A. A-2, B-4, C-1, D-3  
B. A-4, B-2, C-1, D-3  
C. A-2, B-1, C-4, D-3  
D. A-3, B-4, C-1, D-2  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - **Presbyopia:** Aging defect (usually after age 40); ciliary muscles lose contractility; near-point recedes; corrected with **Bifocal lenses**.
  - **Astigmatism:** Cornea is curved like a rugby ball rather than a spherical football; horizontal and vertical rays focus at different planes; corrected with **Cylindrical lenses**.
  - **Cataract (Motiyabind):** Age-related denaturing of lens proteins (*crystallins*); lens becomes cloudy/opaque; treated surgically via phacoemulsification and Intraocular Lens (IOL) implantation.
  - **Glaucoma (Kala Motia):** Obstruction in the drainage canal (Canal of Schlemm) leads to elevated Intraocular Pressure (IOP > 21 mm Hg), permanently damaging optic nerve axons.
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** Myopia uses Concave lens; Hypermetropia uses Convex lens; Presbyopia uses Bifocal lens; Astigmatism uses Cylindrical lens!

### Question 305
**Subject:** Uttar Pradesh Special | **Topic:** Literacy Demographics of UP (Census 2011)

Consider the following statements regarding literacy rates in Uttar Pradesh:
1. As per Census 2011, the overall literacy rate of Uttar Pradesh is 67.72%.
2. The male literacy rate in Uttar Pradesh is 77.28%, while the female literacy rate is 57.18%.
3. The male-female literacy gap in Uttar Pradesh stands at approximately 20.10 percentage points.

Which of the statements given above are correct?

**Options:**
A. 1 and 2 only  
B. 2 and 3 only  
C. 1 and 3 only  
D. 1, 2 and 3  

**Answer:** D

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - All three statements are correct:
    - **Overall UP Literacy:** **67.72%** (Male: 77.28%, Female: 57.18%).
    - **National Average Comparison:** India's literacy in 2011 was 73.0% (Male: 80.9%, Female: 64.6%). UP ranks lower than national average on all three counts.
    - **Gender Gap:** The gap between male and female literacy in UP is **20.10%** (Maharajganj has the widest gender gap in literacy).
    - **Highest Literacy:** Gautam Buddha Nagar (80.12%); Lowest: Shravasti (46.74%).
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** Mnemonic for UP Literacy: **Overall ~67%, Male (+10%) ~77%, Female (-10%) ~57%**!

### Question 306
**Subject:** Environment & Ecology | **Topic:** Kyoto Protocol (1997) & Market Mechanisms

Consider the following statements regarding the Kyoto Protocol:
1. The Kyoto Protocol was adopted in 1997 and entered into force in February 2005, committing Annex I industrialized nations to legally binding greenhouse gas reduction targets.
2. It established three flexible market-based mechanisms: Clean Development Mechanism (CDM), Joint Implementation (JI), and International Emissions Trading (IET).
3. Under the Clean Development Mechanism (CDM), an Annex I developed country can earn certified emission reduction (CER) credits by investing in emission-reduction projects in non-Annex I developing nations like India.

Which of the statements given above are correct?

**Options:**
A. 1 and 2 only  
B. 2 and 3 only  
C. 1 and 3 only  
D. 1, 2 and 3  

**Answer:** D

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - All three statements are correct:
    - **Adoption & Status:** Adopted at COP3 in Kyoto, Japan (1997); came into force on 16 February 2005 after Russian ratification; operationalized the UNFCCC under the principle of "Common But Differentiated Responsibilities" (CBDR).
    - **Target GHGs (6 gases):** Carbon Dioxide ($CO_2$), Methane ($CH_4$), Nitrous Oxide ($N_2O$), Hydrofluorocarbons (HFCs), Perfluorocarbons (PFCs), Sulphur Hexafluoride ($SF_6$). (Nitrogen Trifluoride $NF_3$ added in Doha Amendment).
    - **3 Market Mechanisms:**
      - **CDM (Article 12):** Projects in developing countries earning Certified Emission Reductions (CERs).
      - **Joint Implementation (Article 6):** Projects between Annex I developed countries.
      - **International Emissions Trading (Article 17):** Buying/selling excess assigned amount units (AAUs) ("Carbon Credits").
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** Developing countries (like India and China) had **NO legally binding emission reduction commitments** under the Kyoto Protocol!

---

### Question 307
**Subject:** Indian Economy | **Topic:** Taxation Principles & Gini Coefficient

Match List-I (Tax Structure) with List-II (Definition / Impact on Income Inequality) and select the correct answer:

**List-I (Tax Structure)**
A. Progressive Tax  
B. Regressive Tax  
C. Proportional Tax  
D. Degressive Tax  

**List-II (Definition & Impact)**
1. Tax rate remains constant regardless of the taxpayer's income level  
2. Tax rate increases as the income increases, reducing the post-tax Gini coefficient (e.g. Personal Income Tax)  
3. Tax rate increases up to a certain maximum income limit, after which it remains flat / uniform  
4. Effective tax burden falls more heavily on lower-income earners than on the wealthy (e.g. Indirect taxes on essential goods)  

**Options:**
A. A-2, B-4, C-1, D-3  
B. A-1, B-2, C-4, D-3  
C. A-2, B-1, C-4, D-3  
D. A-3, B-4, C-1, D-2  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - **Progressive Tax:** Marginal tax rate increases as taxable base increases; redistributive; narrows the rich-poor gap (reduces Gini coefficient).
  - **Regressive Tax:** Poor people spend a higher proportion of their income on consumed goods; thus indirect consumption taxes (like GST or VAT) place a disproportionately higher percentage burden on lower-income earners.
  - **Proportional Tax (Flat Tax):** Rate remains constant across all income brackets.
  - **Degressive Tax:** Blend of progressive and proportional; rate rises progressively up to a threshold income, and thereafter remains fixed at that upper rate.
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** Direct taxes (income/corporate tax) are generally PROGRESSIVE; Indirect taxes (excise/GST) are inherently REGRESSIVE!

---

### Question 308
**Subject:** Current Affairs | **Topic:** Global Liveability Index 2024

Consider the following statements regarding the Global Liveability Index 2024 published by the Economist Intelligence Unit (EIU):
1. Vienna (Austria) retained its position as the world's most liveable city for the third consecutive year.
2. Damascus (Syria) was ranked as the least liveable city in the world.
3. The index evaluates 173 cities across five broad categories: Stability, Healthcare, Culture & Environment, Education, and Infrastructure.

Which of the statements given above are correct?

**Options:**
A. 1 and 2 only  
B. 2 and 3 only  
C. 1 and 3 only  
D. 1, 2 and 3  

**Answer:** D

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - All three statements are correct:
    - **1st Rank:** **Vienna, Austria** scored near-perfect 98.4 (perfect 100 in stability, healthcare, education, infrastructure), followed by Copenhagen (Denmark), Zurich (Switzerland), and Melbourne (Australia).
    - **Bottom Rank:** **Damascus, Syria** ranked 173rd (score 30.7), followed by Tripoli (Libya) and Algiers (Algeria).
    - **Indian Cities:** New Delhi and Mumbai tied at **140th rank**; Bengaluru ranked 146th.
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** Published by the **Economist Intelligence Unit (EIU)**, NOT Mercer or UN-Habitat! (Mercer publishes the Quality of Living City Ranking).

### Question 309
**Subject:** Ancient Indian History | **Topic:** Mauryan Rock-Cut Architecture (Barabar Caves)

Consider the following statements regarding the Barabar and Nagarjuni rock-cut caves:
1. The Barabar caves located in Jehanabad district of Bihar are the oldest surviving rock-cut cave monuments in India, dating to the Mauryan period.
2. Emperor Ashoka dedicated the caves at Barabar (such as Sudama Cave, Karna Chaupar, and Lomas Rishi) for the use of the monks of the Ajivika sect.
3. Ashoka's grandson Dasharatha dedicated the adjacent Nagarjuni caves (such as Gopika cave) to the Ajivikas.

Which of the statements given above are correct?

**Options:**
A. 1 and 2 only  
B. 2 and 3 only  
C. 1 and 3 only  
D. 1, 2 and 3  

**Answer:** D

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - All three statements are correct:
    - **Barabar Caves (3rd Century BCE):** Monolithic caves carved out of solid granite rock; famous for mirror-like interior glass polish (*Mauryan Polish*).
    - **Ajivika Dedication:** Inscriptions inside Sudama and Karna Chaupar explicitly state King Piyadasi (Ashoka) dedicated them to the **Ajivika sect** (founded by Makkhali Gosala, propounding *Niyati* / fatalism).
    - **Lomas Rishi Cave:** Famous for its carved horseshoe-shaped chaitya entrance facade depicting a frieze of elephants bowing before stupas.
    - **Nagarjuni Caves:** Inscriptions verify they were excavated by Ashoka's grandson **Dasharatha Maurya** for Ajivika ascetics.
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** Even though Ashoka was a devoted Buddhist, he patronized the AJIVIKA sect by carving caves for them at Barabar, demonstrating genuine religious tolerance (as preached in Major Rock Edict XII)!

### Question 310
**Subject:** World Geography | **Topic:** Local Cold Winds of the World

Match List-I (Local Cold Wind) with List-II (Region / Country) and select the correct answer:

**List-I (Cold Wind)**
A. Bora  
B. Mistral  
C. Pampero  
D. Blizzard  

**List-II (Region)**
1. Rhone Valley (France) and Gulf of Lion  
2. Adriatic Coast (Croatia and Italy)  
3. North American tundra and prairies (USA / Canada)  
4. Pampas of Argentina and Uruguay  

**Options:**
A. A-2, B-1, C-4, D-3  
B. A-1, B-2, C-4, D-3  
C. A-2, B-4, C-1, D-3  
D. A-3, B-1, C-4, D-2  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - **Bora:** Fierce, gusty, cold, dry northeasterly katabatic wind blowing from the Dinaric Alps down to the **Adriatic Sea coast**.
  - **Mistral:** Cold, dry northwesterly wind that channels down the **Rhone Valley in southern France** towards the Mediterranean during winter/spring.
  - **Pampero:** Violent, cold polar wind from the south that sweeps across the **Pampas of Argentina and Uruguay**.
  - **Blizzard:** Severe cold, snow-laden wind associated with intense polar high pressure systems across **Canada and northern USA**.
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** *Buran* is a cold blizzard wind blowing over Siberia/Russia; *Khamsin* is a hot, dusty wind in Egypt; *Loo* is a hot dry summer wind in northern India!

### Question 311
**Subject:** Indian Polity | **Topic:** Money Bills (Article 110)

Consider the following statements regarding a Money Bill in the Indian Parliament:
1. Under Article 110, a Bill is deemed to be a Money Bill if it contains ONLY provisions dealing with taxation, borrowings, custody of the Consolidated Fund of India, or matters incidental thereto.
2. A Money Bill can be introduced ONLY in the Lok Sabha, and ONLY on the prior recommendation of the President of India.
3. The Rajya Sabha has no power to amend or reject a Money Bill; it can only make recommendations, which the Lok Sabha may accept or reject, within a mandatory period of 14 days.
4. If a question arises whether a Bill is a Money Bill or not, the decision of the Speaker of the Lok Sabha thereon is final.

Which of the statements given above are correct?

**Options:**
A. 1, 2 and 3 only  
B. 2, 3 and 4 only  
C. 1, 3 and 4 only  
D. 1, 2, 3 and 4  

**Answer:** D

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - All four statements are correct:
    - **Definition (Article 110(1)):** Enlists 7 specific fiscal matters (imposition, abolition, remission, alteration, regulation of any tax; government borrowings; custody of Consolidated or Contingency Fund).
    - **Introduction (Article 109 & 117(1)):** Originates ONLY in Lok Sabha with President's recommendation. Cannot be introduced in Rajya Sabha.
    - **Rajya Sabha Role:** RS cannot amend or reject; has strictly **14 days** to return the bill. If not returned in 14 days, it is deemed passed by both Houses.
    - **Speaker's Endorsement:** Speaker signs a certificate when transmitting it to RS and President.
    - **Joint Sitting:** There is **NO provision for a Joint Sitting (Article 108)** for a Money Bill!
    - **President's Assent (Article 111):** President may give assent or withhold assent, but **CANNOT return a Money Bill** for reconsideration.
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** Joint sitting is available ONLY for Ordinary Bills and Financial Bills (Category A/B), NEVER for Money Bills or Constitution Amendment Bills!

### Question 312
**Subject:** Medieval Indian History | **Topic:** Major Battles of the Sultanate & Early Mughals

Match List-I (Battle) with List-II (Year & Belligerents) and select the correct answer:

**List-I (Battle)**
A. Battle of Chandawar  
B. First Battle of Panipat  
C. Battle of Chanderi  
D. Battle of Ghaghra  

**List-II (Year & Belligerents)**
1. 1526; Babur defeated Ibrahim Lodi, ending Delhi Sultanate  
2. 1194; Muhammad Ghori defeated Jaichand, King of Kannauj (Gahadavala dynasty)  
3. 1529; Babur defeated the joint Afghan forces under Mahmud Lodi and Nusrat Shah of Bengal  
4. 1528; Babur defeated Medini Rai, ruler of Malwa  

**Options:**
A. A-2, B-1, C-4, D-3  
B. A-1, B-2, C-4, D-3  
C. A-2, B-4, C-1, D-3  
D. A-3, B-1, C-4, D-2  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - **Battle of Chandawar (1194):** Fought on Yamuna river near Firozabad; Muhammad Ghori defeated **Raja Jaichand of Kannauj**; established Turkish rule across the Gangetic plain.
  - **First Battle of Panipat (21 April 1526):** Babur introduced firearms, gunpowder artillery, and *Tulghuma* flanking tactics to defeat Ibrahim Lodi.
  - **Battle of Chanderi (1528):** Babur defeated Rajput chieftain **Medini Rai** of Chanderi.
  - **Battle of Ghaghra (May 1529):** Babur defeated allied Afghan forces of Bihar and Nusrat Shah of Bengal; was Babur's last battle.
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** Babur's 4 consecutive annual victories: **1526 Panipat -> 1527 Khanwa -> 1528 Chanderi -> 1529 Ghaghra**! (Babur died at Agra in December 1530).

### Question 313
**Subject:** General Science (Chemistry) | **Topic:** Hydrated Inorganic Salts (Vitriols)

Match List-I (Common Name) with List-II (Chemical Formula & Metal) and select the correct answer:

**List-I (Salt)**
A. Blue Vitriol  
B. Green Vitriol  
C. White Vitriol  
D. Epsom Salt  

**List-II (Formula)**
1. Ferrous Sulphate Heptahydrate ($FeSO_4 \cdot 7H_2O$)  
2. Copper Sulphate Pentahydrate ($CuSO_4 \cdot 5H_2O$)  
3. Magnesium Sulphate Heptahydrate ($MgSO_4 \cdot 7H_2O$)  
4. Zinc Sulphate Heptahydrate ($ZnSO_4 \cdot 7H_2O$)  

**Options:**
A. A-2, B-1, C-4, D-3  
B. A-1, B-2, C-4, D-3  
C. A-2, B-4, C-1, D-3  
D. A-3, B-1, C-4, D-2  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - **Blue Vitriol (Neela Thotha):** $CuSO_4 \cdot 5H_2O$ (Copper Sulphate Pentahydrate); fungicide; used in Bordeaux mixture with slaked lime.
  - **Green Vitriol (Hara Kasis):** $FeSO_4 \cdot 7H_2O$ (Ferrous Sulphate Heptahydrate); used in iron supplements and dyes.
  - **White Vitriol:** $ZnSO_4 \cdot 7H_2O$ (Zinc Sulphate Heptahydrate); used in eye lotions and skin ointments.
  - **Epsom Salt:** $MgSO_4 \cdot 7H_2O$ (Magnesium Sulphate Heptahydrate); used as a saline purgative/laxative.
  - **Oil of Vitriol:** Concentrated Sulphuric Acid ($H_2SO_4$).
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** Blue Vitriol = **Copper**; Green Vitriol = **Iron**; White Vitriol = **Zinc**; Epsom Salt = **Magnesium**!

### Question 314
**Subject:** Uttar Pradesh Special | **Topic:** Folk Dances and Cultural Traditions

Consider the following statements regarding traditional folk arts of Uttar Pradesh:

2. 'Kajari' is a classical-folk monsoon song originating from the Mirzapur-Varanasi region.
3. 'Alha' singing in Bundelkhand narrates the chivalrous exploits of the 12th-century Banafar warrior heroes Alha and Udal.

Which of the statements given above are correct?

**Options:**
A. 1 and 2 only  
B. 2 and 3 only  
C. 1 and 3 only  
D. 1, 2 and 3  

**Answer:** D

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - All three statements are correct:
    - **Charkula Dance:** Practiced in Braj (Mathura/Vrindavan) on the third day after Holi (Radha's birthday); dancers veil their faces and carry 108 oil lamps arranged on 4-5 circular wooden tiers (*charkula*).
    - **Kajari:** Sung during the rainy season (Shravana); portrays the agony of separation from the beloved; two forms: *Dharau* (sung at home by women) and *Akhade ki Kajari* (sung competitively on stage).
    - **Alha:** Composed originally in Bundeli/Bhojpuri by court poet Jagnik (*Alha-Khand* / *Parmal Raso*); recounts 52 epic battles of generals Alha and Udal.

### Question 315
**Subject:** Environment & Ecology | **Topic:** Food Chains & Trophic Energy Flow

Consider the following statements regarding Grazing Food Chains (GFC) and Detritus Food Chains (DFC):
1. In a Grazing Food Chain, the primary energy source is solar radiation captured by living green plants (producers).
2. In a Detritus Food Chain, the initial energy source is dead organic matter (detritus) decomposed by microorganisms (fungi and bacteria).
3. In terrestrial ecosystems, a much larger fraction of energy flows through the Detritus Food Chain than through the Grazing Food Chain.

Which of the statements given above are correct?

**Options:**
A. 1 and 2 only  
B. 2 and 3 only  
C. 1 and 3 only  
D. 1, 2 and 3  

**Answer:** D

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - All three statements are correct:
    - **GFC:** Living Plants $
ightarrow$ Herbivores $
ightarrow$ Primary Carnivores $
ightarrow$ Top Carnivores. In aquatic ecosystems (oceans/lakes), GFC is the major conduit for energy flow.
    - **DFC:** Dead Plant/Animal Matter $
ightarrow$ Decomposers (bacteria/fungi) / Detritivores (earthworms, millipedes). Decomposers secrete extracellular digestive enzymes to break down organic matter into inorganic nutrients (*Mineralization*).
    - **Terrestrial Energy Flow:** In forests and grasslands, only ~10% of plant biomass is consumed while green by herbivores; the remaining **~90% dies and enters the Detritus Food Chain**, making DFC the dominant pathway for energy and nutrient cycling on land.
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** In Aquatic ecosystems, GFC is dominant; in Terrestrial ecosystems, DFC is dominant!

2. Gambling and Betting (including casinos)
3. Atomic Energy generation
4. Cigars, cheroots, cigarillos, and cigarettes manufacturing

A. 1, 2 and 3 only  
B. 1, 2 and 4 only  
C. 2, 3 and 4 only  
D. 1, 2, 3 and 4  

**Answer:** D

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - Under Government of India regulations, FDI is strictly **PROHIBITED in 9 specific sectors**:
    1. Lottery business (government/private/online)
    2. Gambling and betting (including casinos)
    3. Chit funds
    4. Nidhi company
    5. Trading in Transferable Development Rights (TDRs)

    8. Activities / sectors not open to private sector investment (e.g., **Atomic Energy**)

### Question 318
**Subject:** Modern Indian History | **Topic:** Administration of Lord Ripon (1880–1884)

Consider the following statements regarding Viceroy Lord Ripon:
1. He is known as the "Father of Local Self-Government in India" due to his landmark Resolution of May 1882.
2. He repealed Lord Lytton's repressive Vernacular Press Act in 1882.
3. The First Factory Act of 1881, which regulated child labor in mechanized factories, was enacted during his tenure.
4. The Ilbert Bill controversy (1883) erupted during his viceroyalty over granting Indian magistrates parity to try Europeans in criminal cases.

Which of the statements given above are correct?

**Options:**
A. 1, 2 and 3 only  
B. 2, 3 and 4 only  
C. 1, 3 and 4 only  
D. 1, 2, 3 and 4  

**Answer:** D

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - All four statements are correct:
    - Lord Ripon (appointed by Gladstone's Liberal government) was hailed by Florence Nightingale as the *"Saviour of India"*:
      - **Resolution of 1882:** Laid down the framework of sub-divisional, district, and municipal local self-governing boards with elected non-official majorities.
      - **Repeal of Vernacular Press Act (1882):** Restored press equality between Indian and Anglo-Indian newspapers.
      - **Factory Act of 1881:** Banned employment of children under 7; capped working hours of children aged 7–12 at 9 hours/day.
      - **Ilbert Bill (1883):** Drafted by Law Member Sir Courtenay Ilbert; violent racist agitation by European settlers (*White Mutiny*) forced the government to amend it, allowing Europeans to demand trial by a jury with at least 50% Europeans.
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** First official Decennial Census of India was conducted under Lord Ripon in **1881**! (Lord Mayo had initiated the first non-synchronous census in 1872).

### Question 319
**Subject:** Geography of India | **Topic:** Physiographic Plateaus of India

Match List-I (Plateau Region) with List-II (Distinct Geographical Feature) and select the correct answer:

**List-I (Plateau)**
A. Malwa Plateau  
B. Chota Nagpur Plateau  
C. Meghalaya (Shillong) Plateau  
D. Deccan Trap  

**List-II (Feature)**
1. Separated from Rajmahal Hills by Malda Gap; receives world's highest rainfall  
2. Step-like volcanic basaltic lava landscape; black cotton soil (Regur)  
3. Dissected sandstone plateau drained by Chambal; famous for badland ravines  
4. Known as the "Ruhr of India"; richest mineral belt containing Gondwana coal  

**Options:**
A. A-3, B-4, C-1, D-2  
B. A-1, B-4, C-3, D-2  
C. A-3, B-2, C-1, D-4  
D. A-4, B-3, C-1, D-2  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - **Malwa Plateau:** Lava plateau bounded by Aravallis to the west and Vindhyas to the south; drained by Chambal, Sindh, Betwa, and Ken; heavily gullied into **Chambal Badlands / Ravines**.
  - **Chota Nagpur Plateau:** "Mineral Heartland / Ruhr of India"; rich in iron, coal (Jharia, Bokaro), mica, and bauxite; drained by Damodar, Subarnarekha, and Barakar rivers.
  - **Meghalaya (Shillong) Plateau:** An eastward geological extension of the Peninsular shield (NOT the Himalayas!); severed from Chota Nagpur by the **Garo-Rajmahal Gap (Malda Gap)**; houses Garo, Khasi, and Jaintia hills.
  - **Deccan Trap:** Cretaceous volcanic fissure basaltic eruptions covering Maharashtra, parts of Gujarat and MP; weathering gives rich black soil.
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** The Shillong (Meghalaya) plateau is structurally part of the **PENINSULAR DECCAN SHIELD**, not the Himalayan orogen!

### Question 321
**Subject:** Ancient Indian History | **Topic:** Vedic Literature & Social Organization

Consider the following statements regarding the Vedic literature:

2. The Gayatri Mantra is addressed to the solar deity Savitr and is composed in the Third Mandala of the Rigveda by sage Vishvamitra.
3. The 'Sabha' and 'Samiti' were described as the two twin daughters of Prajapati in the Atharvaveda.

Which of the statements given above are correct?

**Options:**
A. 1 and 2 only  
B. 2 and 3 only  
C. 1 and 3 only  
D. 1, 2 and 3  

**Answer:** D

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - All three statements are correct:
    - **Purusha Sukta (Rigveda Mandala X, Hymn 90):** Originates the four Varnas from the cosmic giant *Purusha* (Brahmins from mouth, Kshatriyas from arms, Vaishyas from thighs, Shudras from feet).
    - **Gayatri Mantra (Rigveda Mandala III, Hymn 62):** Composed by sage **Vishvamitra** in honor of the solar deity **Savitr**.

### Question 322
**Subject:** General Science (Biology) | **Topic:** Chemical Names of Vitamins

Match List-I (Vitamin) with List-II (Chemical Name) and select the correct answer:

**List-I (Vitamin)**
A. Vitamin A  
B. Vitamin B2  
C. Vitamin C  
D. Vitamin E  

**List-II (Chemical Name)**
1. Riboflavin  
2. Retinol  
3. Tocopherol  
4. Ascorbic Acid  

**Options:**
A. A-2, B-1, C-4, D-3  
B. A-1, B-2, C-4, D-3  
C. A-2, B-4, C-1, D-3  
D. A-3, B-1, C-4, D-2  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - **Vitamin A:** **Retinol** (deficiency causes Xerophthalmia and Nyctalopia/Night Blindness).
  - **Vitamin B2:** **Riboflavin** (deficiency causes Cheilosis - cracking of lip corners, and Glossitis - red swollen tongue).
  - **Vitamin C:** **Ascorbic Acid** (heat-labile, antioxidant; deficiency causes Scurvy).
  - **Vitamin E:** **Tocopherol** ("Beauty Vitamin" / anti-sterility vitamin; powerful fat-soluble antioxidant).
  - **Vitamin K:** **Phylloquinone / Naphthoquinone** (blood clotting factor).
  - **Vitamin B6:** **Pyridoxine** (deficiency causes peripheral neuropathy and convulsions).
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** Vitamin B2 deficiency causes **Cheilosis** (cracks at mouth corners); Vitamin B3 (Niacin) deficiency causes **Pellagra**!

D. 1, 2 and 3

### Question 324
**Subject:** Environment & Ecology | **Topic:** In-Situ vs Ex-Situ Conservation

Which of the following is an example of **EX-SITU** (off-site) biodiversity conservation?
1. Botanical Gardens
2. Zoological Parks (Zoos)
3. Wildlife Sanctuaries
4. Cryopreservation / Seed Banks

Select the correct answer from the codes given below:

**Options:**
A. 1, 2 and 4 only  
B. 1, 2 and 3 only  
C. 2, 3 and 4 only  
D. 1, 2, 3 and 4  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - **In-Situ Conservation (On-Site):** Conserving species in their natural habitats:
    - National Parks
    - Wildlife Sanctuaries
    - Biosphere Reserves
    - Sacred Groves (*Sarnas* of Jharkhand, *Kavu* of Kerala, *Deorais* of Maharashtra)
    - Marine Protected Areas (MPAs).
  - **Ex-Situ Conservation (Off-Site):** Conserving species outside their natural habitats under human management:
    - **Botanical Gardens**
    - **Zoological Parks / Aquariums**
    - **Seed Banks / Gene Banks**
    - **Cryopreservation** (storing gametes in liquid nitrogen at $-196^\circ 	ext{C}$).
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** Sacred Groves are IN-SITU conservation, NOT ex-situ! Botanical Gardens and Seed Banks are EX-SITU.

### Question 325
**Subject:** Indian Economy | **Topic:** Reserve Bank Open Market Operations (OMOs)

Given below are two statements, one is labelled as Assertion (A) and the other as Reason (R):

**Assertion (A):** When the Reserve Bank of India wants to absorb excess liquidity from the financial system to curb inflation, it sells government securities in the open market.  
**Reason (R):** Selling government securities transfers cash liquidity from commercial banks into the RBI's accounts, thereby shrinking the credit creation capacity of commercial banks.

Select the correct answer from the codes given below:

**Options:**
A. Both (A) and (R) are true and (R) is the correct explanation of (A)  
B. Both (A) and (R) are true, but (R) is not the correct explanation of (A)  
C. (A) is true, but (R) is false  
D. (A) is false, but (R) is true  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - Open Market Operations (OMOs) are outright purchases and sales of Government Securities (G-Secs) by the RBI under Section 17(8) of the RBI Act 1934:
    - **To Absorb Liquidity (Fight Inflation):** RBI **SELLS** government securities $
ightarrow$ Commercial banks buy them and pay money to RBI $
ightarrow$ Cash reserves of commercial banks contract $
ightarrow$ Money supply falls $
ightarrow$ Interest rates rise.
    - **To Inject Liquidity (Fight Recession):** RBI **BUYS** government securities $
ightarrow$ RBI injects cash into banking system $
ightarrow$ Banks' lending capacity expands.
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** Sale of G-Secs by RBI = CONTRACTS liquidity. Purchase of G-Secs by RBI = EXPANDS liquidity!

---

### Question 327
**Subject:** Medieval Indian History | **Topic:** Mansabdari System Evolution under Jahangir

Consider the following statements regarding the Mughal Mansabdari system:
1. Akbar established the Mansabdari system, where every officer held a dual rank: *Zat* (personal rank determining status and salary) and *Sawar* (military rank determining cavalry contingent).
2. The *Du-Aspa Sih-Aspa* system was introduced by Emperor Jahangir.
3. Under the *Du-Aspa Sih-Aspa* system, a noble's military contingent was effectively doubled without increasing his *Zat* rank.

Which of the statements given above are correct?

**Options:**
A. 1 and 2 only  
B. 2 and 3 only  
C. 1 and 3 only  
D. 1, 2 and 3  

**Answer:** D

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - All three statements are correct:
    - **Akbar's Framework (1571):** Combined civil and military administration into 33 numerical grades (ranging from Mansab of 10 to 10,000). By 1595–96, ranks were bifurcated into **Zat** (personal status/pay) and **Sawar** (cavalry required to maintain). The *Sawar* rank could never exceed the *Zat* rank!
    - **Jahangir's Innovation (*Du-Aspa Sih-Aspa*):** Literally "two-horse, three-horse"; allowed trusted nobles to maintain a larger sawar contingent with additional allowance without raising their *Zat* status.
    - **Shah Jahan's Innovation:** Introduced the *Month-Scale (Mahana)* system (paying salaries for 10, 8, 6, or 4 months instead of full year) due to fiscal shortfalls.
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** Mansabdari was introduced by **Akbar**; *Du-Aspa Sih-Aspa* was introduced by **Jahangir**; Month-scale was introduced by **Shah Jahan**!

### Question 328
**Subject:** World Geography | **Topic:** Submarine Mid-Oceanic Ridges

Match List-I (Mid-Ocean Ridge / Plateau) with List-II (Ocean Location) and select the correct answer:

**List-I (Ridge)**
A. Mid-Atlantic Ridge  
B. Ninety East Ridge  
C. Carlsberg Ridge  
D. East Pacific Rise  

**List-II (Ocean Basin)**
1. Arabian Sea / Northwest Indian Ocean  
2. S-shaped divergent boundary running longitudinally through the Atlantic Ocean  
3. Fast-spreading divergent plate boundary across the southeastern Pacific Ocean  
4. Remarkable 5,000 km linear north-south volcanic ridge along the 90° E meridian in the Indian Ocean  

**Options:**
A. A-2, B-4, C-1, D-3  
B. A-1, B-4, C-2, D-3  
C. A-2, B-1, C-4, D-3  
D. A-3, B-4, C-1, D-2  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - **Mid-Atlantic Ridge:** World's longest continuous undersea mountain range (~16,000 km); S-shaped; separates Eurasian/North American and African/South American tectonic plates; surfaces above sea level in Iceland.
  - **Ninety East Ridge:** Linear submarine ridge along 90° E meridian in the **eastern Indian Ocean**; formed by the Kerguelen hotspot as the Indian plate moved northward.
  - **Carlsberg Ridge:** Divergent boundary separating the African and Indo-Australian plates in the **Arabian Sea / Central Indian Ocean**.
  - **East Pacific Rise:** Broad, gently sloping ocean ridge on the Pacific plate boundary with the fastest spreading rates (~15 cm/year).
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** Ninety East Ridge is located in the **INDIAN OCEAN**, running parallel to the Andaman & Nicobar island arc!

### Question 329
**Subject:** Indian Polity | **Topic:** Election of the Vice-President of India

Consider the following statements regarding the election of the Vice-President of India:
1. The Vice-President is elected by an Electoral College consisting of members of both Houses of Parliament (Article 66).
2. Both elected and nominated members of the Rajya Sabha and Lok Sabha participate in the Vice-Presidential election.
3. Members of State Legislative Assemblies do NOT participate in the election of the Vice-President.

Which of the statements given above are correct?

**Options:**
A. 1 and 2 only  
B. 2 and 3 only  
C. 1 and 3 only  
D. 1, 2 and 3  

**Answer:** D

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - All three statements are correct:
    - **Article 66(1):** Vice-President is elected by an electoral college consisting of the **members of both Houses of Parliament** in accordance with the system of proportional representation by means of the single transferable vote.
    - **Contrast with Presidential Election:**
      - *Nominated MPs:* Participate in VP election; but do NOT participate in President's election.
      - *State Assemblies (MLAs):* Do NOT participate in VP election; but elected MLAs participate in President's election.
    - Qualifications: Citizen of India, at least 35 years of age, qualified for election as a member of the Rajya Sabha.
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** A resolution for the removal of the Vice-President can be introduced **ONLY in the Rajya Sabha** (Article 67(b)), requiring an effective majority in RS and agreed to by Lok Sabha!

### Question 330
**Subject:** General Science (Physics) | **Topic:** Wave Optics & Interference of Light

Consider the following optical phenomena:
1. The brilliant multi-colored patterns observed on thin soap bubbles illuminated by sunlight.
2. The iridescent colors seen on thin oil slicks floating on wet asphalt roads.
3. The alternating bright and dark fringe bands produced in Thomas Young's Double-Slit Experiment.

All three of the above phenomena are direct consequences of which optical principle?

**Options:**
A. Dispersion of light  
B. Interference of light waves  
C. Total Internal Reflection  
D. Polarization of light  

**Answer:** B

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - **Interference of Light:** The redistribution of wave energy caused by the superposition of two or more coherent light waves.
  - **Thin Film Interference (Soap bubbles and Oil films):** When white light strikes a thin film of soap or oil, reflections occur from both the top and bottom surfaces of the film. These reflected waves interfere with each other constructively for certain wavelengths and destructively for others depending on film thickness and angle of incidence, producing brilliant rainbow colors.
  - **Young's Double Slit Experiment (1801):** Conclusively established the wave nature of light by generating constructive (bright) and destructive (dark) interference fringes on a screen ($y = rac{n\lambda D}{d}$).
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** A rainbow in the sky is formed by **Dispersion, Refraction, and Internal Reflection** in water droplets; but colors on a soap bubble or oil film are caused by **INTERFERENCE**!

3. The revised target aims for up to a 40% reduction in particulate matter concentrations by 2026 across 131 non-attainment cities.

Which of the statements given above are correct?
