A. 1 and 2 only  
B. 2 and 3 only  
C. 1 and 3 only  
D. 1, 2 and 3  

**Answer:** D

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - All three statements are correct:
    - **Launch:** January 2019; first comprehensive pan-India framework for air quality improvement.
    - **Non-Attainment Cities:** Cities that consistently failed to meet the National Ambient Air Quality Standards (NAAQS) for 5 consecutive years. Initially 102 cities, later expanded to **131 cities** (including 17 cities in Uttar Pradesh such as Lucknow, Kanpur, Agra, Varanasi, Prayagraj, Ghaziabad, Noida).

### Question 334

A. Department of Economic Affairs (DEA)  
B. Department of Investment and Public Asset Management (DIPAM)  
C. Department of Public Enterprises (DPE)  
D. Department of Financial Services (DFS)  

**Answer:** B

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - **DIPAM (Department of Investment and Public Asset Management):**
    - Originally set up as the Department of Disinvestment in 1999; renamed as **DIPAM** in the Union Budget 2016–17.
    - Functions under the **Ministry of Finance**.
    - Mandated with: strategic disinvestment, minority stake sales via Offer for Sale (OFS), IPOs, ETFs (Bharat 22, CPSE ETF), and asset monetization of CPSEs.
  - Department of Public Enterprises (DPE) was also transferred to the Ministry of Finance in 2021 (previously under Ministry of Heavy Industries).
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** Strategic Disinvestment involves the sale of a substantial portion of government shareholding (usually 50% or more) along with the transfer of **management control** to a strategic buyer (e.g. sale of Air India to Talace Pvt Ltd / Tata Sons)!

### Question 336
**Subject:** Ancient Indian History | **Topic:** Gupta Administrative Terminology

Match List-I (Gupta Administrative Officer) with List-II (Department / Responsibility) and select the correct answer:

**List-I (Officer)**
A. Uparika  
B. Vishayapati  
C. Sandhivigrahika  
D. Mahadandanayaka  

**List-II (Responsibility)**
1. Governor of a Province (*Bhukti*)  
2. Minister for Foreign Affairs, War, and Peace (first created by Samudragupta)  
3. Head of a District (*Vishaya*)  
4. Chief Police / Judicial Executive and Military Commander  

**Options:**
A. A-1, B-3, C-2, D-4  
B. A-3, B-1, C-2, D-4  
C. A-1, B-2, C-3, D-4  
D. A-2, B-3, C-1, D-4  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - **Uparika:** Provincial governor appointed directly by the emperor to govern a *Bhukti* (province).
  - **Vishayapati:** District magistrate/collector administering a *Vishaya* (district), assisted by the *Adhishthana Adhikarana* (district council comprising Nagarasreshthi, Sarthavaha, Prathamakulika, and Prathamakayastha).
  - **Sandhivigrahika:** "Minister of peace and war"; instituted under Samudragupta (Harishena held this title).
  - **Mahadandanayaka:** Chief justice and general of forces.
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** In Gupta times, territory was divided into: **Empire -> Bhukti (Province) -> Vishaya (District) -> Vithi (Sub-district) -> Gram (Village)**!

### Question 337
**Subject:** Geography of India | **Topic:** West-Flowing Rivers of India

Consider the following statements regarding the west-flowing rivers of Peninsular India:
1. The Sabarmati river originates from the Dhebar Lake in the Aravalli range in Udaipur district of Rajasthan.
2. The Mahi river originates in Madhya Pradesh and cuts across the Tropic of Cancer twice before emptying into the Gulf of Khambhat.
3. The Luni river originates near Ajmer in the Aravallis and terminates in the marshy expanse of the Rann of Kutch without reaching the open sea.

Which of the statements given above are correct?

**Options:**
A. 1 and 2 only  
B. 2 and 3 only  
C. 1 and 3 only  
D. 1, 2 and 3  

**Answer:** D

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - All three statements are correct:
    - **Sabarmati:** Originates in Aravallis (Udaipur); flows through Rajasthan and Gujarat; Gandhinagar and Ahmedabad are situated on its banks; drains into Gulf of Khambhat.
    - **Mahi:** Originates in Vindhyas (Dhar, MP); flows through MP, Rajasthan, and Gujarat; is the **only Indian river that crosses the Tropic of Cancer ($23.5^\circ 	ext{ N}$) twice**.
    - **Luni (Salt River / Lavanavari):** Originates in Pushkar valley near Ajmer; flows through Thar Desert; water is fresh up to Balotra (Barmer) and becomes saline downstream; classic example of **Inland / Ephemeral Drainage**, disappearing into the Great Rann of Kutch.
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** Ghaggar in the north and Luni in Rajasthan are India's two most prominent rivers of **Inland Drainage**!

### Question 338
**Subject:** Indian Polity | **Topic:** President's Rule (Article 356) & S.R. Bommai Guidelines

Consider the following statements regarding President's Rule under Article 356:
1. A proclamation of President's Rule must be approved by both Houses of Parliament within two months by a simple majority.
2. The proclamation can be extended for a maximum period of three years with parliamentary approval every six months.
3. In the landmark S.R. Bommai case (1994), the Supreme Court ruled that a proclamation under Article 356 is subject to judicial review.

Which of the statements given above are correct?

**Options:**
A. 1 and 2 only  
B. 2 and 3 only  
C. 1 and 3 only  
D. 1, 2 and 3  

**Answer:** D

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - All three statements are correct:
    - **Grounds:** Article 356 (failure of constitutional machinery in state) and Article 365 (failure to comply with union directions).
    - **Parliamentary Approval:** Within **two months** by a simple majority in each House.
    - **Duration:** Valid for 6 months at a time, up to a maximum of **3 years** (beyond 1 year requires meeting two conditions under 44th CAA: National Emergency in operation and Election Commission certifying difficulty in holding assembly elections).
    - **S.R. Bommai (1994):** 9-judge bench laid down that: Article 356 is an exceptional power; state assembly cannot be dissolved until Parliament approves the proclamation; proclamation is subject to judicial review; if struck down, the court can revive the dismissed government and assembly.
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** National Emergency (Art 352) requires approval within **1 month by special majority**; President's Rule (Art 356) requires approval within **2 months by simple majority**!

### Question 339
**Subject:** General Science (Biology) | **Topic:** Cellular Elements of Human Blood

Match List-I (Blood Cell Type) with List-II (Biological Feature / Lifespan) and select the correct answer:

**List-I (Cell Type)**
A. Erythrocytes (RBCs)  
B. Leucocytes (WBCs)  
C. Thrombocytes (Platelets)  
D. Monocytes  

**List-II (Feature / Lifespan)**
1. Nucleated; short-lived (1–15 days); defend body against pathogens; divided into granulocytes and agranulocytes  
2. Biconcave circular discs lacking nucleus in mature state; contain hemoglobin; lifespan ~120 days  
3. Largest white blood cells; kidney-shaped nucleus; differentiate into tissue macrophages  
4. Non-nucleated cytoplasmic fragments formed from megakaryocytes; essential for hemostasis and clotting  

**Options:**
A. A-2, B-1, C-4, D-3  
B. A-1, B-2, C-4, D-3  
C. A-2, B-4, C-1, D-3  
D. A-3, B-1, C-4, D-2  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - **RBCs:** Most abundant ($5–5.5 	ext{ million/mm}^3$); lack nucleus, mitochondria, and Golgi apparatus in mammals (to maximize space for hemoglobin); destroyed in the **Spleen ("Graveyard of RBCs")** after ~120 days.
  - **WBCs:** $6,000–8,000/	ext{mm}^3$; combat infection; Neutrophils are the most numerous (~60–65%); Basophils secrete histamine and heparin; Eosinophils combat allergic reactions.
  - **Platelets:** $1.5–3.5 	ext{ lakh/mm}^3$; pinched off from bone marrow megakaryocytes; release platelet factor 3 for coagulation (dengue causes thrombocytopenia).
  - **Monocytes:** Largest WBCs (6–8%); phagocytic.
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** Camels and Llamas are the only mammals that have nucleated, oval RBCs! All other mammals have enucleated circular biconcave RBCs.

### Question 341
**Subject:** Environment & Ecology | **Topic:** Water Pollution Parameters (BOD vs COD)

Consider the following statements regarding Biochemical Oxygen Demand (BOD) and Chemical Oxygen Demand (COD):
1. BOD measures the amount of dissolved oxygen consumed by aerobic microorganisms to decompose biologically degradable organic matter in water.
2. COD measures the amount of oxygen required for the chemical oxidation of BOTH biodegradable and non-biodegradable organic pollutants using a strong chemical oxidant like potassium dichromate ($K_2Cr_2O_7$).
3. In any polluted water sample, the COD value is always greater than the BOD value ($	ext{COD} > 	ext{BOD}$).

Which of the statements given above are correct?

**Options:**
A. 1 and 2 only  
B. 2 and 3 only  
C. 1 and 3 only  
D. 1, 2 and 3  

**Answer:** D

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - All three statements are correct:
    - **BOD ($BOD_5$ at 20°C):** Measures biological decomposition over 5 days; reflects organic sewage pollution; cannot oxidize toxic or biologically inert compounds (cellulose, lignin, detergents).
    - **COD:** Determined chemically within 2–3 hours using acidified potassium dichromate ($K_2Cr_2O_7$) and sulphuric acid; oxidizes virtually all organic matter.
    - **Mathematical Rule:** Because COD oxidizes everything that BOD oxidizes PLUS recalcitrant non-biodegradable pollutants, **COD is ALWAYS greater than or equal to BOD** ($	ext{COD} \ge 	ext{BOD}$).
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** A low BOD/COD ratio indicates that the wastewater contains a high proportion of toxic or non-biodegradable chemicals that cannot be treated by biological methods alone!

### Question 342
**Subject:** Modern Indian History | **Topic:** 19th Century Reformers of Western India

Match List-I (Reform Movement / Leader) with List-II (Organization / Historic Role) and select the correct answer:

A. Gopal Hari Deshmukh  
B. Dadoba Pandurang  
C. Vishnu Shastri Pandit  
D. Behramji M. Malabari  

**List-II (Organization / Contribution)**
1. Paramahansa Mandali (1849, Bombay); secret society promoting monotheism and breaking caste taboos  
2. Wrote under the pen-name 'Lokahitawadi' in weekly *Prabhakar*, attacking orthodox traditions  
3. Founded the Parsi Seva Sadan (1908) and campaigned tirelessly for the Age of Consent Act, 1891  
4. Founded the Widow Remarriage Association (1850s) in Maharashtra  

**Options:**
A. A-2, B-1, C-4, D-3  
B. A-1, B-2, C-4, D-3  
C. A-2, B-4, C-1, D-3  
D. A-3, B-1, C-4, D-2  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - **Gopal Hari Deshmukh (Lokahitawadi):** Judge and social reformer; advocated modernization and equality; wrote 108 essays (*Shatapatre*) in *Prabhakar*.
  - **Dadoba Pandurang:** Founded **Paramahansa Mandali** in 1849 along with Durgaram Mehtaji; members ate bread cooked by lower-caste persons at secret meetings.

### Question 343
**Subject:** Indian Economy | **Topic:** Lorenz Curve and Gini Coefficient

Consider the following statements regarding the measurement of income inequality:
1. The Lorenz Curve is a graphical representation of the cumulative distribution of income across a population.
2. The Gini Coefficient is the mathematical ratio of the area between the Line of Perfect Equality and the Lorenz Curve to the total area under the Line of Perfect Equality.
3. A Gini coefficient of 0 indicates absolute mathematical equality, while a Gini coefficient of 1 indicates absolute inequality.

Which of the statements given above are correct?

**Options:**
A. 1 and 2 only  
B. 2 and 3 only  
C. 1 and 3 only  
D. 1, 2 and 3  

**Answer:** D

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - All three statements are correct:
    - **Lorenz Curve (Max O. Lorenz, 1905):** Plots cumulative percentage of population against cumulative percentage of income received. A 45° diagonal represents the "Line of Perfect Equality".
    - **Gini Coefficient (Corrado Gini, 1912):**
      $$G = rac{	ext{Area between Line of Equality and Lorenz Curve}}{	ext{Total Area under Line of Equality}}$$
    - **Range:** $0 \le G \le 1$:
      - **$G = 0$:** Complete equality (everyone has identical income).
      - **$G = 1$:** Complete inequality (one individual has all income, others zero).
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** The closer the Lorenz curve bends away from the 45-degree diagonal, the HIGHER the Gini coefficient and the GREATER the income inequality!

### Question 344
**Subject:** Current Affairs | **Topic:** Paris Olympic Games 2024 (Medals Table)

In the final medals tally of the Paris 2024 Olympic Games, which country finished at the top of the leaderboard?

**Options:**
A. United States of America (USA)  
B. People's Republic of China  
C. Japan  
D. France  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - The **United States of America (USA)** finished 1st on the medals table with **40 Gold, 44 Silver, and 42 Bronze (Total: 126 medals)**.
  - **China** finished 2nd with **40 Gold, 27 Silver, and 24 Bronze (Total: 91 medals)**.
  - (Both USA and China won 40 Gold medals each, but the USA topped the standings by virtue of winning more silver medals: 44 vs 27).
  - Japan finished 3rd (20 Gold), Australia 4th (18 Gold), and host nation France 5th (16 Gold).
  - India finished 71st with 6 medals (1 Silver, 5 Bronze).
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** USA and China tied with exactly 40 Golds each, but the USA edged ahead on Silver medal count!

**Subject:** Medieval Indian History | **Topic:** Mughal Moti Masjids (Agra vs Delhi)

Match List-I (Mughal Mosque) with List-II (Builder & Location) and select the correct answer:

2. Shah Jahan; constructed entirely in chaste white marble inside Agra Fort

B. A-1, B-2, C-4, D-3  
C. A-2, B-4, C-1, D-3  
D. A-3, B-1, C-4, D-2

**Explanation:**

  - **Moti Masjid (Delhi Red Fort):** Built by **Aurangzeb** in 1659–1660 as a private prayer chapel for himself and the imperial harem.

### Question 346
**Subject:** World Geography | **Topic:** Major World Canals

Match List-I (Canal) with List-II (Country & Strategic Function) and select the correct answer:

**List-I (Canal)**
A. Corinth Canal  
B. Kiel Canal  
C. Welland Canal  
D. Panama Canal  

**List-II (Location & Function)**
1. Germany; connects North Sea with Baltic Sea through Jutland peninsula  
2. Greece; cuts across Isthmus of Corinth connecting Gulf of Corinth with Saronic Gulf  
3. Central America; connects Atlantic and Pacific Oceans using 3 sets of locks  
4. Canada; bypasses Niagara Falls to connect Lake Erie with Lake Ontario  

**Options:**
A. A-2, B-1, C-4, D-3  
B. A-1, B-2, C-4, D-3  
C. A-2, B-4, C-1, D-3  
D. A-3, B-1, C-4, D-2  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - **Corinth Canal (Greece):** Spectacular deep, narrow vertical-walled rock cutting across the Isthmus of Corinth (~6.4 km long).
  - **Kiel Canal (Germany):** World's busiest artificial waterway, linking Brunsbüttel (North Sea) to Kiel-Holtenau (Baltic Sea).
  - **Welland Canal (Ontario, Canada):** Vital link in the St. Lawrence Seaway; allows ocean-going freighters to bypass the unnavigable 51-meter drop of **Niagara Falls** between Lake Erie and Lake Ontario.
  - **Panama Canal:** Cuts across Central America; opened in 1914.
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** Lake Erie has a higher water elevation than Lake Ontario; the Welland Canal uses 8 lift locks to safely lower ships over the Niagara Escarpment!

### Question 347
**Subject:** Indian Polity | **Topic:** Joint Sitting of Parliament (Article 108)

Consider the following statements regarding a Joint Sitting of both Houses of Parliament:
1. Under Article 108, the President summons a Joint Sitting to resolve a legislative deadlock between the Lok Sabha and the Rajya Sabha.
2. The Speaker of the Lok Sabha presides over a Joint Sitting, and in his absence, the Deputy Speaker of the Lok Sabha presides.
3. If both Speaker and Deputy Speaker are absent, the Chairman of the Rajya Sabha (Vice-President) presides over the Joint Sitting.
4. A Joint Sitting cannot be summoned for a Money Bill or a Constitution Amendment Bill.

Which of the statements given above are correct?

**Options:**
A. 1, 2 and 4 only  
B. 1, 3 and 4 only  
C. 2, 3 and 4 only  
D. 1, 2, 3 and 4  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - Statements 1, 2, and 4 are correct: Article 108 summons joint sitting if a bill passed by one house is rejected, unamended for 6 months, or houses disagree on amendments. Bill is passed by simple majority of total members present and voting.
  - Statement 3 is incorrect: **The Chairman of the Rajya Sabha (Vice-President of India) NEVER presides over a Joint Sitting!** This is because the Vice-President is not a member of either House. In the absence of both Speaker and Deputy Speaker, the **Deputy Chairman of the Rajya Sabha** presides!
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** Joint sittings have occurred only 3 times in India's history:
  1. Dowry Prohibition Bill (1961)
  2. Banking Service Commission (Repeal) Bill (1978)
  3. Prevention of Terrorism Bill / POTA (2002).

### Question 348
**Subject:** General Science (Chemistry) | **Topic:** Modern Periodic Table Principles

Consider the following statements regarding the Modern Periodic Table:
1. Henry Moseley demonstrated in 1913 that the atomic number ($Z$) of an element is a more fundamental property than its atomic mass ($A$).
2. The Modern Periodic Law states that the physical and chemical properties of elements are periodic functions of their atomic numbers.
3. In the modern periodic table, elements are arranged in 18 vertical columns called 'Groups' and 7 horizontal rows called 'Periods'.

Which of the statements given above are correct?

**Options:**
A. 1 and 2 only  
B. 2 and 3 only  
C. 1 and 3 only  
D. 1, 2 and 3  

**Answer:** D

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - All three statements are correct:
    - **Moseley's Law ($\sqrt{
u} = a(Z - b)$):** Proved X-ray spectral frequencies correlate directly with atomic number (nuclear charge $Z$).
    - **Mendeleev vs Moseley:** Dmitri Mendeleev (1869) arranged elements by **atomic mass**; Moseley modified it to **atomic number**.
    - **Architecture:** 7 Periods (Period 1 has 2 elements; Periods 2 & 3 have 8; Periods 4 & 5 have 18; Period 6 has 32; Period 7 is incomplete). 18 Groups (Group 1: Alkali metals; Group 2: Alkaline earths; Group 17: Halogens; Group 18: Noble gases).
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** Across a period (left to right), atomic radius **DECREASES** and electronegativity **INCREASES**! Down a group, atomic radius **INCREASES**.

**Subject:** Environment & Ecology | **Topic:** IUCN Red List Conservation Categories

D. 3 — 1 — 2 — 4

- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - Ascending hierarchy of threat categories established by IUCN:
    - Least Concern (LC)
    - **Near Threatened (NT)**

      1. **Vulnerable (VU):** High risk of extinction in the wild (e.g. Greater One-Horned Rhino, Snow Leopard).
      2. **Endangered (EN):** Very high risk of extinction in the wild (e.g. Asiatic Lion, Bengal Tiger, Red Panda).
      3. **Critically Endangered (CR):** Extremely high risk of extinction in the wild (e.g. Great Indian Bustard, Gharial, Hangul, Pygmy Hog).
    - Extinct in the Wild (EW)
    - Extinct (EX).

ightarrow$ Endangered $

### Question 351
**Subject:** Indian Economy | **Topic:** Inflationary Dynamics and Economic Terms

Match List-I (Economic Phenomenon) with List-II (Definition / Real-World Manifestation) and select the correct answer:

**List-I (Phenomenon)**
A. Disinflation  
B. Deflation  
C. Reflation  
D. Shrinkflation  

**List-II (Definition)**
1. Persistent fall in the general price level of goods and services (negative inflation rate)  
2. Deliberate monetary/fiscal stimulus to revive price levels and output from a recessionary slump  
3. Slowing down of the rate of inflation (e.g., inflation falls from 7% to 4%)  
4. Practice of reducing product size or quantity while retaining the same retail selling price  

**Options:**
A. A-3, B-1, C-2, D-4  
B. A-1, B-3, C-2, D-4  
C. A-3, B-2, C-1, D-4  
D. A-2, B-1, C-4, D-3  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - **Disinflation:** Inflation is still positive, but the *rate* of price increase is decelerating (e.g., prices rise at 3% instead of 6%).
  - **Deflation:** General price level actually drops ($	ext{Inflation} < 0\%$); harmful because it prompts consumers to delay purchases, triggering economic depression.
  - **Reflation:** Policy designed to expand output and curb deflation through monetary easing or deficit spending.
  - **Shrinkflation:** Stealth inflation where the pack size or weight is reduced (e.g. chocolate bar shrunk from 100g to 80g) while the price remains unchanged.
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** Disinflation $
eq$ Deflation! In Disinflation, prices are STILL RISING, just at a slower speed. In Deflation, prices are FALLING.

2. It is India's first dedicated polarimetry space observatory to study intense celestial X-ray sources like black holes and neutron stars.

Which of the statements given above are correct?

B. 2 and 3 only  
C. 1 and 3 only  
D. 1, 2 and 3  

**Answer:** D

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - All three statements are correct:
    - **Launch:** January 1, 2024, into a 650 km low Earth orbit with 6-degree inclination.
    - **Science Goal:** Measures polarization of X-rays from cosmic sources (pulsars, black hole binaries, supernova remnants).
    - **Payloads:**
      1. **POLIX (Polarimeter Instrument in X-rays):** Developed by Raman Research Institute (RRI), Bengaluru (8–30 keV range).
      2. **XSPECT (X-ray Spectroscopy and Timing):** Developed by Space Astronomy Group at URSC (0.8–15 keV).

### Question 353
**Subject:** Modern Indian History | **Topic:** Milestones of Mahatma Gandhi's Life

Arrange the following events of Mahatma Gandhi's life in chronological order:
1. Founding of the Phoenix Settlement in South Africa
2. Return to India on Pravasi Bharatiya Divas
3. Signing of the Poona Pact
4. Historic Fast at Yerwada Jail against the Communal Award

Select the correct answer from the codes given below:

**Options:**
A. 1 — 2 — 4 — 3  
B. 1 — 2 — 3 — 4  
C. 2 — 1 — 4 — 3  
D. 1 — 4 — 2 — 3  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  1. **Phoenix Settlement (Durban, South Africa, 1904):** Founded after reading John Ruskin's *Unto This Last*; dedicated to community living and printing *Indian Opinion*.
  2. **Return to India (9 January 1915):** Returned permanently with Kasturba aboard SS Arabia; landing at Apollo Bunder, Bombay; celebrated as **Pravasi Bharatiya Divas**.
  3. **Fast unto Death at Yerwada Jail (20 September 1932):** Begun to protest British Prime Minister Ramsay MacDonald's Communal Award granting separate electorates to Depressed Classes.
  4. **Poona Pact (24 September 1932):** Signed between Dr. B.R. Ambedkar and Madan Mohan Malaviya (on behalf of caste Hindus); abandoned separate electorates and replaced them with reserved seats in joint electorates (increased reserved provincial seats from 71 to 147).
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** Fast began on **20 September 1932**; Poona Pact was signed 4 days later on **24 September 1932**!

B. Vikramashila University  
C. Vallabhi University  
D. Odantapuri University  

**List-II (Location & Patron)**
1. Bhagalpur (Bihar); founded by Pala king Dharmapala; destroyed by Bakhtiyar Khalji  
2. Badgaon, Rajgir (Bihar); founded by Gupta emperor Kumaragupta I; Mahayana Buddhism  
3. Bihar Sharif (Bihar); founded by Pala king Gopala I  
4. Saurashtra (Gujarat); patronized by Maitraka rulers; Hinayana Buddhism center

A. A-2, B-1, C-4, D-3  
B. A-1, B-2, C-4, D-3

D. A-3, B-1, C-4, D-2

### Question 355
**Subject:** Geography of India | **Topic:** Saline and Alkaline Soils Reclamation

Consider the following statements regarding Saline and Alkaline soils in India:
1. Saline soils contain high amounts of free salts of sodium, potassium, and magnesium, and are locally known in Uttar Pradesh as *Reh*, *Kallar*, *Usar*, or *Thur*.
2. They develop predominantly in arid and semi-arid tracts and in poorly drained, canal-irrigated command areas with high water tables.
3. Alkaline soils can be chemically reclaimed for agriculture by the application of Gypsum ($CaSO_4 \cdot 2H_2O$) and green manuring with *Dhaincha* (*Sesbania aculeata*).

Which of the statements given above are correct?

**Options:**
A. 1 and 2 only  
B. 2 and 3 only  
C. 1 and 3 only  
D. 1, 2 and 3  

**Answer:** D

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - All three statements are correct:
    - **Formation:** Intense capillary action under hot dry climate draws dissolved sodium and magnesium salts to the surface, leaving white saline crusts (*Reh*).
    - **Geographical Extent:** Extensive in southwestern and central UP (Aligarh, Mainpuri, Etawah, Kanpur, Unnao, Rae Bareli) due to excessive canal flooding without sub-surface drainage.
    - **Reclamation:** **Gypsum ($CaSO_4 \cdot 2H_2O$)** supplies calcium ions that displace sodium ions from the clay complex:
      $$	ext{Clay-Na} + CaSO_4 
ightarrow 	ext{Clay-Ca} + Na_2SO_4 	ext{ (leached out)}$$
      Cultivation of acid-tolerant salt-absorbing crops (*Dhaincha*, sugarbeet, barley) restores organic humus.
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** To reclaim ACIDIC soil, add **LIME / Limestone ($CaCO_3$)**; to reclaim ALKALINE / USAR soil, add **GYPSUM ($CaSO_4 \cdot 2H_2O$)**!

### Question 356
**Subject:** Indian Polity | **Topic:** Suspension of Fundamental Rights (Article 358 vs Article 359)

Match List-I (Emergency Article) with List-II (Effect on Fundamental Rights) and select the correct answer:

**List-I (Article)**
A. Article 358  
B. Article 359  
C. Article 20 & Article 21  

**List-II (Effect)**
1. Automatic suspension of the six freedoms guaranteed under Article 19 immediately upon proclamation of external emergency  
2. Empowers the President to suspend the right to move any court for the enforcement of specified Fundamental Rights by presidential order  
3. Can NEVER be suspended or deprived even during the operation of a National Emergency  

**Options:**
A. A-1, B-2, C-3  
B. A-2, B-1, C-3  
C. A-1, B-3, C-2  
D. A-3, B-1, C-2  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - **Article 358:** Deals ONLY with Article 19. Operates automatically without requiring a separate presidential order; applies to the entire country. Under 44th CAA 1978, Article 19 is suspended ONLY when emergency is proclaimed on grounds of **War or External Aggression**, NOT on Armed Rebellion.
  - **Article 359:** Deals with the suspension of the *right to move courts* for specified Fundamental Rights; does not suspend the rights themselves, only their judicial enforcement; requires a specific presidential order.
  - **44th Amendment Act 1978 Inviolability:** The President **CANNOT suspend the enforcement of Articles 20 and 21** under Article 359 under any circumstance!
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** Article 358 operates AUTOMATICALLY (only for Article 19); Article 359 requires a PRESIDENTIAL ORDER (covers other FRs except Arts 20 & 21)!

### Question 357
**Subject:** General Science (Physics) | **Topic:** Atmospheric Refraction & Day Length

Consider the following statements regarding atmospheric refraction:
1. The Sun is visible to an observer on Earth about 2 minutes before the actual sunrise and about 2 minutes after the actual sunset.
2. The apparent flattening (oval shape) of the Sun's disc at sunrise and sunset is caused by atmospheric refraction.
3. Because of atmospheric refraction, the duration of daylight from sunrise to sunset is increased by approximately 4 minutes every day.

Which of the statements given above are correct?

**Options:**
A. 1 and 2 only  
B. 2 and 3 only  
C. 1 and 3 only  
D. 1, 2 and 3  

**Answer:** D

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - All three statements are correct:
    - **Early Sunrise & Delayed Sunset:** As light from the Sun enters from vacuum into progressively denser layers of the atmosphere, it bends towards the normal. When the Sun is slightly below the actual horizon ($~0.5^\circ$), its refracted rays reach the observer's eye, causing the Sun to appear already above the horizon ~2 minutes early. The same occurs at sunset.
    - **Total Extra Daylight:** $2 	ext{ minutes (sunrise)} + 2 	ext{ minutes (sunset)} = \mathbf{4 	ext{ minutes}}$.
    - **Oval Shape:** Rays from the lower rim of the Sun's disc bend more than rays from the upper rim due to the density gradient of air, compressing the vertical diameter and giving it an oval appearance.
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** The blue color of the sky is due to SCATTERING; but early sunrise/delayed sunset is due to **REFRACTION**!

### Question 360
**Subject:** Indian Economy | **Topic:** Direct Taxes vs Indirect Taxes

Which of the following is classified as a **DIRECT TAX** levied by the Central Government in India?
1. Corporation Tax
2. Securities Transaction Tax (STT)
3. Goods and Services Tax (GST)
4. Customs Duty

Select the correct answer from the codes given below:

**Options:**
A. 1 and 2 only  
B. 1 and 3 only  
C. 2 and 4 only  
D. 1, 2 and 4 only  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - **Direct Taxes (Incidence and impact fall on the same person; cannot be shifted):**
    - Personal Income Tax
    - **Corporation Tax** (tax on net profits of domestic and foreign companies)
    - **Securities Transaction Tax (STT)** (levied on purchase/sale of equities)
    - Equalisation Levy (tax on digital advertising services)
    - Capital Gains Tax.
  - **Indirect Taxes (Tax liability can be shifted to the final consumer):**
    - **Goods and Services Tax (GST)**
    - **Customs Duty** (levied on import/export of goods)
    - Central Excise Duty (on petroleum crude, diesel, petrol, ATF, natural gas).
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** STT (Securities Transaction Tax) is a DIRECT tax administered by the Central Board of Direct Taxes (CBDT)!

Match List-I (Maharashtra Bhakti Saint) with List-II (Literary Composition / Salient Feature) and select the correct answer:

**List-I (Saint)**
A. Sant Dnyaneshwar (Jnanadeva)  
B. Sant Tukaram  
C. Sant Eknath  
D. Samarth Ramdas  

**List-II (Work / Tradition)**
1. Composed *Bhavartha Dipika* (Dnyaneshwari), a pioneering Marathi commentary on Bhagavad Gita at age 15  
2. Wrote devotional lyrical hymns (*Abhangas*); contemporary of Shivaji and Jahangir  
3. Author of *Dasbodh*; spiritual preceptor (*Guru*) of Chhatrapati Shivaji; founded Dharakari panth  
4. Wrote *Bhavartha Ramayana* and introduced *Bharud* dramatic folk songs; insisted on Marathi over Sanskrit  

**Options:**
A. A-1, B-2, C-4, D-3  
B. A-2, B-1, C-4, D-3  
C. A-1, B-4, C-2, D-3  
D. A-3, B-2, C-1, D-4  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - **Dnyaneshwar (1275–1296):** Laid foundation of Maharashtra Bhakti; authored *Dnyaneshwari* (*Bhavartha Dipika*) and *Amrutanubhav*.
  - **Tukaram (1608–1650):** Greatest poet of the Varkari sect; worshipped Lord Vitthala (Vithoba) of Pandharpur; composed thousands of *Abhangas*.
  - **Eknath (1533–1599):** Bridged early and late Bhakti; wrote *Bhavartha Ramayana* and *Eknathi Bhagavata*; proclaimed: *"If Sanskrit was made by God, was Prakrit born of thieves?"*
  - **Samarth Ramdas (1608–1681):** Worshipped Rama and Hanuman; authored *Dasbodh*; represented the activist *Dharakari* school.
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** Varkari sect worships **Lord Vithoba (Krishna)** at Pandharpur on the banks of the Bhima (Chandrabhaga) river!

**Subject:** World Geography | **Topic:** Major World Straits and Border Passages

Match List-I (Strait) with List-II (Landmasses Separated) and select the correct answer:

**List-I (Strait)**
A. Palk Strait  
B. Bering Strait  
C. Strait of Dover  
D. Bass Strait  

**List-II (Landmasses Separated)**
1. Separates mainland Australia (Victoria) from the island of Tasmania  
2. Separates Tamil Nadu (India) from Jaffna Peninsula (Sri Lanka)  
3. Separates Great Britain (England) from mainland Europe (France)  
4. Separates Asia (Siberia, Russia) from North America (Alaska, USA)  

**Options:**
A. A-2, B-4, C-3, D-1  
B. A-1, B-4, C-3, D-2  
C. A-2, B-3, C-4, D-1  
D. A-4, B-2, C-1, D-3  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - **Palk Strait:** Connects Bay of Bengal with Palk Bay / Gulf of Mannar; separates **India and Sri Lanka**.
  - **Bering Strait:** Connects Arctic Ocean (Chukchi Sea) with Pacific Ocean (Bering Sea); separates **Russia (Chukchi Peninsula) from USA (Alaska)**; International Date Line passes through it.
  - **Strait of Dover:** Narrowest part of the English Channel (34 km); separates **Dover (UK) from Calais (France)**; underlain by Channel Tunnel.

### Question 364
**Subject:** Indian Polity | **Topic:** Seventh Schedule & 42nd Amendment Transfers

Under the 42nd Constitutional Amendment Act, 1976, how many subjects were transferred from the State List to the Concurrent List?

**Options:**
A. 3  
B. 4  
C. 5  
D. 6  

**Answer:** C

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - The 42nd Constitutional Amendment Act 1976 transferred **FIVE subjects** from the State List (List II) to the Concurrent List (List III):
    1. **Education**
    2. **Forests**
    3. **Weights and Measures**
    4. **Protection of Wild Animals and Birds**
    5. **Administration of Justice; constitution and organization of all courts except the Supreme Court and the High Courts**.
  - This historic shift empowered Parliament to legislate nationally on school education, higher education, national forest conservation (Forest Conservation Act 1980), and wildlife protection.
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** Memorize the 5 transferred subjects: **Education, Forests, Weights & Measures, Wildlife Protection, and Administration of Subordinate Courts**!

### Question 365
**Subject:** General Science (Biology) | **Topic:** Human Genetics and Chromosomal Basis of Sex

Consider the following statements regarding human genetics:
1. Human somatic cells contain 23 pairs of chromosomes (46 chromosomes): 22 pairs of autosomes and 1 pair of allosomes (sex chromosomes).
2. Human females are homogametic, producing ova carrying only the X chromosome ($22 + X$).
3. Human males are heterogametic, producing two types of sperms in equal proportions: $22 + X$ and $22 + Y$.
4. Genetically, the sex of the child is determined entirely by the genetic contribution of the father.

Which of the statements given above are correct?

**Options:**
A. 1, 2 and 3 only  
B. 2, 3 and 4 only  
C. 1, 3 and 4 only  
D. 1, 2, 3 and 4  

**Answer:** D

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - All four statements are correct:
    - **Chromosomal Constitution:** Females = $44 + XX$; Males = $44 + XY$.
    - **Sex Determination:** Since the mother can only donate an $X$ chromosome, the genetic sex is determined at fertilization by which sperm fertilizes the ovum:
      - If an $X$-bearing sperm fertilizes $
ightarrow 44 + XX 
ightarrow$ **Female child**.
      - If a $Y$-bearing sperm fertilizes $
ightarrow 44 + XY 
ightarrow$ **Male child**.
    - The biological father is 100% responsible for the genetic sex of the offspring (debunking social taboos blaming women for female children).
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** Turner's Syndrome is monosomy ($44 + X0$, 45 chromosomes, sterile female); Klinefelter's Syndrome is trisomy ($44 + XXY$, 47 chromosomes, sterile male with gynecomastia); Down's Syndrome is trisomy of **Chromosome 21**!

B. Dadri Cattle Fair  
C. Syed Salar Masud Ghazi Fair  
D. Nauchandi Fair  

**List-II (District & Origin)**
1. Ballia; ancient fair named after sage Bhrigu's disciple Dardar  
2. Saharanpur; biannual Navratri fair at the foot of Shivaliks  
3. Meerut; historic month-long post-Holi fair celebrating communal harmony  
4. Bahraich; celebrated at the historic 11th-century Dargah shrine

B. A-1, B-2, C-4, D-3  
C. A-2, B-4, C-1, D-3  
D. A-3, B-1, C-4, D-2

### Question 367
**Subject:** Environment & Ecology | **Topic:** Ecological Food Chains & Energy Pathways

Given below are two statements, one is labelled as Assertion (A) and the other as Reason (R):

**Assertion (A):** In a terrestrial forest ecosystem, a substantially larger fraction of energy flows through the Detritus Food Chain (DFC) than through the Grazing Food Chain (GFC).  
**Reason (R):** A vast proportion of primary plant biomass (leaves, branches, roots, trunks) is not consumed by living herbivores, but falls to the ground as litter and dead organic matter to be decomposed by bacteria and fungi.

Select the correct answer from the codes given below:

**Options:**
A. Both (A) and (R) are true and (R) is the correct explanation of (A)  
B. Both (A) and (R) are true, but (R) is not the correct explanation of (A)  
C. (A) is true, but (R) is false  
D. (A) is false, but (R) is true  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - Assertion is true: In forests, ~90% of primary production enters the detritus pathway.
  - Reason is true: Living herbivores (deer, insects) consume only about 10% of green standing biomass. Massive wood, bark, roots, and leaf litter fall into the soil horizon (*duff/humus*), where saprophytic fungi and bacteria break down complex lignocellulose, releasing nutrients and driving the Detritus Food Chain.
  - In deep aquatic ecosystems, by contrast, microscopic phytoplankton are grazed almost immediately by zooplankton, making the Grazing Food Chain dominant.
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** Aquatic ecosystem = **GFC is dominant**; Terrestrial forest ecosystem = **DFC is dominant**!

### Question 368
**Subject:** Modern Indian History | **Topic:** Charter Acts and End of EIC Commercial Monopoly

Match List-I (Charter Act) with List-II (Core Commercial & Administrative Provision) and select the correct answer:

**List-I (Charter Act)**
A. Charter Act of 1813  
B. Charter Act of 1833  
C. Charter Act of 1853  
D. Regulating Act of 1773  

**List-II (Provision)**
1. Completely abolished the commercial monopoly of East India Company (including tea and China trade); Governor-General of Bengal became Governor-General of India  
2. Ended Company's commercial trade monopoly EXCEPT in Tea trade and trade with China; allocated ₹1 lakh for education  
3. Separated legislative and executive functions of Governor-General's Council; introduced open competition for civil services  
4. First written constitution for British rule in India; established Supreme Court at Calcutta; Governor of Bengal made Governor-General  

**Options:**
A. A-2, B-1, C-3, D-4  
B. A-1, B-2, C-3, D-4  
C. A-2, B-3, C-1, D-4  
D. A-4, B-1, C-3, D-2  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - **Charter Act 1813:** Deprived Company of commercial monopoly in India, opening trade to all British merchants, EXCEPT retained monopoly in **Tea trade and trade with China**; allowed Christian missionaries to enter India; sanctioned ₹1,00,000 annually for education.
  - **Charter Act 1833:** **COMPLETELY abolished all commercial activities** of the Company (ended tea and China trade); transformed Company into purely an administrative body; Lord William Bentinck became first Governor-General of India; Macaulay Law Commission appointed.
  - **Charter Act 1853:** Added 6 legislative councillors creating Indian (Central) Legislative Council; introduced open merit competition for ICS.
  - **Regulating Act 1773:** Warren Hastings appointed Governor-General; established Supreme Court at Fort William (1774).
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** Partial end of trade monopoly = **1813**; Complete termination of all trade monopoly = **1833**!

### Question 369
**Subject:** Indian Economy | **Topic:** Gross Value Added (GVA) at Basic Prices

Consider the following statements regarding the national income accounting methodology adopted in India since 2015:
1. Headline economic growth is measured in terms of Real GDP at constant market prices, rather than GDP at factor cost.
2. The relationship between GDP at market prices and Gross Value Added (GVA) at basic prices is given by: $	ext{GDP}_{	ext{Market Prices}} = 	ext{GVA}_{	ext{Basic Prices}} + 	ext{Product Taxes} - 	ext{Product Subsidies}$.
3. The base year for national income calculation is currently 2011–12.

Which of the statements given above are correct?

**Options:**
A. 1 and 2 only  
B. 2 and 3 only  
C. 1 and 3 only  
D. 1, 2 and 3  

**Answer:** D

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - All three statements are correct:
    - In January 2015, the Central Statistics Office (CSO, now NSO) aligned Indian national accounting with the international System of National Accounts (SNA 2008):
      - Shifted headline GDP from Factor Cost to **Market Prices**.
      - Shifted sectoral production analysis from Factor Cost to **GVA at Basic Prices**.
      - Equation:
        $$	ext{GDP at Market Prices} = 	ext{GVA at Basic Prices} + 	ext{Net Product Taxes (Taxes - Subsidies)}$$
      - Base year updated from 2004–05 to **2011–12**.
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** GVA captures the SUPPLY side (income earned by factors of production + net production taxes); GDP captures the DEMAND side (final expenditure on goods and services)!

### Question 371
**Subject:** Ancient Indian History | **Topic:** Buddhist Canonical Literature (Tripitakas)

Match List-I (Pitaka of Buddhist Canon) with List-II (Subject Matter / Contents) and select the correct answer:

**List-I (Pitaka)**
A. Sutta Pitaka  
B. Vinaya Pitaka  
C. Abhidhamma Pitaka  

**List-II (Subject Matter)**
1. Rules of monastic discipline, monastic ethics, and conduct for monks and nuns  
2. Discourses and sermons of the Buddha on ethical and doctrinal matters (divided into 5 Nikayas)  
3. Profound philosophical, metaphysical, and psychological analysis of the Dhamma (compiled at 3rd Council)  

**Options:**
A. A-2, B-1, C-3  
B. A-1, B-2, C-3  
C. A-2, B-3, C-1  
D. A-3, B-1, C-2  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - The *Tripitakas* (Three Baskets) written in Pali:
    - **Sutta Pitaka:** Recited by **Ananda** at the 1st Buddhist Council (Rajgriha, 483 BCE); contains Buddha's religious teachings across 5 Nikayas (Digha, Majjhima, Samyutta, Anguttara, Khuddaka Nikaya - which includes *Dhammapada* and *Jatakas*).
    - **Vinaya Pitaka:** Recited by **Upali** at the 1st Council; contains 227 rules of monastic discipline (*Patimokkha*), Suttavibhanga, and Khandhaka.
    - **Abhidhamma Pitaka:** Compiled at the 3rd Buddhist Council (Pataliputra, 250 BCE) under **Moggaliputta Tissa**; contains 7 books (including *Kathavatthu*).
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** Sutta and Vinaya were compiled at the **1st Council (Rajgriha)**; Abhidhamma was compiled at the **3rd Council (Pataliputra)**!

4. Sikkim; Chumbi Valley; connects Kalimpong/Gangtok with Lhasa (Tibet)

B. A-1, B-2, C-4, D-3

D. A-3, B-1, C-4, D-2

### Question 373
**Subject:** Indian Polity | **Topic:** Kesavananda Bharati Judgment (1973)

Consider the following statements regarding the landmark Kesavananda Bharati v. State of Kerala judgment:
1. It was decided on April 24, 1973, by the largest bench in the history of the Supreme Court of India, comprising 13 judges.
2. The judgment was delivered by a narrow majority of 7 to 6, headed by Chief Justice S.M. Sikri.
3. The Supreme Court upheld the constitutional validity of the 24th Amendment Act, 1971, ruling that Parliament can amend any part of the Constitution, including Fundamental Rights, provided it does not alter the "Basic Structure".

Which of the statements given above are correct?

**Options:**
A. 1 and 2 only  
B. 2 and 3 only  
C. 1 and 3 only  
D. 1, 2 and 3  

**Answer:** D

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - All three statements are correct:
    - **Date & Bench:** Decided on **24 April 1973** (same day Sachin Tendulkar was born); 13-judge constitutional bench presided by Chief Justice S.M. Sikri.
    - **Majority:** Narrow 7:6 majority (Sikri, Shelat, Grover, Hegde, Mukherjea, Reddy, and Khanna in majority; Ray, Palekar, Mathew, Beg, Dwivedi, and Chandrachud in minority). Justice H.R. Khanna's opinion tipped the scales.
    - **Basic Structure Doctrine:** Overruled *Golaknath (1967)*; affirmed Parliament's amending power under Article 368 is broad but not unlimited; Parliament cannot destroy the essential framework or basic features of the Constitution (supremacy of Constitution, republican and democratic form, secularism, separation of powers, federalism, judicial review).
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** The Supreme Court did NOT define a comprehensive, exhaustive list of what constitutes the "Basic Structure", leaving it to be determined on a case-by-case basis!

### Question 374
**Subject:** General Science (Physics) | **Topic:** Doppler Effect in Sound and Light

Consider the following statements regarding the Doppler Effect:
1. The Doppler Effect is the apparent change in the observed frequency or wavelength of a wave due to relative motion between the source and the observer.
2. When a light-emitting star or galaxy moves away from Earth, its observed spectral lines shift towards longer wavelengths, known as 'Redshift'.
3. The Doppler Effect is utilized in speed radar guns, echocardiography, weather radars, and proving the expansion of the universe.

Which of the statements given above are correct?

**Options:**
A. 1 and 2 only  
B. 2 and 3 only  
C. 1 and 3 only  
D. 1, 2 and 3  

**Answer:** D

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - All three statements are correct:
    - Formulated by Christian Doppler in 1842:
      - **Sound Waves:** When a train approaches an observer blowing its horn, apparent pitch/frequency is **HIGHER** ($f' > f$). When receding, apparent pitch is **LOWER** ($f' < f$).
      - **Light Waves:**
        - Source moving away $
ightarrow$ Wavelength increases $
ightarrow$ **Redshift** (Edwin Hubble used redshift of distant galaxies in 1929 to prove the Universe is expanding).
        - Source moving closer $
ightarrow$ Wavelength decreases $
ightarrow$ **Blueshift**.
    - **Applications:** Police traffic RADAR guns, Doppler Weather Radar (cyclone tracking), Medical Doppler Ultrasonography (blood flow measurement).
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** Redshift means wave is moving AWAY; Blueshift means wave is moving CLOSER!

### Question 376
**Subject:** Modern Indian History | **Topic:** Landmark Peasant Satyagrahas Chronology

Arrange the following peasant movements in chronological order:
1. Indigo Revolt (Neel Bidroha) in Bengal
2. Deccan Riots in Maharashtra
3. Champaran Satyagraha in Bihar
4. Bardoli Satyagraha in Gujarat

Select the correct answer from the codes given below:

**Options:**
A. 1 — 2 — 3 — 4  
B. 2 — 1 — 3 — 4  
C. 1 — 3 — 2 — 4  
D. 1 — 2 — 4 — 3  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  1. **Indigo Revolt (1859–1860):** Started in Nadia, Bengal, led by Digambar Biswas and Bishnu Biswas against oppressive European indigo planters.
  2. **Deccan Riots (1875):** Agrarian uprising in Pune and Ahmednagar districts of Maharashtra against Marwari and Gujarati moneylenders (*Sahukars*); led to the Deccan Agriculturists' Relief Act, 1879.
  3. **Champaran Satyagraha (1917):** First Civil Disobedience in India led by Mahatma Gandhi against the Tinkathia indigo system.
  4. **Bardoli Satyagraha (1928):** Led by **Vallabhbhai Patel** against a 22% unjust enhancement of land revenue in Surat district; its success earned Patel the title of **"Sardar"** conferred by the women of Bardoli.
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** Mahatma Gandhi conferred the title "Sardar" on Vallabhbhai Patel on behalf of the women of Bardoli!

---

### Question 377
**Subject:** Indian Polity | **Topic:** Constitutional Amendment Procedure (Article 368)

Which of the following constitutional provisions requires an amendment by a **Special Majority of Parliament AND Ratification by not less than one-half of the State Legislatures** by simple majority?
1. Election of the President and its manner (Articles 54 & 55)
2. Extent of the executive power of the Union and the States (Articles 73 & 162)
3. Representation of States in Parliament
4. Any of the Lists in the Seventh Schedule

Select the correct answer from the codes given below:

**Options:**
A. 1, 2 and 3 only  
B. 1, 3 and 4 only  
C. 2, 3 and 4 only  
D. 1, 2, 3 and 4  

**Answer:** D

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - Under Article 368(2), federal provisions can be amended ONLY by a **Special Majority of Parliament** (majority of total membership of each House and $\ge rac{2}{3}$ of members present & voting) **PLUS Ratification by the legislatures of at least 50% of the States** by simple majority.
  - **Entrenched Federal Provisions:**
    - Election of President and manner (Articles 54, 55).
    - Extent of executive power of Union and States (Articles 73, 162).
    - Supreme Court and High Courts (Part V Chap IV, Part VI Chap V, Article 241).
    - Distribution of legislative powers (Seventh Schedule Lists).
    - Representation of States in Parliament (Fourth Schedule).
    - Provisions of Article 368 itself!
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** Fundamental Rights (Part III) and DPSPs (Part IV) require ONLY a Special Majority of Parliament, and DO NOT require ratification by States!

2. Meghalaya (Garo Hills); gene sanctuary for wild citrus species (*Citrus indica*)  
3. Madhya Pradesh and Chhattisgarh border; source of Narmada, Son, and Johilla rivers  
4. Western Ghats (Kerala and Tamil Nadu border); home to the ancient Kanikkar tribe  

**Options:**
A. A-2, B-1, C-4, D-3  
B. A-1, B-2, C-4, D-3  
C. A-2, B-4, C-1, D-3  
D. A-3, B-1, C-4, D-2  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - **Nokrek (Garo Hills, Meghalaya):** Located on the highest peak of Garo Hills (1,412 m); global gene sanctuary for wild mandarin orange (*Citrus indica*); red panda habitat.
  - **Similipal (Mayurbhanj, Odisha):** Encompasses pristine sal forests; only habitat of unique wild **melanistic (black) tigers**; part of UNESCO MAB.
  - **Agasthyamalai (Kerala/TN):** Home to over 2,000 species of medicinal plants and the indigenous Kanikkar hunter-gatherer tribe.
  - **Achanakmar-Amarkantak:** Straddles MP (Anuppur/Dindori) and Chhattisgarh (Bilaspur); crucial watershed separating drainage into Arabian Sea (Narmada), Bay of Bengal (Mahanadi), and Ganga (Son).
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** Cold Desert Biosphere Reserve is located in **Himachal Pradesh** (Pin Valley, Chandratal, Sarchu, Kibber)! Dehang-Debang is in **Arunachal Pradesh**; Dihru-Saikhowa is in **Assam**!

### Question 379
**Subject:** General Science (Biology) | **Topic:** Pineal & Thymus Gland Physiology

Consider the following statements regarding the human endocrine glands:
1. The Pineal gland secretes the hormone 'Melatonin', which regulates the 24-hour diurnal rhythm (sleep-wake cycle) and body temperature.
2. The Thymus gland secretes 'Thymosins', which play a major role in the differentiation and maturation of T-lymphocytes (cell-mediated immunity).
3. The Thymus gland is large in infants and children, but progressively degenerates and atrophies in old age, leading to weakened immune responses in the elderly.

Which of the statements given above are correct?

**Options:**
A. 1 and 2 only  
B. 2 and 3 only  
C. 1 and 3 only  
D. 1, 2 and 3  

**Answer:** D

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - All three statements are correct:
    - **Pineal Gland:** Located on the dorsal side of the forebrain; called the "third eye"; secretes **Melatonin** in darkness (inhibited by light); regulates the sleep-wake cycle, metabolism, skin pigmentation, and menstrual cycles.
    - **Thymus Gland:** Lobular gland located behind the sternum on the ventral side of the aorta; acts as the primary lymphoid organ where **T-lymphocytes mature and acquire immunological competence** ("Training School of T-cells").
    - **Age Atrophy:** Unlike other endocrine glands, the thymus shrinks steadily after puberty and is largely replaced by adipose tissue in old age, which explains the heightened susceptibility of senior citizens to infectious diseases.
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** T-cells mature in the **Thymus**; B-cells mature in the **Bone Marrow**!

### Question 381
**Subject:** Environment & Ecology | **Topic:** Polar Stratospheric Clouds & Ozone Hole

Given below are two statements, one is labelled as Assertion (A) and the other as Reason (R):

**Assertion (A):** The Antarctic ozone hole appears prominently each year in the months of September and October (Antarctic spring).  
**Reason (R):** In the extreme winter cold of Antarctica, Polar Stratospheric Clouds (PSCs) provide surfaces for stable chlorine reservoirs to convert into reactive chlorine radicals, which are rapidly released by sunlight in spring to trigger catalytic ozone destruction.

Select the correct answer from the codes given below:

**Options:**
A. Both (A) and (R) are true and (R) is the correct explanation of (A)  
B. Both (A) and (R) are true, but (R) is not the correct explanation of (A)  
C. (A) is true, but (R) is false  
D. (A) is false, but (R) is true  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - **Antarctic Winter (June–August):** Circum-polar vortex isolates Antarctic air; temperatures plunge below $-80^\circ 	ext{C}$, forming Type-I (nitric acid trihydrate) and Type-II (water ice) **Polar Stratospheric Clouds (PSCs / Nacreous clouds)**.
  - Inactive chlorine reservoirs ($HCl$ and $ClONO_2$) react on PSC ice crystal surfaces to release molecular chlorine ($Cl_2$) and $HOCl$.
  - **Spring (September–October):** Sunlight returns; UV photolyzes $Cl_2$ into free **Chlorine free radicals ($Cl^ullet$)**. A single chlorine radical can catalytically destroy over **100,000 ozone molecules**:
    $$Cl^ullet + O_3 
ightarrow ClO^ullet + O_2$$
    $$ClO^ullet + O 
ightarrow Cl^ullet + O_2$$
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** Ozone hole forms primarily over **ANTARCTICA** (South Pole) rather than the Arctic because Antarctica has much colder winter temperatures and a stable, isolated circumpolar vortex!

### Question 382
**Subject:** Indian Economy | **Topic:** Currency Devaluation vs Depreciation

Match List-I (Currency Adjustment) with List-II (Mechanism & Governing Regime) and select the correct answer:

**List-I (Term)**
A. Currency Devaluation  
B. Currency Depreciation  
C. Currency Appreciation  
D. J-Curve Effect  

**List-II (Mechanism & Regime)**
1. Fall in the exchange value of domestic currency determined by market forces of demand and supply in a floating exchange regime  
2. Deliberate official downward reduction in the value of domestic currency by the central bank under a fixed exchange rate regime  
3. Phenomenon where a currency depreciation worsens the trade balance initially before improving it in the medium term  
4. Increase in the external value of domestic currency due to market demand under a floating exchange regime  

**Options:**
A. A-2, B-1, C-4, D-3  
B. A-1, B-2, C-4, D-3  
C. A-2, B-4, C-1, D-3  
D. A-3, B-1, C-4, D-2  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - **Devaluation:** Deliberate governmental reduction of currency value against foreign currencies under a **Fixed / Pegged Exchange Rate** (India devalued the rupee in 1949, 1966, and 1991).
  - **Depreciation:** Automatic reduction of currency value driven by market forces of demand and supply in a **Floating / Flexible Exchange Rate** system.
  - **J-Curve Effect:** Following currency depreciation, export volumes take time to expand while import prices rise immediately, so the trade deficit initially widens before reversing into surplus, tracing a 'J' curve.
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** Devaluation is an OFFICIAL GOVT ACTION under a fixed regime; Depreciation is a MARKET DRIVEN PHENOMENON under a floating regime!

### Question 384

2. It conclusively identifies Lumbini as the historical birthplace of the Gautama Buddha (*Hida Budhe Jate Sakyamuniti*).
3. Ashoka exempted the village of Lumbini from religious tax (*Bali*) and reduced the land revenue tax (*Bhaga*) to one-eighth ($rac{1}{8}	ext{th}$).

D. 1, 2 and 3  

**Answer:** D

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - All three statements are correct:
    - **Rummindei Pillar (Lumbini, Nepal):** Erected in the 20th regnal year of Ashoka (c. 249 BCE) during his imperial pilgrimage with guru Upagupta.
    - **Inscriptional Text:** States: *"King Devanampiya Piyadasi visited in person and worshipped this stone because the Buddha, the sage of the Sakyas, was born here."*

### Question 385
**Subject:** World Geography | **Topic:** World Deserts and Hyper-Arid Basins

Match List-I (Desert) with List-II (Country & Geographical Feature) and select the correct answer:

**List-I (Desert)**
A. Atacama Desert  
B. Dasht-e Kavir  
C. Rub' al Khali ("Empty Quarter")  
D. Taklamakan Desert  

**List-II (Location & Feature)**
1. Arabian Peninsula (Saudi Arabia/UAE); world's largest contiguous sand desert  
2. Northern Chile; driest non-polar desert on Earth; rich in sodium nitrate deposits  
3. Tarim Basin, Xinjiang (China); cold desert bordered by Tian Shan and Kunlun Mountains  
4. Central Iran; great salt desert (*Kavir*) with extensive salt crusts  

**Options:**
A. A-2, B-4, C-1, D-3  
B. A-1, B-4, C-2, D-3  
C. A-2, B-1, C-4, D-3  
D. A-3, B-4, C-1, D-2  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - **Atacama Desert (Chile):** Rainless for decades; weather station at Arica recorded zero rainfall over a 14-year period; major source of iodine, lithium, and sodium nitrates.
  - **Dasht-e Kavir & Dasht-e Lut:** Deserts of the Iranian plateau; Dasht-e Kavir is a great salt desert; Dasht-e Lut recorded some of the highest land surface temperatures on Earth (~70.7°C).
  - **Rub' al Khali:** "Empty Quarter"; occupies southern Arabian peninsula; vast shifting sand dunes (*Erg*).
  - **Taklamakan:** Shifting sand desert in Tarim Basin (China); name means "you enter and you will not exit".

### Question 386
**Subject:** Indian Polity | **Topic:** Attorney General for India (Article 76 & Article 88)

Consider the following statements regarding the Attorney General for India:
1. The Attorney General is appointed by the President and must be a person qualified to be appointed a Judge of the Supreme Court.
2. The Attorney General holds office during the pleasure of the President and receives such remuneration as the President may determine.
3. Under Article 88, the Attorney General has the right to speak and take part in the proceedings of both Houses of Parliament, but does NOT possess the right to vote.
4. The Attorney General is a full-time government servant and is completely debarred from private legal practice.

Which of the statements given above are correct?

**Options:**
A. 1, 2 and 3 only  
B. 1, 3 and 4 only  
C. 2, 3 and 4 only  
D. 1, 2, 3 and 4  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - Statements 1, 2, and 3 are correct: Article 76 provides for the highest law officer of the country; appointed by the President; holds office during presidential pleasure (no fixed tenure; convention resigns when government resigns). Under **Article 88**, can speak in LS, RS, and joint sittings, and be named a member of any committee, but **CANNOT vote**.
  - Statement 4 is incorrect: The Attorney General is **NOT a whole-time government servant** (not classified as a civil servant); the AG is **NOT debarred from private legal practice**, provided he does not advise or plead against the Government of India, nor defend accused persons in criminal prosecutions without permission.
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** Solicitor General of India and Additional Solicitors General are STATUTORY officers, NOT mentioned in the Constitution (Article 76 mentions only the Attorney General)!

### Question 387
**Subject:** Medieval Indian History | **Topic:** Amir Khusrau's Historical Masnavis

Match List-I (Amir Khusrau's Literary Work) with List-II (Theme / Subject Matter) and select the correct answer:

**List-I (Masnavi)**
A. Qiran-us-Sadain (1289)  
B. Miftah-ul-Futuh (1291)  
C. Khazain-ul-Futuh (Tariq-i-Alai)  
D. Nuh Sipihr (1318)  

**List-II (Theme)**
1. Poetic celebration of India's flora, fauna, climate, sciences, and languages under Mubarak Shah Khalji  
2. First historical poem describing the emotional reunion of Sultan Kaiqubad with his father Bughra Khan  
3. Military campaigns and victories of Jalaluddin Khalji  
4. Detailed prose account of Alauddin Khalji's Deccan campaigns led by Malik Kafur  

**Options:**
A. A-2, B-3, C-4, D-1  
B. A-1, B-3, C-4, D-2  
C. A-2, B-4, C-3, D-1  
D. A-3, B-2, C-1, D-4  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - **Qiran-us-Sadain ("Meeting of Two Auspicious Stars"):** Composed under Kaiqubad; describes the reconciliation between Sultan Kaiqubad and his father Bughra Khan (governor of Bengal) on the banks of Sarayu at Ayodhya.
  - **Miftah-ul-Futuh ("Key to Victories"):** Chronicles the conquests of Sultan Jalaluddin Khalji.
  - **Khazain-ul-Futuh:** Sole prose work; official record of Alauddin Khalji's northern conquests and Malik Kafur's southern campaigns (Warangal, Dwarasamudra, Madurai).
  - **Nuh Sipihr ("Nine Skies"):** Celebrates the superiority of India over all other lands; praises India's peacocks, flowers, chess, mathematics (zero/decimal), and Sanskrit language.
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** *Ashiqa* (or *Ishqia*) narrates the tragic romance between Khizr Khan (Alauddin's son) and Deval Devi (princess of Gujarat)!

**Subject:** General Science (Chemistry) | **Topic:** Industrial Polymers and Elastomers

Match List-I (Polymer / Plastic) with List-II (Monomer / Chemical Category) and select the correct answer:

A. Neoprene  
B. Buna-S  
C. Polystyrene (Thermocol)  
D. Melamine  

**List-II (Monomer / Composition)**
1. Styrene; packaging material, disposable cups, and thermal insulation  
2. Chloroprene (2-chloro-1,3-butadiene); synthetic rubber resistant to oils  
3. Copolymer of Melamine and Formaldehyde; unbreakable crockery  
4. Copolymer of 1,3-Butadiene and Styrene; automobile tire treads  

**Options:**
A. A-2, B-4, C-1, D-3  
B. A-1, B-4, C-2, D-3  
C. A-2, B-1, C-4, D-3  
D. A-3, B-2, C-1, D-4  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**

  - **Neoprene:** Synthetic rubber formed by polymerization of **Chloroprene**; superior resistance to oils, solvents, and heat; used in gaskets and conveyor belts.

2. Awadhi; *Ramcharitmanas* and *Kavitavali*

### Question 390
**Subject:** Environment & Ecology | **Topic:** Hydrarch Ecological Succession

Arrange the seral stages of **Hydrarch Succession** (succession starting in water) in correct chronological order (from pioneer to climax community):
1. Submerged plant stage (e.g. *Hydrilla, Vallisneria*)
2. Phytoplankton stage (pioneer: blue-green algae, diatoms)
3. Reed-swamp stage (e.g. *Typha, Phragmites*)
4. Floating-leaved plant stage (e.g. *Nymphaea, Nelumbo, Eichhornia*)
5. Climax Forest stage

Select the correct answer from the codes given below:

**Options:**
A. 2 — 1 — 4 — 3 — 5  
B. 1 — 2 — 4 — 3 — 5  
C. 2 — 4 — 1 — 3 — 5  
D. 2 — 1 — 3 — 4 — 5  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - Standard stages of Hydrosere (Hydrarch Succession):
    1. **Phytoplankton Stage (Pioneer):** Unicellular algae, diatoms, cyanobacteria.
    2. **Submerged Plant Stage:** Rooted submerged hydrophytes like *Hydrilla*, *Potamogeton*, *Vallisneria*.
    3. **Submerged Free-Floating Stage:** Rooted hydrophytes with floating leaves like *Nelumbo* (Lotus), *Nymphaea*, and free floaters like *Pistia*, *Azolla*.
    4. **Reed-Swamp Stage (Amphibious):** *Typha* (cattail), *Phragmites*; plants anchored in silt with shoots exposed to air.
    5. **Marsh-Meadow Stage:** Sedges like *Carex*, *Cyperus*.
    6. **Scrub / Woodland Stage:** Shrubs like *Salix*, *Cornus*.
    7. **Climax Forest Stage:** Mature mesic forest trees (oak, sal, teak).
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** Both Hydrarch succession (starting in water) and Xerarch succession (starting on dry rock) culminate in the same climax condition: a **MESIC (moderate moisture) community**!

### Question 391
**Subject:** Indian Economy | **Topic:** Standing Deposit Facility (SDF) and MSF

Consider the following statements regarding the liquidity management framework of the Reserve Bank of India:
1. The Standing Deposit Facility (SDF) serves as the floor of the Liquidity Adjustment Facility (LAF) corridor.
2. The SDF allows the RBI to absorb overnight liquidity from commercial banks without having to pledge government securities as collateral.
3. The Marginal Standing Facility (MSF) rate serves as the ceiling of the LAF corridor, through which banks can borrow overnight funds against their excess SLR quota.

Which of the statements given above are correct?

**Options:**
A. 1 and 2 only  
B. 2 and 3 only  
C. 1 and 3 only  
D. 1, 2 and 3  

**Answer:** D

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - All three statements are correct:
    - **LAF Corridor Architecture (Width = 50 bps):**
      - **Ceiling (Repo Rate + 25 bps):** **Marginal Standing Facility (MSF)** rate and Bank Rate (overnight penal borrowing window for banks dipping into SLR).
      - **Center:** **Policy Repo Rate** (key operational rate).
      - **Floor (Repo Rate - 25 bps):** **Standing Deposit Facility (SDF)** (uncollateralized liquidity absorption window introduced in April 2022 under amended Section 17 of RBI Act 1934).
    - SDF replaced the fixed-rate Reverse Repo as the primary liquidity absorption tool because it does not lock up the RBI's sovereign bond portfolio as collateral!
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** In the LAF corridor: **MSF is at the TOP (Ceiling), Repo Rate is in the MIDDLE, and SDF is at the BOTTOM (Floor)**!

### Question 392

2. Kazhuveli Bird Sanctuary (Tamil Nadu)
3. Tawa Reservoir (Madhya Pradesh)
4. Magadi Kere Conservation Reserve (Karnataka)

A. 1, 2 and 3 only  
B. 1, 2 and 4 only  
C. 2, 3 and 4 only  
D. 1, 2, 3 and 4

- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - All four sites were designated as Ramsar sites in 2024:

### Question 393
**Subject:** Modern Indian History | **Topic:** Commissions on Education in British India

Match List-I (Educational Commission / Despatch) with List-II (Year & Core Recommendation) and select the correct answer:

**List-I (Commission)**
A. Charles Wood's Despatch  
B. Hunter Commission  
C. Saddler University Commission  
D. Hartog Committee  

**List-II (Year & Recommendation)**
1. 1882; emphasized state responsibility for primary and secondary education; female education  
2. 1854; "Magna Carta of English Education in India"; recommended universities at Calcutta, Bombay, Madras  
3. 1929; focused on mass education and quality control, warning against hasty expansion of higher education  
4. 1917; investigated problems of Calcutta University; recommended 12-year school course (Intermediate)  

**Options:**
A. A-2, B-1, C-4, D-3  
B. A-1, B-2, C-4, D-3  
C. A-2, B-4, C-1, D-3  
D. A-3, B-1, C-4, D-2  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - **Wood's Despatch (1854):** Drafted by Sir Charles Wood under Lord Dalhousie; rejected "Downward Filtration Theory"; advocated vernacular primary schools, Anglo-vernacular high schools, grants-in-aid, and universities at Calcutta, Bombay, and Madras (established 1857).
  - **Hunter Education Commission (1882):** Appointed by Lord Ripon; recommended transfer of primary education management to newly formed district and municipal boards.
  - **Saddler Commission (1917):** Appointed by Lord Chelmsford; included Indian members Sir Asutosh Mukherjee and Dr. Ziauddin Ahmad; recommended creation of intermediate colleges and separation of secondary from university education.
  - **Hartog Committee (1929):** Auxiliary committee of Simon Commission; emphasized primary education consolidation to avoid "wastage and stagnation".
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** Raleigh Commission (1902) was under Curzon; Saddler Commission (1917) was under Chelmsford; Wardha Scheme (1937) was Mahatma Gandhi's *Nai Talim*!

### Question 394
**Subject:** Geography of India | **Topic:** Kaveri River Drainage and Waterfalls

Consider the following statements regarding the Kaveri (Cauvery) River:
1. The Kaveri originates at Talakaveri in the Brahmagiri Range of the Western Ghats in Kodagu (Coorg) district of Karnataka.
2. The river forms two sacred river islands in Karnataka: Srirangapatna and Shivanasamudra.
3. Hemavati, Shimsha, and Arkavathi are major left-bank tributaries, while Kabini, Bhavani, and Amravati are major right-bank tributaries of the Kaveri.

Which of the statements given above are correct?

**Options:**
A. 1 and 2 only  
B. 2 and 3 only  
C. 1 and 3 only  
D. 1, 2 and 3  

**Answer:** D

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - All three statements are correct:
    - **Course & Hydrology:** Length ~800 km; drains parts of Karnataka, Kerala, Tamil Nadu, and Puducherry. Because its upper catchment receives rainfall from South-West Monsoon (summer) and its lower catchment receives rainfall from North-East Monsoon (winter), the **Kaveri carries water almost throughout the year with less fluctuation than other peninsular rivers**.
    - **Islands:** Splitting into two branches, it forms 3 sacred islands: **Srirangapatna** and **Shivanasamudra** (in Karnataka), and **Srirangam** (in Tamil Nadu).
    - **Tributaries:**
      - *Left Bank:* Harangi, **Hemavati**, Lokapavani, **Shimsha**, **Arkavathi**.
      - *Right Bank:* Lakshmantirtha, **Kabini**, Suvarnavathi, **Bhavani**, Noyyal, **Amravati**.
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** Mettur Dam (Stanley Reservoir) is in Tamil Nadu on the Kaveri; Krishnarajasagara (KRS) Dam is in Karnataka on the Kaveri!

### Question 395
**Subject:** Indian Polity | **Topic:** Administrative Tribunals (Articles 323A and 323B)

Consider the following statements regarding Tribunals in the Indian Constitution:
1. Part XIV-A (Articles 323A and 323B) was added to the Constitution by the 42nd Amendment Act, 1976.
2. While Tribunals under Article 323A can be established ONLY by Parliament, Tribunals under Article 323B can be established by BOTH Parliament and State Legislatures.
3. In the L. Chandra Kumar case (1997), the Supreme Court ruled that decisions of Tribunals are subject to the judicial review of the High Court under Articles 226/227.

Which of the statements given above are correct?

**Options:**
A. 1 and 2 only  
B. 2 and 3 only  
C. 1 and 3 only  
D. 1, 2 and 3  

**Answer:** D

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - All three statements are correct:
    - **Added by 42nd CAA 1976:** Based on Swaran Singh Committee recommendations.
    - **Article 323A vs 323B:**
      - *Article 323A:* Deals ONLY with public service recruitment and service disputes of civil servants (Central Administrative Tribunal - CAT, set up under Administrative Tribunals Act 1985). Can be enacted **ONLY by Parliament**.
      - *Article 323B:* Deals with tribunals for other matters (taxation, foreign exchange, industrial disputes, land reforms, food stuffs). Can be established by **BOTH Parliament and State Legislatures** within their legislative competence.
    - **L. Chandra Kumar (1997):** Struck down clause (2)(d) of Article 323A and 323B that excluded the jurisdiction of High Courts; held that **Judicial Review under Articles 226/227 is an inviolable basic structure of the Constitution**; an aggrieved party must approach the Division Bench of the High Court before appealing to the Supreme Court.
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** Article 323A can be enacted ONLY by Parliament; Article 323B can be enacted by BOTH Parliament and State Legislatures!

### Question 396
**Subject:** Ancient Indian History | **Topic:** The 16 Hindu Samskaras (Sacraments)

Match List-I (Vedic Samskara) with List-II (Life Stage / Ritual Meaning) and select the correct answer:

**List-I (Samskara)**
A. Garbhadhana  
B. Pumsavana  
C. Upanayana  
D. Samavartana  

**List-II (Ritual & Meaning)**
1. Sacred thread ceremony marking initiation into formal Vedic education; rebirth as *Dvija*  
2. Conception ritual performed for invoking divine blessings for childbearing  
3. Ritual performed to ensure the birth of a male child and healthy fetus  
4. Convocation / graduation ceremony performed on completion of studenthood at Gurukula  

**Options:**
A. A-2, B-3, C-1, D-4  
B. A-3, B-2, C-1, D-4  
C. A-2, B-1, C-4, D-3  
D. A-1, B-3, C-2, D-4  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - The Grihyasutras systematize **16 Samskaras (Shodasha Samskaras)** governing Hindu life:
    1. *Garbhadhana* (Conception)
    2. *Pumsavana* (Fetus protection / quickening)
    3. *Simantonnayana* (Parting of hair of pregnant mother)
    4. *Jatakarma* (Birth ceremony, touching honey and ghee to lips)
    5. *Namakarana* (Naming ceremony on 10th/12th day)
    6. *Nishkramana* (First outing to see sun)
    7. *Annaprashana* (First solid food feeding at 6 months)
    8. *Chudakarana* / *Mundan* (First haircut)
    9. *Karnavedha* (Ear piercing)
    10. *Vidyarambha* (Learning alphabets)
    11. **Upanayana** (Sacred thread *Yajnopavita* initiation into Gayatri mantra; makes one *Dvija* - twice-born)
    12. *Vedarambha* (Beginning of Vedic study)
    13. *Keshanta* / *Godana* (First shaving of beard at age 16)
    14. **Samavartana** (Graduation bath *Snataka* returning home from Gurukula)
    15. *Vivaha* (Marriage - entering Grihastha ashrama)
    16. *Antyeshti* (Funeral rites).
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** Upanayana marks the start of Brahmacharya; Samavartana marks the graduation from Brahmacharya into Grihastha!

### Question 397
**Subject:** General Science (Physics) | **Topic:** Archimedes' Principle and Buoyancy

Consider the following statements regarding Archimedes' Principle:
1. Archimedes' Principle states that when a body is immersed fully or partially in a fluid, it experiences an upward buoyant force equal to the weight of the fluid displaced by it.
2. A Hydrometer used to determine the relative density of liquids operates on the principle of floatation derived from Archimedes' Principle.
3. A Lactometer used to determine the purity of milk operates based on Archimedes' Principle.

Which of the statements given above are correct?

**Options:**
A. 1 and 2 only  
B. 2 and 3 only  
C. 1 and 3 only  
D. 1, 2 and 3  

**Answer:** D

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - All three statements are correct:
    - **Archimedes' Principle:** Buoyant Force $F_B = 
ho_{	ext{fluid}} 	imes V_{	ext{submerged}} 	imes g$.
    - **Law of Floatation:** A floating body displaces fluid whose weight equals the total weight of the body ($W = F_B$).
    - **Hydrometer & Lactometer:** Both instruments are sealed glass tubes weighted at the bottom; sink deeper in less dense liquids and rise higher in denser liquids; calibrate density based on displaced liquid volume.
    - **Submarines and Ships:** Iron nail sinks because iron density > water density, but a massive steel ship floats because its hollow hull displaces a volume of water whose weight is far greater than the ship's weight.
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** When a floating ice cube in a glass of water melts completely, the **water level in the glass remains UNCHANGED**! (Because the volume of water produced by melting equals the volume of submerged ice).

3. District with the highest percentage of forest cover: Sonbhadra (approx. 35.24%)
4. District with the lowest percentage of forest cover: Bhadohi (approx. 0.37%)

How many of the above pairs are correctly matched?

A. Only two pairs  
B. Only three pairs  
C. All four pairs  
D. Only one pair  

**Answer:** C

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - All four pairs are perfectly accurate based on the Forest Survey of India (ISFR):
    - **Maximum Forest Area:** **Sonbhadra (2,536.40 sq km)**.
    - **Minimum Forest Area:** **Bhadohi (3.71 sq km)**.
    - **Maximum Forest Cover %:** **Sonbhadra (35.24%)**.

Which of the following endemic mammal species is natively found in the **Western Ghats Biodiversity Hotspot**?

2. Nilgiri Tahr (*Nilgiritragus hylocrius*)
3. Malabar Civet (*Viverra civettina*)

Select the correct answer from the codes given below:

**Options:**
A. 1 and 2 only  
B. 2 and 3 only  
C. 1 and 3 only  
D. 1, 2 and 3  

**Answer:** D

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - All three mammals are endemic flagship species of the **Western Ghats (Sahyadri)**:
    - **Lion-tailed Macaque:** Endangered arboreal primate with a silver-white mane; inhabits evergreen rainforest canopies of Silent Valley (Kerala) and Anaimalai.
    - **Nilgiri Tahr:** Endangered mountain ungulate; state animal of Tamil Nadu; inhabits montane shola grasslands of Eravikulam National Park (Munnar).
    - **Malabar Civet:** Critically endangered carnivore endemic to the coastal lowland forests of Western Ghats.
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** The Great Indian Bustard is endemic to arid grasslands of **Thar Desert (Rajasthan)**; Sangai (Dancing Deer) is endemic to **Loktak Lake (Manipur)**!

### Question 400
**Subject:** Indian Economy | **Topic:** Tendulkar vs Rangarajan Poverty Metrics

Consider the following statements comparing the Suresh Tendulkar Committee (2009) and C. Rangarajan Committee (2014) on poverty estimation in India:
1. Tendulkar Committee shifted the poverty line away from calorie norms to an explicit consumption basket covering food, education, and health.
2. Tendulkar Committee calculated the all-India poverty ratio at 21.9% for the year 2011–12 (Rs 816 per capita/month rural and Rs 1,000 urban).
3. Rangarajan Committee calculated the all-India poverty ratio at 29.5% for the year 2011–12 (Rs 972 per capita/month rural and Rs 1,407 urban).

Which of the statements given above are correct?

**Options:**
A. 1 and 2 only  
B. 2 and 3 only  
C. 1 and 3 only  
D. 1, 2 and 3  

**Answer:** D

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - All three statements are correct:
    - **Tendulkar Committee (2009):** Used Mixed Reference Period (MRP); estimated poverty line at **₹27.2 per day in rural areas and ₹33.3 per day in urban areas**; gave 2011–12 all-India poverty at **21.92% (approx. 27 crore people)**.
    - **Rangarajan Committee (2014):** Used Modified Mixed Reference Period (MMRP); accounted for nutritional requirements plus non-food essentials; estimated poverty line at **₹32 per day in rural areas and ₹47 per day in urban areas**; gave 2011–12 all-India poverty at **29.5% (approx. 36.3 crore people)**.
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** Tendulkar poverty line = **21.9%**; Rangarajan poverty line = **29.5%**! (Rangarajan poverty line was significantly higher than Tendulkar).
