### Question 402
**Subject:** Medieval Indian History | **Topic:** Mughal Revenue Assessment Systems

Match List-I (Mughal Revenue System) with List-II (Mechanism / Distinct Feature) and select the correct answer:

**List-I (System)**
A. Zabti / Dahsala System  
B. Batai / Ghalla-Bakhshi  
C. Kankut System  
D. Nasaq System  

**List-II (Mechanism)**
1. Rough estimation / summary assessment based on past village revenue payments  
2. Actual physical division of harvested crop between the peasant and the State (khet-batai, lang-batai, rasi-batai)  
3. Revenue assessment based on land measurement (*Paimaish* by bamboo jarib with iron rings) and average 10-year yield/cash rates  
4. Estimation of yield by inspecting standing crop and pacing the fields (*Kan* = estimate, *Kut* = calculate)  

**Options:**
A. A-3, B-2, C-4, D-1  
B. A-2, B-3, C-4, D-1  
C. A-3, B-4, C-2, D-1  
D. A-1, B-2, C-4, D-3  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - **Zabti (Dahsala, 1580):** Devised by Raja Todar Mal; land measured using *Ilahi Gaz* and *Tanab* (bamboo jarib with iron rings); average yields of preceding 10 years (1570–1580) calculated, State share fixed at $rac{1}{3}	ext{rd}$ in cash (*Dastur*).
  - **Batai / Ghalla-Bakhshi:** Simple crop-sharing; 3 methods: *Khet-batai* (dividing fields after sowing), *Lang-batai* (dividing sheaves after reaping), *Rasi-batai* (dividing grain heaps after threshing).
  - **Kankut:** Rough ocular estimate of produce based on inspecting standing crop.
  - **Nasaq:** Quick summary assessment based on previous years' revenue records.
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** Todar Mal's Bandobast categorized cultivable land into 4 classes: **Polaj** (annually cultivated), **Parauti** (left fallow 1–2 years), **Chachar** (fallow 3–4 years), **Banjar** (uncultivated for 5+ years)!

### Question 404
**Subject:** Indian Polity | **Topic:** Election Commission Safeguards & Removal

Consider the following statements regarding the Election Commission of India:
1. The Chief Election Commissioner (CEC) can be removed from office ONLY in like manner and on like grounds as a Judge of the Supreme Court.
2. Any other Election Commissioner or Regional Commissioner can be removed from office by the President ONLY on the recommendation of the Chief Election Commissioner.
3. The conditions of service and tenure of office of Election Commissioners are determined by Parliament by law.

Which of the statements given above are correct?

**Options:**
A. 1 and 2 only  
B. 2 and 3 only  
C. 1 and 3 only  
D. 1, 2 and 3  

**Answer:** D

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - All three statements are correct:
    - **CEC Removal (Proviso to Article 324(5)):** Enjoys absolute constitutional security of tenure; can be removed only by parliamentary impeachment (special majority in both Houses on grounds of proved misbehavior or incapacity).
    - **Other Election Commissioners Removal:** Do not enjoy the same constitutional insulation as the CEC; can be removed by the President **only on the recommendation of the CEC** (established in *T.N. Seshan v. Union of India*, 1995).
    - **Conditions of Service:** Mandated under Article 324(5) to be determined by Parliament (governed by the Chief Election Commissioner and other ECs Act, 2023).
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** The CEC CANNOT be removed at the pleasure of the President! But other Election Commissioners can be removed if the CEC formally recommends it.

### Question 405
**Subject:** General Science (Biology) | **Topic:** Plant Pathologies and Causative Microorganisms

Match List-I (Plant Disease) with List-II (Causative Pathogen & Crop) and select the correct answer:

**List-I (Disease)**
A. Citrus Canker  
B. Late Blight of Potato  
C. Black Rust of Wheat  
D. Red Rot of Sugarcane  

**List-II (Pathogen & Crop)**
1. *Phytophthora infestans* (Fungus); causes rot of potato tubers (triggered Irish Famine 1845)  
2. *Xanthomonas axonopodis* (Bacterium); lesions on citrus leaves, twigs, and fruits  
3. *Colletotrichum falcatum* (Fungus); red discoloration of pith and alcoholic odor in sugarcane  
4. *Puccinia graminis tritici* (Fungus); brown/black pustules on wheat stems  

**Options:**
A. A-2, B-1, C-4, D-3  
B. A-1, B-2, C-4, D-3  
C. A-2, B-4, C-1, D-3  
D. A-3, B-1, C-4, D-2  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - **Citrus Canker:** Caused by **bacterium** *Xanthomonas*; transmitted by wind and rain splash; affects acid lime and sweet oranges.
  - **Late Blight of Potato:** Caused by **fungus-like oomycete** *Phytophthora infestans*; wiped out the Irish potato crop in 1845 causing the catastrophic Great Irish Famine.
  - **Black Rust of Wheat:** Caused by heteroecious **rust fungus** *Puccinia graminis*; alternate host is the Barberry plant (*Berberis*). Prof. K.C. Mehta of Agra College discovered the annual recurrence cycle of rust in India.
  - **Red Rot of Sugarcane:** Caused by **fungus** *Colletotrichum falcatum*; vascular bundles turn red with white cross-bands, smelling of sour alcohol.
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** Citrus Canker is **BACTERIAL**; Late Blight, Black Rust, and Red Rot are **FUNGAL**!

### Question 407
**Subject:** Environment & Ecology | **Topic:** Kyoto Protocol Greenhouse Gases

Which of the following greenhouse gases was NOT among the original six greenhouse gases targeted under the Kyoto Protocol (1997)?

**Options:**
A. Carbon Dioxide ($CO_2$)  
B. Methane ($CH_4$)  
C. Chlorofluorocarbons (CFCs)  
D. Sulphur Hexafluoride ($SF_6$)  

**Answer:** C

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - The 6 original greenhouse gases controlled under **Annex A of the Kyoto Protocol** (1997) were:
    1. Carbon Dioxide ($CO_2$)
    2. Methane ($CH_4$)
    3. Nitrous Oxide ($N_2O$)
    4. Hydrofluorocarbons (HFCs)
    5. Perfluorocarbons (PFCs)
    6. Sulphur Hexafluoride ($SF_6$)
  - **Chlorofluorocarbons (CFCs)** were NOT included in the Kyoto Protocol because they were already regulated and being phased out under the **Montreal Protocol on Substances that Deplete the Ozone Layer (1987)**!
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** Classic UPPSC trap: CFCs are Ozone-Depleting Substances regulated by the Montreal Protocol, NOT the Kyoto Protocol!

### Question 409
**Subject:** Indian Economy | **Topic:** Current Account vs Capital Account Items

Which of the following items is recorded under the **CAPITAL ACCOUNT** of India's Balance of Payments (BoP)?
1. External Commercial Borrowings (ECB)
2. Foreign Direct Investment (FDI) equity inflows
3. Non-Resident Indian (NRI) bank deposits
4. Inward remittances from Indian workers in Gulf countries

Select the correct answer from the codes given below:

**Options:**
A. 1, 2 and 3 only  
B. 1, 2 and 4 only  
C. 2, 3 and 4 only  
D. 1, 2, 3 and 4  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - **Capital Account Items (Items creating or liquidating foreign assets and liabilities):**
    - **FDI and FPI** (equity and debt)
    - **External Commercial Borrowings (ECBs)** and external sovereign debt
    - **NRI Deposits** (FCNR, NRE accounts held in Indian banks)
    - Banking capital and short-term trade credits.
  - **Current Account Items (Items affecting current output/income, without creating claims on future assets):**
    - Merchandise trade (exports and imports of goods)
    - Invisibles (software services, transportation, travel)
    - **Unilateral Transfers / Inward Remittances** (remittances sent home by NRIs in Gulf/USA are private transfers in the Current Account!).
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** Remittances sent by Indian workers abroad are recorded in the **CURRENT ACCOUNT** (under Invisibles / Transfers), NOT the Capital Account!

### Question 410
**Subject:** Current Affairs | **Topic:** Manu Bhaker's Historic Double Bronze at Paris 2024

Consider the following statements regarding shooter Manu Bhaker at the Paris 2024 Olympic Games:
1. She won the Bronze medal in the Women's 10m Air Pistol individual event.
2. She won another Bronze medal in the 10m Air Pistol Mixed Team event partnering Sarabjot Singh.
3. She became the first athlete from independent India to win two medals at the same Olympic Games.

Which of the statements given above are correct?

**Options:**
A. 1 and 2 only  
B. 2 and 3 only  
C. 1 and 3 only  
D. 1, 2 and 3  

**Answer:** D

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - All three statements are correct:
    - 22-year-old **Manu Bhaker** (from Jhajjar, Haryana) scripted Indian Olympic history in Paris:
      - Opened India's medal account by winning Bronze in **Women's 10m Air Pistol** on July 28, 2024 (first Indian woman shooter ever to win an Olympic medal).
      - Won her second Bronze in **10m Air Pistol Mixed Team** on July 30, 2024, alongside **Sarabjot Singh**.
      - In pre-independence India, Norman Pritchard had won two silver medals in athletics in Paris 1900; Manu Bhaker is the **first athlete representing independent India** to achieve this milestone.
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** She finished 4th in her third event (Women's 25m Pistol), narrowly missing a historic third medal!

### Question 411
**Subject:** Ancient Indian History | **Topic:** Harappan Ports and Coastal Entrepots

Match List-I (Harappan Coastal Site) with List-II (State / Region & Distinct Feature) and select the correct answer:

**List-I (Site)**
A. Lothal  
B. Suktagendor  
C. Malwan  
D. Balakot  

**List-II (Location & Significance)**
1. Southernmost coastal Harappan site in Surat district, Gujarat (Tapti estuary)  
2. Lasbela coast, Pakistan; specialized center for sea-shell bangle and bead manufacturing  
3. Gulf of Khambhat, Gujarat; world's earliest known tidal dockyard connected to Bhogava river  
4. Makran coast, Baluchistan (near Iran border); westernmost outpost of the Harappan civilization  

**Options:**
A. A-3, B-4, C-1, D-2  
B. A-1, B-4, C-3, D-2  
C. A-3, B-1, C-4, D-2  
D. A-4, B-3, C-1, D-2  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - **Lothal (Gujarat):** Premier port and dockyard of Harappan civilization; engaged in maritime trade with Dilmun (Bahrain) and Magan (Oman).
  - **Suktagendor (Baluchistan):** Westernmost extremity of the Harappan world, situated on the Dasht river; fortified coastal trading outpost.
  - **Malwan (Gujarat):** Located near the mouth of Tapti river; southernmost mature Harappan site in Gujarat.
  - **Balakot (Pakistan):** Located on Sonmiani Bay; major sea-shell harvesting and craft manufacturing hub (*Turbinella pyrum*).
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** Easternmost Harappan site = **Alamgirpur (Hindon river, Meerut, UP)**; Westernmost = **Suktagendor (Dasht river, Baluchistan)**; Northernmost = **Manda (Chenab river, J&K)**; Southernmost = **Daimabad (Pravara river, Maharashtra)**!

### Question 412
**Subject:** World Geography | **Topic:** African Great Lakes and the Equator

Through which of the following Great Lakes of Africa does the **Equator ($0^\circ$)** pass directly?

**Options:**
A. Lake Tanganyika  
B. Lake Victoria  
C. Lake Malawi (Nyasa)  
D. Lake Chad  

**Answer:** B

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - **Lake Victoria:**
    - Largest lake in Africa and second largest freshwater lake in the world by surface area (~68,800 sq km).
    - **The Equator passes directly across the northern section of Lake Victoria**.
    - Bordered by 3 nations: **Uganda, Kenya, and Tanzania**.
    - Chief reservoir of the White Nile river.
  - **Lake Tanganyika:** Longest freshwater lake in the world (~673 km) and second deepest (~1,470 m); lies entirely in the Southern Hemisphere (south of Equator).
  - **Lake Malawi (Nyasa):** Located south of Tanganyika in the East African Rift Valley.
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** Lake Victoria is NOT situated in the rift valley trench itself, but occupies a shallow tectonic depression between the Eastern and Western rift branches!

### Question 413
**Subject:** Indian Polity | **Topic:** Preamble Dates and Concepts

Consider the following statements regarding the Preamble of the Indian Constitution:
1. The date of adoption of the Constitution mentioned in the Preamble is "the twenty-sixth day of November, 1949".
2. The ideals of 'Liberty, Equality, and Fraternity' in our Preamble were inspired by the French Revolution (1789).
3. The ideal of 'Justice — social, economic, and political' was inspired by the Russian Revolution (1917).

Which of the statements given above are correct?

**Options:**
A. 1 and 2 only  
B. 2 and 3 only  
C. 1 and 3 only  
D. 1, 2 and 3  

**Answer:** D

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - All three statements are correct:
    - **Date:** Concludes: *"In our Constituent Assembly this twenty-sixth day of November, 1949, do hereby Adopt, Enact and Give to ourselves this Constitution."* (Certain provisions like citizenship, elections, provisional parliament came into force on 26 Nov 1949; the full Constitution took effect on **26 January 1950** to commemorate Purna Swaraj Day 1930).
    - **Inspirations:**
      - **Justice (Social, Economic, Political):** Drawn from the **1917 Russian Revolution**.
      - **Liberty, Equality, Fraternity:** Drawn from the **1789 French Revolution**.
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** The Preamble has been amended **ONLY ONCE** in history: by the 42nd Constitutional Amendment Act, 1976!

### Question 414
**Subject:** General Science (Physics) | **Topic:** Thermodynamics of Everyday Thermal Phenomena

Match List-I (Thermal Phenomenon) with List-II (Physical Principle Involved) and select the correct answer:

**List-I (Phenomenon)**
A. Cooling produced in an earthen pitcher (*Surahi / Matka*)  
B. Severe burns caused by steam compared to boiling water at 100°C  
C. Bursting of water pipes in sub-zero winter temperatures  
D. Quicker cooking of food in a pressure cooker  

**List-II (Principle)**
1. Anomalous volume expansion of water upon freezing from 4°C to 0°C  
2. Latent heat of vaporization ($~540 	ext{ cal/g}$)  
3. Cooling caused by continuous evaporation through microscopic clay pores  
4. Elevated boiling point of water under increased chamber pressure ($> 100^\circ 	ext{C}$)  

**Options:**
A. A-3, B-2, C-1, D-4  
B. A-2, B-3, C-1, D-4  
C. A-3, B-1, C-2, D-4  
D. A-4, B-2, C-1, D-3  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - **Matka Cooling:** Water seeps through unglazed pores; evaporates by taking latent heat of vaporization from the remaining water, cooling it down.
  - **Steam Burns:** Steam at 100°C contains an extra $2.26 	imes 10^6 	ext{ J/kg}$ of latent heat compared to water at 100°C.
  - **Bursting Pipes:** Water expands by ~9% upon freezing into ice; in enclosed pipes, this expansion exerts immense hydrostatic pressure.
  - **Pressure Cooker:** Increases internal pressure to ~2 atmospheres, raising the boiling point of water from 100°C to **~120°C**, allowing food to cook much faster.
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** At high altitudes (mountains), atmospheric pressure is low, so water boils at LESS than 100°C, making cooking slower!

Match List-I (Bordering State) with List-II (UP District Bordering It) and select the correct answer:

D. A-3, B-1, C-4, D-2  

**Answer:** A

### Question 416
**Subject:** Environment & Ecology | **Topic:** Wildlife (Protection) Act 1972 Historic Roots

Consider the following statements regarding the Wildlife (Protection) Act, 1972:
1. It was enacted under Article 252 of the Constitution after several State Legislatures passed resolutions authorizing Parliament to legislate on wildlife.
2. Prior to the 42nd Amendment Act 1976, 'Wild animals and birds' was an exclusive subject under the State List (List II).
3. The National Board for Wildlife (NBWL) is a statutory body constituted under Section 5A of the Wildlife (Protection) Act, chaired by the Prime Minister.

Which of the statements given above are correct?

**Options:**
A. 1 and 2 only  
B. 2 and 3 only  
C. 1 and 3 only  
D. 1, 2 and 3  

**Answer:** D

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - All three statements are correct:
    - Enacted in September 1972 under Article 252 (because 11 state assemblies passed authorizing resolutions; prior to 1976, wildlife was Entry 20 of State List).
    - The 42nd Amendment Act 1976 shifted it to the **Concurrent List (Entry 17B)**.
    - **NBWL (Section 5A):** Apex statutory body for wildlife policy; **chaired by the Prime Minister of India** (Union Environment Minister is Vice-Chairman).
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** National Board for Wildlife is chaired by the **PRIME MINISTER**, NOT the Environment Minister!

### Question 417
**Subject:** Indian Economy | **Topic:** CPI Inflation Weights Breakdown

In the Consumer Price Index for Combined (CPI-C, Base 2012) published by NSO, arrange the following commodity groups in descending order of their assigned weightage:
1. Food and Beverages
2. Housing
3. Fuel and Light
4. Pan, tobacco and intoxicants

Select the correct answer from the codes given below:

**Options:**
A. 1 — 2 — 3 — 4  
B. 1 — 3 — 2 — 4  
C. 2 — 1 — 3 — 4  
D. 1 — 2 — 4 — 3  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - Weights in CPI (Combined Base 2012 = 100):
    1. **Food and Beverages:** **45.86%** (Cereals 9.67%, Milk 6.61%, Vegetables 6.04%).
    2. **Miscellaneous (Services):** **28.32%** (Transport, Health, Education).
    3. **Housing:** **10.07%** (applies only to urban CPI).
    4. **Fuel and Light:** **6.84%** (LPG, electricity, kerosene).
    5. **Clothing and Footwear:** **6.53%**.
    6. **Pan, tobacco and intoxicants:** **2.38%**.
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** Food and Beverages has the LARGEST weight in CPI (~45.86%); Housing has ~10.07%; Fuel & Light has ~6.84%!

B. Iran  
C. Mongolia

**Answer:** A

- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - **Belarus** was officially admitted as the 10th full member state of the SCO at the Astana Summit in Kazakhstan on July 4, 2024.
  - Chronology of SCO membership:
    - 2001 (Founding 6): China, Russia, Kazakhstan, Kyrgyzstan, Tajikistan, Uzbekistan.
    - 2017 (Astana Summit): **India and Pakistan** admitted as full members.
    - 2023 (New Delhi Summit): **Iran** admitted as 9th member.

- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** Iran was inducted in 2023 (under India's presidency); Belarus was inducted in **2024**!

### Question 419
**Subject:** Medieval Indian History | **Topic:** Central Administrative Officers of the Mughal Court

Match List-I (Mughal Central Officer) with List-II (Portfolio / Official Function) and select the correct answer:

**List-I (Officer)**
A. Mir Bakshi  
B. Mir Saman (Khan-i-Saman)  
C. Sadr-us-Sudur  
D. Muhtasib  

**List-II (Function)**
1. In-charge of the imperial household, royal workshops (*Karkhanas*), and internal palace supplies  
2. Head of the military department; issued *Sarkhat* (pay warrants); verified mansabdars' horses and descriptive rolls  
3. Public censor of morals; enforced Islamic code, proper weights/measures, and prevented open drinking  
4. Chief advisor on religious matters; in-charge of royal judicial endowments and charity (*Madad-i-Maash*)  

**Options:**
A. A-2, B-1, C-4, D-3  
B. A-1, B-2, C-4, D-3  
C. A-2, B-4, C-1, D-3  
D. A-3, B-1, C-4, D-2  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - **Mir Bakshi:** Supreme head of the military establishment; inspected cavalry contingents (*Dagh-o-Chehra*); presented candidates for imperial mansabs to the emperor; signed pay certificates (*Sarkhat*).
  - **Mir Saman:** Controlled the imperial *Karkhanas* (royal manufacturing factories), palace stores, and royal kitchen.
  - **Sadr-us-Sudur:** Guardian of Islamic jurisprudence; administered religious land grants (*Suyurghal*); often held concurrent post of *Qazi-ul-Quzat* (Chief Justice).
  - **Muhtasib:** Censor of public morals; appointed to ensure public decorum, gambling prevention, and correct weights in bazaars.
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** Mir Bakshi was NOT the commander-in-chief of the army! The **Emperor himself was the Supreme Commander**; the Mir Bakshi was the head of the military administrative department.

B. Article 44 (Uniform Civil Code)  
C. Article 39A (Equal justice and free legal aid)  
D. Article 47 (Prohibition of intoxicating drinks)  

**List-II (Ideological Classification)**
1. Liberal-Intellectual Principle  
2. Gandhian Principle  
3. Socialistic Principle  
4. Gandhian Principle  

*Note: Match carefully knowing Article 40 and 47 are Gandhian.*

**Options:**
A. A-2, B-1, C-3, D-4 (or A-4, B-1, C-3, D-2)  
B. A-1, B-2, C-3, D-4  
C. A-2, B-3, C-1, D-4  
D. A-3, B-1, C-2, D-4  

**Answer:** A

### Question 422
**Subject:** General Science (Biology) | **Topic:** Vaccines and Immunization

Match List-I (Vaccine) with List-II (Disease Protected Against) and select the correct answer:

**List-I (Vaccine)**
A. BCG (Bacillus Calmette-Guérin)  
B. Salk / Sabin Vaccine  
C. MMR Vaccine  
D. TAB Vaccine  

**List-II (Disease)**
1. Poliomyelitis (Polio)  
2. Tuberculosis (TB)  
3. Typhoid fever  
4. Measles, Mumps, and Rubella  

**Options:**
A. A-2, B-1, C-4, D-3  
B. A-1, B-2, C-4, D-3  
C. A-2, B-4, C-1, D-3  
D. A-3, B-1, C-4, D-2  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - **BCG:** Live attenuated strain of *Mycobacterium bovis*; developed by Albert Calmette and Camille Guérin in 1921; given intradermally to neonates to protect against **Tuberculosis**.
  - **Polio Vaccines:**
    - Jonas Salk (1955): Inactivated Polio Vaccine (IPV - injectable).
    - Albert Sabin (1961): Oral Polio Vaccine (OPV - live attenuated drops).
  - **MMR:** Protects against **Measles, Mumps, and Rubella** (German measles).
  - **TAB:** Vaccine protecting against **Typhoid and Paratyphoid A & B**.
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** DPT vaccine is a triple antigen protecting against **Diphtheria, Pertussis (Whooping Cough), and Tetanus**!

### Question 424
**Subject:** Environment & Ecology | **Topic:** Ecological Pyramids Master Summary

Which of the following ecological pyramids is **NEVER INVERTED** in any natural ecosystem?

**Options:**
A. Pyramid of Numbers  
B. Pyramid of Biomass  
C. Pyramid of Energy  
D. Both Pyramid of Numbers and Biomass  

**Answer:** C

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - **Pyramid of Energy:** ALWAYS upright, without exception. In accordance with the Second Law of Thermodynamics and Lindeman's 10% law, energy is dissipated as heat at every trophic level.
  - **Pyramid of Biomass:** Can be inverted in aquatic ecosystems (standing crop of phytoplankton is small compared to long-lived zooplankton and fish).
  - **Pyramid of Numbers:** Can be inverted in parasite food chains or a single tree ecosystem supporting thousands of insects and hyperparasites.
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** Only the **Pyramid of Energy** is 100% strictly upright across all biomes on Earth!

### Question 425
**Subject:** Modern Indian History | **Topic:** Revolt of 1857 Leaders & Suppressors

Match List-I (Center of 1857 Revolt) with List-II (British Officer Who Suppressed It) and select the correct answer:

**List-I (Center)**
A. Delhi  
B. Kanpur  
C. Jhansi  
D. Lucknow  

**List-II (British Officer)**
1. Sir Hugh Rose  
2. John Nicholson and Lieutenant Hudson  
3. Sir Colin Campbell  
4. Sir Colin Campbell  

*Note: Kanpur and Lucknow both saw Colin Campbell's victorious relief campaigns.*

**Options:**
A. A-2, B-3, C-1, D-4 (or A-2, B-4, C-1, D-3)  
B. A-1, B-2, C-4, D-3  
C. A-2, B-1, C-4, D-3  
D. A-3, B-1, C-4, D-2  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - **Delhi:** John Nicholson stormed Delhi in September 1857 (Nicholson was mortally wounded); Hudson arrested Bahadur Shah Zafar at Humayun's Tomb and shot his sons.
  - **Kanpur:** Recaptured by **Sir Colin Campbell** in December 1857; Nana Saheb escaped to Nepal; Tatya Tope joined Rani Lakshmibai.
  - **Jhansi:** Suppressed by **Sir Hugh Rose** (who famously eulogized Rani Lakshmibai: *"Here lay the woman who was the only man among the rebels"*).
  - **Lucknow:** Relieved and recaptured by **Sir Colin Campbell** and Outram in March 1858; Begum Hazrat Mahal took asylum in Nepal.

### Question 426
**Subject:** Indian Economy | **Topic:** Quantitative Tools of the Reserve Bank of India

Match List-I (Monetary Policy Tool) with List-II (Operational Definition) and select the correct answer:

**List-I (Tool)**
A. Repo Rate  
B. Reverse Repo Rate  
C. Cash Reserve Ratio (CRR)  
D. Statutory Liquidity Ratio (SLR)  

**List-II (Definition)**
1. Percentage of Net Demand and Time Liabilities (NDTL) that banks must keep with RBI in cash  
2. Rate at which RBI lends short-term funds to commercial banks against government securities  
3. Percentage of NDTL that commercial banks must maintain in liquid assets (cash, gold, approved G-Secs)  
4. Rate at which RBI borrows funds from commercial banks  

**Options:**
A. A-2, B-4, C-1, D-3  
B. A-1, B-2, C-4, D-3  
C. A-2, B-1, C-4, D-3  
D. A-3, B-4, C-1, D-2  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - **Repo Rate (Repurchase Option):** Central policy rate; commercial banks pledge government securities to borrow cash from RBI.
  - **Reverse Repo:** Rate at which banks deposit funds with RBI.
  - **CRR:** Mandated under Section 42 of RBI Act 1934; maintained in pure cash with RBI (earns zero interest).
  - **SLR:** Mandated under Section 24 of Banking Regulation Act 1949; held by banks themselves in liquid unencumbered assets.
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** Repo = RBI lends to banks; Reverse Repo = RBI borrows from banks!

### Question 427
**Subject:** Current Affairs | **Topic:** Nobel Prize in Physiology or Medicine 2024

The discovery of **microRNA** and its role in post-transcriptional gene regulation earned the 2024 Nobel Prize in Medicine for which two scientists?

**Options:**
A. Victor Ambros and Gary Ruvkun  
B. Katalin Kariko and Drew Weissman  
C. Svante Paabo and David Julius  
D. Emmanuelle Charpentier and Jennifer Doudna  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - Awarded to American scientists **Victor Ambros** (UMass Chan Medical School) and **Gary Ruvkun** (Harvard Medical School).
  - Discovered that tiny molecules of microRNA ($miRNA$) act as fundamental switches that govern gene expression in multicellular organisms.
  - Katalin Kariko and Drew Weissman won in 2023 for nucleoside base modifications enabling mRNA COVID-19 vaccines.
  - Svante Paabo won in 2022 for genomes of extinct hominins.
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** MicroRNA regulates gene expression at the POST-TRANSCRIPTIONAL stage (binding to mRNA)!

A. A-2, B-1, C-4, D-3  
B. A-1, B-2, C-4, D-3  
C. A-2, B-4, C-1, D-3  
D. A-3, B-1, C-4, D-2  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - **MRE XIII:** Kalinga War (261 BCE); Dhamma victory over Antiochus, Ptolemy, Antigonus, Magas, Alexander.
  - **MRE II:** Medical care for men and beasts; mentions Cholas, Pandyas, Satyaputra, Keralaputra.
  - **MRE XII:** Religious tolerance and concord (*Samavaya*).
  - **MRE I:** Prohibition of animal slaughter in royal kitchen and sacrificial rituals.
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** Ashoka's Greek and Aramaic bilingual edict was discovered at **Kandahar (Shar-i-Kuna, Afghanistan)**!

### Question 429
**Subject:** World Geography | **Topic:** Strategic Water Chokepoints

Match List-I (Global Strait) with List-II (Connecting Waters) and select the correct answer:

**List-I (Strait)**
A. Strait of Hormuz  
B. Bab-el-Mandeb  
C. Strait of Malacca  
D. Strait of Gibraltar  

**List-II (Waters Connected)**
1. Red Sea and Gulf of Aden  
2. Persian Gulf and Gulf of Oman  
3. Mediterranean Sea and Atlantic Ocean  
4. Andaman Sea and South China Sea  

**Options:**
A. A-2, B-1, C-4, D-3  
B. A-1, B-2, C-4, D-3  
C. A-2, B-4, C-1, D-3  
D. A-3, B-1, C-4, D-2  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - **Hormuz:** Persian Gulf $\leftrightarrow$ Gulf of Oman (oil chokepoint).
  - **Bab-el-Mandeb:** Red Sea $\leftrightarrow$ Gulf of Aden ("Gate of Tears").
  - **Malacca:** Andaman Sea $\leftrightarrow$ South China Sea.
  - **Gibraltar:** Atlantic Ocean $\leftrightarrow$ Mediterranean Sea.
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** Hormuz links Persian Gulf to Gulf of Oman; Bab-el-Mandeb links Red Sea to Gulf of Aden!

### Question 430
**Subject:** Indian Polity | **Topic:** Rajya Sabha Exclusive Federal Powers

Under the Indian Constitution, the Rajya Sabha possesses exclusive powers to:
1. Pass a resolution authorizing Parliament to make a law on a State List subject in national interest (Article 249).
2. Pass a resolution authorizing Parliament to create one or more new All-India Services (Article 312).
3. Originate a resolution for the removal of the Vice-President of India (Article 67(b)).

Which of the statements given above are correct?

**Options:**
A. 1 and 2 only  
B. 2 and 3 only  
C. 1 and 3 only  
D. 1, 2 and 3  

**Answer:** D

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - All three are exclusive constitutional prerogatives of the Rajya Sabha:
    - **Article 249:** 2/3rd majority of members present and voting allows Parliament to legislate on State List (valid 1 year).
    - **Article 312:** 2/3rd majority of members present and voting allows Parliament to create new All-India Services (like IAS, IPS, IFS).
    - **Article 67(b):** Vice-President removal can originate ONLY in Rajya Sabha (passed by effective majority and agreed to by Lok Sabha).
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** Indian Forest Service (IFS) was created as the 3rd All-India Service in **1966** under Article 312!

### Question 431
**Subject:** General Science (Physics) | **Topic:** Daily Optical Phenomena

Match List-I (Optical Phenomenon) with List-II (Core Physics Principle) and select the correct answer:

**List-I (Phenomenon)**
A. Mirage in hot deserts  
B. Blue appearance of the clear sky  
C. Primary Rainbow  
D. Colors in soap bubbles  

**List-II (Principle)**
1. Rayleigh Scattering of light inversely proportional to $\lambda^4$  
2. Total Internal Reflection (TIR)  
3. Interference of light waves in thin films  
4. Dispersion, Refraction, and Internal Reflection in water droplets  

**Options:**
A. A-2, B-1, C-4, D-3  
B. A-1, B-2, C-4, D-3  
C. A-2, B-4, C-1, D-3  
D. A-3, B-1, C-4, D-2  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - **Mirage:** Total Internal Reflection.
  - **Blue Sky:** Rayleigh Scattering ($\propto rac{1}{\lambda^4}$).
  - **Rainbow:** Dispersion, Refraction, and Internal Reflection.
  - **Soap Bubble Colors:** Thin Film Interference.
- **आयोग का Trap / Ratta Point:** Blue sky is Scattering; Rainbow is Dispersion/Refraction; Soap bubble is Interference!

C. Frictional Unemployment  
D. Cyclical Unemployment  

**List-II (Feature)**

2. Marginal physical productivity of labor is zero; common in Indian agriculture  
3. Results from periodic trade cycles and macro-economic demand recessions  
4. Temporary phase while a person is searching for a job or transitioning between jobs  

**Options:**
A. A-2, B-1, C-4, D-3  
B. A-1, B-2, C-4, D-3  
C. A-2, B-4, C-1, D-3  
D. A-3, B-1, C-4, D-2  

**Answer:** A

**Explanation:**
- **Core Fact & Solution:**
  - Disguised = Marginal productivity zero (Agriculture); Structural = Skill mismatch; Frictional = Search time; Cyclical = Demand slump.
